## IORIO Signal Engine — probabilistic trade-scoring proposal (research phase complete 2026-09-16; Phase 0 data foundation started 2026-09-21)

- **New valuation model for Pluto (2026-10-10, approved by Marcelo; pushed to staging 2026-10-12).** Plain-language: each option is now valued like an insurance policy priced from how much the stock has really moved in the last 21 trading days, with time counted in trading sessions (a weekend adds almost nothing; at 10:00 about a third of today's movement is still ahead) and with the occasional big jumps these stocks make. From that one valuation Pluto gets the expected profit per contract (the new Edge $), the edge margin (how far the price is above fair value; Strong ≥ 20%, Good ≥ 10%, Weak ≥ 0%, as in `gradeMarginCutPoints`), the chance of profit, average win and loss, the worst-5% loss, and what history says such a sale really earned; it sizes with quarter-Kelly on that historical figure, which in practice keeps it to contracts of about two weeks or less unless the margin is very large. Every morning prediction is logged (`signal_model_predictions`) to check the model monthly. Also fixed: the jobs report is now in the macro calendar (heavy). Details, decisions and evidence: `docs/signal-engine-model-review/REPORT.md` (Round 4). Same day, Marcelo: a covered call's risk figures count the shares with the call (judged exactly like a cash-secured put); the history table is fitted so a richer price never maps to a lower result, with confidence ranges. Screens built 2026-10-12 from the approved mockup (https://claude.ai/artifact/9hpMdktY6KQnHXYn4JmDC2): Signals table grouped Valuation model / Day basis / Old Edge, rows and top signal ranked by Hist Yield (API `pickBestCandidate`, rolls by `compareRollsByModel`, roll badge on the model grade), phone cards, ticker modal (day-basis header, rolls and opportunities on the model, chain cells graded by the model), order panel valuation box with Pluto's Kelly · stress caps (`POST /pluto/sizing-caps`, display only), Pluto compare panels (top pick by Hist Yield with the runner-up, stored in `summarizeTopPick`). Next: check on staging after the open (seed, `signal_model_predictions`, Pluto passes, live Signals).
- **Model grade cut points lowered (2026-10-12, formula approved by Marcelo; pushed to staging 2026-10-12).** Plain-language: the valuation model's grades now read Strong at an edge margin of 20% or more, Good 10% to 20%, Weak 0% to 10%, Avoid below 0% (they were 30 / 20 / 10%). Pluto's minimum grade stays at Good on staging (Marcelo), so Pluto is now offered contracts from a 10% margin instead of 20%. The buy-back rule keeps its threshold: a credit roll with an edge margin of 10% or more withholds the buy-back, now a margin of its own (`buybackWithheldAtRollEdgeMargin`) instead of the Weak grade. Pluto prompt v4.3. The old Edge grade (volatility points; Telegram upgrade alerts) is unchanged.
- **Exposure, yields and Pluto's minimum (2026-10-12, Marcelo; pushed to staging 2026-10-12).** Plain-language: EXP $ now subtracts the premium received everywhere: a put's cash reserved less its premium, a covered call's shares less the call's premium. Positions (EXP $, EXP %, P&L %), Telegram's P&L %, Pluto's budget and limits, the order checks, Signals' yield and the model's yields all use it. Every yield (Edge Yield, Hist Yield, premium yield) is a yearly rate over calendar days, weekends included. Pluto's quality minimum is now an Edge Yield (% a year) instead of dollars per contract, so short-dated contracts are not penalized; default 15% (approved). Unchanged on purpose (approved): free cash (IBKR's balance already holds the premium) and the live exposure behind concentration and allocation (the option at its live price). **Technical:** `positionQueries.ts` (open short leg's premium; covered call: open short calls), `orderLimits.ts` (`premium`, `rollFromPremium`; route `GET /order-checks/limits` requires `premium`), `postModelGates.ts` (unit notional at the limit price), `actionExposure.ts` (buyback frees strike − sale premium), `contractValuation.ts` / `signalCandidates.ts` / `rollSignalCandidates.ts` (dollar risk was strike × 100 − per-share mid, now (strike − mid) × 100); migration `20261012100001` (`min_edge_yield_pct`); app forms send the premium. REPORT Round 6.
- **Option chain and order yields are after costs (2026-10-12, Marcelo; pushed to staging 2026-10-12).** Plain-language: the Yield column on the Signals option chain, the yield on the order form for a contract picked from it, and the Order Review panel's Ann. Yield now take the trading costs off the premium first, the same costs the valuation model charges, so the chain's Yield matches the model's premium yield. Formula: (mid × 100 − friction) ÷ exposure × 365 ÷ DTE, friction = spread cost share (Risk & Limits) × half-spread × 100 + $0.68 commission per contract. Example: $40 put, bid 0.45 / ask 0.55, 7 DTE, 50% spread cost: friction $3.18, yield 62% (was 66%). Without a two-sided quote there is no half-spread to charge, so no yield is shown. **Technical:** the chain payload and the order quote stream (`GET /positions/orders/:id/quote/stream`) carry `spreadShareCharged` (`signalsChain.ts`, `signalsChainStore.ts`, `routes/positions.ts`); app `lib/payoff.ts` (`computeFrictionDollarsPerContract`, optional `frictionDollarsPerContract` on `computeAnnualizedYield`), `SignalsOptionChainCard.tsx`, `ChainContractOrderSetupForm.tsx`, `OrderReviewPanel.tsx`; the tooltips name the exposure net of the premium.
- **Signals "not accounted for" list brought up to date (2026-10-12, Marcelo; pushed to staging 2026-10-12, API `8975b48` / app `b894857`).** Plain-language: the list of what the model doesn't use yet had gone stale since the valuation model. Skew and SVI stability are gone (the model review settled both); friction shows the Risk & Limits spread cost actually in force (it said λ = 1.0); sizing now says half-Kelly on Edge $ exists and what's still missing (Kelly on proven outcomes, CVaR); earnings waits on a decision, not data; drift/momentum and the risk-adjusted ratio wait on the ThetaData backtests. **Technical:** `signalsRoadmap.ts` (no date-projected items left; counts are fills + `spreadCostChargedPct`), `loadRoadmapCounts` drops the night and earnings queries; app footnote in `SignalsPage.tsx`.
- **Wheel research from a naive baseline (Marcelo 2026-10-13; committed, not pushed; backtests local only).** Plain-language: instead of tuning Pluto's dozens of settings (21 runs on AAOI/GSAT/HOOD/INTC 2022–23 gained nothing reliable: [report](https://claude.ai/artifact/FefJpV6XYNSsgKc5RhtWsR)), the backtest now has a deliberately naive wheel to build up from, adding one rule at a time and keeping it only if return and drawdown improve across many stocks and years. Findings and plan: [Wheel Research Log](https://claude.ai/artifact/7obX1ikB3NYtRQYDJWPn5d). So far: measured against a static mix of the same stocks and T-bills with the same exposure, the naive wheel shows no edge (it is a small stock exposure plus T-bills; one stock, AAOI, carries the portfolios); the Signals filter (sell only when IV beats the 21-day forecast) and Edge Yield ranking did not improve it on 5 stocks; skipping sales that span earnings is the only rule pointing the right way; writing fewer calls adds exposure, not edge. Rules are now judged by alpha over that matched mix with paired tests. **Technical:** decision maker `"baseline"` (`backtestBaselineLook.ts`, settings `baseline.*`: entry, delta/DTE ranges, call strike rule (incl. `none`), `callDeltaRange` entry_range/own_range with `callMinAbsDelta`/`callMaxAbsDelta`, take-profit, share stop, earnings skip, max % per stock, look frequency, `volatilityEdgeFilter` off/entries/all_sales with `minVolatilityEdgePoints`, `ranking` premium_yield/edge_yield); ledger event `buyBackCoveredCallKeepingShares`; `backtest_trades` model columns nullable (migration `20261013300001`); app shows "—" for a baseline trade's model figures. Split check: a gap of up to 30 file days between two stretches of the same factor takes it when its median ratio is nearest it (AAOI 2022's thin quotes; cache version 2). Pluto mode unchanged (full-row diff IDENTICAL on session 6be468fd). Parked, inactive: per-session Signals model parameters (surface-fit limits, forecast window, grade cut points, ranking, Kelly basis) in the shared libs, defaults = live.
- **Sortino Strategy Lab (Marcelo 2026-10-13; research only, nothing in the engine yet).** Plain-language: looking for strategies that come close to buy and hold (CAGR within 2 points a year) with a clearly better Sortino (+0.2), trading only the stock itself and its own options plus T-bills, no leverage; adding another ticker that did better does not count, and signals come only from the stock's own prices and options; ETFs count as symbols when they have tradable options (Marcelo 2026-10-13). INTC 2020–26 is the lab, AAOI, HOOD, MU, NOK, TLT the design set, 17 unseen symbols the hold-out. Nothing viable so far ([Sortino Strategy Lab](https://claude.ai/artifact/5FYvVqGanjagwHpEgmhn8Q)): 12-month momentum and stepping out around earnings looked close on the six and failed the hold-out (2–3 of 17); 17 option variants (puts through earnings, puts while momentum is out, tail puts, cheap-only earnings puts, calls instead of shares, calls while out) each help INTC and few others; volatility-managed exposure, an SPY trend filter, report persistence and post-report drift fail on 23 symbols. Why: Sortino does not move when part of the account sits in T-bills, and on these symbols puts paid back less than they cost and calls more (the stocks' rise), so protection is dear; timing missed the V-shaped rebounds of 2020, 2023 and 2025. Long-dated options (2026-10-14, once the files held every expiry; 8 rules): none viable. T-bills plus 2-year calls rolled once a year cleared the bar on INTC at every end date (CAGR 22.9% vs 10.6%, Sortino 1.08 vs 0.58, drawdown −40% vs −70%) and on no other stock: a 2-year call cost 28–54% of the price on MU, HOOD and AAOI, and AAOI listed no long-dated options during its 2023 rebound; calls only while implied volatility is below its median cleared it on INTC and TLT, 1-year puts at 80% on MU only. **Technical:** `tmp/strategy-lab/` (lab.py harness priced like the engine, options.py option layer with the engine's fills and two-sided rule, waves 1–11, option_returns*.py, option_signals.py, long_dated_summary.py); short-dated option work reads `backtest-data/backups-before-every-expiry/` (0–45 days), long-dated (wave8.py, wave11.py) the completed files and splits come from the engine's `splits/<SYMBOL>.json`.
- **Covered calls on Marcelo's rules, INTC first (Marcelo 2026-10-13; uncommitted; backtests local only).** Plain-language: Covered Call entries into INTC and calls on every share (the most premium per day, at or above the average price held, else the break-even), held to expiry, options valued only on the last day. Now with three dials: the account's share in stock (S), calls only above a yield (Y), and otherwise one put at a time, its exposure B% of the account per day to expiry, strike at or below the average. 75 combinations on INTC 2020 → 2026-10 from $200k ([INTC Calls and Puts](https://claude.ai/artifact/58ujBff8VCQsrDWgsHvjXZ)): best +62.9% (S 90, Y 30, B 2; its neighbours +34–49%), median +39%, the old rules +28%, buy and hold +74%. 56 of 75 runs had 3+ years with no premium: assigned puts turn the cash into shares in a fall (puts are not limited by S), then nothing can be sold until the price recovers. A call x standard deviations up was tried and dropped (coverage up, total down). Awaiting Marcelo's verdict. **Technical:** baseline settings `ranking premium_per_day`, `callStrike above_average_cost_else_break_even`, `maxSharesPctOfAccount` (market value), `minCallYieldPct`, `putsWhenNoCall dte_budget` + `putBudgetPctPerDte`, `shareFill ten_oclock_price`, `dayValueOptions excluded`; σ options and the reserve puts removed. All backtests value an option with no bid at the first two-sided quote later that day (`backtestOptionMarks.ts`). Research scripts `tmp/wheel-research/rules_report.py`, `grid_summary.py`.
- **Pluto execution strategies (Marcelo 2026-10-14; Edge Margin is Pluto's existing code, the CC + Put Ladder built in local commits, not pushed).** Plain-language: Pluto gets a switch between execution strategies, one live at a time. The one Pluto runs today becomes "Edge Margin", with its own rules and settings kept inside it; a new "CC + Put Ladder" runs the backtested rules inside Pluto's existing order management and safety rules (Marcelo 2026-10-15: only the strategy criteria split; everything else Pluto does stays for both). Outside Pluto, the screens Juan trades from stay, moved onto the valuation model wherever they still use Net Edge. Decided 2026-10-14: "Net Edge" (the old 63-day Edge score, no strategy carries that name) is retired everywhere (Marcelo 2026-10-14, final): the Signals "Old Edge · phasing out" columns go; the upgrade alerts and Pulse events, Pulse's Top Signals panel, the order-form decay warnings, the ticker modal and the top-signal choice move to the valuation model (Edge Margin); the hidden filters that drop contracts, rolls and buybacks when Net Edge cannot be computed are replaced by the model's own requirements only; verified 2026-10-14: about 25 places besides the three columns, and the Net Edge gates removed nothing the model could value on any recorded data (0 of 9,011 contracts in 150 stored 10:00 snapshots); found alongside: Order Review's first vega is about 100× too large until the live tick, Pluto's "Forecast RV" is blank on every v4.0+ row, expiry-day held legs get no assignment-risk alert (no surface, so no delta), the held-leg fit check refuses P2 buybacks the model could value, and the model grade is cut before costs while Edge $ is after; decided 2026-10-14: upgrade alerts fire when the model grade rises and the edge margin clears the new grade's cut by 2 points, and only with Edge $ above zero; the order-form warning fires when the edge margin falls 2 points or more after the contract was picked; a ticker the model cannot value gets no top signal and its row says why; Pluto history labels its pre-v4.0 figures as the old Edge; fixes approved: Order Review's first vega, Pluto's blank "Forecast RV", and the assignment-risk alert for expiry-day held legs (falling back to the live quote's delta); the ladder's volume rule reads each contract's previous-session volume from IBKR after the close (Marcelo 2026-10-15: no ThetaData dependency for Pluto; measured 2026-10-14: the after-close quote holds the full day's volume for hours and equals ThetaData's end-of-day volume (HOOD 14 Oct: 920 of 922 contracts identical, the 20% cap the same for all), the 3,180 contracts in the ladder's delta range across 20 tickers take about 5 minutes at ~10 a second, and the contract list comes from the stored chain; Marcelo 2026-10-15: captured as its own job run inside the 09:00 UTC chain structure job, after the structure refresh, for the ladder's tickers and its first-look delta band at the previous close, built with the live ladder; if IBKR no longer holds the volume at 04:00–05:00 ET, its own run at 21:30 UTC instead; measured 2026-10-15: at 04:00 ET every test contract read 0 (reset overnight), so the capture runs after the close at 21:30 UTC); the ladder adopts a 50% spread limit and orders at the mid (grid at 70% shares, 10 tickers: median Excess Calmar 0.25 vs 0.23, CAGR 15.1% vs 14.1%, days without options 20% vs 33%, Max drawdown −41.4% vs −39.8%); the backtest's spread limit is a setting (baseline.maxSpreadPctOfMid) whose default stays 15% so past sessions re-run unchanged; live ladder: looks as often as Pluto runs (its first look of the day waits for the 10:00 capture since 2026-10-15, below), each ticker its own pot from Pluto's allocation with dividends and interest to the shared pool, re-quoting designed to cover every contract that can qualify without polling the whole chain, and a plain-English explanation of each strategy (order levels, rungs, order management) shown to users and kept current with every code change; the ladder's own delta range and caps apply to Pluto's ladder orders only, manual orders keep the platform's; CC + Put Ladder decides by rules only (one implementation shared by live and the backtest, no AI call) and its orders get their own delta range and caps instead of the platform's 0.10–0.50 band and 20%/10% limits; each ticker gets an allocation % of Pluto's capital budget, which stays the ceiling over every strategy (default an equal split); checks for the ladder: Pluto-wide safety kept (master switch, trading halt, window, IBKR worker, reconciliation, order-error and fill-slippage breakers, readiness, deploy and crash-loop pauses, max enabled tickers, cash reserve, limit-price and 15 s quote checks, one position per contract, unfilled-order sweep), the daily-loss breaker and the actions cap with ladder-specific values and added to the ladder backtest, SPY stress, ticker cooldown, one working order per ticker and max open positions kept with a value per strategy (Marcelo 2026-10-15), none of Edge Margin's own checks; orders as backtested (several sales per look, limit at the backtest's fill, re-priced at the next look); sells through earnings as backtested; its own share level (default 50%) and a shock the system recommends and the user can override; switching happens with Pluto paused and the active strategy takes over every open position; the ticker configuration screen warns when a ticker cannot work with its values (one ladder Put must fit a rung, the share budget must buy one lot) and shows the ranges that work. Shock formula approved 2026-10-14: the stock's Max drawdown over the 10 years before the cycle, kept within 60–80%; 90% when those years hold under 250 closes (a new listing). In the backtest engine as `ladderShockNoHistoryPct` (default 90). Ticker analysis for curation (Marcelo 2026-10-14, in the same design round): an "Analyse" option on each downloaded ticker in the Backtesting screen, opening a modal; the result (figures and summary) is stored and re-opened without recomputing, recomputed with an "Update Analysis" button; candlestick chart (All, 10Y, 5Y, 2Y, 1Y), Max drawdown, historical volatility chart, option liquidity and yield by year and DTE range in red/amber/green (yield against a minimum the user sets), a monthly bar chart of the contracts passing the strategy's filters (as on the SMCI Close-up page, without the price line; Marcelo 2026-10-14), fit at the configured values, the recommended shock, the ticker's backtest results, implied vs realised volatility, earnings and dividends, data quality and history depth, correlation with SPY and the selected tickers; a chain yield map for the last stored day (Marcelo 2026-10-14): for each delta band (0.05–0.10, 0.10–0.20, 0.20–0.30, 0.30–0.40, above 0.40) a bar from 0% along the yield axis, shaded darker where more contracts sit (scaled within each band), showing where yield sits in the chain, with a toggle that adds the contracts the strategy's filters leave out; stock profile numbers (trend, swings, jumps, trending vs range-bound) shown as information now (everything computed in code), and a plain-language summary in the first version (Marcelo 2026-10-14), written by Claude through Marcelo's subscription CLI as the backtest's AI mode does, from the computed figures only, tested as parameter predictors once ~30 tickers are backtested. HOOD with the approved shock rule: Max drawdown −30.3% / −49.0% / −61.5% at 30/50/70% shares (80% cap: −53.9% / −65.5% / −70.2%). Decided 2026-10-15 (Marcelo): re-quoting at each look covers the contracts whose delta at the current price, from each contract's last-seen IV, is within the ladder's delta range widened by a margin, the range's top being a strategy setting (top + 0.10 and bottom − 0.02, at the first look of the day top + 0.15 and bottom − 0.03, never below 0: so 0.03–0.40 at today's 0.05–0.30); rung pace: at most one new rung every 60 minutes per ticker, backtested 2026-10-15 against any look (30 minutes) and against 60 minutes with at most 2 a session on 10 tickers at 30/50/70% shares (backtest settings baseline.ladderMinutesBetweenRungs and baseline.ladderMaxRungsPerSession, both 0 = off by default): the pace changes results little and either way, never lowers Max drawdown, and leaves 3–4 tickers identical; Marcelo 2026-10-15: no pace rule, a new rung at any look as backtested (extra logic for a negligible change); a ladder Put instead of a Call on free shares whenever its premium yield is higher (no margin; see the Put-versus-Call entry below); unfilled orders wait for the 15-minute sweep and are re-priced at the next look, a partial fill moves on; routine order problems (drifted price, missing quote, tick size, margin refusal) are blocked and retried at the next look instead of tripping the order-error breaker, and limit prices are rounded to the cent for both strategies, a sale up and a buy down (Marcelo 2026-10-21: any cent, no $0.05 step; SMART accepts any cent, verified 2026-10-05); an early assignment settles the cycle on the assignment date (platform fix, also for Edge Margin and Signals); the daily caps no longer stop closes that reduce risk, and the ladder gets its own cap; each pot starts at allocation % × budget and then follows its own P&L; manual trades are locked on ladder tickers; each strategy's "How it works" is shown in Pluto with its numbers read from the live settings, with a CLAUDE.md rule and a test that fails when a described value drifts. Found 2026-10-15 while checking the Edge Margin explanation against the code (awaiting a decision): an unfilled odd-lot share sale (P1) is sent again at the first round after the 15-minute sweep, without the one-hour wait the earnings closes have, and each attempt counts toward the session's order cap; a roll the model picks is re-checked on fresh quotes only for still being a candidate and the Covered Call break-even, not for its grade or the new leg's open filters (opens get the full check); a cooling ticker's rolls still reach the model and are refused afterwards. Decided 2026-10-15 (Marcelo, live build moved to its own session): the active strategy is a typed setting on pluto_settings (`execution_strategy`), changed only while Pluto is paused; the ladder's strategy-wide values are typed `ladder_*` columns on pluto_settings (validated, audited, versioned per pass like every Pluto setting), named after the backtest keys; the choices the strategy design fixes (entry, Call strike rule, coverage, ladder top, shock method, Put-versus-Call) stay constants in the strategy code, not settings; per-ticker overrides (allocation %, share level, shock override; empty = the default) go in a new `pluto_ticker_settings` table with its own audit trail, read through one function, `loadLadderSettingsForTicker(symbol)`, which the Analysis modal also uses; a pot's value for the ladder's base is the pot's own ledger (allocation + premiums − purchases + call-away proceeds − assignments − commissions, shares and options at market), so it follows the ticker's own P&L as in the backtest; the ladder keeps its own running average cost (as the backtest does), not IBKR's FIFO cost; the Net Edge retirement and the four approved fixes follow the ladder in the same session. Ladder defaults approved 2026-10-15 (Marcelo): delta 0.05–0.30, 1–45 DTE, spread ≤ 50% of the mid, 5 rungs, share level 50%, shock 60–80% (90% under 250 closes) over 10 years; a ticker without an allocation gets (100% − the allocations set) ÷ the enabled tickers without one, and saving is refused above 100%. At most 5 of a ticker's ladder orders work at once, the rest go at the next looks (Marcelo 2026-10-15: the backtest's 14 sales at one look were one decision split by the volume rule across contracts, AAOI covering 89 lots on thin options; live pots are smaller). Built 2026-10-15 (step 1 of 8, uncommitted; no screen yet): migration 20261015300001 (the strategy switch and the ladder's columns on pluto_settings, pluto_ticker_settings with its audit), the per-ticker store with the equal split (src/pluto/tickerSettingsStore.ts), `loadLadderSettingsForTicker` (src/pluto/ladderSettings.ts, the design's fixed choices as constants), routes GET/PUT /pluto/ticker-settings[/:symbol], /pluto/ticker-settings/audit and GET /pluto/ladder-settings/:symbol; the switch is refused unless Pluto is off or paused. Ladder safety values (Marcelo 2026-10-15): SPY stress 5%, ticker cooldown off, and the daily-loss breaker stays one Pluto-wide value for both strategies (5% of the account); at most 5 open positions per ticker (Marcelo: the backtests gave some tickers more money than their options could take; caveat: 5 rungs plus one open Call is already 6, so the cap can stop the last rung; it bound on 17 of 19 tickers in the $200k adopted-settings runs); actions cap 30 orders per ticker per day, every order sent counting, re-prices included (the backtest's busiest days reached 18 sales; one unfilled order can be re-priced about 26 times a day). Step 2 built 2026-10-15: those four values are settings (migration 20261015300002); Pluto's block reason includes the selected strategy, so while the ladder's decision code does not exist, Resume and switching on are refused under it and the screen, the agent and the system checks all say why; Edge Margin runs exactly as before. Also built 2026-10-15 (commits local, not pushed; none in the VPS worker's closure): the recommended shock per ticker from the stored daily closes, with the backtest's own rule (06e1d35); the backtest's sale steps moved to src/backtest/engine/baselineSaleSteps.ts behind a book interface, so live and the backtest share one implementation (81308ec; every result row identical on 7 sessions, same speed); the re-quoting band and the day's universe (1fb1fc7; the IV-learning steps of the design, the median shift per expiry and the smile for unknown IVs, are formulas still to approve); a person's orders locked on the ladder's tickers while it is the active strategy (0db9b49); an early assignment settles on the assignment day's close, so the break-even no longer waits for the expiry date's bar (27749e3; no early assignment on dev or staging yet, tested on a synthetic one). Held back: closes exempt from the daily caps, because the actions cap is today the only limit on the unfilled odd-lot share sale being re-sent every round (the P1 finding above); the two go together. Approved fixes done 2026-10-15 (app, local commits): Pluto's "Forecast RV" shows again on v4.0+ prompts (the prompt renamed it forecast_vol; app 5e6fda3); Order Review's first vega is per volatility point like IBKR's (the Signals candidate's vega is per 1.00 of volatility, 100× IBKR's: median ratio 99.4 on 3,000 stored quotes; app 0c49613). Not done, needing a decision: the expiry-day assignment-risk alert (the Day Signals quotes carry no IBKR delta, so the fallback needs the greeks line for that day's expiring held legs, testable only with the market open) and the poorly-fitted-surface fix (same IBKR delta fallback; its P2 rule, a buyback when buying back at the ask keeps at least 75% of the premium, is not in the approved list here). Per-ticker overrides extended 2026-10-15 (Marcelo, via the Analysis modal session): rungs and the |delta| range per ticker, empty = the strategy's value (cbbd300); the ladder settings reader also returns the strategy's values and the ticker's overrides apart; the Pluto screen's per-ticker settings UI is built by the Analysis modal session; the per-ticker pot stays the allocation % (pending Marcelo's answer). The ladder's minimum bid ($0.05) and volume share (20%) stay fixed constants (Marcelo 2026-10-15, asked by the backtesting session). Volume capture built 2026-10-15 (Marcelo approved the design the same day; not yet run against IBKR): job `option_session_volume_capture` (`job:option-session-volume`, Heroku Scheduler entry to add, 21:30 UTC market days), for every ticker Pluto is enabled on whichever strategy is active; it stores each quoted contract's full-session volume and IBKR implied volatility in `option_session_volumes`; the contracts are the next session's 1–45 DTE universe (every strike, both rights) narrowed to the first-look re-quoting band at the stock's last price from each contract's last stored IV, every contract with no stored IV included, so the first night quotes the whole universe (~35 min for 20 tickers) and later nights the band (~5 min); a ticker without a stored chain is an error of its own (completed with errors), all tickers failing is a failure. Pots decided 2026-10-15 (Marcelo, via the Analysis modal session), replacing "the pot's own ledger" above: a ticker's pot is its allocation % of Pluto's capital budget, recomputed from the current account value, with no per-ticker ledger; the ladder's base is the larger of allocation × budget × the account at the cycle's start and allocation × budget × the account today, so it moves with the whole account. Decided 2026-10-15 (Marcelo): a cycle already open at the switch starts the ladder's memory at the switch; the order gate checks IBKR's delta (tolerance proposed: 0.01, awaiting his answer); commissions as Pluto computes them (the trailing estimate per order size, the flat $0.68 when there are no fills), shared code since it is IBKR's and not the strategy's, the backtest keeping the flat rate; the three Edge Margin findings above are to be fixed, then closes exempt from the daily caps; the expiry-day assignment-risk alert may request IBKR's delta for the day's expiring held legs, and the poorly-fitted-surface fix (IBKR delta fallback, P2 buyback when buying back at the ask keeps at least 75% of the premium) is approved. The volume job's staging Scheduler entry exists (21:30 UTC) before the code is pushed. Also decided 2026-10-15 (Marcelo): positions held at the switch are taken over as they are (shares at the cycle's running average cost, Edge Margin's options to expiry, Puts counting as reserve committed); nothing is sold to fit the pot, the ladder only adds. Asked (open): IV and delta from one source, live and backtest alike (Pluto's surface, IBKR's, or each quote's own IV; the backtested ladder used each quote's own IV); re-quoting reuses Pluto's per-expiry intraday IV shift (Formula 3h) rather than new formulas. Edge Margin findings fixed: the odd-lot share sale now waits like every automatic close (cd027b8); a chosen roll is re-checked on fresh quotes as fully as an open, and a cooling ticker's rolls are no longer offered to the model (b49e18e). Peak (Marcelo 2026-10-15, replacing the market high used until then, live and backtest): the average cost of the shares held; it follows the holdings (down when ladder Puts are assigned lower) and never rises with the market, since selling Puts is meant to lower the break-even, not raise it; a cycle of Puts alone has no peak until it holds shares; nothing is remembered, so a ticker turned back on needs no reset (no reset hook, Marcelo); no backtest re-runs until he says. IV/delta sources measured on staging's last 150 10:00 captures (20,381 contracts in the ladder's range): the engine's own mid IV is 0.19 vol points from Pluto's surface (median; 99.1% within 0.01 delta) and 0.65 points from IBKR's (95.1% within 0.01 delta); IBKR's IV runs about 0.5 points lower than both; 4.1% of these contracts lie outside the surface's fitted range. Recommended: the ladder's own IV and delta everywhere, the order gate included. Decided 2026-10-15 (Marcelo), closing the open questions above: the ladder uses its own IV and delta everywhere (each quote's mid IV on the expiry's forward, then Black-76), the order gate included; the two fallback fixes (the expiry-day assignment-risk alert, the poorly-fitted-surface fix) use the live quote's own delta, not IBKR's; a cycle taken over with no shares and only Puts has no peak until its first shares (the strike, if the Puts are assigned). The expiry-day assignment-risk alert is done: a held leg expiring today, which has no surface delta, takes its own live quote's delta, time to expiry being the hours to today's close (6e9ed3c). The poorly-fitted-surface fix is done (f4c6c62): a held leg on an expiry or strike the surface cannot value also takes its own quote's delta for the assignment-risk alert (time to expiry in calendar days ÷ 365, as the surface counts it), and P2 offers such a leg's buyback to the model when buying back at the ask keeps at least 75% of the premium, with P2's other conditions (single leg, minimum DTE, no roll with a 10% edge margin, a live two-sided quote); a leg unscored for another reason (no quote, no forecast) is still not offered. Forward decided 2026-10-15 (Marcelo): the ladder's own IV and delta use Pluto's forward, the 10:00 put-call parity forward moved in proportion to the stock price (sticky moneyness), live and backtest alike; one shared helper, `forwardAtSpot` (14f8aea), used by Pluto's live scoring too; the backtest change (parity once at the day's first look instead of at every look) is the backtesting session's, re-run and diffed before committing. No extra quotes are needed for the forward. Re-quoting timing decided 2026-10-15 (Marcelo): the ladder's first look of the day waits for the 10:00 chain capture to finish (about 10:35), then quotes its own band; the ladder tickers' 1–45 DTE expiry and strike lists are re-fetched once a day. Decided 2026-10-15 (Marcelo, via the Coordinator session): the ladder's order gate checks the contract's own delta on the fresh quote against the ticker's ladder range with no tolerance (|delta| 0.305 is refused against 0.30); the rung-pace settings (ladderMinutesBetweenRungs, ladderMaxRungsPerSession) are removed from live and backtest; a stock split during a live cycle must be handled (design sent for sign-off: detection before the first look, the memory's prices and the trades read on the new basis, the ticker stopped until positions match IBKR); no push until the unfinished work is done. Built so far (local commits): cycle memory (895ff3f), live look book (0f954d4), its loaders (a43c2ce); pending Marcelo: the five order-route changes for ladder orders, and whether re-enabling a ticker restarts its cycle memory. Re-quoting built 2026-10-15 (41cf10d; Marcelo chose the Day Signals loop): the loop quotes each ladder ticker's band beside Signals' contracts, a contract both want quoted once, the band's quotes stored apart in ladder_band_quotes so the Signals screens are unchanged; a ladder ticker outside Signals gets its price in the spot pass and is never scored for Signals; with no Signals pool the ladder is quoted once the day's seed has finished; the loop runs in the web app, not on the VPS worker. The ladder tickers' expiry and strike lists come from the existing 09:00 UTC structure job (daily, a stored grid reused up to 7 days while the price stays inside it). Signals' refresh time with the band is to be measured on staging. Order route for ladder orders built 2026-10-15 (29fa131; Marcelo approved the five changes and added the pot cap): each Pluto action records its strategy (pluto_actions.execution_strategy, from its pass's settings); a ladder order is checked at confirm on the contract's own delta (the live mid's IV on Pluto's 10:00 forward moved with the price) against the ticker's ladder range with no tolerance, and on its pot (the ticker's exposure after the order, working orders included, at most allocation × capital budget × net liquidation value) in place of the max position and max concentration limits, the cash reserve kept; a Pluto Covered Call entry may ask to buy every share (buyEveryShare); a ladder order's snapshot (kind "ladder") must carry a live quote under 15 seconds old. Deploy note: the gates, limits and order route are web-app code; the VPS worker does not re-run them, it refuses any order whose stored confirm-time verdict has a block or is over 5 minutes old. Splits, part 1 built 2026-10-15 (58474e3): a split IBKR's daily history shows is recorded in stock_splits when detected (nightly bar job, and a ladder_split_check job run inside the 09:00 UTC structure job for the ladder's tickers), and a price move of about a split's factor with none recorded is a split to the ladder's look (splitSuggestedByPrice); part 3 (rewriting the platform's records, the ticker freeze) is the Coordinator session's. Live ladder built 2026-10-15 (local commits up to 462351e, not pushed, not yet run against IBKR): under the CC + Put Ladder each Pluto round is rules-only after the same system checks, at the ladder's SPY stress level (5%), skipping frozen tickers; each ticker's look runs the backtest's sale steps on its live book (positions, cycle, cash, working ladder orders counted as filled) and the loop's band quotes at the mid, saves its cycle memory, and sends the planned sales in order within the ladder's caps (5 working orders, 30 a day, 5 open positions per ticker), each re-quoted live and re-checked on its own delta before going through Pluto's executor; Pluto wakes on the band's quotes too. "How it works" built 2026-10-15 from the approved mockup (https://claude.ai/artifact/TYdARe8Sa6RuF5Lb1E1s2b; not pushed): a 5th Pluto tab explaining both strategies in plain language (Marcelo: "explain Edge Margin as well"), opening on the active one. Every number comes from GET /pluto/how-it-works (Marcelo chose this over numbers typed into the app): the live settings and the code's own constants, with both worked examples computed by the strategies' own functions; howItWorks.test.ts pins every quoted number, and a CLAUDE.md rule keeps the wording current. Strategy selector built 2026-10-15 (app 41206b5, not pushed; Marcelo: build it, no separate mockup, following the approved Pluto Ladder Settings mockup's Strategy group): the first group on Pluto's Settings tab, a select between Edge Margin and the CC + Put Ladder, locked while Pluto runs, saved through Review and save with a take-over warning; the status headline names the active strategy. Fixed the same day (app d4096bf): the status card's SPY stress banner quotes the active strategy's limit (ladder 5%, Edge Margin 3%); Pluto's segmented switches read 5.08:1 in light (87cda22). Checked against the code while writing it, decided above but not built: the daily-loss breaker at 5% (closed 2026-10-15, Marcelo: no migration; staging and local dev already hold 5% saved, the column default stays 2%), limit prices rounded to the cent on the order's side of the mid (Edge Margin rounded to the nearest cent), and routine order problems retried instead of tripping the order-error breaker (only build-time 400/404/409 are blocked; confirm failures and IBKR rejects still trip it). Also found: under Edge Margin a frozen ticker is not skipped by the round, the order route refuses it (409) and the action ends blocked; passRunner.ts's header still describes two agreeing model calls (the code makes one).
- **Analyse modal on Backtesting → Data files (Marcelo 2026-10-15; mockups approved; committed, not pushed: API 36fbc01 and 1c2b69f, app 65156d0; the Lab settings section committed (app 2760e6d); Pluto's ladder settings screen committed (API d5fee00, app f90b902)).** Plain-language: an Analyse button per downloaded ticker opens a near-full-screen window showing how well the ticker suits CC + Put Ladder; the result is stored and recomputed with Update Analysis. Decisions and formulas: [Analyse Modal Decisions](https://claude.ai/artifact/DCFmkZb38zYBxiC8dEwSxp) (recommendations taken unless listed here); mockup: [Analyse Modal Mockup](https://claude.ai/artifact/4xnvYMkPJUgG8enpyaTJ4c). **Lab vs live (Marcelo 2026-10-15, firm):** backtesting settings (the lab) and Pluto's settings (live) are separate and never sync; drift is fine. New backtest sessions start from the global backtest settings plus the ticker's backtest overrides, not from Pluto's settings (today `loadCurrentBacktestSettings` still reads Pluto's: to change, coordinated with the backtesting session). Multi-ticker backtests support per-ticker overrides (choice of per-ticker settings, the default, or global settings; the confirmation lists each ticker's settings); accounting approved 2026-10-15: pots are set in $ but work as shares of one shared account (weight w_i = starting pot ÷ Σ starting pots, session capital = Σ pots, the ladder's base = w_i × max(account at the ticker's cycle start, account today)), interest to the pool (the account), the existing cash check with tickers processed alphabetically plus a report of how often cash bound, book CAGR / Max drawdown / Excess Calmar plus per-ticker P&L and contribution (no per-ticker CAGR), book buy and hold = Σ w_i × each ticker's; a one-ticker session must reproduce today's results; engine work by the backtesting session. Live pots: allocation % of Pluto's capital budget, recomputed from the current account value (no per-ticker pot ledger). The Backtesting screen saves the global lab settings; the Pluto screen saves the live ones ([Pluto Ladder Settings](https://claude.ai/artifact/UbVLEX5mw1m6CRPJPzWYWt) mockup, approved). In the modal: lab settings only (global + per-ticker overrides for pot $, shares, shock, rungs, |delta| range); "Save for HOOD" saves overrides and immediately re-runs the stored analysis; saving global lab values re-runs every stored analysis without an override for them; "Run with these values" recomputes without saving; "Backtest these values" (confirmation; name includes |delta| range and rungs); resets to the ticker's or the global lab values; minimum bid $0.05 and volume share 20% stay fixed engine rules. Fit over the history (formula approved: lot ceiling = pot × p ÷ 100 (the $0.01 dropped 2026-10-15, below), rung ceiling = pot × (1 − p) ÷ (100 × (n − S)), each day's 10:00 price as traded and that day's shock; HOOD at $50,000 / 30%: rung failed on 260 of 1,305 days, all in 2025–26); to flag the periods on the timeline and suggest a pot range (too small: the fit; too large: orders that can't fill at one strike under the volume cap, formula pending sign-off), or flag that no pot range suits the ticker. Approved 2026-10-15: the "too large" side comes from the engine itself: each Update Analysis runs a single-ticker backtest at the ticker's saved lab settings (visible in Sessions like any session), and the analysis reads per-look records (the best contract the strategy was allowed to sell, its volume limit floor(20% × previous session's volume), the contracts wanted, the fills down the ranking, the unsold remainder); the engine records these in every session (backtesting session building; records only, verified by a row-for-row re-run); yield given up = best yield − contract-weighted yield of what was filled; the suggested pot range uses X% of days (default 80%, editable in the window, last 12 months by default with a switch to all history). Decided earlier on 2026-10-15: no minimum yield (yields shown plain; liquidity cells coloured by share of days with a passing contract, 80% / 50%); a Puts toggle between all Puts and those under the first rung's cap (cap = 10:00 price × (1 − S ÷ rungs)); chain map's out-of-range bands follow the delta setting; Claude writes a summary plus a recommendation (settings values and a viability verdict), seeing a snapshot of the ticker's backtests; earnings show the two-session move and the implied move, formula on screen ((at-the-money Call mid + Put mid) ÷ stock price, first expiry after the report, 10:00 quote of the session before; HOOD 29 Jul 2026: $9.65 ÷ $88.47 = ±10.9%); missing data shows a message with a button that starts a background job (no button where no source exists: WBA's prices, dividends); the chain scan stays at 10:00, parallelised across cores; Update Analysis is a background job kind `analysis`. **Built 2026-10-15 (backend, uncommitted):** lab settings store (migration 20261015500001: one global row, per-ticker overrides, audit; seeded from the CC + Put Ladder preset, $200k default pot; routes GET/PUT /backtest/lab-settings, /lab-settings/audit, /lab-ticker-overrides[/:symbol]; New session requests read the lab, not Pluto); analyses (migration 20261015500002: `backtest_ticker_analyses`, job kind `analysis`; src/backtest/analysis/); routes GET /backtest/analyses (status per file), GET /analyses/:symbol (stored, trial, active), POST /analyses/:symbol (body `{}` = Update Analysis, `{values}` = Run with these values), /analyses/:symbol/runs, /analyses/:symbol/sessions (Backtest results, Excess Calmar = (CAGR − T-bill CAGR) ÷ |Max drawdown|, T-bill compounded as the engine credits interest), /analyses/runs/:id[/cancel], /analyses/:symbol/fetch-earnings (API Ninjas, then an update); Save for a ticker and a global lab save start the re-runs. A saved analysis's backtest is reused when the values and the file are unchanged. HOOD end to end: figures 1.8 s, backtest about 30 s, summary about 30 s; every figure matches the design study (scan, Max drawdown, volatility, earnings, implied move, correlation). The $0.01 stock-fill cent is dropped everywhere (Marcelo 2026-10-15): it stood for half the stock's spread when the stock price was a parity mid and options paid half their spread; today's stock price is IBKR's trade price, the lab sells options at the mid and live Covered Call entries limit the shares at the mid. Done in the analysis (lot fit 100 × P ÷ p) and in the engine (backtesting session). **Pot size, decided 2026-10-15 (formulas version 5):** the largest pot is counted on every day with a 10:00 price, not only sale days: the smaller of the Put side (the best Put under the first rung's cap, by premium yield: limit × strike × 100 × n ÷ (1 − p)) and the Call side (the best Call a new Covered Call entry would sell: limit × 100 × P ÷ p); days with no qualifying Put or Call are left out of the X% point and counted apart; the chain scan records each day's two contracts. The fit over the history checks each day against the analysis backtest's pot path: max(account at the open cycle's start, i.e. the close before its first day, last night's account), the last value carried past the backtest's end; a trial shows "Save to see the fit history with a live-like pot". Also approved: Excess Calmar as built, pots rounded to $100, the correlation candidates; analysis jobs in the Backtests list only, not Downloads. **Lab settings section (Backtesting, between Data files and Sessions; built 2026-10-15):** edits only the CC + Put Ladder's global lab values (Marcelo 2026-10-15): the default pot, the fill's spread share (trading.spreadCostChargedPct, which the ladder's fills read) and the Baseline wheel rows; New session still changes any value per session. Save shows a confirmation from POST /backtest/lab-settings/preview (nothing written): each change "from → to", the analysed tickers it re-runs and the tickers keeping their own value (overrides are kept, never cleared by a global save); the save re-runs those analyses and records the count on its audit rows (migration 20261016100001, analyses_rerun). Ticker overrides (each with Analyse) and Change history sit below. **Pluto ladder settings screen, decided 2026-10-15, built 2026-10-15 (API d5fee00, app f90b902; not pushed):** plain-language: each ticker on Pluto's Tickers tab now shows its allocation, pot and whether the ladder fits at the last close, and a Settings button opens a window to set that ticker's allocation, share level, shock, rungs and |delta| range, with the checks recomputed as you type. Decisions: a failing fit check allows "Save anyway"; a strategy-wide save that would break a ticker's |delta| range is refused, naming the tickers; checks use the last close; the window shows the smallest allocation that fits (smallest pot ÷ Pluto's capital budget); the shock field gets a hint, smallest shock that fits = n − pot × (1 − p) ÷ (100 × P) (formula approved; at or below 0% no message, above 100% "fewer rungs or a larger pot"); the Settings tab's info note shows the current settings version; the strategy Pluto isn't running keeps its groups visible and editable, with the note "Not running: changes apply when you switch to this strategy", and the same note on every Edge-Margin-only field inside the shared groups (Marcelo 2026-10-15, option (a)). Checked with Playwright at 1706, 1440 and 390 px in light and dark: every new text at 4.5:1 or more (measured), phone tap targets 44 px, no console errors; a HOOD save at 20% verified in the DB and audit, then restored. The Strategy selector is the Pluto session's (PlutoStrategyCard). **App built 2026-10-15 (uncommitted, except the API types a431bc6):** the Analyse window from the approved mockup (src/components/backtest/analysis/), opened by an Analyse button per data file; the shared DataTable gets opt-in sortable headers (a column's sortValue, the table's defaultSort); analysis jobs listed with the backtests. Checked on HOOD with Playwright at 1706, 1440 and 390 px: every text in the window at 4.5:1 or more in light and dark (measured), phone tap targets 44 px, no console errors; Run with these values, Backtest these values (the session stores the per-ticker values) and ESC/backdrop close tested.
- **Analyse price chart: the ladder's cap, floor and rungs sold (Marcelo 2026-10-20; [mockup](https://claude.ai/artifact/7xRKSoZiutZsidKE7WXnxN) approved; committed, not pushed).** Plain-language: the price chart now shows, for each day the strategy held shares, the highest strike the next ladder Put may have (dashed line) and the ladder's floor (solid line), with a dot for each rung sold; the lot and rung ceiling lines are gone. Each rung sold now shows its number instead of a dot (Marcelo 2026-10-20): the reserve's slice it reaches, (U + Q) ÷ (R0 ÷ rungs) rounded up, 1 to the ticker's rungs (e.g. $11,305 already committed and $25,649 a slice: 1.44 slices, rung 2), the exact figure in the tooltip; read from each sale's recorded reserve, so every session shows them (`ladderRungReached`), and the Analyse window reads a stored analysis's rungs again from its session when it opens (`withFreshLadderRungs`), so saved analyses show them too. Cap = A − (A − F) × (U + Q) ÷ R0 at the day's first look before its orders (the engine's own; equals the first rung's cap while nothing is committed), floor = the last rung's cap. The engine records them per symbol and day in `backtest_ladder_levels` (migration 20261020100001; record only: a GLD analysis session re-run came out IDENTICAL row for row, and every ladder sale's cap matched the recorded one); the analysis backtest's figures carry them split-adjusted with the rungs sold (one per rung order, fills in the tooltip); formulas version 6, so stored analyses show as older until updated. Checked with Playwright at 1706, 1440 and 390 px in both themes (lines 4.42:1 light, 3.36:1 dark). **Account in shares and log scale (Marcelo 2026-10-20; [mockup](https://claude.ai/artifact/BNxfKWcJuXcPU6zJJhNFnR) approved; committed, not pushed):** plain-language: a line under the candles shows how much of the backtest's account sits in shares each day, and a Log scale button (remembered per browser) switches the price axis. Account in shares = shares held × the day's 10:00 price ÷ the account value, both at the 10:00 marks before that day's orders (Puts don't count). The engine records the shares' value per day (`backtest_session_days.shares_value_dollars`, migration 20261020100002; record only: GLD and AAOI copies IDENTICAL row for row); formulas version 7. The log scale plots log10 of every price on a plain axis (ApexCharts' own log axis drops the candles). Also: the Analyse window's "Edit the global lab settings" opens Lab settings over the window (ESC or close returns to it, which then reloads), and opening a group in Lab settings scrolls its title to the top.
- **Signals table: old Edge columns off; IV Rank, IV %ile and FV 63d on (Marcelo 2026-10-20; built, uncommitted).** Plain-language: the Signals table no longer shows the old 63-day Edge (its Grade, Net Edge and Edge $); the Day basis group now has IV Rank and IV Percentile (the approved ivMetrics.ts formulas, 252 days of IBKR's daily IV, as on Screener and Price Performance) and FV 63d (Yang-Zhang over 63 sessions, no fallback) beside FV 21d. Display only. The old Edge itself is still computed and used elsewhere: the Day Signals upgrade alerts (old grade and net Edge), Pulse's Top Signals by Edge $, the ticker modal and order forms, and as gates (a ticker without the old forecast is unscored; rolls need the held leg's old Edge; Pluto close offers need the leg scored); Open: the Analyse window's IV percentile counts days at or below today including today, unlike ivMetrics. Columns ordered ATM IV · FV 21d · FV 63d · IV Rank · IV %ile; a no-signal badge's reason shows in its tooltip.
- **Old 63-day Edge retired everywhere (Marcelo 2026-10-20; built, uncommitted).** Plain-language: everything that still used the old Edge now uses the valuation model (what Pluto already acted on), and the old figures are gone from every screen. Decisions: Day Signals upgrade alerts fire on the model grade, when the Edge margin clears the new grade's cut by 2 points (Strong from 22%, Good from 12%, Weak from 2%; formula approved); Pulse's Top Signals rank by Edge Yield and show Edge $ /10k; contracts and rolls the model cannot value are dropped with a reason ("The valuation model could not value this contract"); the order forms' change notice follows the model's Edge margin (warns at a 2-point move or a grade change). Scoring now needs the model's 21-day forecast (22 bars, the same coverage as before); held legs are unscored without it. Removed: the 63-day forecast and its YZ21 fallback, net Edge, friction in volatility points, Edge $, the old grades and roll Edge, grade counts. Migration 20261020100003 clears the stored Day Signals grades (old ones), so the next re-score is a baseline. Stored events and order snapshots keep their old fields; the toast and Latest Events show older events by their grades only. Release outside market hours.
- **Covered calls with a put ladder (Marcelo 2026-10-14; committed 2026-10-15 in 63f7357, not pushed; backtests local only).** Plain-language: part of the account holds shares and sells covered calls; the rest stays in cash and sells puts only when no call qualifies, each new put at a lower strike the more of that reserve is already in use. Version 4: every stock gets the ladder at 30%, 50% and 70% in shares (shock = deepest fall in the prior 10 years, kept within 60–80%; 5 rungs; from today's price), today's covered calls dropped, buy and hold the yardstick, stock prices from IBKR; 10 stocks: [Covered Calls with a Put Ladder](https://claude.ai/artifact/EZKJYsqzUU8Edi1rDGQz47). Medians: 30% shares 9.4% a year with a −17% worst fall, 50% 12.7% / −28%, 70% 14.5% / −40%, buy and hold 15.9% / −67%. Awaiting Marcelo: the shock cap (80% deepened HOOD's worst fall at 30% shares to −54% from −30% at 90%), the target worst fall and the share-level rule (target ÷ shock proposed; no rule known in advance clearly beats one level for all), a liquidity check (TECH and GSAT had options open on under a quarter of days). **Technical:** baseline settings `putsWhenNoCall reserve_ladder` and `ladder*` (`backtestBaselineLadder.ts`), default `ladderShockMaxPct` 80; report figures from `tmp/ladder-research/option_income.py` (premium yield = Σ premium kept ÷ Σ exposure × days open ÷ 365, approved 2026-10-14) and `share_level_rule.py`; raw data `backtest-data/research/ladder-grid-v4-2026-10-14/` (v3: `ladder-grid-2026-10-14/`); specs and scripts `tmp/ladder-research/`.
- **Backtests with per-ticker pots, and order records (Marcelo 2026-10-15; engine and API 2812af2 + 822b1ea, New session screen app 409add3 from the approved mockup https://claude.ai/artifact/6DGbaP15Pp5YnfRYiDSyNJ, with an "N new sessions" link on the Sessions list; not pushed).** Plain-language: a backtest of several tickers can now give each ticker its own amount of money (its "pot") and its own share level, rungs, shock and delta range, while all tickers still share one account and its cash. Every backtest now also records, for each order, how many contracts it wanted, the best contract it could sell, and why any part went unsold (the cash ran out, the volume limit, or nothing qualified), which the Analyse window will read. Technical: migrations `20261015450001_backtest_sessions_ticker_pots` (`book_mode`, `symbol_settings`) and `20261015450002_create_backtest_look_shortfalls`; request `settingsSource` / `symbolSettings` / optional `startingValue`, the estimate answers `capital`, `bookMode`, `resolvedBySymbol`; the session detail adds `bookMode`, `symbolSettings`, `cashBoundBySymbol`. Formulas in docs/backtesting/FORMULAS.md ("Per-ticker pots", "Order records"). Verified: SMCI and HOOD re-run identical row for row (SMCI also as a one-ticker pots session), HOOD 12.4–13.5 s against 13.3 s before, backtest tests 411/411. No-candidate looks are one row per day (Marcelo: per-look rows were ~16,000 a session). Open: per-ticker Calls/Puts/stock P&L and contribution (formula to sign off), the New session screen (mockup first), the lab settings readers (Analysis session's store; until then no ticker has saved values and there is no default pot).
- **Backtest engine aligned with live (Marcelo 2026-10-15; committed 859dd33 and 9017740, not pushed).** Plain-language: the backtest now prices options and shares the way live Pluto does, so the two give the same answers. Each expiry's forward is fixed at the day's first look and follows the stock after that (Pluto's sticky moneyness); shares trade at the look's price itself (the $0.01 either way is gone, and with it the Baseline's share-fill setting); the shared ladder steps take Pluto's commission estimators (the backtest passes its flat $0.68 / $0.005, results identical). Call orders also record the shares' average cost and the look's stock price for the Analyse window. Every Baseline result shifts a little (SMCI 50%: CAGR 23.73% → 26.43% across both changes, mostly one changed lot early in 2023 sending the path elsewhere; HOOD 14.00% → 14.05%), so the published ladder reports predate them. Row-for-row reference sessions: SMCI b2e66897, HOOD da86a238.
- **Download queue: put a download first (Marcelo 2026-10-15; built and verified; committed 2026-10-15, API 63f7357 / app 562f978, not pushed).** Plain-language: a download can jump the queue ("Update first" on a Data files row, "Download first" in the form, "Move to top" / "Start now" on a queued download); when another download is running, a confirmation offers to pause it so the new one starts now (the paused one goes back in right after it and continues where it stopped) or to keep it running. Columns `put_first_at` and `pause_requested` on `backtest_jobs`; the newest put first starts first; the new job starts only once the paused one has stopped, so ThetaData's single login is never shared. Open: phone tap size of the Data files row buttons (pre-existing, 26 px).
- **Ladder: a Put when its yield beats the Call's (Marcelo 2026-10-15; engine setting built, committed in 63f7357, not pushed).** Plain-language: the ladder sold Puts only when no Call on the free shares qualified; a new backtest setting (`baseline.ladderPutVersusCall` "higher_yield" with `ladderPutYieldMarginPct`, relative) sells the ladder Put instead when its premium yield beats the best Call's by more than a margin. Default unchanged. SMCI at 30/50/70% shares, adopted settings: better at 30% for every margin, about even at 50%, and at 70% even for "any higher" but worse for 25–100%. Adopted 2026-10-15 (Marcelo) for the ladder strategy: "higher_yield" with no margin, so a ladder Put is sold instead of a Call on the free shares whenever its premium yield is higher, or when no Call qualifies; the rungs and the strike cap still apply. Run on every available ticker 2026-10-15 at 30/50/70% shares (the 13 Oct hold-out used once as the ladder's out-of-sample test; WDC from its 2025-02-25 spin-off only; WBA has no IBKR prices; SPY/QQQ still downloading): https://claude.ai/artifact/Mx2FAzBvXafVAhfWCghmwF. "Put if higher" helps a little at 30%, is neutral at 50% and lowers the 70% medians through HOOD alone; Max drawdown is smaller than buy and hold on every ticker, CAGR lower on most; on the hold-out buy and hold scored higher. Marcelo 2026-10-15: this "Current" strategy is the "Baseline" for further variants; the older versions drop out of the comparisons. Variant 1: 0–45 days to expiry instead of 1–45. The engine now allows `baseline.minDte` 0 (approved formulas: an option expiring on the look's day is priced with T = hours from the look to that day's close ÷ 8,760, 13:00 on half days; its premium yield is still annualised over at least one day, as its cash or shares stay committed until the next morning's settlement); Baseline re-runs row for row. Result: no meaningful difference (same medians; same-day contracts were often the best yield but the book was committed on ~90% of expiry days). Account size 2026-10-15 (Baseline at $200k / $100k / $50k, 19 tickers × 30/50/70%): https://claude.ai/artifact/Q9TUR45Z1gJSMUUpoC1uEk; the limits of a small account are whole 100-share lots within the share budget and whole Put contracts within a rung (reserve ÷ 5), which keep some tickers in cash at $50k–$100k; results otherwise move both ways. Also: `baseline.maxSpreadPctOfMid` (default 15) is now a setting.
- **Call yield threshold for new Covered Calls only (Marcelo 2026-10-16; built and verified, uncommitted, not pushed).** Plain-language: a backtest can now open new Covered Calls only when the Call's premium yield clears a level, while Calls on shares already held (assigned ones included) are sold at any premium. New setting `baseline.minCallYieldAppliesTo` ("every_call" default, or "new_covered_calls"), next to "Calls only above a premium yield of". A skipped entry buys no shares and sells no Put; the cash waits for the next look. Default identical row for row (SMCI and HOOD Baseline sessions). Live Pluto: not yet; its Call yield floor is a fixed 0 by design, so adding it live is a separate decision.
- **The ladder's Puts have no |delta| range (Marcelo 2026-10-16; built and verified, backtest and live).** Plain-language: the |delta| range now applies to Calls only; a ladder Put is bounded by its rung's cap (and days to expiry, spread, minimum bid, volume), whatever its |delta|. Backtest: setting `baseline.ladderPutDeltaRange` ("delta_range" default, stored sessions identical row for row; "none"), lab set to "none" and its Calls back on the main range (`callDeltaRange` entry_range, both 0.05–0.30, no change). Live Pluto: fixed choice "none"; the order check (send and route) checks Calls only; the re-quoting band and the nightly volume capture take every Put below the price (measured 1.4–2.3× today's Put requests per look: SPY 845 vs 496), so the look sees what the backtest sees. How it works tab, Pluto and Analyse labels say "Calls' |delta|". Existing backtests all ran Puts on a range, so the Analyse window calls settings untested until backtests run with the new setting.
- **Analyse window: Claude's prompt version 5 on Opus 5.5 (Marcelo 2026-10-16; prompt approved).** Plain-language: the verdict now comes from fixed tests computed in code (approved thresholds: blocking = missing weekdays in 12 months, no pot fits, Calls passing < 50% of days, Max drawdown not below buy and hold; Qualifies also needs the pot fitting ≥ 95% of days, Calls ≥ 80%, Excess Calmar ≥ buy and hold's), and a changed value needs a same-period backtest differing in that setting alone that beats the reference's Excess Calmar by 0.05. Claude reads both ranges, buy and hold's Excess Calmar, the T-bill CAGR and current-engine backtests with their settings (identical results merged); sessions record `engine_version` (migration 20261016300001, 1 = engine of 3020323, set on the 521 sessions since). Opus 5.5 at extra-high effort (backtest decisions keep Sonnet), timeout 600 s. Also: the Backtests list drops an analysis's own backtest (its analysis row shows it), the recommendation names where a value came from ("same as the global value", "SPY's own", "this run's").
- **Dev tooling (2026-10-16).** `npm run sync-dev-db` keeps the local backtest_* tables (backtests, jobs, analyses, lab settings) across a staging pull, runs the local migrations and checks row counts; it refuses while a job runs. App contexts split (providers in contexts/, hooks in hooks/) so Vite hot-swaps them and an open modal survives another session's edits.
- **Backtest batches: several runs, compared with buy and hold and combined (Marcelo 2026-10-17; mockup https://claude.ai/artifact/9rs1PXZGBnkphdMu2wc6yf approved with formulas F1 and F2 and choices D1–D16; built and verified locally, pushed to staging 2026-10-17).** Plain-language: the New session form can start up to 9 runs at once (Baseline only), each its own account with its own tickers and ticker values, all on one period and the same settings; "Make runs" turns a list of values into runs. The batch page shows each run beside buy and hold of its own tickers and of SPY (CAGR vs buy and hold in pts), and ticking two or more runs adds them into one combined portfolio (daily values summed, the session's formulas on the sum, trades pooled; buy and hold and SPY summed the same way). Backtest jobs queue, 6 at a time (`BACKTEST_MAX_PARALLEL_SESSIONS`). A batch is one row on the Sessions list. Every new session is a batch (one run opens its session page).
- **Readable red and green text in both themes (Marcelo 2026-10-17; app-wide, pushed to staging 2026-10-17).** Plain-language: losses and gains shown in red and green were too faint on dark cards (and red on some light tints); every screen now uses darker red in light and lighter red and green in dark, and the Backtesting session links are brighter in dark.
- **Screener → Backtest data (Marcelo 2026-10-16; built and verified locally; committed, app ff31ae5 + fbb0c4b, not pushed).** Plain-language: trending tickers can be queued for backtest data straight from the Screener. Each Screener row has "+ Backtest" (dev builds only, like the Backtesting screens): it queues the same download as the Backtest screen's Download (from 2020, end of queue); when the symbol already has a data file or a queued/running/stopped download, the button is disabled and reads Downloaded / Queued / Downloading / Stopped. Class-share symbols ("PBR A") get no button: the downloader does not take them. `npm run sync-screener` (skill `/screener`) replaces the local `screener_universe` with staging's in one transaction, nothing else touched. Data files table: "Download first" and "Update first" removed (every download joins the end of the queue; the Jobs card's Move to top / Start now reorders it), and the Analyse button replaced by a link on the symbol (new `.iorio-symbol-link` class in theme.css: 5.70:1 dark, 5.93:1 light).
- **Backtesting page: job lists and paged Sessions (Marcelo 2026-10-14; committed 2026-10-15, API 63f7357 / app 562f978, not pushed).** Plain-language: the Background jobs card keeps the 50 latest backtests and, for downloads, the running one, the stopped ones, the queue in the order it will run, then the last to finish, up to 50 (an unfinished download is never dropped), six rows each with their own scroll; the scrollbar now stays visible (macOS hid it, which made rows look missing). The Sessions table reads 50 sessions a page, newest first, with a name search and Symbol and Status filters; ticks to compare stay across pages. The page no longer freezes: while a job ran it redrew every session every second. **Technical:** overview = files + the 50 latest backtests by latest activity + downloads running → stopped → queued (`queued_at, created_at, id`, `kickDownloadQueue`'s order) → finished by `finished_at` desc (`overviewJobsPerKind`, no week cut-off, no sessions); `GET /backtest/sessions` (search, symbol, status, limit 50 up to 200, offset; each item's `latestRun` from its own latest job; `symbols` for the filter); `src/lib/sqlContainsPattern.ts`; app `BacktestSessionsTable.tsx` (`useBacktestBookPage`, `useDebouncedValue`, shared `Pagination`), `BacktestJobsCard.tsx` (six rows measured with a ResizeObserver, `.backtest-job-list-scroll`; the once-a-second clock now ticks inside the card).
- **Stock splits freeze their ticker (Marcelo 2026-10-15; built, API d0dd15b + b959319, app c00dc7e, not pushed).** Plain-language: when a stock splits (or reverse-splits), nobody trades it until a person has checked and converted its records to the new share count and prices. Detected from IBKR's price history (09:00 and 22:00 UTC) or from the positions themselves (shares × ratio with cost ÷ ratio); recording the split freezes the ticker (`tickers.trading_frozen_at`): its orders are cancelled (reason ticker_frozen), the order route refuses it, Pluto skips it, the 60-second position sync and the reconciliation check leave it out (no fake closes or sales; no global Pluto pause). A Telegram alert repeats daily with options expiring within 3 days and a prompt to paste into Claude: `npm run split:rewrite` (preview by default; `--apply` rewrites legs and trades of the ticker dated before the split in one transaction, shares × r, prices ÷ r, strikes ÷ r, contracts × r for whole forward ratios, else shares per contract × r; old values kept for `split:undo`), then `npm run ticker:unfreeze`. Contracts not on 100 shares are left to IBKR (the route refuses closing or rolling them). The UI shows a red top-bar pill and Frozen badges (Positions, Signals, Shortlist, Pluto), order buttons disabled. Untested against a real split: how IBKR reports adjusted options (the preview compares our rewrite with IBKR's holdings the worker keeps on the split).
- **Backtests price the stock from IBKR only (Marcelo 2026-10-14; built, committed 2026-10-15 in 63f7357, not pushed; bars fetched 2026-10-14 for every file but CSCO, whose download fetches its own).** Plain-language: backtests used to work out the stock's price from the option prices; they now take it from IBKR's own price history, kept inside each stock's data file: daily prices for the last 20 years and half-hourly prices from the year its option data starts. A download fetches them when it ends, so the Update button on a data file refreshes them; files already downloaded get them from a one-off script. The option prices still tell where a stock split falls. **Technical:** file tables `ibkr_daily_bars` / `ibkr_intraday_bars` (`backtestFileStockBars.ts`, spec in `DATA-FILES.md` "IBKR stock bars"), fetched by `refreshFileStockBars.ts` (daily `20 Y` + implied volatility, 30-minute `1 Y` per year, 10 s apart, whole set replaced in one transaction) at the end of a download job and by `npm run backtest:backfill-file-stock-bars`; the engine reads the price at a look from the 30-minute bar ending then, closes from the daily bar (else the last 30-minute bar), and stops when either set ends before the session; FORMULAS.md updated. Engine tests write synthetic IBKR bars at the prices the quotes imply, so every earlier expectation still holds. IBKR gives up to 20 years of daily bars, as far back as it holds the listing (INTC 2006, MU 2009, TLT 2016). Same settings, IBKR prices instead of the quotes' (MU's 10:00 price within 0.11% on 95% of days): TLT 50% ladder CAGR −1.95% → −1.89%, INTC +6.82% → +7.85% (one put sold a day earlier in 2020; the rest followed).
- **Data files: Delete, and downloads stop for symbols IBKR doesn't know (Marcelo 2026-10-17; built and verified locally, not committed).** Plain-language: each Data files row has a Delete button (with a confirmation) that removes the symbol's file and its downloads; a download for a stock IBKR has no contract for (delisted, like WBA since 2025-08-28, or misspelt) now fails straight away instead of first spending an hour on option data that can't be backtested. A Gateway outage still lets the option download go ahead. **Technical:** `lookupContractDetails` flags `ibkrHasNoSuchContract` on error 200 or an empty list (not on a timeout), carried by `UnknownSymbolError`; `isIbkrNoSuchContractError` (backfillDailyHistory.ts) also matches a historical request's code 200; `src/backtest/deleteBacktestDataFile.ts`. Checked against the real Gateway (WBA flagged, DOCN found) and with Playwright at 1706, 1440 and 390 px in both themes. Same day, the table's columns: an Options group (From, To, Days = days with option prices) and a Spot group (From, To, Days = IBKR daily stock prices stored, all of them back to 2016); the Intraday days column dropped (still shown in New session's symbol picker and the Analyse window). DataTable: `headerClassName`, and a header shared by several columns gets its group name in the gear list ("Spot From"). Missing and Unavailable folded into Issues (Marcelo 2026-10-17): distinct days with nothing stored, stored with parts missing, or not retrievable (Missing used to count unavailable days twice); pointing at (or tapping) the count lists them by kind, one line per day and expiration, ThetaData's errors in plain words, at most 20 lines (`readBacktestDataFileIssues`, `describeDataFileIssues`; TooltipSpan `multiline`). Splits column fix: the table checked the cached split schedule against the local DB's bar count while the check uses the file's own IBKR bars (20 years), so every stock older than 2016 showed "—"; it now counts the file's bars (`countFileDailyBars`), and an unchecked file reads "not checked". Stop/Keep wording (Marcelo 2026-10-17): a running job's button and its dialog say Stop ("Stop download" / "Keep downloading", "Keep in queue", "Keep running"), ConfirmModal takes `cancelLabel` so two buttons never both say Cancel; the order-cancel dialogs' close button reads "Keep Order" / "Keep the order(s)".
- **Data files: Suitability column, and pot search for every ticker (Marcelo 2026-10-19; mockup https://claude.ai/artifact/ErAZQyHBZFdBkMVLEonatU approved; built and verified locally, not committed).** Plain-language: each downloaded ticker shows Pending, Suitable or Unsuitable with a note; an admin clicks the badge to write the note and mark it, traders see the badge and the note on hover. First values from a pot search (pot up to $100,000, 3–5 rungs, 30–70% shares, 95% range over 12 months, each rung's Put order on one contract on 100% of the last 3 months' days and 90% of 12 months', a qualifying Put on at least 90% of the last 3 months' days; most rungs then highest share level wins; whole contracts as the engine sells them): lab overrides saved for CRWV, DRAM, HOOD, INTC, PLTR, SMCI; Suitable = fits and Analyse verdict Qualifies (HOOD, INTC), Borderline left Pending (CRWV, DRAM, PLTR, SMCI), NOK Unsuitable (does not qualify; its override reset to the global values), the rest Unsuitable or Pending for data problems (AMD, NVDA, SPCX, TSLA: no 30-minute bars; GSAT, PTON, TECH: almost no qualifying Puts; MRNA downloading). Technical: migration `20261019200001_create_backtest_ticker_suitability` (no row = Pending; a `pending` row = Pending with a note, written only by the backfill), `GET /backtest/overview` → `suitabilityBySymbol`, `PUT /backtest/ticker-suitability/:symbol` (admin, `suitable`/`unsuitable` only, ticker must have a data file); scripts `tmp/optimiseLabPotsByTicker.ts`, `tmp/backfillTickerSuitability20261019.ts`. App-wide: `btn-success` now #1e7a33 (white 5.40:1, was 2.74:1) and `btn-danger` labels white with a solid #b52f2f hover (4.66:1, was 4.46:1). Verified: route tests 3/3 plus backtest routes 44/44, UI at 1706/1440/390 in both themes, save through the window read back from the DB, trader view.
- **Analyse window header: name, category and key facts (Marcelo 2026-10-19; proposal approved, no mockup; built and verified locally, not committed).** Plain-language: under the ticker the window now shows the company's full name, whether it is a stock, ADR or ETF, its sector, industry and exchange, and its Suitability badge (an admin can change it from there); below that the last close and day change, the 52-week range, IV with its 12-month percentile, the best Put and the best Covered Call entry Call of the last option day with their yields, and the next earnings date. Names come from TradingView (IBKR's own long name is upper case and cut at 28 characters: checked on BMNR, SPY, HOOD, SPCX), fetched when an analysis runs; until then the stored IBKR name. IV percentile (approved): share of the last 252 sessions with IV at or below the last session's. The best Call is new in the chain scan (the entry Call the engine would sell): older analyses show "Update Analysis to see it". Technical: migration `20261019200002_create_backtest_ticker_profiles`, `fetchTradingViewSymbolProfiles` (one scanner request over NASDAQ/NYSE/AMEX/CBOE, or the stored TradingView ticker), `src/backtest/tickerProfileStore.ts`, `GET /backtest/analyses/:symbol` → `profile`, `suitability`; `chains.lastDay.bestEntryCall`; app `AnalysisTickerHeader.tsx`; the Suitability window stacks at 1100 over the Analyse window, whose ESC waits for it. Backfilled all 33 files (`tmp/backfillTickerProfiles20261019.ts`). Verified: 119 tests, UI at 1706/1440/390 in both themes on HOOD, SPY (ETF) and NOK (ADR).
- **Analyse window: |delta| toggle on Option liquidity and yield (Marcelo 2026-10-19; proposal approved, no mockup; built and verified locally, not committed).** Plain-language: the card's two year × expiry grids can now show every contract ("Any |delta|") or only those inside the lab's shared |delta| range ("In band"), for Calls and Puts alike. With ladder Puts at any |delta| (every ticker's lab today) the in-band best yield is about half (AAOI 1–7 days 2026: 703% → 377%). The monthly chart and Claude's summary keep the lab's own rules. The footer now names the |delta| actually applied (it used to quote the shared range even where ladder Puts ignore it). Analyses from before this show the lab's grid with a note to re-run. Technical: `scanChainDay` selects each right once at any |delta| and splits by the selector's own range test into `cells` (lab rules), `cellsByDelta.any/band`; `chains.gridByDelta`, `spreadShareUnder25ByDelta`; `analysisChainScan.test.ts` checks the band against a direct `selectBaselineCandidates` call. Verified at 1706/1440/390 in both themes on DRAM.
- **Downloads: stock bars after a failed download, and a check for a reused symbol (Marcelo 2026-10-19; built and tested locally, not committed).** Plain-language: when the option download fails partway (ThetaData unreachable), the IBKR 30-minute bars are still fetched for the days it saved, so those days can be analysed and backtested; a download you cancel still stops at once. A file whose options start more than 14 days before IBKR's first stock price now fails with a clear message (the symbol belonged to another security first, as SPCX did before SpaceX) instead of IBKR's "no data" error. Technical: `executeBacktestJob.ts` runs `refreshStockBarsOfFile` unless cancelled; `refreshFileStockBars` takes `firstOptionDay` and throws `FileOptionsPredateStockError` (`optionsStartBeforeStockPrices`, unit-tested). Same day: SPCX deleted and re-downloading from 2026-06-12 (SpaceX only: the old ETF's options end 2026-04-06, SpaceX's start 2026-06-16); AMD, NVDA, TSLA re-queued; NVDA's partial file given its bars with `npm run backtest:backfill-file-stock-bars -- NVDA`.
