- **Update adds only the last month of IBKR stock bars (Marcelo 2026-10-27; built and verified locally, not committed).** Plain-language: Update re-downloaded every IBKR stock price a data file holds (20 years of daily bars and every 30-minute bar since 2020) for each ticker, about 2 minutes each even when one day was new. It now fetches the last month and adds it, about 20 s; when IBKR's prices for the days already stored differ by a cent or more (a split since the last fetch), it fetches everything again as before, so a file never mixes two price scales. **Technical:** `refreshFileStockBars.ts` (`reasonToFetchEveryBar`, `reasonTailDiffers`, 3 `1 M` requests), `addFileStockBars` / `readFileStockBarsTail` / `summarizeFileStockBars` in `backtestFileStockBars.ts`; spec in DATA-FILES.md. Checked on copies of PCG/NVDA against IBKR: tail result identical row for row to a full fetch; a faked 2× adjustment falls back to the full fetch.
- **Cash-Secured Put entry with the put ladder; One Put removed (Marcelo 2026-10-19; pushed to staging 2026-10-20).** Plain-language: a backtest can now start the CC + Put Ladder with Cash-Secured Puts instead of buying shares. With no shares held it sells Puts whose collateral fits the ladder's "Shares at most" budget (the rest stays as the reserve); once assigned, everything runs as before (Calls on the shares, ladder Puts on the reserve). The ladder's cycle starts at the first look with shares held (shock fixed then, account value from the look before); no ladder Put before shares are held. "Puts when no call is sold" now offers None or the ladder: the One Put mode (and Put exposure per day to expiry) is gone; the 76 research sessions of 2026-10-13 that used it keep their results but can't be re-run as they were. The Analyse window still reads Covered Call ladder sessions only. Refusals name settings by label and the lab checks the values with the server as you edit. Verified: Covered Call + ladder sessions DRAM, HOOD and INTC re-run identical row for row; a HOOD session with the Cash-Secured Put entry (2024–2026) sold 33 × $12 Puts ($39,600 of a $40,000 budget) on day one, Calls after the assignment, its first ladder Put in March 2025; API tests 531/531.
- **Drop-risk filters for the CC + Put Ladder (Marcelo 2026-10-19; research; F1 and F2 approved 2026-10-20 and built as the Entry filters below).** Plain-language: looked for a rule that stops the strategy taking on shares just before a big fall (30% within 63 sessions, or the ladder running out of rungs) without leaving it idle, on 22 tickers from 2020, both entries: [Ladder Drop-Risk Filters](https://claude.ai/artifact/2zY1HC9GeuzGU6XvUhNb63). None of the lab's existing features passes (earnings skip, volatility edge filter, share stop-loss, deeper ladder, no minimum yield). Big falls follow run-ups: a stock far above its 200-day average for its own volatility, and for fast falls a high IV Rank. The Signals model's fields, a weak SPY and a steep Put skew point the wrong way, and nothing reliably warns on ladder Puts. Proposed for sign-off: no new entry while extension z ≥ 1 (F1), or z ≥ 1 or IV Rank ≥ 80 (F2); ladder Puts and Calls on held shares unfiltered; then backtests and one hold-out check. The ladder ran out of rungs only on INTC and MRNA. Engine edge case found: a contract expiring on an unscheduled market closure (2025-01-09) can't be settled, so two QQQ/SPY runs failed. **Technical:** `tmp/drop-filter-research/` (phase 1 backtests in `out_phase1/`, day and order studies, `collect_results.py` → `build_report.py`).
- **Entry filters as lab settings; expiries on a closed day (Marcelo 2026-10-20: F1 and F2 approved, each switchable with its own limit; pushed to staging 2026-10-20).** Plain-language: the lab's new Entry filters group has two switches that stop new Cash-Secured Put and Covered Call entries while the stock is stretched (extension z at or above its limit, 1 by default) or its IV Rank is high (80 by default); both are off by default, and Calls on held shares and ladder Puts are never filtered. Backtested 2026-10-20 ([Ladder Entry Filters](https://claude.ai/artifact/L1Pta1QdjoFN53vTAbUpWs)): no filter passes the yardstick; F2 made 20 of 44 runs better and 18 worse (hold-out: 3 better, 8 worse). The filters trim Max drawdown a little but sit out rallies, so Excess Calmar nets to nothing. Recommendation: keep both off; nothing to take to Pluto. A backtest no longer stops on an option expiring on a day the market was closed (9 January 2025): it settles on the previous session's close, as OCC did. **Technical:** `entryDropRiskFilters.ts` (z from IBKR's split-adjusted closes before the day; IV Rank as `ivMetrics.ts`), baseline settings `entryExtensionFilter`, `entryMaxExtensionZ`, `entryIvRankFilter`, `entryMaxIvRank`, read in step 2 of `baselineSaleSteps.ts`; settlement uses `BacktestTradingCalendar.isSession`/`sessionBefore`; the analysis hash reads the lab's baseline in the code's shape (`withCodeKeys`), so a setting added at its default leaves analyses current. Verified: API tests 6,184/6,184; two Phase 1 base runs re-run IDENTICAL row for row with the filters off; the Entry filters group at 1706/1440/390 px in both themes (lowest text contrast 4.64:1). Backtests: `tmp/drop-filter-research/` (`make_specs_phase2.py`, `make_specs_holdout.py` → `out_phase2/`, `out_holdout/`; `run_diagnostics.py`, `collect_phase2.py`, `score_phase2.py`, `build_report2.py`).
- **Wheel cycles table: oldest first, idle stretches, each cycle's trades (Marcelo 2026-10-20, mockups approved; committed, not pushed).** Plain-language: the backtest's Wheel cycles table lists every cycle oldest first on one table (no pages), with a Session # column (the session's trading days numbered from 1, so weekends show as jumps). Columns since the second round (Marcelo 2026-10-20): Cycle, Cycle P&L, Return, B&H, Status, Opened, Closed, Session #, Price, Net premium, Stock P&L, By strategy, Data flags (Put assigned, Recovery, Open for, Shares held and Break-even removed). Return = the account's value at the 10:00 mark of the session after the cycle closed ÷ its value at the opened day's mark − 1 (marks before that day's orders; Idle rows the same over the stretch; blank in a session of several symbols). B&H = C(closed day) ÷ C(trading day before opened) − 1 on split-adjusted closes, as the Idle rows' move. A B-E column after Price: an open cycle's cash break-even now (from its stored trades, so older sessions show it too), and on each call sold on held shares under a break-even rule the break-even its strike was held to (recorded from engine version 3). A Shares column after B-E (Marcelo 2026-10-21): the shares held once each look's orders are done, an open cycle's at the last marks, a stock-only stretch's own; counted across a split on one scale (the engine's cached split schedule), which also fixed the sales' B-E after a split (NFLX: $1,237.89 shown where the floor was $123.79). A put sold as a ladder rung has its description underlined, the tooltip giving its rung and K_max = A − (A − F) × (U + Q) ÷ R0 with the figures its sale recorded (approved 2026-10-21; matched by the sale's instant); since the same day it leads with the decision: why a rung at that look (no call qualified, or its yield beat the best call's), the strike limit that bound (the cap, or the rung's collateral left ÷ 100), why that put (its rank of those within the rules, the best one and why it was passed over) and the stock price, the formula under a divider. A Cash column after Shares (Marcelo 2026-10-21): free cash (cash − strike × 100 × contracts of the open Cash-Secured Puts, the engine's ledgerFreeCash) ÷ the account value at the day's 10:00 marks; a trade once its look is done, a cycle at its end, Idle and Stock only rows at their first day's marks; rebuilt at read time from the day's recorded cash + its fills + call assignments (`sessionFreeCash.ts`; reconciled to the cent with the next day's recorded cash on 3,418 days). A closed cycle's row shows the most shares it held and its lowest Cash (each day's marks and after each look), the day on hover; an open one the latest (Marcelo 2026-10-21). The trade rows show their strategy badge in place of the event name; assigned rows framed in yellow, a 4px bar on the left and no fill (#fcc419 dark, gold #b07d00 light for 3:1; a see-through tint read brown, a solid one too striking); Session # reads 2→7. Put assigned and Call assigned rows are tinted amber. A sold option's row shows its delta and the annualised yield the engine recorded at the sale (its premiumYield: time value net of commission ÷ capital ÷ years to expiry, contract-weighted over the row's fills) instead of its commission (approved 2026-10-20). Price = the 10:00 price as traded on the row's day (a cycle or stretch: its first day), an assignment, hand-over or mark: that day's close; read when the table opens (`GET /backtest/sessions/:id/stock-prices`, `sessionStockPrices.ts`), so older sessions show them too. With one symbol shown, an "Idle" row marks each stretch of whole sessions with no cycle open: its first and last day, sessions, the stock's move (grey) and why no entry was made, the full rules in a tooltip (Entry filter, cash, volume, no candidate with each contract check's rule and the contracts it left out, or none recorded; a day counts under every reason seen). Clicking a cycle opens its trades as rows in the same columns (Sold put, Put assigned, Sold call, Call assigned, To CC, To N/S, Bought back, Marked): each one's date, its expiry under Closed for an option sold, its session, premium, stock P&L and shares, adding up to the cycle's row; "Stock only" rows mark sessions with shares held and no option open at all, put or call, and why no call was sold (renamed from "Uncovered", which also counted days with a put sold or a call on only some shares; Marcelo 2026-10-20; stretches stored under the old rule were cleared, a re-run records the new ones). Sessions run before this show neither. **Technical:** stock move = C(last idle day) ÷ C(session before the first) − 1 on split-adjusted closes; stock only = shares held at a session's Eastern midnight and the next, and no option leg open at any moment between; the shares from the cycle's own trades (since 2026-10-21: the ledger's stock leg keeps its last quantity, so shares a later assignment added to a lot were counted from the lot's start, e.g. NFLX #33 200 for 100; stored stretches are corrected when read). `backtest_cycles.timeline`/`stock_only_stretches` and `backtest_cycle_gaps` (migrations `20261020200001`, `…200002` for `no_candidate`, `…200003` renaming `uncovered_stretches` and clearing it), built in `backtestIdleStretches.ts`/`backtestSessionRecords.ts` from the shortfalls and the look's `noSaleNotes`/`noCandidateNotes` (`baselineSaleSteps.ts`, rules from `describeBaselineRules`); `GET /backtest/sessions/:id/cycles/:symbol/:n`. App: `BacktestCyclesTable`, `DataTable` `extraRows`/`isRowClickable`. Verified: INTC `d05143d2` re-run IDENTICAL row for row; timelines sum to their cycles; backtest tests 486/486; screens at 1706/1440/390 px in both themes (lowest text contrast 4.97:1).
- **Session results: price chart, batch link, buy-and-hold lines (Marcelo 2026-10-20; committed, not pushed).** Plain-language: a session's results show the Analyse window's "Price and Max drawdown" chart over the session's period (after Account value; one symbol at a time; with that session's ladder levels, rungs sold, orders over one strike and, in a one-symbol session, the account in shares; no "Pot too small"). On both that chart and the Analyse one, a range shorter than the history can be dragged (or moved with the arrow keys) across the timeline, and the Max drawdown band and caption follow the bars on screen; Analyse's volatility chart moves with it. A run of a batch links back to its batch ("← Backtesting / Batch test · Run 2 of 3"). Under the subtitle, each ticker of a per-ticker-pots session shows the values it ran on ("SPY: $800,000 pot · 65% shares · 3 rungs · fixed 35% shock · |delta| 0.05–0.30"; each value's source, the ticker's lab or the global, in the tooltip), and the title no longer repeats a symbol the session's name already has. Buy-and-hold lines are solid everywhere: the symbols' white (near-black in light theme), SPY's grey; a second line of the same kind on a chart (another period or symbol group) is dashed or dotted, and a batch run's own buy and hold stays dashed in its run's colour. **Technical:** `GET /backtest/sessions/:id/price-chart?symbol=` (`sessionPriceChart.ts`); shared chart in the app's `priceDrawdownChart.tsx`/`priceDrawdownChartModel.ts` (window drawdown: `maxDrawdownOfBars`, the API's formula). Since 2026-10-21 (mockup approved, "A · Navigator"): the panel under the candles shows Cash available (free cash ÷ the account value at each day's 10:00 marks, `loadCashAvailableByDay`, the whole account's) in place of the account in shares (its figures removed from the analysis backtest), on the session screen and the Analyse window (read from the analysis's session when it opens, `withFreshSessionFigures`); under both panels a navigator (the whole period's closes, the window boxed: drag it, click the strip, arrow keys a month, Home/End) moves the window when the range is shorter than the period.
- **Price and Max drawdown: 50- and 200-day averages and IV Percentile (Marcelo 2026-10-26; [mockup](https://claude.ai/artifact/VFsXTwa4bxWN54wmQJJsTA) approved; pushed to staging 2026-10-27, API 2525e61 / app ca48b83).** Plain-language: on a session's results and in the Analyse window, the candles carry the 50-day (dashed grey) and 200-day (solid) averages of the close, and a panel under Cash available shows the IV Percentile: the share of the last 252 trading days whose IBKR IV was at or below the day's (the Analyse window's tile formula), from the 20th day with an IV. The bottom panel carries the dates. IV Rank was mocked first and dropped: one extreme day caps its range for a year. **Technical:** `barIndicatorsOf` (`analysisPriceFigures.ts`) over the whole data file, so a session's first day has its look-back; cut to the session in `sessionPriceChart.ts` (`barIndicators`) and added to the Analyse figures when read (`analysisStore.ts`, no re-run); app `priceDrawdownChartModel.ts` (`indicatorsInWindow`, `ivPercentilePanel`), legend items in `priceDrawdownChart.tsx`. Verified: the analysis and backtest route tests, tsc in both repos, /ui-ux at 1706/1440/390 px in both themes with contrast measured.
- **Session tiles Shares, Covered Calls and Hedge (Marcelo 2026-10-26; [mockup](https://claude.ai/artifact/KV4queK3JhnKqy2d2x3UNg) approved; pushed to staging 2026-10-27, API 2525e61 / app ca48b83).** Plain-language: after "In the money at expiry", each tile leads with its part of the CAGR (CAGR × the source's share of the account's P&L, so shares, Calls, hedge, Puts sold and interest add up to the CAGR), then its P&L, the median yearly yield quoted at the trades (Calls at sale, hedge at purchase) and the share of the session's days it was held; each shows only when the session held it. **Technical:** `computePnlSources` (`sessionAnalytics.ts`; FORMULAS.md) on the session detail (`pnlSources`, worked out when read: no re-run). The shares' P&L is the account's less interest and every option's, so it also takes in the last day's move between the account's 10:00 marks and the positions' last-look marks. App `BacktestKpiTiles.tsx`. Verified: unit and route tests, tsc in both repos, /ui-ux at 1706/1440/390 px in both themes with contrast measured.
- **Wheel cycles: No-Call days (Marcelo 2026-10-27; [mockup](https://claude.ai/artifact/CVbpSHrDXuWrkQUpDA6Y6K) approved; pushed to staging 2026-10-27).** Plain-language: a "No-Call days" column after Shares says, of the days a cycle held shares, on how many no Call was sold on them ("40 of 58"; "—" when none), and a filter beside Open and closed lists only the cycles that had any. An opened cycle shows a "No Call" row for each run of such days, in place of the Stock only rows: its dates, sessions (41→44), price, shares, length and why no Call was sold ("No Call on 200 shares for 32 sessions, the hedge Put held: no Call passed the hedge rule (shares at $324.38) · stock +3.5%"). Sessions already run show it; no re-run. **Technical:** `src/backtest/cycleNoCallDays.ts` from the cycle's legs when read (FORMULAS.md, No-Call days); shares an assignment delivers at the close count from the next session; Vol Farmer reasons from `backtest_session_days.decision_summary`, others from the stored stock-only stretches, `backtest_look_shortfalls` (held_calls) and the ladder put actions' `chosen_because`. `BacktestCycle.noCallDays`, `BacktestCycleDetail.noCallRuns`; app `BacktestCyclesTable`, `describeNoCallRun`/`describeNoCallRunRules`. Verified: the R5 30% AAPL session matches the mockup; unit and route tests; screens at 1706 and 1440 px in both themes (no phone pass: backtests run only on the Mac).
- **Analyse window before a ticker's first analysis; Options data range (Marcelo 2026-10-25; [mockup](https://claude.ai/artifact/X5CF5BEMA7qwm5AkyfJtog) approved; pushed to staging 2026-10-25).** Plain-language: a ticker that hasn't been analysed opens to a note with Analyse now and, below it, every card the data file gives on its own (tiles, price and drawdown, volatility, backtests, earnings, correlation, profile, data quality); they stay while the first analysis runs. The Analyse price chart has an Options data range (the option data's first to last day) after All and opens on it, analysed or not; the session chart is unchanged. Technical: `GET /backtest/analyses/:symbol/file-figures` (`computeAnalysisFileFigures`: the analysis job's own code without the chain scan, nothing stored).
- **Backtest call floor: the cycle's cash break-even (Marcelo 2026-10-20; formula approved; committed, not pushed).** Plain-language: "calls only at or above the break-even" now really means a cycle can't close at a loss on its shares: the break-even counts what shares that already left the cycle brought in. Before, a cycle could rebuy dearer shares after some were called away below their cost and then sell calls under what the new shares needed (SPY #5 of `b060a02c` closed −$6,849; on a re-run copy it closes +$33,530 a month later, and the session's CAGR goes 12.3% → 11.9% with no losing closed cycle). **Technical:** cash break-even = (cost of every share acquired in the cycle − what the shares that left brought in, at the sale or the call's strike, net of commission − net premium) ÷ shares held (`cycleBreakEven.ts` `cashBreakEvenPerShare`, `disposalProceeds`); used for `above_break_even` and the break-even step of `above_average_cost_else_break_even` (`baselineSaleSteps.ts`). The Positions column and the ladder (its top and levels) keep the pro-rata break-even. Engine version 3; every stored baseline session ran on the old floor.
- **Backtest entry: put or Covered Call, whichever ranks higher (Marcelo 2026-10-20; committed, not pushed).** Plain-language: a third "Entry with free cash" choice: at each look the engine finds the best put and the best Covered Call it could sell, compares them by the session's Ranking (premium yield by default), and enters with the winner's kind only, filling down within it; each kind keeps its own rules (puts in the entry |delta| range and the ladder's room; Covered Calls in the Calls' range, under Entry call strike and "when shares are uncovered"). The actions log says which kind won and by how much. **Technical:** `baseline.entry: "higher_yield"` (`baselineSaleSteps.ts` entry step, `baselineCandidateRank`); validation and settings template updated. Existing entries unchanged: a Covered Call copy (`1916fbfd`) and a put copy (DRAM `0c72dc1c` before the switch) re-ran IDENTICAL; on DRAM the put won every look where both could sell. No engine version change (a new choice).
- **Split check: options that match no standard split are another security's; META re-downloaded from 2022-06-09 (Marcelo 2026-10-20; pushed to staging 2026-10-20).** Plain-language: META's option file held another security's options from July 2021 to January 2022 (a Metaverse ETF had the ticker; Facebook traded as FB until 9 June 2022), and the split check read the mismatch with IBKR's prices as eight splits (9-for-10, 14-for-15, …). A change of scale that isn't one standard split (2:1, 3:2, 10:1, 1-for-15 and so on) now means the options before it are another security's or wrong: a session over those days fails naming the date, and a download that finds one fails with "delete the file and download it again from <date>". META's file was deleted and its download queued from 2022-06-09. **Technical:** `splitFactors.ts` (`isStandardSplitRatio` on `standardSplitRatios`, `startAfterNonStandardChanges`, `unmatchedOptionsBefore`, `UnmatchedOptionsError`; cache version 3), `executeBacktestJob.ts` (the download fails on it). Verified: the 32 other cached schedules come out unchanged (upgraded in place to version 3, so Data files keeps showing their splits), META's old one starts 2022-06-09; API tests 6,183/6,183.
- **LLM trader that learns from its own runs (Marcelo 2026-10-20; experiment done; design drafted for approval, nothing built).** Plain-language: tried letting Claude trade from a full daily snapshot (option chain with open interest and volume, stock and market context, the account) inside four hard limits, then review its results and write an "experience" file for its next run. Setup: HOOD + INTC, $100k, parts of Q2 and Q3 2024. Cost and speed are fine: about $0.04 of subscription usage and 6 s per look. Four findings make the results untrustworthy as specified. (1) Opus named both stocks and the exact period from masked snapshots: 93–95% sure with today's masking (aliases plus shifted dates), still 85–90% with relative days and rescaled prices. So no backtest before the models' training cutoff can show skill, and the existing LLM mode's masking is likely just as transparent. (2) The trader's lead over holding the stocks came from exposure (about 25% invested in falling stocks): against a portfolio matching its exposure it was within about a point, the same as the mechanical wheel. (3) Two runs on identical inputs ended 3.7 and 5.2 points apart over 8 weeks. (4) The reflection was well argued, but its top rule (sell only when IV beats 21-day realised volatility) is the filter the wheel research rejected, and the next runs sized up on it. Decided the same day (Marcelo):
  - Build it into the engine as a decision maker chosen in session settings, to compare with the mechanical wheel.
  - HOOD, PLTR, INTC, over full years, walking forward once.
  - Looks every 30 minutes, with the model asked only when something material changed.
  - Training on the Claude subscription. Validation sessions on gpt-6-luna only with his explicit permission each time.
  
  The design awaiting his approval is `docs/backtesting/LLM-TRADER-DESIGN.md` (decisions D1–D15). **Technical:** harness, runs and probes in `tmp/llm-trader-experiment/` (`run.py --policy llm|wheel|hold --mask weak|strong`, `report.py`, `reflect.py`, `probe.py`, `matched_mix.py`). Two issues it found, fixed the same day in `src/backtest/llm/claudeSubscriptionModelClient.ts` (uncommitted). The CLI's "You've hit your session limit" is now a usage-limit stop: it used to be retried as a passing failure and then stop with the wrong reason, and the Analyse summary showed it as "failed". The answering model recorded is now the one asked for: the CLI's Haiku side-call, which reads the whole prompt, used to be named when the real answer was short. Verified: client tests 11/11, Analyse summary tests, `tsc`, one real CLI call.
- **Strategy research written up; ideas backlog (Marcelo 2026-10-20; docs only, in `docs/strategy-research/` since 2026-10-21).** Plain-language: what the CC + Put Ladder research has found, and the ideas still to test, are now kept in `docs/strategy-research/`. `BACKTEST-FINDINGS.md` holds our backtests since 2026-10-13, with the base runs against buy and hold. `LITERATURE-REVIEW.md` holds the papers, practitioner backtests and income funds from the [Ladder Strategy Ideas](https://claude.ai/artifact/CAQhQ1myv575QkadVGtUqD) review, corrected. `IDEAS-TO-TRY.md` holds 36 ideas, each with a test design and status, plus checks on existing runs, what not to do and what's already tested. When an idea is tested, update its status there.
- **Falling-knife guard and Kelly sizing for the CC + Put Ladder (Marcelo 2026-10-21; built, backtested, then removed: no gain).** Plain-language: two new backtest switches size new buying by what the market says. The guard buys less when a stock falls on its own; Kelly sizing buys less near tops and more near bottoms. Tested alone and together on 22 tickers and a fresh 7-ticker hold-out, none passes, and buying more on the way down deepened drawdowns. Decision: the code was removed and nothing goes to Pluto; the next step is acting on shares already held (I-02, I-01). Report: [Knife Guard and Kelly Sizing](https://claude.ai/artifact/AdrV8SHr5ackXZ5QdqQBFQ); Round 7 in `docs/strategy-research/BACKTEST-FINDINGS.md`. **Technical:** archived in API commit 1bdc501 and app commit 56399f9; reverting b1a9711 and 53b0847 restores it. Research sessions deleted from the local DB; scripts and saved results in `tmp/kelly-research/`.
- **Edge Margin vs CC + Put Ladder, backtested side by side (Marcelo 2026-10-21; research, nothing changed in Pluto).** Plain-language: both execution strategies ran as one book each on 21 tickers with the same $3.27M, Aug 2021 to Oct 2026. They earned about the same (10.3% and 10.0% a year), but Edge Margin's Max drawdown was −38.6% against −14.9%, almost all of it in 2022; from 2023 on it did better. Report: [Edge Margin vs Ladder](https://claude.ai/artifact/SLfkVPPCmLaUoi6KZkrKcR). **Technical:** ladder on the lab; Edge Margin on live (staging) settings with `capitalBudgetPct` 100 and `minCashReservePct` 0. Local DB only: earnings histories added for AAPL, MRNA, PTON (from staging), TradingView symbols and API Ninjas' latest four dates for MSFT, MSTR, NFLX, ORCL, TECH; without them Edge Margin rejects every contract (`earnings_unresolved`). Scripts and results: `tmp/strategy-compare/`.
- **Edge Margin audit and a premium-first strategy (Marcelo 2026-10-22; research, closed: no alpha found).** Plain-language: this checked how Edge Margin measures volatility, then looked for a strategy that earns mostly from option premium on 11 tickers a pot can trade. Two fixes to the volatility model helped only slightly, and Edge Margin's edge doesn't tell which contracts will do better. The only premium found was in Puts with 4–7 days left. One weekly Put per ticker, never holding stock, made 6.6% a year with a −5.2% Max drawdown in our cash account (the IBKR account is not margin), mostly from T-bills and the stocks' rise. Marcelo closed the research; nothing changed in Pluto. Details: Round 10 in `docs/strategy-research/BACKTEST-FINDINGS.md`. **Technical:** API commit `ec5e4ff` added backtest settings `model.forecastMethod` and `model.restOfDayCurve` (live unchanged). Marcelo decided to take them out; once the rules backtests' retirement (2026-10-27) left them unread, the leftovers were removed by hand on 2026-10-29. Restoring them means re-applying `ec5e4ff` by hand. Local DB only: earnings histories from SEC filings for 16 tickers, and TradingView symbols. Scripts and results: `tmp/edge-margin-audit/`.
- **Steady Stake drawdown research and the Steady Stake as a book (Marcelo 2026-10-22/23; research, stopped by Marcelo; nothing built).** Plain-language: looked for ways to make the Steady Stake fall less without giving up return. Put insurance made falls shallower but cost 2–4 points of CAGR a year, and timing it didn't help on fresh tickers. Rules applied to each ticker on its own (not buying back in a downtrend, a smaller stake for volatile stocks, insurance around earnings) didn't improve return per unit of fall. What works is spreading it across many tickers: as one 13-ticker book it made 24.1% a year with a −36% worst fall, against −43% for the median ticker alone. About 81% of the money is in use at the median; the rest mostly earns T-bill interest because the same-day Puts often find no contract to sell. Sharing the spare cash across tickers and a wider delta tolerance were discussed, not decided. Details: Round 12 in `docs/strategy-research/BACKTEST-FINDINGS.md`. **Technical:** local book session `0ff733bd` ("New strategy research · book of 13 · s80 · $1.3M"); scripts and results in `tmp/steady-stake-drawdown-research/` and `tmp/backtestCashUsage.py`.
- **Lab settings editor rebuilt on Pluto's settings editor (Marcelo 2026-10-19; mockup approved: [Lab Settings Editor](https://claude.ai/artifact/HtMFfejDNMTnSbz1Jec7y9); built and verified locally, not committed).** Plain-language: the Backtesting screen's Lab settings now open from a "Lab settings" button in the header, in a near-full-screen window laid out like Pluto's Settings tab: a section list on the left, one group open at a time (Lab defaults on every opening), changed values highlighted with "was …", a save bar with Discard and Review and save, and recent changes on the right. Every setting a new session starts from can now be edited, not only the CC + Put Ladder's. The groups are How the session runs, Put ladder, Calls and Covered Calls, Contract choice and Exits, then, under "Pluto's rules and the AI model", the groups the CC + Put Ladder never reads (Capital and sizing, Candidate quality, Model, System checks, Rules' choices). A row that isn't read with the other values as they are says "Not applied now" and why (e.g. the fixed shock while the shock comes from history). New session's settings table uses the same groups. Closing with unsaved changes asks first; the backdrop never closes it. Ticker overrides table kept without its Analyse button; Change history replaced by Recent changes. Technical: the API template places rows through `backtestSettingLayout` (group, `section`, `appliesWhen` conditions from the engine's reads; module-load check that every row is placed once); a lab save re-runs analyses only for values an analysis reads (`analysisReadsLabPath`: lab pot, baseline.*, model.*, spread cost, look window), and the analysis hash covers only those (stored runs re-hashed on read, so no analysis turned stale). Checked with Playwright at 1440 and 390 px, light and dark: all text 4.5:1 or more (alpha blended), no console errors; API backtest tests 469/469.
- **Analyse window tiles reworked; Data files select-all (Marcelo 2026-10-19; built and verified locally, not committed).** Plain-language: the header shows the ticker with its name and category beside it (no facts row); above the Settings card the tiles are Last close, Implied volatility (with its 12-month percentile), Volatility 21-day, Open interest and Options volume; below it two fit tiles, Backtesting fit (Today's fit checked on every day of the last 12 months at the configured pot, share level and rungs; fits when the lot and all rungs fit on 95% of days) Today's fit (the same check at the last option day's price) and Put sales, last 30 days (volume only: days the best-yield ladder Put's volume limit, 20% of the previous session's volume, covered a rung's order of floor(rung ÷ (strike × 100)) contracts, at least 1; no-Put days count as not sold; median share of the previous session's volume); Max drawdown, Recommended shock and Best CC + Put Ladder backtest tiles removed. The Settings card's two buttons are Save and Analyse and Analyse Only, the same full analysis (backtest included) with the first also saving the values; Backtest these values removed from that card (it stays in Claude's recommendation); days to expiry and the spread limit are no longer fields there (global only, set in the global lab settings; Claude's Try keeps the global values). The Data files table has a select-all checkbox in its header. Tooltips show after 500 ms app-wide. Technical: `analysisOptionActivity.ts` (end-of-day volume over 21 and 252 sessions, open interest on the last day, each with the Puts at or under the first-rung cap) in `figures.optionActivity`, and the ladder Put's previous-session volume as a 7th `fitDays` field,, shown after each ticker's next Update Analysis; DataTable `headerContent`; `runAnalysisJob` runs the analysis backtest for trials too, the trial's values passed as the session's symbolSettings (global-only ones as overrides), and trials may reuse an earlier session with the same values.
- **Option files keep every expiry (Marcelo 2026-10-13; built, not committed).** Plain-language: the downloaded option prices stopped at 45 days to expiry; they now hold every expiry (long-dated options, LEAPS, included), so strategies mixing long- and short-dated options can be tested. Files already downloaded are topped up day by day, fetching only what they lack. Backtests still read 0–45 days, so past sessions replay unchanged; a strategy that needs longer expiries opens that up. **Technical:** downloader schema 3 (`days.all_expiries`, `intraday_days.all_expiries`, meta `max_dte` 'all'; spec in `DATA-FILES.md`), swapped in 2026-10-13 while the SMH download ran; the engine accepts schema 3 and caps its reads at 45/46 days in SQL (`backtestSymbolFile.ts`), day issues included (a far expiry's gap would otherwise add a note to a session day). INTC's 2024 and 4-year sessions replay identically on its old and topped-up file (all seven result tables, `tmp/dumpBacktestSessionReplay.ts`). Queue 2026-10-13: top-ups of INTC, AAOI, HOOD, MU, NOK, TLT and SMH, then CSCO, COIN, PLTR, BE, NBIS, WDC, PTON, WBA, SPY, QQQ, IWM (every expiry from the start). Copies from before the top-up: `backtest-data/backups-before-every-expiry/`.
- **Model backtesting (Marcelo 2026-10-12; built, pushed 2026-10-12; runs only locally).** Plain-language: a screen to replay the valuation model and Pluto's rules on 2020-onward option prices, to tune the model for the best risk-adjusted yield and total return. Decisions: runs only on Marcelo's Mac (data and the personal ThetaData licence stay there); prices come from the official ThetaData Python library (no Terminal; one request per ticker-day, about 0.1 MB and 3–12 s each); one file per symbol in a folder set once in `.env` inside the project (gitignored), downloads and backtest runs start from the screen but run as separate background processes (a server restart cannot cut them; downloads resume where they stopped) and add only missing days; the nav item shows only when the app runs locally; 10:00 ET first; results saved to the DB per session with every day's return (monthly returns, CAGR later); headline metrics Sortino and total return; stock prices from our IBKR daily bars and earnings history from the API Ninjas backfill. ThetaData: Options Value, stocks free (no stock history before 2023-06). Mockup approved 2026-10-12 (https://claude.ai/artifact/YKWFqLm2Q79wkKQQd1i86T; the built chart gets a legend and axis values). Also decided: idle cash earns the 3-month T-bill rate (FRED); the volume filter uses the previous day's full volume; the only baseline is buy and hold. Formula sheet: `docs/backtesting/FORMULAS.md` (approved 2026-10-12: interest on the whole cash balance, no dividends in version 1). Also decided: files keep expiries up to 45 days (every expiry since 2026-10-13); IBKR daily bars and implied volatility backfilled to 2016 in the local DB so walk-forward tables exist from 2020. Data files, jobs and history: `docs/backtesting/DATA-FILES.md`; migration `20261012300001`; code in `src/backtest/`, `scripts/backtest/`. Built 2026-10-12: ThetaData downloader (Python, `npm run backtest:setup`), background jobs (detached processes, progress/heartbeat in `backtest_jobs`), routes `/backtest/*` (mounted only when `BACKTEST_DATA_DIR` is set), engine (replays `scoreTicker` → Pluto filters → deterministic top pick → `runPostModelGates` → order-limit check; matched live on 4 tickers' 2026-10-06 snapshots), screens (dev build only). IBKR bars + implied volatility backfilled to 2016 for all 28 local tickers (`npm run backtest:backfill-history`); walk-forward tables build for 2020–2026. Splits: IBKR bars are split-adjusted, ThetaData options are as traded, so each day's split factor is inferred from closing parity ÷ adjusted close (Marcelo 2026-10-12) and cached in `splits/<SYMBOL>.json`; the 10:00 parity stock price uses only quotes with a spread ≤ 50% of the mid (approved 2026-10-12). **Next (Marcelo 2026-10-12): full fidelity** — version 1 holds to expiry and cash-settles in-the-money puts; the backtest must replay everything Pluto does except the LLM call (assignment into shares, calls on held shares, rolls, buybacks, stock sales, wheel cycles, every position's P&L). Design approved 2026-10-12: `docs/backtesting/FULL-FIDELITY-DESIGN.md` (multi-symbol sessions on one book, looks every 30 min inside Pluto's window, a ledger shaped like the live tables calling the live cycle, roll, close and sizing code, fixed rules standing in for the LLM with an optional real-LLM mode that is off by default and capped at $10 a session). Spec inputs: `INVENTORY-PLUTO-BEHAVIOUR.md`, `INVENTORY-WHEEL-ACCOUNTING.md`. Built locally, not committed: step 1 (downloader stores the 12 intraday looks in one request per ticker-day, survives corrupt days and a lost ThetaData session; one login per API key at a time), step 2 (ledger shaped like the live tables; `positionSelectors.ts`, `cycleInputRows.ts`, optional `settledAs` on cycle legs, all pinned by SQL parity tests), step 3 (the engine runs on the ledger: assignment, call-away, calls on held shares, rolls, the close rules via `buildCloseOffersFromBook`, the stand-in policy as session settings, storage for positions, legs, fills, actions, cycles and per-ticker statistics; first real SMCI sessions reconcile to the cent). Step 4 done (several symbols on one book). In progress: step 5 (30-minute looks, system checks, macro-event history; build status at the end of the design doc). Rulings (Marcelo 2026-10-12): the results headline is Wheel Cycle P&L as on the platform; no backtest-only criteria (rolls and closes follow Pluto's own code); an earnings date counts as known 30 days ahead; baselines are equal-dollar buy and hold of the session's symbols and buy-and-hold SPY (IBKR bars), for comparison only; after a blocked choice the stand-in tries the next one; the real-LLM mode runs through the Claude subscription (Claude Code CLI, as in sgtechno), off by default. Step 5 done (looks every 30 minutes, system checks per look, SPY buy-and-hold baseline, earnings known 30 days ahead; rules approved 2026-10-12, listed in FORMULAS.md). LLM mode built (decided 2026-10-12: Claude Sonnet 5.5, medium effort, through Marcelo's Claude subscription via the Claude Code CLI installed globally, path in `BACKTEST_CLAUDE_CLI_PATH`; no usage cap, a session stopped by the plan's limit resumes from the call cache; symbols shown as STOCK_A…, dates moved 1,044 weeks; `src/backtest/llm/`, migration `20261013100001`; off by default; about 5 s a call, so one year ≈ 4–8 h). Step 6 done (result screens: Wheel Cycle P&L headline, per-symbol statistics, cycles, positions, actions log and fills page by page, both buy-and-hold baselines, Linear/Log value chart, up to 30 symbols per session, the AI model's estimate before Start). Also 2026-10-12 (Marcelo): rules approved (return on capital in use on cycles, daily-loss breaker vs the previous close, SPY day change from 30-minute bars); an open too big for the order size tries the next candidate; edge capture on cycles counts share gains; the rules skip an open their own close-before-event rule would close at once (puts closed later in profit stay); symbols scored in parallel worker threads (10 symbols 618 → 336 ms a day, results identical). A platform fix found by it: the wheel-cycle code (`src/lib/cycles.ts`) read shares bought and sold within one moment as sold first ("never acquired"); purchases now go first in that case. HOOD + INTC every 30 minutes, 2021-08 → 2026-10: +48.8%, CAGR 8.0%, Sortino 1.52, max drawdown −16.3% (buy and hold: SPY +76.4%, HOOD + INTC +82.2%). The AI-model mode ran end to end on 3 HOOD days (12 calls, ~5.5 s each). Downloader schema 2 swapped in 2026-10-12 (`day_issues.attempts`; files upgrade on their next download). New session period (Marcelo 2026-10-12, pushed): whole years (From year / To year, the years every picked file shares; an edge year missing more than 4 calendar days is labelled, e.g. "2021 · from 4 Aug") or custom dates; opens on whole years. Two live fixes the inventory found, built and tested locally, not committed: the open-positions cap no longer refuses a call on shares already held (`postModelGates.ts`); a whole covered-call close (call bought back, shares sold) is recorded as an app close, not "assigned" (`ibkrGatewayReconcilePositions.ts`, in the worker's closure, so its push redeploys the worker). Speed: 2020 option days take ~15–25 s to download (2024 days ~2 s); a session runs ~30 ms a day. SMCI options before 2020-05 do not exist (relisted 2020-01) and its 2020 chain is too thin to fit. Downloader (2026-10-28): ThetaData publishes a session's options hours after it ends and answers no data until then, so a weekday under 7 days old with no data in any part is recorded as unavailable and asked for again on each run; only at 7 days old does it become a final `no_data` day.
- **Backtest engine speed (Marcelo 2026-10-12; pushed to staging 2026-10-12).** Plain-language: backtests run 4–5× faster with exactly the same results; HOOD + INTC + SMCI over 2022–2025 (every 30 minutes) went from about 4 minutes to under 1. Every change was checked by re-running sessions and comparing every stored row (days, trades, positions, fills, actions, cycles, metrics). **Technical:** the simulated ledger is indexed and versioned (lookups no longer scan the whole history; cycle inputs and the open-cycle summary are kept until the book changes); the symbol files are read a few days ahead on a reader thread (`backtestDayDataReader.ts`); each symbol's worker scores the next look ahead and reuses that answer when the book did not change, and builds the next day's inputs during the day's last look; the implied-volatility solve and contract valuations are kept by their exact inputs, the bisection stops once converged, and the SVI fit no longer allocates per step; only the open wheel cycle is derived for a close check (`deriveOpenCycle`). The shared library changes (`impliedVolatilitySurface`, `contractValuation`, `cycles`, `closeLiveState`, `easternIsoDate`) give the same numbers live, faster. Later the same day: each symbol is scored on two workers taking looks in turn (each warmed two looks ahead; 6 workers for 3 symbols), open legs and positions kept per ledger revision, look quotes selected without per-quote keys (a further ~15–25%); a day built ahead is no longer read twice (that leak was in the first push). Peak memory ~2.6 GB on the 4-year session (1.2 GB before the work).
- **Backtest engine speed, round 2 (Marcelo 2026-10-28; committed to main 2026-10-29, staging push by Marcelo).** Plain-language: backtests run faster with exactly the same results. Over 2020–2026: a Vol Farmer v2 run that decides at 10:00 went from 20 s to 6 s, the 24-ticker Vol Farmer v2 book from 8 minutes to under 3, a CC + Put Ladder run from 25 s to 18 s. Most of a queued run's time was spent reading its option file from disk (the files are too big to stay in memory), so the engine now reads much less of it. **Technical:** the CC + Put Ladder's candidate selection works out each quote's DTE, volume limit, forward, IV and delta once per look, shared by the look's selections (`BaselineLookFigures`; live Pluto's CC + Put Ladder runs the same code). The reader thread reads intraday quotes one look at a time, so the 45-day expiry bounds the primary key (about a third of the pages). Sessions that decide at 10:00 only (Vol Farmer v2 `first_look`, Steady Stake) no longer read the later looks' rows: the reader probes each look for one row in the 10:00 price's strike range (`probeIntradayQuotesOnly`), and the session reads the held contracts by key; a Vol Farmer v2 symbol waiting on the zero-quote rule still reads its looks in full. Checked IDENTICAL on every stored row against copies from ce52146: XLE CC + Put Ladder, COIN and TQQQ Vol Farmer v2, SMCI Steady Stake, a 13-ticker Steady Stake book, a 24-ticker Vol Farmer v2 book (TQQQ and SMCI across splits).
- **Backtest engine speed, round 3: Vol Farmer runs (Marcelo 2026-10-29; committed to main 2026-10-29, not pushed).** Plain-language: Vol Farmer backtests run two to three times faster again, with exactly the same results. Over 2020–2026, read cold from disk: one Vol Farmer v2 run takes 2.5 s instead of 6.7 s, six side by side (as the queue runs them) about 6 s instead of 11 s, the 24-ticker Vol Farmer v2 book 44 s instead of 2½ minutes. The engine stopped reading what no rule uses (the open interest, and the model tables, which only the CC + Put Ladder reads), stopped working out the marks of every look after 10:00 when only the last one counts, and reads up to three days of a file at the same time. **Technical:** in sessions that decide at 10:00 only, each later look's marks wait in `marksOnlyLooksBySymbol`, and `applyMarksOnlyLooks` sets them from the last look back before anything reads them (a later-look decision, the day's end): the latest look with quotes in range and an IBKR price sets the stock's mark, the latest that quotes an option two-sided sets that option's. The reader threads read no intraday rows for these sessions (`readsIntradayQuotes`) and check only the day's last look, in the 10:00 price's strike range (`IntradayLookCheck`); an option unmarked at 10:00 reads the following looks one at a time until its first two-sided quote (`nextIntradayLookTime`). The open interest is no longer read (`openInterestByContract` removed). Only the baseline prepares the model tables: Vol Farmer and Steady Stake sessions skip loading them (0.2 s a run) and rebuilding them after a data change (13.5 s a year), so such a session no longer fails when its years' tables cannot be built. Three reader threads (`dayDataReaderThreadCount`, one per day read ahead), each with its own connection to the files. The daily split alignment tests open legs first, and a symbol's share-cost tracing is kept per ledger revision (the end-of-run records derived it again for each price scale). Checked IDENTICAL on every stored row against copies from 2b80eb6: COIN, TQQQ and ORCL Vol Farmer v2 (ORCL hits the zero-quote rule), an ORCL + WMT + TQQQ book with a later-look decision, the 24-ticker Vol Farmer v2 book, the 13-ticker Steady Stake book, SMCI Steady Stake, XLE Ladder at every look, AAPL Vol Farmer, SKHY baseline `first_look`.
- **Parked until the ThetaData chains (Marcelo 2026-10-10): longer-dated contracts.** Plain-language: for options with more than about two weeks left, history says sales the model rates well still lost money on average, so Pluto sizes them to zero. The likely cause is the 21-day volatility forecast going stale over a longer life. To test on real option prices: a forecast that leans on longer history as the life grows, and grading on the history-corrected margin instead of the raw one. Evidence: `docs/signal-engine-model-review/REPORT.md` Round 5.
- **Edge on one clock (2026-10-09, formula approved by Marcelo; pushed to staging 2026-10-09).** Plain-language: the volatility forecast counts trading days, the market's implied volatility counts calendar days; Edge compared them as they were, which made 1–4-day contracts with no weekend in their life look rich and short contracts across a weekend look poor. Now each contract's forecast is put on its own clock first: forecast × √((trading sessions to expiry ÷ 252) ÷ (calendar days ÷ 365)). SMCI $43 Call · 9 Oct (Thu → Fri) on 10-08: forecast 76.6% → 92.2%, Edge +5.9 → −9.7 vp (at the money); AAPL · 12 Oct (Thu → Mon, 2 sessions in 4 days): 29.6% → 25.2%; 30-day contracts move under 2 vp. Evidence: on that clock the market's own IVs line up (AAPL Thu→Fri 23.2 vs Thu→Mon 22.9; NVDA 28.4 vs 28.6), and a Fri→Mon session moves 0.9× a weekday one in our bars (26 tickers, 5 years). The order panel's forecast line shows the contract's figure (label unchanged; Marcelo rejected extra text). **Technical:** `forecastOnCalendarDayBasis` (renamed from `forecastOnOptionClock` 2026-10-10) / `tradingSessionsByExpiry` in `volatilityEdge.ts`; `TickerSignalsInputs.openSessionDatesIso` (market calendar, weekdays past its end); new candidates and the held leg in rolls (`signalCandidates.ts`, `rollSignalCandidates.ts`); Pluto's event stress prices with sessions ÷ 252 (worked examples now $43.04 / $391.28 / $116.03, were $24.84 / $390.25 / $93.37); the stress cap counts market sessions (holidays excluded); Pluto prompt v3.9 (migration `20261009100001`): candidates carry `forecast_vol` and the edge_vp line names it, so the payload stays consistent.
- **Splits detected from IBKR, not guessed; the 70% rule removed (2026-10-09, Marcelo; pushed to staging 2026-10-09).** Plain-language: a stock split used to be guessed from a big overnight move, which wrongly threw out MRNA's real +84% jump on 2026-08-19 (a vaccine trial result), leaving MRNA on its 21-day forecast (86%) and out of Pluto until mid-November. Now every time daily prices are saved, the days already stored are compared with IBKR's, which IBKR adjusts for splits: if they all differ by one ratio, the whole history is downloaded again and a Telegram message says so. Big real moves now count in the forecast: MRNA's 63-day forecast becomes about 155% until 08-19 leaves the window (snapshot of 2026-11-18). **Technical:** `ibkr/dailyBarSplitCheck.ts` (`detectPriceAdjustment`: a shared day whose open/high/low/close all differ by one ratio, ±0.5%; `storeDailyBarsCheckingForSplit`: with no shared day it fetches back to the newest stored day, which also fills missed days; on a split a 20-year re-fetch replaces the rows, keeps implied volatility, deletes older uncovered days and the intraday cache); `priceBarCache.storeFreshDailyBars` used by the nightly job (now `lookupRecentDailyBars`, both bars of the 2-day window), the shortlist top-up, the chart cache and ticker setup. `realizedVolatility.ts`: the whole suspected-split guess is removed (Marcelo, 2026-10-09): the ±70% rule and the ratio-plus-volume rule, with the `suspected_split` unscored reason, its Signals caveat and the Shortlist readiness note (app too). Verified on the dev DB with a simulated 2-for-1 (with and without a shared day, `tmp/verifySplitRepairOnDevDb.mts`); staging's 28 tickers match IBKR (`tmp/scanStoredBarsForSplits.mts`, read-only). Only MRNA had a >50% overnight move in a recent 63-day window.

Marcelo's ultimate goal: remove human execution from CC/CSP trading entirely, with Genosuke (or similar) placing/closing/rolling orders and IORIO Pulse as the monitoring/override layer. Before touching execution, he asked for a rigorous, literature-backed probabilistic model to rank which alerts are actually worth trading — "maximum profit, minimum risk over an infinite timeline," with a risk throttle that need not scale reward linearly. Also asked whether expanding to SPX/XSP/SPY is worthwhile.

**Research basis, findings and proposed architecture: see the guiding artifact linked at the top of this file (§01–§12).** The earlier copy of that text lives in `PROGRESS-ARCHIVE.md`.


**Correction (2026-09-25 staleness pass): every "(uncommitted)" / "not yet run" / "nothing consumes it yet" / "UI not started" tag below, from the 2026-09-21 entries through the 2026-09-24 Day Signals / Roll Signals entries, is stale.** Verified via `git log`: the Phase 0 capture/backfill/surface-fit code (lines below dated 2026-09-21/22) shipped in api `532bc8e` (2026-09-23); dividend-schedule + risk-adjusted-ratio shipped in `bd0fb73`; Day Signals shipped in `4e68627`; the 2026-09-24 audit-fix rounds shipped in `a6e168a`; Roll Signals shipped in `385c7dc`; notification hysteresis shipped in `dbd435b` — all on `origin/main`, i.e. live on staging. Tilt/UncompensatedShare/Friction/Volatility-Edge are all consumed by `signalCandidates.ts`/`signalsLiveScoring.ts` (net Edge, drift, and skew all read them) — none of them are dead code waiting on a consumer anymore. The per-entry commentary below (verification steps, real-data checks, decisions) is still accurate and kept for the record; only the commit/deploy-status tags were wrong.

**Decisions made 2026-09-16** (via AskUserQuestion, all four "recommended" options chosen):
1. Default risk-throttle starting point: **quarter-Kelly** (conservative, given the VRP-decline finding above).
2. Build order: **read-only scoring/ranking dashboard in IORIO Pulse first** — no execution authority — before any sizing or automation work.
3. SPX/XSP/SPY: **deferred** until the single-name scoring model is validated.
4. Directional tilt (momentum + options-skew): **included from day one** alongside the volatility-edge model (the one decision where Marcelo went against the "defer it" recommendation — build it in from the start, not as a later phase).

- **Built 2026-09-21 (the parts needing no IBKR; uncommitted, not deployed)**: migration `20260921000001_create_option_chain_snapshots.ts` (`option_chain_snapshots` + `option_quote_snapshots`, exactly as approved plus a `contracts_with_any_tick` column for the approved starved-ticker rule); `src/lib/optionChainCaptureWindow.ts` (Formula 7: `computeStrikeWindow`, `selectContractsToCapture`); `src/lib/optionChainCaptureClock.ts` (`isWithinChainCaptureClockWindow`, reuses `easternInstant`/`easternDateIso` from `marketSessionStatus.ts`); `src/lib/realizedVolatility.ts` (approved Yang-Zhang + split guard: ±70% rule, ratio detector confirmed by volume, ≥5 prior bars needed to check volume else conservatively flagged). Parameters approved as listed: split detector ±15% of 1/m for m∈{2,3,4,5,10,20}, volume ≥ 0.5·m × prior-20-bar median; ~60 lines/batch; starved = <90% of contracts with any tick, one re-capture, only if <45 min elapsed, 10-min cap; guard 10:00–10:30 ET.
- **Verified 2026-09-21**: 36 new tests + full suite 133/133, `tsc --noEmit` clean. Yang-Zhang checked against an **independent Python implementation** (every component to 12–15 digits), against a large seeded simulation with known true vol (no drift and 150%/yr drift, within 6%), and **mutation-tested** (dropping the overnight term or swapping the RS weight makes 3 tests fail; file restored). Clock guard tested at all EDT/EST boundaries, both DST switch days, and **every day of 2026–2027: exactly one of the 14:00/15:00 UTC Scheduler slots passes the guard, even with a 2-minute start delay**. Migration applied to local dev DB only (Batch 30), verified directly: write/read-back, unique(ticker, day), option_right CHECK, contract PK, delete-header-cascades-to-contracts then re-insert (the approved replace-on-re-run), same contract stored across days, all rolled back to 0 rows; rollback and re-apply both clean. Prod gets the migration on the next deploy (release phase) after Marcelo pushes.
- **Real-data check of the Yang-Zhang engine** (`tmp/checkYangZhangOnRealBars.ts`, read-only, dev DB): plausible on all 23 tickers (e.g. TLT ~10%, CSCO ~25–38%, LITX ~176–203%), no split flags anywhere in the last year. **Observation, not a bug**: on trending names (COIN 77 vs 98, HOOD 74 vs 93, IGV 30 vs 40 for YZ21 vs close-to-close 21-day) Yang-Zhang reads well below close-to-close, because its range term treats a steady intraday move as drift (RS < open-close on those names) and it assumes overnight and intraday moves are independent — in those windows they point the same way. It measures diffusive vol; the spread of a multi-day option's terminal price also depends on trend, so the richness comparison (Phase 1/2) should consider close-to-close alongside it — a forecast-model decision for later, formula unchanged. Guard behaved correctly on NBIS's 2022 Yandex-era history: 2022-02-24 (open 0.527× prior close on 66.5M shares ≈ 10× normal volume — the Russia-invasion crash, the accepted "crash looks like a split" false positive) and 2022-03-24 (zero-volume trading-suspension artifact, 8.00 → 18.94, caught by the ±70% rule). SMCI's Oct-2024 10-for-1 split can't be checked until the 5-year backfill exists.
- **Also built 2026-09-21, second pass (offline-verified, uncommitted)**: `src/ibkr/captureOptionQuoteBatch.ts` — the archive's quote collector (streaming `reqMktData` with generic tick 101; captures bid/ask/last, bid/ask sizes, open interest, volume, IBKR model IV + greeks, model price and the spot IBKR used; real-time and delayed tick types both accepted; IBKR "no data" sentinels normalized to null; errors recorded per contract; every subscription cancelled and every listener removed). **A new module, not a change to `fetchQuotesForContracts`**, because alerts and the Ticker Detail chain depend on that one — an implementation choice made without asking (fully reversible; Marcelo can veto and have it merged instead). `src/lib/optionChainCaptureCoverage.ts` (approved constants: 60-line batches, starved <90% any-tick, re-capture only inside 45 min / 10-min cap; `splitIntoBatches`, coverage, status complete/partial/failed, market-data-type derivation, re-capture rule). `src/lib/optionChainSnapshotStore.ts` (`saveOptionChainSnapshot`: same-day re-run replaces, one transaction, chunked inserts, duplicate contracts collapse to the last). Verification: 27 new tests (collector tested against a **fake IBKR feed**: real-time, delayed, sentinels, ceiling, errors, cleanup, foreign req ids) — full suite 160/160, `tsc` clean; the writer round-tripped against the real dev DB (read-back, same-day replace with 0 orphans, 4,000-contract chunked insert, cleanup). **Live-verified 2026-09-21 (pre-market probe, see below)**: open interest, bid/ask sizes and volume arrive per contract in streaming mode (tickSize 27/28, 0/3, 8) under generic tick 101. **Still unverified (only flows during market hours)**: model computation ticks 13/83 (IV, greeks, model price, spot used) and real bid/ask prices — the probe must be repeated at the open.
- **Verification pass 2026-09-21 (Marcelo: "test and double check everything, check sources")** — *Sources checked*: IBKR's tick-types docs confirm every tick type used (sizes 0/3/5/8, open interest 27/28 via `tickSize` under generic tick 101, delayed 66/67/68/69/70/74, computation 10–13 and delayed 80–83). The `@stoqey/ib` v1.6.6 decoder already turns IBKR's "not computed" markers into `undefined` (IV −1, delta −2, optPrice −1, pvDividend −1, gamma/vega/theta −2, undPrice −1) — my null handling is a second layer on top; a theta of exactly −2 can never be seen (library drops it). IBKR docs: **50 messages/second from the client side, shared by all clients on a Gateway**, and **100 market-data lines per TWS *username*, combined across TWS and every API app on that login** (not per connection); the library has its own limiter (default 40 msg/s, 4 per 100 ms), so a 60-contract batch costs ~3 s of subscribe+cancel messaging before any data. `option_chain_params` (cached 24 h) returns a **union of strikes across expiries** — the job must validate each (expiry, strike) with `getCachedValidStrikes`, and the existing code fires those checks with `Promise.all`: fine for dozens, **not for thousands on the first night — the job needs a concurrency cap** (message limit). *Two real flaws found in my own code and fixed*: open-interest ticks (27/28) were counted as "real-time" evidence, which would mislabel delayed data as "mixed" (removed; static daily data says nothing about the feed); and the fallback request-id counter was per call, so two concurrent batches on one one-shot socket could collide (now module-level). *Test rigour*: 12 deliberate breakages of the new modules ("mutations") — 10 caught immediately, **2 survived, exposing 2 real test gaps** (no quote with a bid but no ask; no call and put at the same strike), both closed and re-run: all 12 now caught. Suite 163/163, `tsc` clean. Live probe `tmp/liveOptionCollectorProbe.ts` (typecheck caught that `selectContractsToCapture` returns strike+side only — the job must attach the expiry) **run with Marcelo's permission, 2026-09-21 04:34 ET (pre-market), AMAT Oct-2 expiry, 9 contracts, one batch, read-only** — and it found a **real bug the docs didn't reveal**: for every option contract IBKR sends BOTH open-interest ticks, **tick 27 = open interest of CALLS, tick 28 = PUTS, and the tick for the other right is always 0**. My first version kept whichever arrived last, so every call's real OI (152, 3, 83, 45 on AMAT 445/447.5/450/452.5C) was overwritten with 0 (looked implausible for a liquid name). Fixed: a call reads tick 27, a put tick 28, in either arrival order; new test proven to fail against the old behavior; re-probed and confirmed. Other live observations: pre-market, bid/ask arrive as −1 (→ null, sentinel handling works), sizes and volume arrive as 0, **no computation ticks at all** (no IV/greeks without quotes, so `settled` never triggers and the batch runs to its ceiling — expected off-hours), plus tick 9 (previous close) and tick 5 (last size), and several ticks arrive twice per contract. Batch of 9 subscribes took the full 15 s ceiling (nothing to settle on).
- **Adding SPY/QQQ to the shortlist — what it would trigger (checked 2026-09-21)**: works with no hardcoding (the archive universe is shortlist + held, exactly like `run-daily-market-data-job`/`run-daily-calendar-capture-job`, which would also give them daily bars/IV/calendar for free, and `findOrCreateTicker` backfills ~1y of history + IV on add; Price Performance would list them too). **But `runTradeAlertGeneration` scans every shortlisted ticker with both strategies**, so SPY/QQQ would also generate covered-call/CSP alerts and Telegram messages — contradicting "data only". SPY/QQQ are not in `tickers` yet (dev DB). Adding to the real shortlist is Marcelo's action via the UI.
- **Gotcha found 2026-09-21**: another session's `sync-dev-db` recreated the local dev DB from prod (DB created 14:13 +08) between two of my checks, which **wiped the unreleased archive migration** (local `knex_migrations` reverted to prod's batch 29). After any `sync-dev-db`, re-run `npm run migrate:latest` for migrations not yet in prod. Re-applied (Batch 30) and re-verified.
- **5-year backfill script BUILT 2026-09-21 (uncommitted, never run against IBKR).** `npm run backfill:signal-engine-history [-- --dry-run] [SYMBOL…]`: fetches 5Y daily bars + IV per ticker (universe = shortlist + held), prints a per-ticker sanity report incl. split-guard hits (this is the SMCI Oct-2024 check), `--dry-run` writes nothing. Reuses a new `fetchDailyHistoryFromIbkr` split out of `priceBarCache.ts`; report logic in `src/lib/backfillBarSummary.ts` (tested). **Pacing default (10 s between tickers, 2 requests each) is my choice — needs Marcelo's OK before any real run.**
- **DESIGN AGREED 2026-09-21 (Marcelo): new-ticker backfill pipeline + progress modal, not yet built.** Every add path (manual, screener, re-add) runs ONE shared pipeline: (1) 5Y daily bars + IV with split-guard report, (2) earnings/ex-dividend calendar, (3) option chain params + strike-cache warm-up (0–90 DTE, 20 in flight), (4) first chain snapshot if the market is open (else 'will run tonight'). Delivery: server-side job writing step progress to a status table, streamed to the UI over SSE (survives modal close/refresh). The ticker appears on the shortlist immediately with a 'preparing' badge. UI modal (progress bar + status messages; informational, closes on ESC/backdrop) needs an approved Artifact mockup before coding. Open: how nightly jobs treat a ticker still 'preparing'.
