The union of strikes `reqSecDefOptParams` returns is a poor proxy for a given expiry — 49% of its strike × expiry pairs don't exist (28% on the front expiry, ~60% a month out) — so the per-expiry grid is also *more correct*, not just faster; cross-check against every strike the old cache had confirmed: zero disagreements. **Rules that came out of the tests (Marcelo): the wildcard is issued one at a time, per connection, always** — concurrency 1, 2 and 4 all took the same total time (1 slightly faster; IBKR queues these per client) and 17 at once earlier timed out 14 of them; the fully ambiguous form (no expiry) is never used (that was the 2026-08-14 AMAT throttling). **Data model (Marcelo): the nightly capture and `chain_warmup` are the only things that fetch chain structure from IBKR; they store it (`option_chain_params` + new `option_chain_expiry_strikes`, 0-90 DTE only, no TTL); the Ticker Detail chain, position quotes, quote snapshots and the alert scan read the DB only and never go to IBKR for it.** A ticker with nothing stored shows "Option chain … is not prepared yet" in its chain section (the alert scan logs a warning and skips it) rather than fetching. Deleted: `checkStrikeExists`, `getCachedValidStrikes`, `getCachedOptionParams`, `validateStrikesWithCap`, the 20-in-flight cap, the candidate buffer, the 23h TTLs, and the `option_chain_strike_checks` table. Per-ticker timings (expiries-list ms, each grid's ms and strike count, total) are printed by the job and stored in `job_runs.details.chainRefreshBySymbol` (Marcelo: "log timings when the job runs so we can inspect it later"). Verified live on AAOI: writer stored 8 grids in 39.8s (3.7-6.6s each), reader built the chain from the DB in 0.1s with no IBKR connection open; 445 tests pass. Benchmark scripts kept for re-running: `tmp/benchmarkPerStrikeProbe.ts`, `tmp/benchmarkWildcardPerExpiry.ts` (`--concurrency=N`), `tmp/verifyStoredOptionChain.ts`. **Caveat**: measured at night with the market closed; the first market-hours nightly run's timings are in `job_runs.details` — check them.

**RESOLVED 2026-09-23 by the redesign above (8 requests per ticker instead of ~1,370, each with its own timing logged). Original finding, kept for the record: `chain_warmup` could hang indefinitely with zero progress reporting.** QQQ's own pipeline run (started clicking the old buggy "Backfill Price History" before the fix above landed) sat at `chain_warmup` — "Checking which strikes exist..." — for 29+ minutes with the step's `message` field never updating, right at the edge of the 30-minute `staleBackfillRunMinutes` cutoff. `prepareTicker`'s `validateStrikesWithCap` (`runOptionChainCapture.ts`) processes every expiry in the 0-90-day window fully sequentially, capped-but-serial within each expiry too, with no per-group progress write and — more importantly — no timeout on the underlying IBKR strike-validation call, so one hung `getCachedValidStrikes` call blocks the entire step forever. QQQ is a plausible worst case (dense, many-expiry ETF) but 29 minutes of literally zero measurable progress is a hang, not just slow pacing — this is the same "job needs a concurrency cap for thousands of strikes" risk flagged back on 2026-09-21 (Signal Engine section) never fully addressed. Did not restart the process or the run to "fix" it (would destroy the only evidence of what's actually stuck); left it running for follow-up investigation with real logs rather than guessing. **Needs a dedicated session**: add a timeout to the strike-validation call, and/or write incremental progress (e.g. "expiry 4/12") so a future stall is visibly distinguishable from real work in the UI instead of an unmoving progress bar. **Update: this specific run was manually interrupted 2026-09-23** (restarting the dev API server via a normal `tsx watch` reload, triggered by an unrelated code edit — not a blind "restart to fix," the root cause above was already found first) after it reached 40+ minutes with zero progress, well past what even a fully-timed-out worst case should take (individual strike checks are bounded at 5s each, 20 in flight per group — worst case for a dense ETF's expiry count lands around 7-8 minutes, not 40); the gap between that estimate and reality means something beyond "QQQ has an unusually large chain" is going on, not yet identified. The server restart kills the in-memory process but does not update the DB row (nothing runs `store.finish` on a kill) — manually flipped the orphaned `ticker_backfill_runs` row to `partial` with an explanatory step message so `GET /shortlist`'s `backfillNeedsRetry` reflects reality again (verified: was `false`/silently-looked-fine before the fix, `true` after).
**Also fixed 2026-09-23 (Marcelo: "first snapshot: fix it"): the `first_snapshot` pipeline step no longer captures immediately on add.** It always skips now, unconditionally, with the nightly 10:00 ET job left to capture it like every other ticker — previously it captured immediately whenever `isMarketOpen()` was true, which could be any time from 9:30am-4pm ET depending purely on when the ticker happened to be added, giving that one ticker's first data point a different time-of-day reference than the fixed-clock-window capture (`optionChainCaptureClock.ts`, 10:00-10:30 ET) every other snapshot uses — the same class of timing inconsistency already flagged once before (2026-09-11 capture-timing finding). `isMarketOpen`/`getRiskFreeRate`/`captureSnapshot` removed from `BackfillStepWorkers` entirely (no longer called by anything); tests updated (445/445, down from 447 after merging the now-redundant market-open/closed test cases into one). Not yet deployed.

**Volatility Surface modal BUILT 2026-09-23 (both repos, uncommitted; Playwright-verified).** In plain terms: clicking the "X/Y ok" surface-fit count on either the Shortlist or Signals screen now opens a 3D chart of that ticker's fitted volatility curve (one interactive sheet, log-moneyness × time-to-expiry × implied volatility), plus a table of each expiry's fit-quality numbers — prototyped first as a claude.ai Artifact against real AAOI data and approved before building it into the app. Backend: `option_surface_fits`' `point_count`/`rmse_volatility`/`min_butterfly_density`/`dropped_counts`/`calendar_checks`/`calendar_violations` are now selected by `loadSlices()` (`signalsStore.ts`) and added to `SignalSurfaceSlice` (`signalCandidates.ts`); `GET /signals/:symbol` now returns the raw per-expiry slices too, via a new `TickerSignalsDetail` type kept off the whole-screen `SignalsScreenRow`/`GET /signals` payload so the list endpoint doesn't carry every ticker's SVI parameters. Frontend: new `plotly.js-dist-min` + `@types/plotly.js` dependency (a local `.d.ts` re-exports the `plotly.js` types for the dist-min import path, since `@types/plotly.js` doesn't type several gl3d-only trace/layout attributes this chart uses — worked around with a small local loose trace type rather than fighting the incomplete community types); new `VolatilitySurfaceModal.tsx` reconstructs the surface client-side from the SVI parameters (same `sviTotalVariance` formula as the backend), reads `option_surface_fits.k_min`/`k_max` per expiry to show true fitted-range gaps rather than extrapolating, uses `uirevision` to preserve the viewer's camera angle across knot-toggle/resize, and follows the app's existing informational-modal convention (`modal-xl` sized to content per Marcelo's feedback, not full-bleed; backdrop/Escape dismiss). Sequential color ramp anchored on `--tblr-primary`. Live-verified with Playwright against the real dev DB/AAOI snapshot in both themes and at 390px mobile width, from both entry points, including the backdrop-dismiss and knot-toggle interactions; both repos typecheck clean; API test suite 447/447 (fixture builders in the four affected test files extended with the new slice fields). Not yet committed or deployed.
**Debugging note (real find, worth remembering): `plotly.js-dist-min` 2.x–4.x's gl3d `surface` trace goes solid black across the whole canvas — ignoring `paper_bgcolor`/`scene.bgcolor` entirely — the moment the trace's `colorbar` uses the nested `title: { text, font }` form; the flat `colorbar: { title: "IV %" }` string form (or omitting title) doesn't trigger it.** Confirmed by isolated bisection (stripping one trace property at a time against the real AAOI data, on both a floating scratch div and the actual modal container) after several false leads (a large per-point `text` hover matrix, `showscale`, `lighting`, the custom colorscale ramp — all fine in isolation) turned out to be contaminated by leftover floating test `<div>`s left in the DOM from earlier bisection steps, which silently became `document.querySelectorAll('.js-plotly-plot')[0]` on later checks instead of the real modal — always clean up (or scope your selector past) scratch DOM nodes mid-debugging, or a "confirmed fix" can be testing the wrong element. Fixed here by dropping the colorbar title text (the z-axis title already labels the color scale).
**Follow-up UI pass on the same modal, 2026-09-23 (Marcelo's feedback): two more real bugs found, not just polish.** (1) The scene's axis tick/title text was rendering as literally nothing (not small — absent) in every screenshot; root cause was `layout.font.family: "inherit"` — a valid CSS keyword for a DOM element but meaningless to the raw Canvas 2D context gl3d rasterizes 3D tick/axis text with, so `ctx.font` silently fails to parse and nothing gets drawn; fixed by using a real font stack there instead. (2) The chart looked tiny inside a lot of empty canvas; fixed by tightening the camera (`eye` moved closer), capping the chart's own width (620px, centered, was stretching to the full `modal-xl`), and shrinking Plotly's margins. Also found and fixed a real pre-existing bug while re-checking mobile: the `ResizeObserver` effect that sets `chartHeight`/font-size had an empty `[]` dependency array, but the chart `<div>` it measures only exists once the async `signals` fetch resolves — so at mount `containerRef.current` is null, the effect bails, and since it never re-runs, the chart was silently stuck at desktop sizing on every phone-width open (masked earlier because a 460px-tall chart in a narrow viewport doesn't look obviously broken); fixed by keying the effect off `traces` instead of `[]`. Both re-verified with Playwright at desktop and 390px mobile, light and dark.

**Idea flagged 2026-09-23 (Marcelo, not yet scoped/approved): replace the current Screener (IBKR market-scanner discovery tool, `ScreenerTab.tsx`/`screener_scan_results`) with the Signals framework.** I.e. retire the separate scan-code-based discovery path in favor of running Signals scoring itself as the discovery/ranking tool. Not analyzed yet — would need to work out how a not-yet-shortlisted ticker gets a surface fit/forecast to score against (Signals currently only scores the shortlist, which has 5Y history + daily snapshots; the Screener scans the whole market via IBKR scan codes with no such backfill). Logged here per Marcelo's ask; needs its own design pass before estimating.

**Residual UI, 2026-09-21.** Residual label on both Dashboard cards is a dotted-underline `DottedLabelTooltip` (components/HelpTooltip.tsx) with a footnote; verified desktop dark + mobile touch light, no overflow. The Dashboard "P&L Over Time" chart is stacked columns (one bar per day, Residual on top; the stack total equals the account's daily P&L) instead of overlapping areas, because ApexCharts stacked areas cross over on mixed-sign data.
**Delta-drift day-count bug fixed 2026-09-24: Delta drift risk showed 0% for every short-dated (1-2 calendar day) opportunity.** `uncompensatedShare.ts`'s Monte Carlo stepped the simulation in *trading* days (`steps = round(yearsToExpiry * 252)`) while `yearsToExpiry` itself is computed on a *calendar*-day convention elsewhere in the codebase (`yearsBetweenIsoDates`) — for a 2-calendar-day option this rounded `steps` to 1, collapsing the whole simulation to a single step and making `timingShare` (and therefore the drift %) always exactly 0, silently, for every near-dated candidate. Fixed by switching the step count to calendar days (`steps = round(yearsToExpiry * 365)`), matching the day-count `yearsToExpiry` already uses (Marcelo's choice between the two consistent fixes on offer). Verified numerically (COIN 2-DTE example: 0% → ~11.6%) and against the full backend suite (443/443 relevant tests, one pre-existing unrelated failure confirmed via `git stash` to predate this change). **Known residual, out of scope for this fix:** 1-DTE options still show exactly 0% — a separate, smaller model limitation (even 1 calendar day still rounds `steps` to 1).

**Signals tab on Risk & Limits BUILT 2026-09-24 (both repos).** New `signal_settings` singleton table (migration `20260924000002`/`20260924000003`, one row, no per-strategy key — unlike `strategy_settings`) holding six thresholds the Signals screen will score/filter candidates against: Max Delta drift % (10), Min Annualised Yield % (50), Max Net Delta (0.4), Max position % of portfolio (10), Max concentration per ticker % (20), Min cash reserve % (5). **Explicitly independent from `strategy_settings`' own max-position/concentration/cash-reserve fields** (Marcelo's call via an options question, 2026-09-24) even though three of the six names overlap — Signals and Trade Alerts each keep their own separate values rather than sharing one set, so a change on one tab never silently moves the other. New `GET`/`PUT /signal-settings` route (`routes/signalSettings.ts`, same validation/shape convention as `routes/riskLimits.ts`'s strategy-settings endpoints), new `api/signalSettings.ts` frontend client, and a form on the Risk & Limits page's "Signals" tab (previously a placeholder) reusing the existing `NumberField`/`HelpTooltip` components from the Trade Alerts tab. Live-verified against the real dev DB: seeded defaults render correctly, Save round-trips to Postgres with the correct `updated_by_user_id`/timestamp, and "Last updated by X" renders correctly after a reload.

**Signals tab settings WIRED INTO ENFORCEMENT, 2026-09-24 (both repos).** The six thresholds above now split into two enforcement mechanisms, per Marcelo's decisions this session: **filters** (max net delta, min annualised yield) exclude candidates in `buildSignalCandidates` itself, alongside the existing structural filters; **max delta drift** filters lazily inside `scoreTicker` once `uncompensatedSharePercent` is actually known (the Monte Carlo only runs for the open modal's ticker — a candidate whose drift share isn't known yet is never dropped for it, only one that's known and over the limit). All three read from a new shared `loadSignalSettings()` (`signalSettingsStore.ts`), threaded through every `scoreTicker`/`buildSignalCandidates` call site (REST, `signalsScreen`/`signalsTicker` live producers). **Blocks** (max position % of portfolio, max concentration per ticker %, min cash reserve %) are a new shared `evaluateSignalOrderLimits` (`signalOrderLimits.ts`) reusing the platform's existing valuation conventions exactly — total portfolio value is `netLiquidationValue` (same as `riskLimits.ts`'s `/exposure`), existing per-ticker exposure is `computePositionExposures` (same figure as the concentration-by-ticker card), and free cash is the same `totalCashValue` minus CSP collateral the Signals screen already uses. This order's own notional: a cash-secured put reserves `strike*100*quantity`; a covered call only adds notional for the shares it actually has to buy — `shortfall = max(0, quantity*100 - availableUncoveredShares)` times spot, the exact same shortfall math `positions.ts`'s order-build auto-fill already uses, so a fully-covered covered call contributes 0 and a partially-covered one is charged only for the difference. **Fails closed** (Marcelo's call): if account/portfolio data can't be verified, every check reports blocked rather than being silently skipped, since this gates real order confirmations, unlike the informational-only exposure page. Enforced in three places sharing this one function: (1) the Order Setup card (`SignalOrderSetupForm.tsx`) calls a new `GET /signal-settings/order-limits-check` endpoint, debounced 400ms as contract quantity changes, combined with the existing `insufficient_cash` flag into one "Cannot place now" alert and Review-Order-button disable — cosmetic only, since (2) is the real gate; (2) `POST /positions/orders/:id/confirm` hard-blocks with a 409 for any order that came from the Signals flow (`signal_snapshot` present); (3) the order's live quote stream (`/orders/:id/quote/stream`) re-evaluates every 10s (not every quote tick — each evaluation is several IBKR/DB round trips) and surfaces a `signalLimits` field alongside the existing delta `compliance`, which `OrderReviewPanel` now combines into its Confirm-button block reason. Scoped to Signals-originated orders only — Trade Alerts keeps its own separate, still-unenforced copy of the same-named settings. Verified: full backend suite passes (one pre-existing unrelated failure, `captureOptionQuoteBatch.test.ts`, confirmed via `git stash` to predate this work), both repos typecheck clean, `order-limits-check` exercised directly against the real dev DB/IBKR paper account (block/allow/validation/unknown-symbol paths), and the Order Setup card verified live in the browser (AAOI covered call: qty 500 blocks with all three reasons and disables Review Order, qty 1 clears and re-enables it).

**Max delta drift filter DISABLED 2026-09-24 (Marcelo's call, both repos).** The drift share is only ever computed for the open modal's ticker (Monte Carlo), so filtering on it made the screen and modal disagree and would have complicated the Day Signals loop (below). `scoreTicker` no longer drops candidates over `maxDeltaDriftPct`; the setting stays in `signal_settings` and on the Risk & Limits Signals tab with a visible "Not applied yet" note under the field (new optional `note` prop on `NumberField`). Test updated to assert the non-filtering.

**DAY SIGNALS — intraday refresh, DESIGN AGREED 2026-09-24 (Marcelo), build starting; nothing built yet beyond the drift-filter change above.** Plain-language: Signals were scored once a day from the 10:00 ET chain capture, so an opportunity that appeared at 13:00 was invisible. Day Signals keeps fresh bid/ask for a curated pool of contracts all session long on a permanent 10 IBKR market-data lines, feeds them into the same scoring, and notifies when a contract's grade goes up. Mockup (approved, rev 2): https://claude.ai/artifact/VnJt98eoRQmaSktf3jLgHC · guiding artifact updated (Formula 3h + Day Signals section). **Two findings that shaped it:** (1) under the existing formula, intraday quotes only reach net Edge through the friction term — the 10:00 SVI surface never learns that the market pays more for vol at 13:00 — hence **Formula 3h (approved): per-expiry parallel IV shift** = median over the expiry's fresh two-sided quotes (spread ≤ 50 %) of (mid IV − surface IV), ≥ 5 quotes else 0, applied to edge/delta/vega/friction of every strike in that expiry, computed inside `scoreTicker`; (2) a "score every contract live" first pass on 10 lines would take 17–45 min and duplicate the capture that finished 30 min earlier, so **the pool is seeded from the 10:00 snapshot scoring**. **Decisions:** seed step runs inside `run-option-chain-capture-job.ts` right after the surface fit — per ticker with a complete/partial snapshot + ok fit: `scoreTicker` at snapshot spot with the Signals-tab filters → keep `netEdge > 0` → top 10 by `edgeDollars` → distinct expiries in first-appearance order → cap 3; both day tables wiped + re-seeded in one transaction; a ticker with nothing positive has no pool. **Tables:** `day_signal_expiries` (PK ticker_id+expiry; trading_date, snapshot_id FK cascade, rank, seed_best_edge_dollars, seed_best_net_edge, seeded_at) and `day_signal_quotes` (PK ticker_id+expiry+strike+option_right; trading_date, bid, ask, last, error_code, quoted_at, cycle_number, last_grade) — quotes only, no scores (re-derived in 0.5–1.5 ms; they depend on live spot/account), no history; `ibkr_market_data_line_reservations` gains `priority boolean`. **Loop** (web dyno, `DAY_SIGNALS_LOOP_ENABLED=true` required — off in dev by default because local dev and staging share one Gateway login but not a reservation table; resumable from DB state): states re-evaluated every 30 s — `disabled` / `idle` (market closed, or no `day_signal_expiries` with today's Eastern trading_date — this keeps it idle 09:30–~10:35 and through the capture window) / `running`; holder `daySignalsLoop` = 10 lines renewed every 60 s; a **rolling window of 10** over every captured contract of the pooled expiries ordered ticker→expiry→strike on `sharedLiveConnection` (not `captureOptionQuoteBatch`, whose batch barrier waits for the slowest contract): each contract settles on a two-sided quote, an explicit IBKR "no data" (−1) tick, or an error, per-contract timeout **4 s (assumption — verify on staging)**, line cancelled and refilled at once; one **transient stock slot per ticker** (queued as the ticker's last item, released immediately) supplies the spot for the ticker's re-score; writes batched (~20 rows / 1 s); after each ticker's contracts: re-score (grade only, drift not needed since the filter is off), notify **upward transitions from any tier incl. Avoid** vs `last_grade` (first score after seed/restart = baseline), emit an in-process `dayQuotesUpdated(tickerId)`; estimated cycle ~6 min at ~2,500 contracts, ~13 msg/s. Health endpoint status + watchdog heartbeat during market hours. **Scoring merge:** `TickerSignalsInputs.dayQuotes` (valid only when `trading_date` = the snapshot date being scored), precedence pooled live > day > snapshot per contract, `SignalQuote.source` gains `"day"`, `SignalCandidate.quotedAt`; producers reload one ticker's day quotes on its event, rescore, markDirty; modal live lines = selected expiry only. **Line priority (Fit variant):** capture job holds one priority reservation of 50 for its run (batch size 60→50, batches skip their own reservation); non-priority callers see `90 − priority`; failed reservations return a structured reason; `marketDataPool` finally honours `ok:false` (today it only warns — a real gap) by shedding to fit (option contracts first, newest first, stocks last), shed entries paused via the existing reqId=−1 mechanism with a `status` to subscribers and a 30 s retry; new subscriptions under the block start paused. UI: centre slot of the top bar shows "Live data restricted" (amber, hover explanation) while active — IORIO Pulse link moves right before the theme switch; phone: the env strip. **Notifications:** Telegram (existing notifier) + in-app toast (same stack as "position closed") + persisted `signal_upgraded` notification event (Pulse Latest Events); one per contract per transition, no cooldown. **UI (mockup):** Signals screen gets a day-quote status (state · age range, no cycle counter) and a gear-hideable Quotes column (Live / age / 10:00 / —); modal header names the expiry holding the live lines, a "Intraday IV shift (expiry) +x vp from N quotes" caption, a live/day/snapshot tally, and a Quote column per row (replacing the Spread asterisk convention; asterisk kept). **Also approved (Marcelo 2026-09-24):** while the Signals screen is open, each ticker's best contract holds a pooled live line (+1 per ticker, ≤ ~20, follows "best" as it changes, deduped across viewers); the screen stream takes a `bestContractLines` parameter the client turns off while the modal is open (the modal holds its own selected-expiry lines) and back on when it closes. **Verification plan:** unit tests for every pure piece; Playwright both themes/desktop/mobile; then one full-session staging soak (IBKR pacing over 6.5 h of subscribe/cancel churn, reservations, dyno CPU/memory, real cycle time, DB growth) before it counts as done. **Build order:** migrations → line priority + pool shedding → seed step → loop → scoring merge + 3h → producers → frontend.

**DAY SIGNALS BUILT 2026-09-24 (both repos, uncommitted at time of writing; NOT yet deployed, NOT yet soak-tested on staging).** Plain-language: everything in the design entry above now exists in code and was verified locally end-to-end except the one thing that needs a live market — the loop actually refreshing quotes through IBKR — which is what the staging soak day is for. **API:** migrations `20260924000004` (day tables) and `20260924000005` (`priority` on the line reservations); `marketDataLineBudget.ts` (priority reservations, `loadMarketDataLineRestriction`, `describeMarketDataLineShortage`, pure `computeAvailableLines`); `marketDataPool.ts` rewritten to reserve-before-subscribe with shed-to-fit (`marketDataPoolCapacity.ts`, options first / newest first, resume in reverse, 15 s reconcile) — **live-verified**: with a fake 80-line priority row the pool holding 32 real lines dropped to 10 within 18 s and went back to 32 after the row was removed; `runOptionChainCapture.ts` holds one 50-line priority reservation for its run (batch size 60→50, 20 s shedding grace, batches skip their own reservation via `captureOptionQuoteBatch`'s new `lineReservation: "caller"` — which also **fixed the 9 long-failing `captureOptionQuoteBatch` tests**, which had been hitting the DB budget from a unit test); `GET /environment/details` carries `marketDataRestriction`; `daySignalsStore.ts`, `daySignalsSeed.ts` (pure `selectDaySignalExpiries`; seed step wired into `run-option-chain-capture-job.ts` as job `day_signals_seed` after the fit — **dry-run on the dev DB's 2026-09-23 snapshots: 9 scored, 4 pooled (BMNR ×2, COIN, CSCO, HOOD), 5 expiries, 94 contracts**; note the pools are small and dominated by the 2-DTE expiry because Edge $ ranks it first — worth a look once real days accumulate); `daySignalsQuoteWindow.ts` (rolling window, settles on two-sided quote / explicit −1 / error / 4 s timeout); `daySignalsLoop.ts` (state machine, `DAY_SIGNALS_LOOP_ENABLED` required "true"/"false" in env — **staging config must have it set BEFORE the next push or the web dyno will not boot**; heartbeat row `worker_health.process_name = 'day_signals_loop'`); `daySignalsNotifications.ts` + `signal_upgraded` notification type; `daySignalsLiveness.ts` checked by the 10-minute IBKR health-check job (throttled alert while the loop should be running but isn't); `GET /system-health/day-signals`; scoring: `SignalQuoteSource` gains `"day"`, `quotedAt` on quotes/candidates, `TickerSignalsInputs.dayQuotes` (loaded per snapshot date), merge precedence live > day > snapshot, **Formula 3h `computeExpiryIvShifts`** (per-expiry median, ≥ 5 quotes), `TickerSignals.ivShiftByExpiry / dayQuotesAsOf / quoteSourceCounts`; producers: `signalsScreen` takes `bestContractLines` (one pooled line per ticker's best contract, follows "best"), reloads a ticker's day quotes on the loop's in-process event, frame carries `dayQuotes: { loop, status }`; `signalsTicker` live lines = selected expiry only. **App:** Signals toolbar day-quote status (four states), gear-hideable Quotes column, mobile card slot, stream re-opened with `bestContractLines: false` while the modal is open; modal header/IV-shift metric/source tally/Quote column; top bar centre "Live data restricted" pill (Pulse link moved right; env strip on phones); upgrade toast with "Open in Signals" (BackgroundJobs) and Pulse Latest Events entry; `formatShortAge` in the shared formatters. **Verified:** API suite 491/491, both repos `tsc` clean; REST merge on real data (`GET /signals/coin` with simulated +10 % day quotes: 11 day / 33 snapshot candidates, +3.2 vp shift from 16 quotes); Playwright on the real dev servers — Signals screen and modal in dark + light at 1440 px, phone at 390 px (no horizontal scroll), toast via a manual `pg_notify`, Pulse event, topbar pill via a manual priority row. **Two real bugs found on the way, both fixed:** (1) `signalCandidates.ts`'s private normal-CDF approximation computed `erf(x)` where `erf(x/√2)` was needed, so the displayed mid IV had been 2–8 vol points low near the money since the Signals build — it now inverts through the surface library's `impliedVolatilityFromPrice` (one implementation); (2) commit `dcd33f7` put a literal `?` inside an SQL comment in `positionSelect`, which knex reads as a binding placeholder — every positions list request had been failing with "Expected 1 bindings, saw 2" since 11:26; comment reworded. Separately observed, not chased: `GET /signals` returned 500 on the dev server this session while the same functions succeed in a standalone script (the shared read connection after ~15 tsx restarts is the suspect); pools dominated by 2-DTE expiries (ranking observation above).

**Long Signals UI/UX session, 2026-09-24 (app repo).** Order-setup form (`SignalOrderSetupForm.tsx`): DTE display, headline changed to worst-case net Edge + Edge $, several rows removed/renamed (Friction kept per correction), Reference premium/Max gain switched from mid to bid for display only (order still submits at mid), removed the "Adaptive priority" explainer paragraph and the strike/expiry/quote footnote, Patient/Normal/Urgent buttons resized to match the rest of the form, Contracts card spacing tightened to match the Contract IV card, "Position size suggestion" card removed (its save-snapshot checkbox removed too — the signal snapshot is now always saved unconditionally). Signals ticker modal (`SignalsTickerModal.tsx`): Ann. Yield column turned into a 5-tier WCAG-AA green heatmap (font color only, discrete percentile tiers, not continuous), UNCOMP. column renamed to DRIFT, "Uncomp. as of" caption renamed to "Delta drift as of" and right-aligned (needed two passes — the first `ms-auto` had no room to work until the toolbar's root div also got `w-100`, since `DataTable.tsx`'s outer flex wrapper otherwise shrinks a childless-of-width toolbar to its own content size), filter button groups resized to match the Fill-priority buttons. Risk & Limits page: Account Exposure card made collapsible (state in localStorage via the existing `CollapsibleCard`), Concentration figures changed from grey to white/default text, Covered-Calls/Cash-Secured-Puts risk-metric tabs consolidated into one "Trade Alerts" tab (both strategies share one form and write identical values to both `strategy_settings` rows on Save — the covered-call-only existing-position delta override is the one field kept separate, clearly labeled "— covered calls only" in the UI), new empty "Signals" tab added (content specified 2026-09-24, see the entry above). Left sidebar reordered: Dashboard, Positions, Screener, Signals, Trade Alerts, then the rest unchanged. **Confirmed real and still live, 2026-09-25:** `covered_call` and `cash_secured_put` currently differ in the dev DB on `max_aggregate_collateral_pct` (80 vs 70), `min_cash_reserve_pct` (10 vs 15), and the existing-position delta override (0.20/0.40 vs null/null) — `RiskLimitsPage.tsx` still seeds the shared form from `covered_call` only and writes the identical payload to both strategy keys on Save, so the first Save would silently collapse those differences. Not touched since 2026-09-24. **Note:** this may become moot depending on how the "Decommission Trade Alerts" item above is scoped, since this settings tab lives under the "Trade Alerts" name on Risk & Limits.

**Trade Alerts roll credit floor FIXED 2026-09-24 (api, uncommitted).** Plain-language: the old roll scan required a roll to earn back its costs before suggesting it, but it was adding the commission in dollars per contract to prices quoted per share, so every roll had to clear about $1.35 per share more than it should — which is why so few roll alerts ever fired. Found during the Roll Signals analysis (Marcelo asked to be reminded, then said fix it). `lib/rollEconomics.ts`: new pure `rollCommissionPerShare` (2 × per-contract average ÷ 100) and `estimateRollCommissionPerShare` replacing `estimateRollCommissionComponent`; both callers (`generateRollCandidates.ts`, `refreshTradeAlert.ts`) use it, so the batch scan, the on-demand Roll click and the alert refresh agree. Tests: `rollEconomics.test.ts` (3). Effect in prod once deployed: with the account's ~$0.68 commission the floor drops from ≈ $1.36 + spreads to ≈ $0.014 + spreads per share, so roll alerts will start firing on legs that clear their real execution cost. The Signals friction term (`optionFriction.ts`) was always right and is unchanged.

**ROLL SIGNALS BUILT 2026-09-24 (both repos, uncommitted; NOT deployed; the first capture with held-leg contracts has not run yet).** Plain-language: everything in the design entry below now exists in code. Every open short option leg is scored as a contract to keep, every replacement on the same ticker and right is scored against it, and the difference (Formula 3j) is graded, listed in the Signals modal under "Your positions", shown on the Signals page as a roll badge, notified on upgrades by the Day Signals loop, and tradable through the roll order setup with the full snapshot saved on the order. **Backend:** `lib/rollSignalCandidates.ts` (pure: `scoreHeldLegs`, `buildRollCandidates`, `pickBestRoll`, flags near_expiry/assignment_risk/decayed; hard filters same right, different contract, `|delta_B| ≤ |delta_A|`, net credit > 0); `signalCandidates.ts` exports `gradeForNetEdge` (same cut points); `scoreTicker` gains `heldLegs`, `rolls`, `bestRoll`, `rollCount` (legs with a roll above Avoid) and `appendMissingContractQuotes` (a held contract missing from the snapshot still scores from a live or day quote); `selectLiveQuoteContracts` opens one live line per open leg ahead of the selected expiry; `TickerSignalsInputs.openShortLegs` from `loadOpenShortLegs`; the Signals universe is the shortlist ∪ tickers with an open short leg (`loadSignalsUniverseTickers` / `loadSignalsUniverseTicker`, replacing the shortlist-only loaders everywhere — routes, streams, seed, limits check); screen rows drop `rolls` but keep `heldLegs`/`bestRoll`/`rollCount`; capture `prepareTicker` always adds every open leg's exact contract (dependency `loadOpenShortLegContracts`; past expiries skipped); seed appends held-leg expiries present in the snapshot after the ranked ones, outside the cap; loop tracks roll grades in new table `day_signal_roll_grades` (migration `20260924000006` — `…000005` was already taken by the line-priority migration; applied locally as batch 40, wiped by the seed) and notifies upward transitions via `notifyRollSignalUpgrade` (Telegram + persisted `roll_signal_upgraded` event + toast linking `/signals?signal=SYM&roll=<legId>`); `signalOrderLimits.computeSignalOrderNotional` (pure) adds the roll rule (CC roll 0, CSP roll `max(0, newStrike − oldStrike) × 100 × qty`), `GET /signal-settings/order-limits-check` takes `rollFromStrike`; `POST /positions/:id/roll` accepts `signalSnapshot`; confirm/quote-stream enforcement now covers `roll_leg` orders with a snapshot (notional from the strike difference); roadmap fill counter counts `open_%` orders only. **Frontend:** `api/signals.ts` types; Signals page: the "Not accounted for" column is now a blank-headed badges column (`ModelCaveatBadge`, renamed from `NotAccountedForChip` — only when the ticker has its own caveat, popover trimmed to the caveats, no general list, no backfill button; the page's backfill wiring was removed as dead, the Shortlist keeps its own) + `RollBadge` ("N rolls · Grade", best roll's grade colour); phone cards get a badges row; `?roll=<legId>` beside `?signal=` opens the modal on that leg's best roll (`openModalOnRoll` sets both params in one update). Modal: "Your positions" card above the opportunities (per leg: edge of holding, friction to close, Δ, flags; rolls table Roll to / Grade / Net Edge new / Net roll Edge / $ / Net credit / Δ new / DTE / Flags, Show Avoid shared with the opportunities); selecting a roll or a candidate clears the other; the positions card's Roll button selects that leg's best roll or explains that none passes; `RollSignalOrderSetupForm.tsx` mirrors the Signal form (headline "+X vp net roll Edge (+$Y)", three-row breakdown summing in vp and $, decay meter, two-leg table, net credit mid/worst, Δ and capital at risk before/after, drift, roll + new-leg flags in words, contracts fixed to the leg, priority, net credit limit + expected at priority, realised P&L on the held leg, new leg credit, capital at risk after, annualised yield) and builds through `buildRollOrder` with `signalSnapshot {version 1, kind "roll", closeLeg, replacement, roll, ticker, order, timing}`; the stream re-opens after a fill so a rolled leg re-scores. **Tests:** API 542 (was 540 + new: `rollSignalCandidates.test.ts` 9 cases incl. an exact Formula 3j check and the equivalent `netEdge(B) − netEdge(A) − 2·friction(A)` form, ITM held leg through the surface, flags, unscored reasons, filter proof against the fixture, sort/keys, CC legs; `signalOrderLimits.test.ts`; seed held-expiry test; capture held-contract test; loop roll-upgrade test with baseline/second-cycle silence; `appendMissingContractQuotes`, `selectLiveQuoteContracts` with held legs, `toScreenRow` strips rolls); `tsc` clean both repos; oxlint no new warnings. **Verified live (dev servers, Playwright, 07:05–07:15 ET pre-market):** `GET /signals/AAOI` scores the real open $104 put (1 DTE, Δ −0.52, flags near_expiry + assignment_risk, 40 rolls all Avoid — AAOI's edge is −23 vp today); COHR/DELL legs report `no_quote` because their contracts were ITM at the 09-23 capture, which predates the capture change (self-heals on the next capture); the modal showed the positions card, Show Avoid revealed the rolls, selecting one filled the roll pane whose arithmetic matched the list (−40.2 + 22.5 − 17.9 = −35.7 vp; −920 + 78 − 62 = −$904), Review Order built a real `roll_leg` order request (`pending_confirmation`, two legs BUY with `positionLegId` / SELL, full snapshot stored, `kind: roll`, grade, strikes, priority) and Cancel set it `cancelled` with `ibkr_order_id` null — nothing reached IBKR; the limits check with `rollFromStrike=104` returned unblocked and rejected a negative strike with 400. No ticker had a roll above Avoid in today's data, so a temporary dev-only DRAM $61 put (Sep 28) was inserted from a client-side probe (roll to Oct 2 $58 at +14 vp): the page showed "1 roll · Strong" beside "model" on DRAM (desktop dark + phone card row), clicking it opened the modal with `?signal=DRAM&roll=<legId>`, the best roll pre-selected and the pane filled (+14.1 vp / +$27: −28.8 + 43.5 − 0.7); phone width 390 px had no horizontal overflow after letting the held-leg header wrap. Fixture removed afterwards. **Not verified:** a capture run with held-leg contracts (next 10:00 ET capture), the Day Signals loop's roll notifications against a live market (needs `DAY_SIGNALS_LOOP_ENABLED` on staging), a real roll fill through this path (staging paper account, same rule as the open path). **Blocked:** the guiding artifact republish with Formula 3j was refused by the auto-mode classifier (the page is shared by link); the patched page is ready at `scratchpad/signal-engine.html` for Marcelo to publish.

**ROLL SIGNALS — DESIGN AGREED 2026-09-24 (Marcelo), NOTHING BUILT YET.** Plain-language: Signals only ever suggested new positions; the old Trade Alerts system is the only thing that suggests rolling an existing short option, with mechanical triggers (50% decay, 21 DTE). Roll Signals brings rolls into the Signals framework as a *difference of two scores*: how much edge the new contract offers minus what you give up and pay to close the one you hold. Mockup (approved rev 4, badge variant B, modal section + roll order setup): https://claude.ai/artifact/Ac7Gs1LcoVQ2Ufr9AL1spP. **Formula (APPROVED 2026-09-24):** for each open short leg A and each candidate B on the same ticker and right, `netRollEdge = netEdge(B) − edge(A) − friction(A)`, i.e. `netEdge(B) − netEdge(A) − 2·friction(A)` — the held leg is bought back at the ask, so its friction is a cost again, never a credit (Marcelo's first wording, netEdge(B) − netEdge(A), would overstate every roll by twice A's friction; corrected and accepted). Dollar value = each component × its own leg's vega × 100 × contracts (so a 9-DTE held leg and a 23-DTE replacement carry different dollar weights for the same vol points); grade on the vol-point figure with the existing 0/5/10 cut points. No separate commission line: the commission already sits inside every friction term (optionFriction.ts, $0.68/contract). A is scored through the same SVI surface as any candidate whether ITM or OTM (SVI is in log-moneyness, vega is right-agnostic); the max-delta filter applies to B only. **Filters (hard):** same ticker + right; `|delta_B| ≤ |delta_A|` (never roll into a riskier contract — Marcelo; strict, no tolerance; note an OTM same-strike roll-out in time slightly raises delta and is therefore excluded, an ITM one lowers it and passes); net credit `mid_B − mid_A > 0` (credit rolls only). **Flags (not triggers):** `near_expiry` ≤ 21 DTE, `assignment_risk` |delta_A| ≥ 0.5, `decayed` mid_A ≤ 50% of entry credit — the old mechanical triggers demoted to information. **Deferred, separate work (Marcelo):** debit rolls to rescue a deep-ITM leg need their own decision rule (assignment probability, stock leg, realised P&L), so net credit stays signed on every roll candidate and phase two relaxes the filter without a schema change. **Data:** capture always includes every open short leg's exact contract regardless of side/window (+1 contract per leg); the Day loop universe includes them; the modal subscribes one live line per open leg; the inputs loader scores tickers with open short legs even if removed from the shortlist (coverage gap closed). **UI decisions:** Signals page — the "Not accounted for" column loses its title and its per-ticker list of general roadmap items (those stay in the page's roadmap panel) and becomes a blank-headed badges column with two badges: `model` (warning tint, only when the ticker has its own caveat; popover = caveat heading, text, status, ETA; no title, no general list, no backfill button) and the roll badge (solid, best roll's grade colour, "N rolls · Grade"). Roll badge click opens the Signals modal with `?signal=SYM&roll=<legId>`; a new "Your positions" section above the opportunities lists each open short leg (edge of holding, friction to close, delta) with its ranked credit rolls (Roll to, Grade, Net Edge new, Net roll Edge, $, net credit, Δ new, DTE, flags); the best roll of the badge's leg is pre-selected and fills the right pane with a roll order setup mirroring the Signal form's three cards: (1) headline "+X vp net roll Edge (+$Y)" + grade, three-row breakdown summing in vp and $, decay meter; (2) two-leg table (buy to close / sell to open: bid, ask, Δ, mid vs surface IV), net credit at mid and worst case (buy ask, sell bid), Δ before/after, capital at risk per contract before/after, drift risk, flags in words; (3) contracts fixed to the held leg (no partial rolls), fill priority, net credit limit per share and total, realised P&L locked in on the held leg (shown even when a loss), new leg credit, capital at risk after, annualised yield of the new leg; Review Order → shared review panel → `POST /positions/:id/roll` as today's two-leg combo. Labels use DTE, not dates. **Snapshot:** the roll order request stores one `signal_snapshot` covering both legs (closeLeg context, replacement candidate, roll numbers, ticker, order, timing); the roadmap's fill counter will count open and roll fills separately. **Enforcement:** limits check needs a roll variant (added notional 0 for a CC roll, strike difference × 100 × qty for a CSP roll). **Build order proposed:** R1 backend scoring + capture/loop inclusion + tests; R2 modal section + badges column (read-only); R3 loop notifications for roll grade upgrades; R4 roll order setup + snapshot + enforcement. **Out of scope but flagged:** the old roll evaluator's credit floor adds a per-contract dollar commission to per-share premiums (generateRollCandidates.ts vs optionFriction.ts's c/100) — inflates the floor by ~$1.35/share; Marcelo asked to be reminded.

**AUDIT FOLLOW-UPS BUILT 2026-09-24 (both repos, uncommitted).** Plain-language: two of the audit's open items are closed with evidence and one small feature was added. (1) **Yang-Zhang vs close-to-close — CLOSED, formula unchanged.** `tmp/volForecastBacktest.ts` gained a `TARGET` switch (yz | c2c | c2c_raw | terminal) and a `max(YZ63,C2C63)` forecaster; 10 tickers, 5 years, h = 2/5/21/63. With a close-to-close target the two trailing estimators have the same average level (bias +0.046 both at h=21) and YZ63 forecasts the next close-to-close vol better than C2C63 (QLIKE 0.270 vs 0.283 at 21 d, 0.187 vs 0.200 at 63 d); max(YZ63,C2C63) gains 0.005–0.011 (inside noise) while over-forecasting. Today's COIN/HOOD/BMNR gap was a window effect, not a systematic bias. (2) **Macro-event flag (Formula 3i) APPROVED and BUILT.** `src/lib/macroEventCalendar.ts`: curated title patterns (Fed Interest Rate Decision, Fed Press Conference, FOMC Economic Projections, (Core) Inflation Rate MoM/YoY, FOMC Minutes, Non Farm Payrolls, Unemployment Rate, Core PCE Price Index, GDP Growth Rate QoQ, (Core) PPI MoM/YoY — Minutes and PPI added at Marcelo's request) over the stored TradingView feed — a plain `importance >= 1` filter would flag nearly everything (40 such events in two months; importance 2 never occurs in the data). New `SignalFlag` `macro_event_before_expiry`, set when a release lands after the snapshot date and on or before the expiry (`expirySpansEventDate`, generalised from `expirySpansEarnings`); a flag, never an exclusion; grade, ranking, Day Signals pool untouched. `TickerSignalsInputs`/`TickerSignals` carry `macroEvents` so the UI names the releases: app letter "M", explanation + `describeSignalFlag` lists the spanned releases with dates (modal badge tooltip and the order-setup Flags rows). Verified: 23 new tests (suite 525), `tsc` clean both repos, live dev API for HOOD: 70 candidates, the 2-DTE expiry unflagged, every later expiry flagged (PCE + GDP on 09-30, jobs 10-02, CPI 10-14); Playwright: M badges render beside X in the modal, same style. Real effect: a reading aid on rows spanning one of ~5 release families per month; with the calendar as it is, most expiries beyond a week carry it. (3) Phase 2 DTE-bucket outcome report: agreed, not built until the chain archive holds ~30 days (3 today) — spec in the guiding artifact (Formula 6 note, Q4).

**CALCULATION AUDIT, 2026-09-24 (Marcelo asked for a review of every formula and its data source).** Plain-language: every number the platform computes was checked against the papers it came from and against independent calculations; the arithmetic is right everywhere, two small formulas were corrected, and three judgement calls were surfaced for Marcelo (details in the session; summary here). **Verified correct**: Yang-Zhang (formula and k constant per Yang & Zhang 2000; drift independence confirmed by simulation), Black-76 price/vega/delta/IV inversion (vega = finite difference to 1e-6), raw SVI and the Gatheral-Jacquier g(k) butterfly test (matches Lemma 2.2 exactly), forward with discrete dividends, friction, Edge $, UncompensatedShare (reproduces Israelov & Nielsen: ATM monthly at 16% vol -> 25.5% timing / 73% equity), momentum/skew/IV rank/percentile, payoff, annualized yield, P&L SQL, exposure, cycle break-even. **Data sourcing verified**: daily bars are regular-hours-only on both write paths (useRTH=1), capture spot = live last, rate = FRED TB3MS, IV history = IBKR OPTION_IMPLIED_VOLATILITY, forecast/tilt loaders read bars up to the snapshot date only, commissions ARE captured now (131/131 dev trades; the old memory note saying otherwise was corrected). Audit scripts: `tmp/auditNumericChecks.mts`, `tmp/auditRealDataEdgeByStrike.mts`, `tmp/auditEdgeByExpiry.mts`, `tmp/auditYzDiscretization.mts` (read-only). **Findings on the real 2026-09-23 surfaces (9 tickers fitted in dev)**: (1) every ticker's best opportunity was a 2-DTE contract: the 2-day ATM IV sat 8-12 vp above the 9-day IV on 8/9 tickers while the forecast is one flat YZ63 per ticker. Re-running `tmp/volForecastBacktest.ts` at h=2/5 (10 tickers, 5y): YZ63 is still the best-or-within-noise trailing forecaster at 2 days (QLIKE 0.470 vs YZ10 0.523), and IBKR's blended IV exceeds the next 2 days' realized vol 66% of the time (51% at 21 days) -> the short-dated premium is real on average but tail-heavy. **Decision (Marcelo): keep 0-7 DTE, no model change; the variance side belongs to Phase 3 sizing.** (2) The 50% min annualized yield filter removes most 30+ DTE candidates (DELL 30d 63 -> 7, 37d 71 -> 4) because yield scales with 1/sqrt(T); **Marcelo: intended, yield is the objective.** (3) Yang-Zhang reads 5-7 vp below close-to-close over the same 63 days on trending names (COIN 71 vs 78, HOOD 67 vs 72, BMNR 77 vs 82) -- the size of a grade band; an unhedged seller cares about the terminal move. **Open**: re-run the forecast back-test with a close-to-close target before deciding; formula unchanged meanwhile. (4) Strike-level IV vs a strike-independent forecast books the smile as Edge in vol points (COIN 30d calls: Edge 7 -> 15 vp as delta falls 0.29 -> 0.11); Edge $ ranking partly corrects it via vega; the grade, the Avoid boundary (also the Day Signals pool gate) and upgrade notifications remain smile-influenced until Phase 2 fits Composite Rank -- **Marcelo accepted for now.** **Two formula fixes APPROVED and BUILT 2026-09-24**: (a) Recovery path: monthly premium = candidate premium x 100 x contracts x (30 / candidate DTE) (was: the candidate's premium taken as monthly whatever its DTE) -- `computeRecoveryProjection` extracted and tested (4 tests); (b) Probability of profit now uses the FRED risk-free rate in d2, same convention as P(d2): `blackScholesPop.ts` (null rate -> null, never a silent 0%), `generateTradeAlertCandidates.ts` (both scan paths), `refreshTradeAlert.ts`, and the app's Order Review copy (`probabilityOfProfit.ts`), which gets the rate from a new transient `riskFreeRate` field on the POST /positions/orders and roll-build responses (verified live on the dev API: 0.0372 returned, test order cancelled). Effect is under 1 pp at 45 DTE. 6 new POP tests with Python reference values. Suite 491 -> 502, `tsc` clean in both repos. Minor notes not acted on: delta is the discounted forward delta e^(-rT)N(d1), ~0.5% below IBKR's spot delta; calendar-arbitrage violations are recorded but never demote a slice from 'ok'.

## Recent activity log (2026-09-08 → 2026-09-20)

**Pulse "Top Alerts" is now "Top Signals", 2026-09-28. Not yet committed/pushed.** *In plain terms:* the Pulse panel now lists each ticker's best Signals opportunity ranked by Edge $ (columns Tkr, Type, Strike / Expiry, Edge $, Yield) instead of the trade alerts ranked by yield; only positive-Edge $ tickers are shown, the count reads "shown of scored tickers", and it refreshes every minute. *Technical:* front end only (`PulsePage.tsx`/`.css`): reads `GET /signals` (snapshot pricing) every 60 s instead of the live `signalsScreen` stream, so it holds no IBKR market-data lines; the `alert_generated` refetch is gone. Verified in Playwright on desktop; Pulse already clips at phone width.

**Positions keep their strategy for life; every wheel step is its own row, 2026-09-24 (Marcelo, after the TLT/QQQ assignment showed "No strategy" next to "Assigned"; option (c) of three, chosen over relabel-only fixes). NOT yet committed/deployed — worker change, needs Marcelo's go-ahead to push.** *In plain terms:* until now the app re-labelled a position row in place as its shape changed (a covered call whose call expired became "No strategy" on the same row, and when a new call was sold against those shares the row flipped back), so the history only ever showed the last state — ten closed covered calls across AAOI, AMAT, SPCX, NBIS and BMNR are recorded as "No strategy", their premium counted under the wrong strategy, and the roll in between never appeared in Latest Transactions. From now on a position's strategy never changes: when the shares stop fitting the row that holds them, that row closes and a new one opens with the right label, so each step of a wheel (put → assigned shares → covered call → expired → shares → …) is its own row with its own open/close. *Technical:* the reconciliation pass moved out of `ibkrGatewayWorker.ts` into `src/ibkr/ibkrGatewayReconcilePositions.ts` (`reconcileHeldPositions(held, passId, deps)`, deps = Telegram + the worker's buffered-execution drain) so it can run against a database without the worker process; it is inside the worker's `computeSourceClosureHash` closure, so the release-phase deploy still detects it. Order of work is now: close legs IBKR no longer holds → sync held structures → self-heal → expiry-settlement correction. **Handoff convention:** a stock leg moved between rows is closed at its own entry price (zero P&L, the same `exit == entry` the expiry-settlement audit and the cycle engine already treat as "shares moved, not sold") and re-created on the successor at that price. New `close_reason` `stock_rolled_into_covered_call` (leftover row absorbed into a new CC); a covered call that expires with the shares retained now closes as `expired_worthless` (and sends the expiry Telegram, which it never did before because the row never closed) and hands the shares to an `unstructured/cc_expired_leftover_stock` row; the old "move the stock leg / close it with exit NULL" roll handling is a handoff too, so a rolled-away CC keeps its full leg history and closes `closed_via_app`/`closed_via_external_trade`. `determineCloseReason`: CC "assigned" now means a real closing trade on the stock leg (or an exit price that differs from entry) — a handoff no longer reads as an assignment; CSP "assigned" now reads IBKR's own held report (shares held > shares tracked by open legs), so it is right on the very pass that closes the put. **Gap guard (approved: positive evidence only):** a row only hands its shares over when every option leg is gone AND each is past expiry or has a recorded closing trade (`src/lib/optionLegRetirement.ts`, unit-tested); a call that vanished otherwise is an IBKR held-report gap and the row is left alone. **Parameter to confirm with Marcelo:** a call retired by a *trade* only counts as settled after 10 minutes (`tradeRetirementSettleGraceMs`) so a roll whose new call fills seconds later does not create a throw-away leftover row in between; expiry-retired calls settle immediately. **Feed:** `positionEvents.ts` hides the "consequence" halves — a leftover row's open when it stems from an assignment/expiry, and its close when the shares roll into a CC; a leftover row closed because the shares were sold still shows. Front end: `closeReasonLabels` gained the new reason. **Verified:** `src/ibkr/ibkrGatewayReconcilePositions.test.ts` runs the real pass against `TEST_DATABASE_URL` (same convention as `schema.test.ts`, `vi.mock` of `db/connection`) — CSP assignment, leftover→CC handoff + idempotent re-run, CC expiry with retained shares, the gap guard, the roll-in-progress guard, a full CC roll, label immutability: 7/7; whole suite 564/564; replaying the pass on the dev DB with a held report rebuilt from its own open legs changed nothing. **Not done (decisions pending):** the 10 historical mislabelled rows are left as-is (Marcelo: quantify the P&L under "No strategy" first); IBKR gaps on an *option* leg still close that leg with exit NULL as before (pre-existing; a two-pass confirmation would fix it). Watch on staging: the first real CC expiry with retained shares, and the first call sold against the TLT/QQQ leftover rows.

**CSP assignment break-even double-count fix, 2026-09-24 (Marcelo noticed TLT/QQQ leftover-stock rows showing a break-even dash).** *In plain terms:* right after a cash-secured put gets assigned, the leftover-stock row's Break-Even column showed a dash instead of a price. The break-even math was accidentally counting the assigned shares twice — once from the real trade IBKR reported, once again from its own guess based on the option's strike price — so it saw 200 shares in its books when only 100 actually existed, and refused to show a number rather than guess wrong. *Technical:* `cycleBreakEven.ts`'s `summarizeOpenCycle` independently re-derives assignment shares from strike price + expiry-date ITM math for any short option that settled with no closing trade, even when `positions.close_reason` (stamped by `ibkrGatewayWorker.ts`'s reconciliation the moment it detects the real assignment) already confirms a real stock trade recorded the same shares. Fixed by matching the assigned leg's exit against `stockTrades` (same side, same share count, within the existing 120 s grouping window) before applying the strike-price synthesis — a match found skips it, no match falls back to today's behavior unchanged (covers the case where IBKR's execution report for the assignment never arrives). Added `positionCloseReason` to `CycleOptionLeg`/`cycleBreakEvenQueries.ts`'s SQL. New `src/lib/cycleBreakEven.test.ts` (4 tests, reproduces the exact TLT numbers from dev DB); full suite (549 tests) still green; verified live against the running dev API — TLT/QQQ now return `breakEven: 80.9113` / `739.9413` instead of null. Not yet committed/pushed.
**Pulse resizable side panels, 2026-09-22.** *In plain terms:* on the Pulse screen you can now drag the line between the two left panels (Positions / Top Alerts) and between the two right panels (Trades / Latest Events) to make one taller and the other shorter. Each side remembers its own setting in your browser; double-click the line to put it back to half-and-half. Desktop only. *Technical:* `components/pulse/ResizableRail.tsx` wraps each rail; the split is the top panel's share (%) in localStorage (`pulse.leftRailTopPercent` / `pulse.rightRailTopPercent`), clamped 20-80%, drag by pointer travel (a jump-to-cursor version broke double-click), arrow keys nudge 2%, hidden and ignored at <=600px. Verified in Playwright: drag, clamp, reload persistence, plain click doesn't move it, double-click reset (also clears storage), keyboard, mobile hides the handles. Pulse's colour tokens are fixed dark (not theme-driven), so there is no light variant to check.
**Pulse live account refresh, 2026-09-21 (Marcelo asked for a periodic refresh).** *In plain terms:* the Pulse numbers for Account Value, Available/Total/Reserved cash and the Allocation bar now refresh by themselves every minute while the tab is open, and immediately when you come back to the tab. Yesterday's P&L is untouched (still last night's snapshot). *Technical:* `/dashboard/available-cash` now also returns live `netLiquidationValue` (same single IBKR account-summary call, no extra load). Pulse polls it every 60 s while visible + on refocus, keeps last good values on failure. Account Value tile, cash % and the Allocation denominator use the live value; Cash slice = 100 - other slices (the server's 'unallocated' row is computed at stream open and goes stale). Unrealised-% base and Yesterday's P&L still use the nightly snapshot. *Timing (local, via tunnel):* `available-cash` 0.62-0.86 s, `account-value` (DB) 2 ms, one-shot `/exposure` 1.5-2.0 s, exposure stream first event 1.1-1.4 s -> 1/min is ~1.4% duty and 1 IBKR request/min against a 50 msg/s cap; not aggressive. Verified in Playwright: values changed on the 60 s poll, refocus fired an immediate fetch. Mobile Pulse clips the KPI tiles (fixed wall-display layout; not changed here).
**Prod verification + open list, 2026-09-20 (after Marcelo pushed and ran the SQL).** Release v196 (`24a4347`) live; migration batch 29 created `last_known_prices` (still empty right after deploy — fills as price screens are used; re-check after opening Positions on prod). Prod snapshot fix applied (stock/unrealized snapshot totals -44,954.85 / -3,335.84 = the dev result). Nightly `expiry_settlement_audit` ran 2026-09-19 22:00 UTC in `apply` mode: 19 corrections, realized P&L +4,385.06 (= 2,936.78 first set + 1,448.28 MU exit-price fix, as predicted). Commission backfill (110/110), AAOI/PLTR leg fixes and the HOOD-fills patch are all run on prod. **Still open:** (1) Monday market hours: greeks parity chain vs Positions; first live fill should populate `trades.commission` via the worker's `commissionReport` handler (deployed 2026-09-19, currency assumed USD — verify); watchdog's first check of the new job (Mon 22:30 UTC); (2) FROZEN fallback for the option chain (blank on a closed market); (3) Return on capital is per-handoff and not annualised (low priority); (4) the other session's connection-multiplexing work — rerun the direct IBKR frozen-price probe after it lands (price service sits above the connection layer, should be unaffected); (5) assignment-sale commissions (~USD 6, no trade rows) are not in P&L; (6) positions with several stock legs were not covered by the snapshot lag fix; (7) dividends in cycle P&L (actual IBKR cash transactions only, not the ex-dividend calendar) — not built.
**Price/greeks consistency audit + modal fix, 2026-09-19 (Marcelo: 'make the modal work like the positions table; check the whole platform').** *Trigger:* AAOI showed $104.90 on Positions but $98.06 in Ticker Detail. Root cause: the modal's pricing stream (live connection, REALTIME-only) gets no `last` on a closed market and only tick 9 `previousClose` (a session old); the header did `last ?? previousClose`. **Fixed (committed, not yet pushed):** `streamTickerDetail` now also runs `streamLivePrices` (the Positions source: frozen last, then live, last-trade only) and sends a `spot` event; the header, change %, chain strike selection and technicals use it; the modal never shows a previous close as a price; Positions' Price column now prefers the stock leg's live market value (same source as its P&L) before the option tick's underlying price. **Inventory of price paths:** (A) FROZEN->REALTIME, last-only: Positions P&L, Dashboard exposure/portfolio (`positionExposure`), Price Performance current prices, Trade Alerts current prices, modal header (now). (B) REALTIME pricing snapshot with `last ?? previousClose` fallback (stale outside hours): `fetchTickerQuoteSnapshot` (Genosuke), `generateTradeAlertCandidates`, `refreshTradeAlert`, `evaluateRecoveryPathForPosition`, `streamPositionQuote` (New Position form), and the modal's strike/technicals fallback. (C) Nightly P&L snapshot: `fetchDailyClosingPrices` (fixed 2026-09-18). (D) Option greeks: FROZEN->REALTIME model greeks (Positions, PositionCard, snapshot job) vs REALTIME-only chain quotes (`quoteOptionChain`: Ticker Detail chain, alerts, roll candidates, assignment-risk, order review) — blank outside market hours and never cross-checked against (A). **Findings:** (1) seven `pricing.last ?? previousClose` spots still read a session-old price outside market hours. (2) *Every stored nightly P&L snapshot to date is one session stale for stock legs* (SPCX 9/18 snapshot marks 154.81 = 9/17's close, not 152.71; 9/17 = 9/16's close; 9/16 = 9/15's close) — the old FROZEN handler let tick 9 override `last`; fixed in code 9/18-19 so snapshots from Monday 9/21 should be right, but history (and `strategyPeriodPnl`, and the snapshot fallback the Positions screen shows when live prices are missing) is lagged. (3) Frozen `last` availability is intermittent per symbol and decays over a closed weekend (measured: AAOI live 104.90 earlier this evening, null in 3 standalone runs and the idle pnl stream later; NBIS stable at 222.25; SPCX null) — so two screens can disagree simply because one asked earlier; there is no shared last-known-good price. (4) Option chain (REALTIME-only) is empty on a closed market while the same contracts have greeks in Positions. **BUILT 2026-09-19 (Marcelo approved; committed, deploys with the next push — its migration `20260920000001_create_last_known_prices` runs in the release phase):** `src/lib/priceService.ts` + table `last_known_prices`. Hierarchy: real last trade (live or frozen) > stored last known good (<= 14 days) > latest daily close if newer > null — never a previous close. Every real last received anywhere is recorded (throttled): `streamLivePrices`/`fetchLivePrices` (wrapped: they now also FILL stock gaps from the store, so Positions P&L, dashboard exposure, price performance, trade-alert prices and the modal header/strikes all agree), `lookupPricingSnapshot`/`streamPricingUpdates`, and the nightly snapshot job's closes. The five `last ?? previousClose` fallbacks (Genosuke quote, alert generation, alert refresh, recovery path, New Position form) now use `getBestKnownStockPrice` before previousClose. Verified on dev: registry fills AAOI/SPCX when IBKR returns nothing; pnl stream returns live-source stock values (asOf null) for all three open positions; modal spot events for AAOI = 104.90 = table. Measured on the way: IBKR's frozen last for AAOI is erratic (one snapshot call got 104.90; a direct one-shot probe got nothing in 12s while SPCX/NBIS arrived in ~1s) — it is IBKR-side (not our connection sharing: the probe used its own connection; Gateway up 7h, worker not restarted) — the store makes it invisible. **Snapshot history:** 24 of 29 single-stock-leg snapshot rows (all except early intraday/after-hours-correct ones) were marked at the PREVIOUS session's close; `tmp/fix_snapshot_price_lag.sql` (idempotent, run by Marcelo on prod) moves them to that date's close — dev result: 25 rows updated, total stock/unrealized P&L across snapshots -$354.00, 0 lagged rows remain, rerun updates 0. Still open: FROZEN fallback for the option chain (blank on a closed market) and the Monday greeks parity check.
**Dead option legs on open stock-only positions, fixed 2026-09-19 (front end):** an open position whose call expired (AAOI, NBIS — bare stock, N/S) showed 'expired' in the Positions Expiry column and listed the dead call in the Ticker Detail Position card. `positionExpiryDate` now returns null for an OPEN position with no open option leg (closed positions keep the fallback to their last expiry; also fixes the Pulse page), and `PositionCard` lists/uses only live legs for an open position (closed positions show all). The dead legs' history lives in the Wheel cycle card. Verified in Playwright: AAOI/NBIS Expiry '—', AAOI card shows only the stock leg, SPCX (call + stock) unchanged.
**Cycle card columns (2026-09-19, Marcelo's request):** the timeline gained Type (Stock/Option), Qty (shares-equivalent, always positive: 1 contract = 100), Strike (options only) and Stock price (the real fill for stock trades, else that date's daily close — the only stock price stored for option fills), plus a column show/hide gear; event labels were shortened (strike/qty/price now live in columns) and stock purchases now get a zero-P&L 'Bought shares' row. Cycle totals unchanged (SPCX 615.34 / break-even 143.84 before and after).
**Break-Even column BUILT 2026-09-19 (committed, deploys with the next push; the rest of the cycle work — Cycle tab in Ticker Detail and the fair-view strategy scoreboard — is NOT built yet):** `src/lib/cycleBreakEven.ts` + `cycleBreakEvenQueries.ts`, returned as `breakEven`/`breakEvenUnavailableReason` on `GET /positions` for open rows. Built on a **cash ledger**, not leg entry prices (those are IBKR avgCost — see memory): stock buys/sells from `trades` fills + commission, put assignment = shares in at strike and call assignment = shares out at strike (expired short option ITM by >= $0.05 on the expiry-date daily close), option premium from legs net of closing commissions (open short at its credit). **break-even = (stock cash paid - stock cash received - net premium) / shares held.** Equals the approved 'cost - premium / shares acquired' when nothing was sold; after a partial sale the realized result of the sold shares flows into it (the consolidated per-cycle view) — **differs from the approved pro-rata wording, flagged to Marcelo for confirmation** (AAOI: 96.25 this way vs 101.66 pro-rata). Returns null + a tooltip reason instead of guessing (no expiry bar, expiry within $0.05 of strike, ledger shares != open stock legs). Verified on dev vs the IBKR statement by hand: AAOI 96.25, SPCX 143.84, NBIS 207.27; UI checked in Playwright (dark/light, desktop, 390px; green/red/Free/dash branches via a mocked response). Older 'deferred' design notes below still describe the full plan. ***(previous deferral note:)*** **Break-Even column deliberately deferred — design agreed with Marcelo, not built:** introduce a **cycle** above positions (opens with the first option sold or stock bought on a symbol, closes when flat, one open cycle per symbol; positions stay as-is and point to it). Cycle gives total net premium (CSP + all CCs, net of buybacks/commissions), stock P&L and break-even = avg stock cost − total net premium ÷ shares ("Free" if ≤ 0). **Strategy attribution:** option premium to the strategy that collected it; on CSP assignment the stock is handed to the next owner at **market value on the assignment date** — the CSP is charged (assignment-day price − strike) × shares, later stock P&L belongs to the CC; days between assignment and the first call written go to the **Unstructured** bucket. Dividends: use actual IBKR cash transactions only (not the ex-dividend calendar estimate) — first confirm the Flex cash data includes them. Backfill of existing positions: script run on dev by Claude, on prod by Marcelo. Needs a mockup + formula sign-off before code. **Update 2026-09-19:** mockup approved (Break-Even column after Price, "Cycle" tab in Ticker Detail, collapsible strategy scoreboard above the Positions table, fair-view vs premium-only columns, per-bucket Return on capital). Break-even counts the open call at credit received. Row P&L columns show the row's own bucket; cycle totals live in the Cycle tab. **Low priority / future:** improve Return on capital (annualise using days-in-bucket; today it's a simple per-bucket %). **Finding while preparing formulas:** for CSP-assigned stock, `position_legs.entry_price` = strike − put premium (verified on PLTR/INTC/BMNR/HOOD: e.g. PLTR $167.50 put, premium $2.0426, stock entry $165.4574) while the put leg also closes at exit 0 (full premium kept) — so the CSP premium is credited twice today (put's Premium P&L AND lower stock cost basis → inflated Stock P&L). Cycle formulas must use true stock cost = strike; whether to also fix existing legs is a pending decision. **Second finding (same day): `trades.commission` is NULL on all 110 prod trades** — the worker listens to `execDetails` but never to IBKR's `commissionReport`, so no commission has ever been captured, and every P&L formula (positionSelect realized/premium/stock, snapshots, roll-economics' 'trailing average commission') is gross of commissions; roll-economics' commission floor has always been $0 (<5 samples). Marcelo wants P&L net of commissions and costs everywhere (2026-09-19). Plan pending his approval: Phase A cycles/break-even/scoreboard derived at read time, true stock cost = strike; Phase B commission capture (leading option: nightly Flex Trades job matching `ibExecID`→`ibkr_exec_id`, no VPS deploy; needs a Flex Trades query configured in IBKR) then net-of-commission P&L in all surfaces; Phase C double-count source fix bundled with the next worker deploy. **Update (same day, Marcelo's decisions):** order A→B→C approved; commissions must be captured **real-time** (no Flex reliance); historical trades (before capture) stay gross and will be labelled. **Built, committed, NOT deployed:** `commissionReport` handler in `ibkrGatewayWorker.ts` (updates `trades.commission` by exec id; holds a report in memory if the trade row doesn't exist yet) — needs `npm run deploy:worker` (ask first) and a live check after the next fill (also check whether the post-reconnect execution replay backfills recent trades). **WIP, uncommitted, not wired:** `src/lib/cycleBreakEven.ts` + `cycleBreakEvenQueries.ts` (cycle derivation + break-even) — parked because leg `entry_price` is IBKR avgCost (commission-inclusive, premium-adjusted, drifting; see memory) so it double-counts; plan is fills-based cost/P&L. **Fills-vs-positions audit (dev DB = prod copy):** PLTR, BMNR, AMAT, SPCX, NBIS reconcile exactly once put-assignments (shares in at strike, no fill) and call-aways (shares out, no fill) are counted; mismatches: AAOI (its Aug-27 $107 put was assigned — daily close 106.23 on Aug 28 — but the position is labelled `closed_via_app`, so the 100 assigned shares never got a leg/fill), HOOD (Aug-24 sale of 300 sh has no trade row), MU (200 sh bought, legs closed with exit_price NULL on 8/27, no fills), INTC (assigned 100 sh, no fill for their exit). Ground truth needed: IBKR Activity Statement export (one-off, Aug 24 – today, Trades with codes + commission). **Expiry-classification audit (2026-09-19, read-only, daily close on expiry date vs strike, 47 expired short legs):** 5 are ITM but NOT recorded as assigned, and each explains a share mismatch above — MU calls $930 & $925 (8/26, close 938.40; shares called away, legs closed with exit_price NULL so the ~$6k stock gain is missing from realized P&L, moderate confidence), HOOD call $107 (9/4, close 122.11, recorded expired_worthless; ~+$200 stock gain missing), INTC call $90 (9/4, close 95.80, recorded expired_worthless), AAOI put $107 (8/28, close 106.23, recorded closed_via_app; assigned shares never tracked). Root cause: the worker labels an option that vanishes without a closing trade as worthless/closed and closes the stock leg at exit_price = entry_price (zero P&L) or NULL, instead of detecting assignment/call-away. Fix design (needs sign-off): on call-away stock leg exit_price = strike; on put assignment stock entry = strike; classify from the stock-quantity change in IBKR's positions at the expiry pass, not from absence of a trade. Then backfill the 5 legs (dry-run SELECT first; Marcelo runs the prod UPDATE). **BUILT 2026-09-19 (approved: nightly Heroku job instead of a worker fix; committed, not yet deployed/scheduled):** `src/lib/expirySettlementAudit.ts` + `scripts/run-daily-expiry-settlement-audit-job.ts` (`npm run job:expiry-settlement-audit`; prod `node dist/scripts/run-daily-expiry-settlement-audit-job.js`). For every expired short option that finished ITM by >= $0.05 on the expiry-date daily close: call -> the position's stock legs (closed within a day at NULL or exit = entry) get exit_price = strike (only if their shares exactly equal the call's), close_reason -> assigned; put -> close_reason -> assigned and the assigned stock chain's entry -> strike (closed chains only; open chains are re-synced by the worker to IBKR avgCost, so they're reported, not edited). Marginal (< $0.05), no-bar, share-mismatch and open-chain cases are reported in the job log, never changed. `EXPIRY_SETTLEMENT_MODE` (required, no default) = `dry_run` (runs the corrections in a transaction and rolls back, so its reported realized-P&L change equals apply's exactly) or `apply`. **Tested on the dev DB (prod copy):** dry-run changed nothing; apply = 17 corrections, measured realized P&L 28,636.65 -> 31,573.43 (+2,936.78 = reported); second run 0 corrections; 12-check synthetic edge-case suite (marginal, qty mismatch, no bar, OTM, NULL exit, idempotency, dry==apply) all pass. Notable: it also corrects every ordinary call-away recorded at exit = entry, e.g. AMAT 100 sh at $437.50 vs entry $476.36 (-$3,886) and HOOD 300 sh at $116 (+$809), plus removes the assigned-put premium double count from closed chains. Prod rollout: `EXPIRY_SETTLEMENT_MODE=dry_run` already set on Heroku; add a Scheduler entry (daily, 10:00 PM UTC, runs weekends too — legs close on Saturday), review the first dry-run Telegram, then switch to `apply`. Not yet in the watchdog's daily-job list. **IBKR Transaction History statement received 2026-09-19 (Aug 24–Sep 18, base currency SGD) — ground truth:** confirms every audit finding (assignment lines: MU sells 100@925 and 100@930 on 8/26, AAOI buy 100@107 and HOOD buy 100@106 and BMNR buy 100@24 on 8/28, INTC buy 100@91 on 8/31, HOOD sell 100@107 and INTC sell 100@90 on 9/4, PLTR buy 200@167.5 on 9/11, AMAT sell 100@437.5 and HOOD sell 300@116 on 9/18; AAOI: bought 100@107.65 8/24, assigned +100@107 8/28, sold 100@107.10 8/31, 100 remain). Also shows the DB `trades` table is missing some partial fills (HOOD 8/24: call buy-back is 3 contracts but the row says 1; stock sells 100+101+99 but only 100 recorded). **Commission backfill:** 110/110 prod trade ids matched to statement Buy/Sell lines (108 exact, 3 lines folded into the nearest same-instrument row), commissions converted SGD->USD per line from the implied FX (gross / (qty x mult x price)); total USD 106.74. SQL at `tmp/commission_backfill_from_ibkr_statement.sql` (gitignored, one transaction, `WHERE commission IS NULL`), to be run by Marcelo via `heroku pg:psql -f`. Assignment lines (call-away sells carry a small commission, ~USD 6 total) have no trade rows and are not in it. Unverified: the currency IBKR's live `commissionReport` uses (handler stores the raw number, assumes USD) — check on the first live fill. **Commission backfill run on prod by Marcelo 2026-09-19** (110/110 trades now have a commission, total USD 106.74, verified). **AAOI/PLTR stock-leg fixes run on prod 2026-09-19** (`tmp/fix_aaoi_pltr_stock_legs.sql`: PLTR leg -> 200 sh @167.50 exit 167.43; AAOI sold lot entry 107.65 exit 107.10, assigned lot 107.00/107.00; realized P&L 28,636.65 -> 28,382.23, matching the rolled-back dev test). **Net-of-commission P&L built 2026-09-19 (committed, deploys with the next push):** finding first — IBKR's leg `entry_price` (avgCost) already embeds the OPENING commission (entry minus fill matches the commission on 62 legs; ~$93 of the $106.74), exit prices are raw fills, so realized P&L now = gross spread minus commissions of the leg's CLOSING trades only (~$13.92 to date), never the opening ones (would double count). One shared SQL fragment, `src/lib/legRealizedPnlSql.ts`, now replaces the copy-pasted formula in `positionQueries.ts` (3x), `strategyPeriodPnl.ts` (2x); `positionEvents.ts` does the same arithmetic in JS. Verified on dev after applying the commission backfill: realized total 28,382.23 -> 28,368.31 (-13.92; premium -7.66, stock -6.26), strategy-period totals shift by the same -13.92, Positions Closed totals row and API show it, no console errors. Unrealized P&L is unchanged (future closing cost unknown); nightly snapshots left as they are; the ~USD 6 of commissions on assignment sales (no trade rows) is not included. Positions `P&L $` header tooltip explains the net basis. Approved by Marcelo.
- **Ticker Detail: "History (N)" card removed, 2026-09-20 (front end, uncommitted).** The collapsible list of a ticker's closed positions (Strategy / Structure / Opened / Closed / Realized P&L) is gone from the modal at Marce's request; the "IV History" chart is a different section and stays, and the Wheel cycle card still shows for tickers that only have closed positions (the modal's closed-positions check now exists only for that). Closed positions remain visible on the Positions screen's Closed view.
- **Price Performance redesign, 2026-09-20 (both repos, uncommitted, not deployed).** *Plain summary:* the page used to ask IBKR for a year of daily prices for every shortlisted ticker (~18) on every page load just to draw two trend columns, so it took 30-60+ s and hung under a slow Gateway, and every reload piled another copy on top.
