- **Order polling replaced with a push SSE notification stream, 2026-08-27** — (both apps): the order-status side of the toast system above originally client-polled `GET /orders/:id` every 2s, which only ever… *(full entry archived: PROGRESS-ARCHIVE.md)*
**Order-id collision bug found, fixed, deployed, and prod history repaired — 2026-08-27**: the new toast system surfaced two separate "SMCI — Open Order" toasts for what looked like one order — turned out to be two real `order_requests` rows sharing `ibkr_order_id = 5`, one a two-day-old row stuck non-terminal from a prior Gateway session, one today's real order. IBKR's `orderId` is only unique within one Gateway connection session and gets reused after every restart (the worker also restarted 5+ times today alone during testing, so ids 1-6 were each reused across 2-4 unrelated orders); `ibkrGatewayWorker.ts`'s fill/status listener matched purely on that number with no session-scoping, so a single real fill could flip every historical row sharing that id. Real position/P&L data was never at risk — `trades`/`positions` are keyed by IBKR's unique `ibkr_exec_id`/live holdings, not `order_requests.status` — but the audit-trail column itself was actively corrupting in prod through the whole testing session.

Fix, deployed to the VPS worker same day: `order_requests.ibkr_perm_id` (already in the schema since 2026-08-24, never wired up) is now captured on an order's first status callback and required to match on subsequent updates — IBKR's `permId` is globally unique forever, unlike `orderId`. A row already in a final status (`filled`/`cancelled`/`rejected`/`error`) is now excluded from ever being matched again, closing the gap where a terminal row's `ibkr_order_id` could still be hit by a future reuse. Added `reconcileStaleOrderRequests()` (`ibkrGatewayFetchOpenOrders.ts`), run at worker startup and on every reconnect: any local `submitted`/`partially_filled`/`cancel_requested` row IBKR's `reqAllOpenOrders()` no longer reports gets flagged `error` and its `ibkr_order_id` cleared before a reused id can hit it.

Prod history repair (one-off `tmp/` script, Marcelo ran it against prod `DATABASE_URL` — the harness's auto-mode classifier blocks Claude from running writes against prod directly): every corrupted row was reconciled against its actual matching trade by `(ibkr_order_id, same calendar day, same symbol)` instead of trusting its corrupted status. 11 rows had a real fill hidden behind a wrong `submitted`/`cancelled` status and were corrected to `filled` (including the SMCI order from the bug report, and one AAOI open that had been wrongly marked `cancelled` despite a real fill); 8 had no matching trade at all — including one, an AAOI CSP, that had falsely shown `filled` with zero supporting execution — and were marked `error`/unconfirmable with their source trade alert reverted to `pending`. 6 rows from today were left untouched as still genuinely in flight.

## Vision
Multi-strategy options trading system. Business user checks in daily, reviews suggested opportunities and open positions, approves/rejects/modifies trades. Trades execute (semi-automated) via IBKR once confirmed. Node.js + React + Postgres, hosted on Heroku.

## Status
Hosted on Heroku. Per CLAUDE.md (updated 2026-09-21), a push to `main` auto-deploys to the **staging** apps (`iorio-staging-*`) only; the original `iorio-reloaded-*` apps (`app.ioriore.com` / `api.ioriore.com`, future prod) have auto-deploy off and are promotion-only. **`iorio-reloaded-api` (backend) is on a Basic web dyno** (switched back 2026-08-25 after a one-day Eco experiment — Eco's 30-min idle-sleep was suspected of correlating with Scheduler one-off dynos getting SIGTERM'd mid-run; moderate confidence, not worth the ~$14/mo saved since this app runs every Scheduler job). **`iorio-reloaded-app` (frontend) is on Eco** (runs no scheduler jobs, no exposure to that risk). Heroku Scheduler's per-job dyno size is set to Basic on all entries (manual, dashboard only). IBKR paper Gateway live on a Hetzner VPS via a restricted SSH tunnel; `ibkrGatewayWorker.ts` also runs there as `iorio-worker.service` (systemd, `Restart=always`) — see "Order execution platform" below. **All 10 screens built** (see "Screen list"). **10 scheduled jobs on Heroku Scheduler** (corrected 2026-09-25 — was previously miscounted as 7/8 in several places in this file; see "Scheduled jobs" section below for the current list, verified against `runWatchdogCheck.ts`'s `DAILY_JOB_NAMES` and `package.json`'s `job:*` scripts). Telegram alerting live.

**Major architecture change, started 2026-08-24, mostly complete:** Iorio moved from a manually-entered position ledger to a real order-placing/monitoring platform where IBKR is the source of truth. Full detail in "Order execution platform" below — order placement, fills, closes, cancels, and rolls are all verified against real paper trades; a few edge paths remain untested (see that section's open items).

## Currently open / needs attention

**Prod was reading the paper account's cash flows (found 2026-10-02) — prod config fixed; account guard and rate-limit retry built, committed/pushed not yet.** Plain-language: the nightly check that separates deposits and withdrawals from trading profit asks IBKR for a "Flex report". Prod had been given staging's report and token, so it was looking at the paper account, not the live one (a real deposit would have been counted as profit, with no error), and prod and staging also shared one token, which IBKR throttled once (staging alert 2026-10-01). Done: a live-account query (`Activity Cash Last7 live`, id 1657122) and token (expires 2027-09-02, reminder due ~2027-08-02; staging's token expiry still unchecked) set on prod, release v212. New guard in `fetchFlexCashTransactions`: the report's account must match the account the worker is bound to (`worker_health.ibkr_account_ids`), exactly one statement, and an unreadable or stale worker row refuses (fail closed). Also a retry: if IBKR answers the first Flex request with "too many requests", it waits 45 s and tries again, up to 3 attempts, logging each retry (any other Flex error fails at once; the alert text is unchanged if all 3 are refused). Tests 913/913; checked end to end against the real Flex service (right account accepted, live report against the staging worker refused, stale worker refused, two simulated throttles then a real success). **Still to do:** confirm tonight's 22:30 UTC runs on both apps; the first real live deposit will confirm the exact `Deposits & Withdrawals` label on the live account.

**Hedges (long options bought outside the app) — built 2026-10-01, verified on dev, not yet pushed.** Plain-language: Marcelo bought a long TLT call directly at IBKR as a hedge. It used to show as "needs review" with a $902,000 capital figure and sat inside the Covered Calls numbers. Now any long option is its own strategy, **Hedge** (badge HDG): capital at risk is the premium paid (about $43k), and it has its own bucket in the cycle/P&L tables. Its P&L is market value minus cost, unrealised until it is sold or expires, and it lives in the symbol's one cycle like every other strategy (so the Positions Cycle P&L column includes it). Its cost never enters a per-share break-even. Decisions (Marcelo): automatic by structure, name `hedge`, own bucket within the cycle, counted as positive notional in concentration, capital = premium paid. Technical: reconciler `upsertHedgePosition` (worker file, needs `deploy:worker`), migration `20261001000001` relabels existing long-option-only unstructured positions, cycle ledger bucket `hedge`, hedge row in Dashboard/Pulse/scoreboard/blotter filter/Genosuke. Hedge cost is carried as unrealized while held; a hedge with no nightly mark yet is carried at cost. **Open:** a long call exercised at expiry is recorded as expired worthless (shares appear, loss booked); not handled until a hedge nears expiry. If puts/shares are ever opened on a symbol while a hedge is held, the cycle stays open until the hedge is gone too. No payoff chart or Roll for hedges yet.

**IBKR data outage + daily half-empty chain capture — root-caused and fixed 2026-09-28 (Marcelo approved).** Plain-language: every morning the job that reads each ticker's option list asked IBKR ~180 heavy questions in a row; IBKR throttled it after ~8 min, which froze the whole Gateway, and half the shortlist (HOOD onward) was never captured — both jobs still reported "success". On 09-28 the freeze ended in a silent Gateway reconnect whose session IBKR then refused market data (error 10197) until a Gateway restart; nobody was logged in elsewhere. Fixes: the structure job reuses strike grids younger than 7 days while spot is inside their range (09-28 rerun: 20/20 in 97 s, 12 lookups instead of ~180) and stops at the first IBKR timeout instead of piling on; the expiry list is written last so a half-done ticker never looks complete; the capture stops when its connection drops (it ran blind for 20 min holding 50 lines); jobs with failed tickers are recorded and alerted as failures; the health check restarts the Gateway once on 10197, then alerts; a red "Live prices stopped" pill appears in the top bar within a second of a refusal; the capture logs line usage every 10 s and a run total (lines in flight, average in use, hold time per contract, what it waited on). Pulse shows total IBKR data lines across all processes (was the web pool only). Latest Events shows one row per order. Open: tune the capture from the new line-usage logs (open interest looks like the slow field); the live Gateway container had `restart=always`, so a VPS reboot would have logged into the live account: now `restart: "no"` plus `profiles: ["live"]` in `/opt/ibkr/docker-compose.yml`, so it only starts via `docker compose --profile live up -d ib-gateway-live` (go-live must remove both deliberately).

**Wheel cycles double-counted assigned puts — fixed 2026-09-28 (Marcelo approved).** Plain-language: five tickers (AAOI, COHR, HOOD, QQQ, TLT) showed an inconsistent share count in their cycle, so their numbers were left out of the Positions P&L card and the Close form refused to close them. The cause was the ledger counting an assigned put's shares twice: once from IBKR's real assignment fill and once inferred from the expired put. `cycles.ts` now drops a stock buy that matches an inferred put assignment (same shares, price = strike, within 36h of the leg's exit; one fill per put). Checked against staging data: 0 flagged cycles, totals now include those five. Takes effect on staging with the next push.

**Wheel cycle card: dated marks + live "Today" rows (built and verified locally 2026-10-14; committed, not pushed).** Plain-language: the rows that value what is still open now show the date their price is from (e.g. "Oct 13" for the last close and the nightly option snapshot) instead of "Today". While live prices stream, extra "Today" rows show the move since then at live prices, and the tiles and footer include it. Formulas approved by Marcelo 2026-10-14: option row = live premium P&L − the stored mark (snapshot, else credit); share row = shares × (live price − marked close); no live data → no Today row. An option mark's stock price is the close of its snapshot's own day. API: `markDate` on mark rows, `openOptionPositions` on the cycle (`cycles.ts`); app: `liveCycleTimelineRows` (`cycleLivePnl.ts`), `CycleCard`. **Also fixed 2026-10-14 (committed, not pushed):** loading a ticker's chart during market hours saved the day's unfinished bar into `daily_price_bars`, read everywhere as that day's close and never refreshed by the chart (it counted as current); `upsertDailyBars` now stores only sessions that have closed. Rows already stored that way are overwritten by the nightly market-data job.

**Signals says why a ticker has no candidates, and order fixes — 2026-09-28 (Marcelo approved).** Plain-language: a scored ticker with no candidates used to show a bare "Unscored". Now "Filtered" means every contract failed the Signals settings (tooltip: best yield vs the minimum, and how many were over the max delta; e.g. TLT); "Unscored" with a tooltip says which expiries failed the surface fit or span earnings (e.g. NBIS). Mobile cards and the ticker modal show the same text. Order Review lists economic-calendar events one per line (new `order_requests.calendar_warning_events`). Closing an order modal no longer cancels an order whose Confirm was already sent. Signals modal: the chain's price divider shows only the price; a Day Signals quote under a minute old reads "Now".

**Decommission Trade Alerts, raised by Marcelo 2026-09-25 — not scoped/designed yet.** Retire the old Trade Alerts system now that Signals/Day Signals/Roll Signals cover new-trade and roll discovery: delete the Trade Alerts scheduled jobs, delete the Trade Alerts screen, delete the old Ticker Detail modal (superseded by the Signals Ticker Modal), update the Positions screen to incorporate the Signals Engine, and repoint every ticker hyperlink in the app at the Signals Ticker Modal instead of the old one. This is a real deletion of a shipped, currently-live system (trade_alerts table/routes, generateTradeAlertCandidates.ts, the roll-alert scan, TickerDetailModal.tsx and everywhere it's opened from) — needs a scoping pass (what exactly in Positions changes, whether trade_alerts data/history is kept or dropped, what happens to Genosuke's Trade-Alerts-related tools) and Marcelo's sign-off before any code changes, per the standing "ask before deciding" rule.

**Trade Alerts retired — backend done 2026-09-29 (Marcelo approved; pushed to staging 2026-09-29).** Plain-language: the old daily Trade Alerts scan and its screen's server side are gone — Signals, Day Signals and Roll Signals replace them. The "a position is close to being assigned" warning now comes from the live Day Signals loop instead of the once-a-day scan: it fires when a held short option's delta reaches 0.50, stays quiet until the delta drops back below 0.45, and never more than once per option per trading day (Telegram + an in-app notification that opens the roll view). The Recovery Path button on leftover-stock positions keeps working.

**Roll from the option chain + Roll Option button, 2026-09-30 (both repos, uncommitted).** Plain-language: on a position card the Roll button is now a full-size **Roll Option** button next to Close Position. After clicking it you can click any contract of the same kind (put for a put, call for a call) on the option chain to roll to it, not only the ranked list; a chain click on a listed roll just selects that list row. A roll outside the list's rules (costs money, or riskier delta) is allowed and shows a warning; a contract Signals can't score can still be ordered but has no net roll Edge. Technical: `scoreRollPair` (rollSignalCandidates.ts) is Formula 3j for one pair with the two hard filters as `warnings` (`debit`, `higher_delta`), and `buildRollCandidates` now calls it; `/signals/:symbol/contract` returns `rolls` per held leg; `RollSignalOrderSetupForm` takes `roll | unscoredReplacement`. Roll mode lives in `SignalsTickerModal` (`rollModeLegId`, cleared by Cancel roll / any new-trade pick). Same day, UI polish: option chain has a Yield column (annualised yield at the mid, approved formula, both sides); a chain pick keeps the previous order setup dimmed under a spinner while it loads; the roll banner is solid blue; the payoff chart spans break-even ±15% (widened to include the current price) with the Breakeven/Current labels staggered; every outline button follows one standard in `theme.css` (see memory note). Live pricing for the order setup: `signalsQuotes` takes a `pinned` list (max 4) that gets its own IBKR lines on top of the on-screen ones and is scored every frame like `/contract` (filters lifted, rolls against the held legs, Monte Carlo through the worker, throttled); the modal pins the selected candidate, roll (held leg + replacement) or chain pick, so every order form is scored live even when the chain is scrolled away (this also restores live scoring of listed candidates' forms, lost when the ticker stream dropped its option lines on 09-29). Plain-language: the numbers and the decay meter on an order setup now move with the market for any contract you pick. Also this session: Weak/warning badges are true amber with black text, negative P&L badges true red, chain columns mirror around the strike (calls: grade, yield, delta, bid, ask), Exit column removed from position cards. Fixed 09-30: the nightly greeks snapshot asked IBKR for REALTIME, which returns no option greeks after the close (nothing saved since 09-18); the job now asks for FROZEN (always the last close, never live); live views stay REALTIME and fall back to the saved snapshot. Also: `signalsTicker`/`signalsQuotes` hold their first frame for the live price (1.5 s grace) and the modal holds the REST scores 4 s, so the list no longer flashes grades scored at the stale 9:30 spot. Position card: payoff chart left (40%) of the legs table, Max Gain/Max Loss/Breakeven in the stats row, MV and Premium/Stock P&L removed, P&L column in the legs table (per leg type).

Reasons come from a new `onContractExcluded` hook in `buildSignalCandidates` (candidates unchanged, tested). Order-limits check now accepts any known ticker. **Revamp Screener to identify suitable stocks for the strategies, raised by Marcelo 2026-09-25 — needs exploration, not yet scoped.** Goal: scan for tickers with enough liquidity and enough usable option-chain data to fit the Signal Engine's models, and classify them accordingly — not the current IBKR-scanner-based discovery tool. Open question, needs investigation before any design: can a ticker be given a meaningful Net-Edge-style sample without the full daily-capture/backfill pipeline the shortlist gets today? Not sure this is possible with acceptable IBKR line/request cost — needs a spike before committing to an approach.

**LIVE ACCOUNT CHANGE OF PLAN, 2026-09-24 (Marcelo).** Plain-language: the live-money side of Iorio will no longer run on Juan's IBKR account. Marcelo has his own IBKR account and has created a dedicated **sub-account** on it, so Iorio's live trading is isolated from his other activity. Everything in this file that gates the live cutover on Juan's account (`U18059120`, usernames `johndom873`/`iorio-bot10`, the "Juan confirms the live connection works" condition in the Staging section) is superseded. What is needed before the live Gateway can be configured, all on the IBKR website (steps handed to Marcelo 2026-09-24): (1) a dedicated bot **username** on Marcelo's account, restricted to the Iorio sub-account with trading rights only (no funding/withdrawal), first Client Portal login completed so the permanent password is set; (2) two-factor sorted for unattended logins — Secure Login System *partial* opt-out on the bot username (2FA stays on Client Portal, off for Gateway/TWS; recommended) or IB Key with a human approving every Gateway restart; (3) API permission: "Enable ActiveX and Socket Clients" ticked in Global Configuration (IBC sets it, but the disclaimer dialog once silently prevented the save); (4) **market-data subscriptions on the bot username** (subscriptions are per username, not per account): NYSE (Network A/CTA), Nasdaq (Network C/UTP), OPRA (US Options Exchanges) — the exact three Juan had to add on 2026-08-31 before real-time stock data worked — plus **BATS/Cboe (Network B) coverage** for BATS-listed names such as DRAM/LITX (the 2026-09-09 gap, error 10089 with zero ticks; closed on Juan's side 2026-09-14 by an account-management change), and the account must be over IBKR's funding threshold for real-time entitlements or the subscriptions stay inert; (5) the new sub-account's `U…` id, which becomes `IBKR_EXPECTED_ACCOUNT_ID` for the live environment (the worker refuses to trade against any other id). Credentials go only into `/opt/ibkr/.env.live` on the VPS (`TWS_USERID`, `TWS_PASSWORD`, `TRADING_MODE=live`, `READ_ONLY_API=no`), never into Heroku config or the repo. **Status 2026-09-30:** the live Gateway (`ib-gateway-live` on the VPS) is logged in as the bot username on sub-account `U21518308`, read-only, and the API handshake is verified on host port 4001. The earlier "no response" was our own bug: compose mapped host 4001 to container port 4004, but the live image relays its API on 4003 (paper: 4004); fixed in `/opt/ibkr/docker-compose.yml`. `/opt/iorio-worker/.env` is set to live (`IBKR_TRADING_MODE=live`, `IBKR_EXPECTED_ACCOUNT_ID=U21518308`, `APP_ENVIRONMENT=production`) and the worker is running against the prod DB, connected, read-only. No 2FA opt-out exists on this account: a cold Gateway login needs a phone approval, but the 05:30 auto-restart and IBC's `RESTART` command keep the session (verified 2026-09-30, about 20s, no 2FA). Both Gateways run IBC's command server (127.0.0.1 only, via a derived template mounted in compose: `scripts/vps/enable-ibc-command-server.sh`); `gateway-control.sh recover paper|live` (forced-command keys) is the health check's restart: session restart first, cold restart only on paper, live alerts instead. Genosuke's `resend_gateway_2fa` covers a missed approval. Weekly forced re-login (after Sun 01:00 ET) still needs a phone approval; first occurrence Sun 2026-10-04. Still to do: wipe prod trading tables (Marcelo's decision; reference data stays), set prod Heroku config (`APP_ENVIRONMENT=production`, `IBKR_TRADING_MODE=live`, `IBKR_MARKET_DATA_LINES_ENABLED`, `DAY_SIGNALS_LOOP_ENABLED`, `API_NINJAS_KEY`), then promote after market close. **Update 2026-09-30:** prod API config is set (release v202) and the live worker `.env` has `EXPIRY_SETTLEMENT_MODE=apply`. The frontend now reads its API URL at runtime (`/config.js` served by `server.js`, app `1cb5b3e`, verified on staging desktop and mobile), so the staging build can be promoted to prod unchanged. Wipe script: `tmp/wipeDatabaseKeepSeededConfig.ts` (dry run by default; tested on a copy of the dev DB). It keeps `knex_migrations*`, `strategy_settings` and `signal_settings` (migration-seeded), and `market_calendar` until Marcelo decides (nothing scheduled refills it); users, tickers, price history and all trading data are wiped, so run it after the promotion and then create new users before turning maintenance off. **Live go-live, 2026-09-30:** staging 8bb4c1e promoted to prod (API v204, app v106), prod DB wiped (only `knex_migrations*`, `strategy_settings`, `signal_settings` kept; `market_calendar` wiped and refilled by running the sync job once), users copied from staging (juan, marce, `genosuke_prod`; not pablo or claude-test), prod web dyno scaled 0 to 1, `GENOSUKE_WEBHOOK_URL` moved to `api.ioriore.com`, maintenance off. Live worker deployed by the release phase, read-only (`READ_ONLY_API` still on; flipping it is Marcelo's step). Prod health check re-enabled with its own `recover live` key (`IBKR_HEALTHCHECK_SSH_PRIVATE_KEY_BASE64`); first run healthy. Still to load: 5Y price bars, tickers, risk-free rate.

**Trading enabled on prod, 2026-10-03 (Marcelo).** Plain-language: the live Gateway can now place orders; it was read-only until today. Staging 7c0e137 / app 3bb058b is on prod (API v215, app v114). Prod and staging now carry `IBKR_EXPECTED_ACCOUNT_ID` (`U21518308` / paper `DUR854038`; the web process needs it for the order-setup commission what-if). Prod data checked: 9 tickers with price bars, risk-free rate, calendar and settings loaded. **Technical:** `READ_ONLY_API: no` on the live service in `/opt/ibkr/docker-compose.yml` (backup `docker-compose.yml.bak.*` beside it), container recreated, 2FA approved, IBC logged "Read-Only API checkbox is now set to: false", worker re-bound to `U21518308`. **Still open:** Sun 2026-10-04 weekly forced re-login needs a phone approval (about 13:00 SGT); Monday pre-open checks (live ticks on prod Signals, sub-account cash and option permissions, then one small first order) are the only real test of the order path.
- **Gateway alert + Genosuke readiness tool, built and tested 2026-10-03, not pushed.** Plain-language: if the live Gateway is waiting for a phone approval (the weekly Sunday login, or a missed push), the Telegram alert now says so and how to get a new push; and you can ask Genosuke "is Iorio ready?" to get one answer on the Gateway link, the worker, live prices and the last health check. Sunday 2026-10-04's weekly expiry produced eight bare "restart didn't recover it" alerts (`needs_manual_login`, nothing said what to do), so the alert was reworded 2026-10-05: it now says a manual login is needed and to reply when the phone is ready. **Technical:** `describeLiveGatewayManualLoginHeadline` replaces the headline of the health check's "restart didn't recover it" error for live `needs_manual_login`/`waiting_for_2fa` (it must sit before the first `): `, which runJob cuts at for Telegram); `bot.ts` now quotes the replied-to message even when it is the bot's own, so a reply to an alert carries the alert text to the model; new read tool `get_gateway_readiness` (`gatewayReadinessSummary.ts`, from `/environment/details`, `/system-health/gateway`, `/system-health/status`). `/system-health/gateway` still reports `connected: true` on a stale heartbeat (no staleness check); the summary uses heartbeat age instead. Touches the worker's import closure (a push redeploys the staging worker). Pre-open readiness message (option C) decided 2026-10-03, being built on branch `pre-open-readiness` (6:00 ET readiness + 9:35 ET open-confirmation in the ops monitor, contract-id collision fix done, tick-size fix, Genosuke reject/fill notifications for its own orders, Genosuke gate parity). **Staging first (Marcelo 2026-10-05): prod is untouched until after the launch. Staging's structure-refresh Scheduler entry moves to 09:00 UTC together with the `jobDeadlines.ts` change; the prod entry moves only when he promotes.**
- **One place for limits, one gate for every order, pre-open readiness check — built 2026-10-05 (Marcelo), tested on a clone DB + staging soak pending, NOT pushed, prod untouched.** Plain-language: the two overlapping settings screens are now one (Risk & Limits); the three limits that block an order (max position size, max exposure per ticker, min cash reserve) and the delta band now apply to EVERY order, whether it comes from Signals, the position form or Genosuke, and Genosuke shows the same warnings as the web before you tap Yes. Genosuke tells you when its own orders are placed, fill, partly fill, are cancelled or fail (never for orders placed in the web UI). At 6:00 ET the platform checks everything needed to trade (config values, worker, an IBKR what-if proving orders are accepted, account, limits, leftover orders, jobs, last session's data, live quotes) and tells Telegram; it re-checks while red, gives a GO/NO-GO at 9:20 ET and confirms live option data at 9:35 ET. **Technical:** table `trading_settings` (singleton, migration `20261005000001` copies the values that were enforced; `signal_settings`/`strategy_settings` stay unread until a later release drops them — drop is NOT done); `src/lib/orderGates.ts` runs at confirm (trading gate, `orderLimits.ts` incl. in-flight orders' notional, `deltaBandGate.ts`, `closeGate.ts`) and stores `order_requests.gate_evaluation`; `GET /positions/orders/:id/gates`; routes `/order-checks/*` replace `/signal-settings/*`; Signals filters on one delta band (min and max); Genosuke `prepareConfirmation` builds the order, reads its gates and shows warnings before the card; `orderFollowUp.ts` + `genosuke_notified_status` (migration `20261005000003`); readiness in `preOpenReadiness*.ts`, run by the ops monitor, on demand at `GET /system-health/readiness` and Genosuke's `get_gateway_readiness`. Contract-id collision fixed (unique request ids). **Tick size: no defect** — SMART options and combos accept any cent price (what-if on paper, also above $3 on non-penny classes); only sub-cent prices are rejected; combo net now rounded to cents. Close gate and delta-band gate come from the Pluto branch's code (commits d3d1c15/b55da7f), adapted: expect Pluto-merge conflicts in `positions.ts`, `closeGate.ts`, `deltaBandGate.ts`; Pluto's `f7378bd` (limits from strategy_settings for non-Signals orders) is superseded. **Before this ships to prod:** staging Scheduler structure refresh to 09:00 UTC is already set on staging; prod entry moves only at promotion, together with the `jobDeadlines.ts` change that is already in main. **Read-only detection verified 2026-10-05 on the paper Gateway** (flipped to `READ_ONLY_API: yes`, then back to `no`; worker reconnected): IBKR rejects what-if orders for options and stock with error 321 "The API interface is currently in Read-Only mode", so the readiness order-path probe detects a read-only Gateway and names it. Option permissions are proven only by the probe contract. Fractional contract quantities are now refused at build, roll and the limits check. Not built: VPS-side checks of Gateway config beyond that.
- **Readiness: "Order path" passes a margin refusal; new "Pluto cash" warning (Marcelo 2026-10-28; built and tested, committed, not pushed).** Plain-language: when the account is short of cash, IBKR refuses the test order for margin, which still proves orders can reach IBKR, so it no longer shows as a problem; the test contract is named when IBKR refuses it. A new line warns (never blocks) when Pluto's free cash is below 5% of Pluto's budget. Formula (approved): free cash = the smaller of (budget % × net liquidation − committed on Pluto's tickers) and (cash − open Puts' strikes − working orders), as Pluto's rounds measure it; skipped when Pluto has no tickers or no budget. Technical: `evaluateOrderPath` / `evaluatePlutoCash` in `src/lib/preOpenReadiness.ts`, `loadPlutoCash` in `preOpenReadinessCollectors.ts`; the account summary is read once for both checks.
- **Readiness: reporting-job failures no longer block (2026-10-05, Marcelo).** Plain language: a failed P&L snapshot, screener scan or calendar capture now shows as a warning ("look at") and the verdict stays ready; any other failed or missing job still says not ready. It showed "not ready" from the weekend because Friday's snapshot (old code, expiring options unpriced) had failed and cannot be re-run. **Technical:** `nonGatingScheduledJobs` in `preOpenReadiness.ts` (`daily_pnl_snapshot`, `daily_screener_scan`, `daily_calendar_capture`); `evaluateJobs` returns warn for those, fail for every other job including unlisted ones, and the warning is shown on every readiness run until the job next succeeds.
- **Readiness data checks follow today once the capture has run (2026-10-07, Marcelo).** Plain language: the morning "not ready" message described yesterday's data even after today's 10:00 ET capture, and it falsely reported the Day Signals pool as empty (the pool table only holds the latest day, so yesterday's pool disappears when today's is seeded). The data checks now describe today once today's `option_chain_capture` has finished (even if it failed), otherwise the last completed session. BSBR (9 option contracts, 1-3 two-sided) was removed from the staging shortlist because it failed the 75% quote gate, and so the capture job, every day. **Technical:** `dataSessionIso` dependency replaces `previousSessionIso` in `preOpenReadinessCollectors.ts` (job_runs `finished_at` on today's ET date). Not pushed.
- **Ticker setups survive a restart (2026-10-07, Marcelo).** Plain language: adding tickers while a new release deployed left them stuck on "Preparing" forever, because the queue of setups lives in the server's memory and dies with it. Now, when the server starts, it closes any setup a restart cut off (marked failed with "Interrupted by a server restart") and starts it again from scratch, in the original order. A setup that was itself such a restart is not restarted a second time (it shows Retry Full Setup instead), and a ticker removed from the shortlist is not restarted. Retry Full Setup is now also offered on a setup that has sat "running" past 30 minutes. **Technical:** `ticker_backfill_runs.resumed_from_run_id` (migration `20261007100003`); `resumeInterruptedBackfillRuns()` called from `server.ts` after listen; `backfillNeedsRetry` in `GET /shortlist` includes stale running rows. Safe only with one web dyno and preboot off.
- **Readiness messages after 6:00 ET list only changes (2026-10-10, Marcelo; pushed to staging 2026-10-10).** Plain language: the 6:00 ET message is still the full list. The 9:20 ET verdict, the 9:35 ET market-open confirmation and the re-checks while red now show any problems and warnings in full, then only what changed since the last message (a check that started or stopped failing, a new release), and count the rest ("27 checks pass, unchanged since 06:00 ET"). The 9:35 ET message always shows the three live-data lines (market data, SPY quote, option quote). A data check keeps its identity when its session date moves, so the date rolling over no longer sends an extra alert. **Technical:** `snapshotReadiness` / `describeReadinessChanges` / `readinessCheckKey` in `preOpenReadiness.ts` (status changes only, plus the Release detail; a check the last message lacked is not a change); runner stores the last sent message in `alert_state` `readiness:last-sent:<date>` (JSON statuses + release); signature keyed without the date.
- **Readiness: errors are only what stops the open (2026-10-12, Marcelo; pushed to staging 2026-10-12).** Plain language: the morning check now says NOT READY only for something that would stop trading at the open and needs fixing now (worker, order path, account, trading halt, a 0% limit, an order left in flight, live quotes, the core settings). Problems with the previous day's data, job failures, the Gateway health check and settings for side features are warnings. Prompted by prod's 2026-10-12 "Day Signals pool empty" alarm: Friday's seed had run on good data and found nothing worth trading, and today's pool is rebuilt at 10:00 ET anyway. That check is gone from the morning messages and the 10:45 digest; the seed job itself now warns when the pool is empty. New check "Today's chain structure": the 10:00 ET capture refuses a ticker without structure stored today (verified on the dev DB), so no ticker with it is an error and some missing is a warning naming them. Pluto's check: switched off is a warning (no failure, no re-checks), its failed probes are warnings while off; on but paused stays an error. **Technical:** `preOpenReadiness.ts` (`warnOnly` config expectations, `evaluateJobs` warn-only, `blockingInvariantNames` = Capture universe, `evaluateTodaysChainStructure`), `dataInvariants.ts` (pool removed), `buildSeedEmptyPoolWarning` as the seed job's `notify`, Pluto results carry `status` (`assemblePlutoReadinessResults`); app: `plutoPresentation.ts` reads `status` (older events without it count failed tests as failing), event log shows "passed, with warnings".
- **Readiness option-quote probe: no more false "OPRA not delivering" (2026-10-10, Marcelo; pushed to staging 2026-10-10).** Plain language: the 9:35 ET check that proves live option prices arrive gave up after 3 seconds (a fresh option quote with its delta takes 4–8 s) and blamed the account's option data whenever it timed out. It now waits 10 s, blames the option data only when IBKR itself refuses the quote (quoting IBKR's code and message), says "not tested" (a warning) when the probe was paused because the 10:00 ET capture holds the market-data lines, and otherwise says plainly that no quote arrived. **Technical:** `readinessQuoteWaitMs` (10 s) via a new `graceMs` argument on `waitForFirstReading`; stock and option probes run in parallel; `QuoteProbe.paused` / `ibkrError` read from `pooledLineState` / new `pooledLineError` while still subscribed; the pool now keeps each subscription's last IBKR error, including 10089/10091/10167, which it still does not log (on the REALTIME-only pool connection they mean "not entitled"). Live proof needs staging (local dev has no market-data lines).
- **IBKR entitlement codes: open, analysis in progress (2026-10-10, Marcelo).** Plain language: when the account lacks a market-data subscription for something we quote, IBKR answers with an error code and no prices; three of those codes (10089/10091/10167) are dropped without a log line, so a missing price would have no explanation. Today every shortlist contract is entitled (probe on the paper Gateway). Weekend probe: on REALTIME and FROZEN an unentitled listing (VOD on LSE, 7203 on TSEJ) gets 10090/354 and no ticks; only DELAYED / DELAYED_FROZEN, which no code path requests, gets 10167 plus delayed ticks; DRAM on 2026-09-09 got 10089 and nothing on REALTIME. Market-hours re-run due Monday 2026-10-12 (`tmp/probeMarketDataEntitlementCodes.ts`); the fix is decided after it. Matters most for the move to the new live sub-account, whose subscriptions start fresh.
- **Shortlist: per-ticker Signals switch, notes removed; Price Performance 3M and 1Y (2026-10-07, Marcelo; pushed to staging 2026-10-07).** Plain language: each Shortlist ticker has a Signals switch, off for every newly added ticker. Off means the ticker only shows on Price Performance: it is not scored on Signals, not captured in the nightly option-chain job, not listed on Pluto's Tickers tab, and Pluto cannot trade it (its Pluto switch is greyed out; turning Signals off also turns Pluto off). It still gets its daily price and IV history bars and its earnings/dividend calendar. Turning Signals on sets up the ticker's option chain in the background (same progress popup as an add); the nightly capture picks it up after that. Every ticker already on the Shortlist is switched on by the migration, so nothing changes for them. The Notes column and its stored notes are gone. Price Performance has 3M and 1Y columns (last close on or before 91 / 365 calendar days back, live like the others); a Signals-off ticker shows "—" for IV % and Avg Vol. **Technical:** migrations `20261007100001` (drop `shortlist_entries.notes`) and `20261007100002` (`signals_enabled` default false, existing active rows and any `bot_enabled` row set true, CHECK `shortlist_entries_bot_requires_signals`); `PATCH /shortlist/:id/signals-enabled` (on starts `startTickerBackfill(..., "option_chain")`, off clears `bot_enabled` and logs `ticker_disabled`); `bot-enabled` refuses with 409 while Signals is off; universes filter via `src/lib/shortlistQueries.ts` (capture, Signals, roadmap count, Pluto tickers); daily market-data job skips `captureMarketDataSnapshot` for Signals-off tickers with no open position; stale-bar readiness check keeps Signals-off tickers; the add pipeline skips the chain steps while Signals is off; Genosuke `add_shortlist_ticker` takes `signalsEnabled`, `update_shortlist_notes` removed. **To check on staging:** turning Signals on completes the strike step (it cannot locally, market-data lines are off).
- **A hedge alone no longer triggers the option-chain capture (2026-10-08, Marcelo; on staging since API v177 `93676b4`).** Plain language: the nightly capture fetches every ticker with an open position, so TLT's long-call hedge would have kept TLT in it after its put expires, which is wasted work, because nothing reads a hedge's snapshot. Positions with strategy `hedge` no longer count; a ticker with a hedge plus any other position, or with Signals on, is still captured. **Technical:** `loadCaptureUniverse` filters `strategy_key <> 'hedge'` (also covers retries, structure refresh and the capture-universe invariant). To check: the Mon 10-12 staging capture has no TLT row and no missing-snapshot alert.
- **Price chart line labels on the left (2026-10-09, Marcelo; on staging, app `3b67dc7`).** Plain language: the MA7, MA25, MA99, Support and Resistance labels now sit at the chart's left edge, so they no longer cover the most recent candles; each line's price stays on the right axis. Labels whose prices are close together are stacked so they don't overlap. **Technical:** `LeftEdgeLabelPrimitive` series primitive in the app's `TickerPriceChart.tsx` (the library has no left-title option); the price lines keep `axisLabelVisible` with no title.
- **Price Performance: live changes measured from today's session (fixed 2026-10-07, formula approved by Marcelo; superseded 2026-10-12, next entry).** Plain language: during the trading day every change column compared the live price with the close one session too far back. NOK at $10.55 showed 24hr +3.43%, which was really "vs two closes ago" ($10.20); the right figure is −3.83% vs the last close ($10.97). 48hr and 72hr were each one session off the same way, and 1W–1Y started a day early. Now a live price is measured back from the session it belongs to: from 09:30 ET on an open day that is today, so 24hr = live vs the last close, 48hr vs the close before it, 72hr two before, and 1W/1M/3M/1Y vs the last close on or before 7/30/91/365 days before today. Before the open, and once today's bar is stored, it is unchanged. The stored figures (no live price) were already right. **Technical:** `liveSessionDate()` in `marketSessionStatus.ts`; the snapshot adds `liveReferenceCloses` and `meta.liveSessionDate`; the app uses them for live prices and reloads the snapshot once the session opens if it was loaded before 09:30.
- **Price Performance: live changes measured from the Last Close at any hour; 1D/2D/3D labels (2026-10-12, formula approved by Marcelo).** Plain language: before the 09:30 open, and in the evening once the day's close was stored, 24hr compared the live price with the close before the Last Close, so it disagreed with Current vs Last Close (AAOI pre-market at $107.01 showed +1.05% instead of −2.40%). Now 1D is always Current vs Last Close, 2D and 3D the two closes before it, and 1W/1M/3M/1Y the last close on or before 7/30/91/365 days before today. The 24hr/48hr/72hr columns are now 1D/2D/3D; anyone who had hidden them sees them again once. **Technical:** live 1D/2D/3D anchored on the row's latest bar, calendar windows on today's ET date (`meta.liveReferenceDate` replaces `meta.liveSessionDate`); `liveSessionDate()` removed; fields `change1d/2d/3d`, `close1d/2d/3dAgo`; the app reloads the snapshot when the ET date rolls over.
- **"Live prices stopped" pill now clears itself, built and tested 2026-10-03, pushed to staging.** Plain-language: a brief IBKR refusal (error 10197) left the red pill up all weekend on staging even though prices were fine, because the pill only cleared on a live price and a closed market sends none. While the pill is up the app now re-checks IBKR every minute and removes it as soon as data flows; the tooltip says so. **Technical:** `marketDataPool.ts` runs `probeCompetingLiveSession` (SPY, new shared `liveDataProbeSymbol`) on the shared live connection every 60 s while `feedRefusal` is set; `flowing` clears it, `blocked`/`unknown` keep it. The probe holds one unreserved line for up to 5 s, like the health check's. Tests in `marketDataPoolFeedRefusal.test.ts`; tooltip in the app's `AppLayout.tsx`.

**No silent failures, 2026-09-30.** Plain-language: on the first prod run the FRED rate lookup timed out, so every snapshot was saved without a rate, no surface was fitted (Signals showed "No fitted surface") and nothing reached Telegram, because both jobs still counted as successes. An audit of every scheduled job found the same hole in many places; all closed in three steps, and any problem now arrives as a Telegram alert naming the cause.
- **A, safety net:** crashes before a job starts alert (`runScript`); `runJob` alerts on skipped/dead runs and no longer drops `notify` or hides DB errors; `notifyTelegram` retries and undelivered alerts are listed in the digest; an ops monitor in the web dyno (`opsMonitor.ts`, always on) alerts when a scheduled job has not started or is stuck (deadlines from `jobDeadlines.ts`, built from the prod Scheduler screenshot) and sends one digest at 10:45 ET on open days (after the 10:00 ET capture chain) with every job's last status and 11 data checks (`dataInvariants.ts`); the monitor and the health check watch each other. The old watchdog stays until the monitor has a week of clean digests.
- **B, per job:** calendar sync parses status strictly (null = not published yet); capture flags fallback spots, partial/thin snapshots, non-real-time data, empty universe; seed, market data, calendar capture, P&L, structure, expiry audit and screener report every degraded outcome as a failed run; stored data is no longer overwritten by empty results; FRED retries 3 x 10 s. The expiry audit's "skipped items never notify" rule is reversed for the nightly job.
- **C, web dyno:** Day Signals loop and Signals stream errors alert (one per source per hour); shared IBKR connections down 10 min alert once per process (read and live together, 2026-10-10; recovery waits for both and is timed from the first drop); health check catches a hung loop (no quote 15 min) and a hung worker (heartbeat over 5 min); web crash alert registered before imports; every web start posts a notice.
- Review round (4 independent reviewers, then 2 more on the fixes) found and fixed: a per-minute alert spam (alert texts must not contain changing numbers), alerts lost when the DB is down, flapping sources re-alerting, a 15 s exit delay, ETFs and still-open assigned chains failing nightly, a 36 s risk-free-rate wait on request paths, screener wiping on empty scans. The monitor's capture slot is tied to `optionChainCaptureClock.ts` by a test.
- Not covered: Genosuke, notification broadcaster, the worker's own warnings. Touches the worker's import closure, so a push to `main` also redeploys the staging worker.

**IBKR data-capture audit, 2026-09-23 (Marcelo, still his to formally sign off — do not mark it done from Claude's side).** Triggered by a real design failure (option-chain strike discovery was probing IBKR one strike at a time — ~1,370 requests/ticker — when one wildcard call per expiry returns the whole grid). Audited every IBKR API method's callers for the same class of mistake. **Rule that came out of it: the nightly job fetches and stores; everything else reads the DB and never goes to IBKR for structural data.** Result: no second instance of the original mistake found anywhere else. Two minor issues found and fixed same day: a write-only `option_chain_params.strikes` column (dropped, migration `20260923000002`), and `fetchTickerQuoteSnapshot.ts` (Genosuke's ticker-quote tool) bypassing the price-bar cache on every call (fixed to use `getCachedChartBars`).

- Dashboard top cards matched to Pulse's four KPIs, and "Portfolio" renamed "Allocation" with a segmented bar — both shipped 2026-09-21, app `633090f`.
- **Stock P&L vs. covered-call cap — investigated 2026-09-19, no change.** Total P&L is correct mark-to-market even though the Stock/Premium split looks lopsided above the strike (the short call's loss offsets the stock's uncapped gain). Marcelo chose to leave the split as-is.
- **Trade Alerts "Run Now" stuck as "already running" after a deploy** — a Heroku SIGTERM abandoned the `job_runs` row mid-flight, and the overlap guard then rejected every later run. Fixed with a SIGTERM shutdown handler + a 15-min staleness backstop. Deployed 2026-09-11 (`10363af`).
- **Ticker Detail modal fully reloading on a background alert refresh** — a `streamKey` bump meant to refresh just the option chain was resetting the whole modal (chart, technicals) too, since the chart unmounts on `chartBars` going null. Fixed to reset only the option chain on that path. Deployed 2026-09-11 (`b26fc57`).
- **IBKR connection contention under concurrent connects** — Gateway's handshake step could freeze one client out under a connect burst; fixed with a FIFO handshake queue (`ibkrHandshakeQueue.ts`). Separately, the shared read connection's fixed `clientId: 43` couldn't handshake at all (a Gateway-side quirk, not app code) — switched to 44. Deployed 2026-09-11 (`813d369`).
- **Restart-safety audit** — a web-dyno restart mid-confirm could leave an order's status ambiguous to the client, and a worker restart could lose a buffered opening fill. Fixed with an atomic confirm update and an on-(re)connect 6h execution replay from IBKR's own log. Deployed 2026-09-09 (`22382ff`).
- **DRAM live pricing gap (BATS subscription) — resolved 2026-09-14**, once real-time entitlements were sorted account-side; no code fix was needed.
- **Ticker history backfill (Trend/IV %ile blank) — found and fixed 2026-09-08, dev DB backfilled, prod backfilled 2026-09-08.** — Marcelo noticed Trend and IV Rk/%ile blank for AAOI on Trade Alerts. Both are expected-for-thin-history, not display bugs: Trend… *(full entry archived: PROGRESS-ARCHIVE.md)*
- **Latest Events widget's blank Value/P&L for retained-stock closes — found and fixed 2026-09-08.** — Marcelo noticed several dashboard rows (expired-worthless covered calls, and "unstructured" leftover-stock closes) showing "–"… *(full entry archived: PROGRESS-ARCHIVE.md)*
- **Session cookie lifetime extended 7 → 30 days, 2026-09-05** — (`src/middleware/session.ts`) — Marcelo's request. Stale "7 days" comment in `src/genosuke/apiClient.ts` corrected to match.… *(full entry archived: PROGRESS-ARCHIVE.md)*
- **Screener redesigned into a real discovery tool + Shortlist tab, shipped 2026-09-05/09.** The old "Screener" page was really just the shortlist; `ScreenerPage.tsx` now has a **Screener** tab (filters against a nightly IBKR market-scanner snapshot, `screener_scan_results`, "Add to Shortlist" per candidate) and a **Shortlist** tab (the old behavior, unchanged). Routes renamed to match (`shortlist.ts` for CRUD, `screener.ts` for the new discovery feature). New nightly `job:daily-screener-scan` (3 IBKR scan codes, one shared connection).
- **Screener empty-results + Sectors-dropdown bugs — fixed 2026-09-09.** Sectors dropdown was self-referentially deriving its options from the filtered rows; given its own `GET /screener/sectors` endpoint. Empty results: the scan job ran 3 hours after market close and IBKR's option-volume/IV scan codes only rank during regular trading hours (confirmed: no market-data-type parameter changes this — US options have no pre/post-market session at all) — rescheduled to 6:00 PM UTC.
- **`job:daily-screener-scan` producing zero candidates every day since at least 2026-09-07 — root cause found 2026-09-25, fix not yet applied.** Confirmed via staging `job_runs` + Better Stack log query ([[reference_betterstack_logtail_query_staging]]): 14+/14+ recorded runs returned 0 pooled candidates, including real market-hours runs (18:00 UTC = 14:00 ET) — not the off-hours limitation above. Root cause, isolated live via 8 filter-combination probes on the paper Gateway: `fetchScannerCandidates.ts`'s typed `ScannerSubscription.marketCapAbove` field maps to a deprecated/non-functional IBKR wire field — every scan including it returns error 165 "no items retrieved", every scan without it returns real rows. Not a paper-account/fundamentals-data limitation (ruled out live: `numRatingsAbove`, a genuinely disallowed filter, gets an explicit "not allowed" error 10360, market cap gets no such rejection). **Fixed 2026-09-25** (`fetchScannerCandidates.ts`): swapped the typed `marketCapAbove` field for the raw filter tag `marketCapAbove1e6` (units: millions, via `scannerSubscriptionFilterOptions`, discovered via a live `reqScannerParameters()` call). No change needed in `run-daily-screener-scan-job.ts`, same `marketCapAboveUsd` option. Verified live end-to-end through the real `runScannerSubscription` function: 50 real candidates (`DV`, `FSLY`, `CLVT`, `LPL`, `SOXL`, etc. — legitimate large/mid-cap names, not the OTC-shell junk seen before the fix), `tsc` clean. **Separately confirmed, real limitation not a bug**: `contractDetails.longName`/`industry`/`category`/`stockType` come back empty on every scanner row regardless of ticker quality (checked against these same 50 legitimate names) — `companyName`/`sector` in `ScannerCandidate` are effectively always null in production; IBKR's scanner response only ever carries `symbol`+`conId` for free, nothing else without a separate per-symbol call. Superseded by the full revamp below (this fix is folded into it).

- **Screener revamp SHIPPED 2026-09-27** (api `5252ceb`, app `90db4d9`, deployed to staging — v69/v34, confirmed via `heroku releases`). Replaces `screener_scan_results` (daily-purged, 3 scan codes) with `screener_universe`: an accumulating table, never purged — every symbol already discovered gets re-enriched nightly regardless of whether it matches again. Query phase: 6 scan codes spanning IV-richness (`HIGH_OPT_IMP_VOLAT`, `HIGH_OPT_IMP_VOLAT_OVER_HIST`, `TOP_OPT_IMP_VOLAT_GAIN`) and option-liquidity (`HOT_BY_OPT_VOLUME`, `OPT_VOLUME_MOST_ACTIVE`, `OPT_OPEN_INTEREST_MOST_ACTIVE`) signals — `HIGH_OPT_VOLUME_PUT_CALL_RATIO` deliberately excluded (skew/sentiment, not richness/liquidity) — plus the `marketCapAbove1e6` fix above, floor raised to $10B (research-backed: $1B admits thin/speculative names with poor option liquidity despite clearing the floor; sources in chat history). `best_rank`/`matched_scan_codes` reflect only the latest refresh (999/empty sentinel when unmatched that night, sorts to the bottom) — approved over two alternatives (sticky-until-rematch, all-time-best) as the simplest design that still lets a rank-bucket filter work across the whole accumulated universe. Per-candidate enrichment: `reqContractDetails` for name/sector/exchange (only for symbols new to the table — identity rarely changes) + the existing quote-based fields (price, avg share/option volume, call/put OI, bid-ask spread %, IV). Market cap itself can't be stored as a value (Reuters Fundamentals entitlement not purchased — confirmed unnecessary for this design, native scan-time filtering doesn't need it); IV Rank/Percentile deliberately excluded from this phase (would need the 5Y history backfill, deferred to the ranking phase). Verified live end-to-end on the paper Gateway: 225 real candidates pooled and enriched, 0 failures, real company names/sectors/IV/OI populated (`avg_option_volume` 0/225 populated, matches the known "consistently never arrives" entitlement limitation). Frontend rewritten to match: filter bar (ticker/company search, sector, min IV, best-rank bucket — replaces the old max-price/IV-ratio/volume/spread filters, several of which were built on scanner fields that were never actually populated), new columns (`id`/`ibkr_contract_id`/`primary_exchange`/`last_price` deliberately not shown, per Marcelo's call). All 572 existing tests pass, `tsc` clean both repos, verified live in the browser (light/dark, desktop/mobile, search/sector/rank filters, Add to Shortlist). **Not yet built**: the ranking phase (Signal-Engine-style per-ticker scoring behind a "Rank"/"Rank All" action, ~45-55s/ticker) — separate, later work.
- **Screener filter-bar follow-up, 2026-09-27** (api `b23005d`, app `50fa575`). Match Type filter (multi-select on `matched_scan_codes`, overlap semantics), Sector converted from single-select to multi-select (same OR semantics), new Min Open Interest filter (`LEAST(call_open_interest, put_open_interest) >= value`). Badge relabeling/recoloring (blue = IV-richness, purple = liquidity, both white text), Call/Put OI and SH VOL switched to compact-number formatting, First Seen/Matched/Refreshed switched to relative-date formatting, "+ Shortlist" button label. New shared `MultiSelectDropdown` component (app repo). Verified: 579 tests pass, `tsc` clean both repos, live in browser (light/dark, desktop/mobile, multi-sector OR filtering, min-OI exclusion).
- **Screener/Shortlist page rename + Price column, 2026-09-29 (Marcelo, not yet pushed).** `last_price` now surfaced as a "Price" column (right before IV %, `formatCurrency`) — reverses the 2026-09-27 entry above ("deliberately not shown"); the column was already being stored via `screener_universe.last_price`, just dropped in the route's response mapping. Outer page renamed Screener → **Shortlist** (`ScreenerPage.tsx` → `ShortlistPage.tsx`, route `/screener` → `/shortlist`, sidebar icon `IconSearch` → `IconBookmark`), Shortlist tab now listed/active first; the inner "Screener" tab (discovery/filters) is unchanged and keeps its name. Verified live in browser (light/dark, both tabs, temp DB row inserted/removed to confirm Price renders since local dev's `screener_universe` is currently empty), `tsc` clean both repos.
- **Screener: ticker renames + failure reasons, 2026-10-08 (committed, not pushed).** Plain-language: when a stock changes its ticker (PSKY became SKYD on 2026-10-06), the nightly screener now renames the stored row instead of keeping a dead copy that fails, and alerts, every night; failed symbols in the alert now say why. A symbol new tonight with the IBKR contract id of a stored, unmatched row is renamed in place (keeps `first_seen_at`, refreshes name/exchange). Enrichment ends at once on IBKR error 200 and carries IBKR's message into the alert. Staging's stale `PSKY` row is gone; prod unchecked.
- **Screener: delisted symbols removed, 2026-10-08 (committed, not pushed).** Plain-language: when a stock in the screener list stops trading (WBD was bought out for cash on 2026-10-06), the nightly screener now removes it instead of failing and alerting on it every night. Rule: a stored symbol that did not match tonight and that IBKR rejects with error 200 is looked up by its contract id (`lookupListingByContractId`); same symbol on IBKR's `VALUE` exchange = delisted, row deleted, listed in the job's `delistedSymbols`, no alert. Under a different symbol = renamed, the alert says "now trades as X". Anything else stays an ordinary failure. WBD deleted by hand on staging and prod.

- **Screener filter bar redesign — clipped input text fixed, 2026-09-10.** — Marcelo flagged the Screener filter bar looking broken from a screenshot. Root cause: `ScreenerTab.tsx`'s 6 numeric filter inputs… *(full entry archived: PROGRESS-ARCHIVE.md)*
- **Juan's 2026-09-02 feedback doc — 6 of 7 items built same day.** Cancel button eligibility widened to match the backend (`confirmed`/`submitted`/`partially_filled`, not just `pending_confirmation`); Adaptive-priority (Urgent/Normal/Patient) picker on `OrderReviewPanel`, since confirmed against a real live fill; "last refreshed" relative-time label on Trade Alerts; POP added to Order Review (`probabilityOfProfit.ts`, client-side Black-Scholes N(d2), only once a real spot price is available); Return/Risk ratio (`maxGain / maxLoss`) added to Order Review; an assignment-price alert (|delta| ≥ 0.50) with a one-notification-per-crossing gate. Auto-refresh cadence and sudden-price-move/fills-at-ask detection were deliberately skipped/backlogged (rate-limit risk, and the same real-time-data dependency, respectively).

- **Genosuke gained Cloudflare WAF + Better Stack log tools (ported from menaris-admin-api's "Jack"), built and API-verified 2026-09-01.** `list_waf_rules`/`fetch_logs` (read tier) and a new **`infra-write` tier** (`add_waf_rule`/`remove_waf_rule`/`unblock_ip`) — gated behind the same real Telegram Yes/Cancel confirm as `financial-write`. `fetch_logs` takes `sourceApp: "api"|"app"` since the two repos landed in separate Better Stack teams with separate SQL credentials. - **Dashboard "Day P&L nonzero while WTD shows $0" bug — fixed 2026-09-01.** `day_start` resolved to last Friday on a fresh Monday morning (before that day's snapshot ran), landing *before* `week_start` and leaking Friday's trades into Monday's Day figure. Fixed with a new `market_calendar` table (synced from MarketData.app) anchoring `day_start` to the last real trading day, plus flipping Day's realized/unrealized boundaries to exclusive.

(1) **Fixed.** `.flash-changed` was never actually applied to a… *(full entry archived: PROGRESS-ARCHIVE.md)*

## Order execution platform (started 2026-08-24)

**Decision**: IBKR is the source of truth for positions, not the app's own database — the app places real orders (paper Gateway) and continuously syncs IBKR's reported state back into Postgres. **Why**: a real prod bug (AAOI's naked covered call from a free-typed quantity field with no cross-leg validation) showed manual position entry could silently drift from reality; the fix was to remove manual entry entirely in favor of real order placement.

**Adaptive is single-leg only (Marcelo, 2026-09-29; not yet pushed — worker file).** Plain-language: IBKR's "fill priority" (Urgent/Normal/Patient) only works on one-part orders, so two-part orders (Covered Call entries, rolls, covered-call closes) no longer offer or send it; they are plain limit orders on the net price, filled together or not at all. IBKR documents Adaptive as unavailable for spreads, and all 19 filled combos in the book filled at exactly the net limit (single-leg Adaptive beat the limit on 8 of 28 option sells, ~$4/order on paper). Worker `buildOrder` sends the BAG without `algoStrategy`; confirm stores a priority only on single-leg orders; Order Review hides the picker on multi-leg orders. Signals order form (Marcelo approved): when the contracts need more shares than are free (a Covered Call entry), the picker is hidden and the expected net Edge / Edge $ / premium assume a fill at the mid (Patient's assumption), since the combo rests at its mid-built net limit; the signal snapshot records `adaptivePriority: null` for it.

**What's built and verified against real paper trades** (opens, fills, a full close, a cancel of a live order, rolls, and reconciliation-driven self-healing have all been confirmed against real IBKR activity):
- `order_requests` table — the web-to-worker order queue (`pending_confirmation → confirmed → submitted → filled|partially_filled|cancelled|rejected|error`).
- `src/ibkr/ibkrGatewayPersistentConnection.ts` — the app's one long-lived IBKR connection (fixed `clientId=42`, everywhere else uses per-request random ids), reconnects with backoff.
- `src/ibkrGatewayWorker.ts` (separate `worker:` process, deployed to the VPS not Heroku — see [[project_worker_runs_on_vps]]) — places orders (single-leg, or an atomic BAG combo for multi-leg), tracks fills, and runs `reconcilePositionsFromIbkr()` every 60s + on connect, with an in-flight mutex so passes never overlap. Pairing heuristic: long stock + short call same symbol → `covered_call`; any short put → `cash_secured_put`, handled unconditionally and independently of a covered call on the same symbol (fixed 2026-08-27 — a real AAOI position had its put stranded as `unstructured` forever after its sibling call/stock rolled off into a separate covered_call position, since the put was only ever considered when no stock/calls existed at all); anything else → `unstructured` ("Needs Review" badge, never hidden). Each distinct short-call conId now splits into its own position + proportional stock slice (fixed 2026-08-25 after MU showed 2 calls wrongly merged into one position).
- **2026-08-27: `ibkrGateway*` naming convention adopted.** Every file whose code ships as part of the VPS-deployed worker (`ibkrGatewayWorker.ts` and its `src/ibkr/ibkrGateway*.ts` imports) is prefixed `ibkrGateway`, so it's obvious at a glance which edits need a manual `npm run deploy:worker`, not just a `git push`.
- **2026-08-27: worker silently disconnected for 8+ hours, root-caused and fixed.** `ibkrGatewayWorker.ts`'s persistent IBKR connection dropped at 12:50 UTC; its reconnect loop logged one failed attempt then went silent forever, even though `systemctl status` still showed the process "active (running)". Root cause: `ibkrGatewayTunnel.ts`'s `openIbkrTunnel()` awaited `sshClient.connect()` with no timeout — a stalled SSH attempt left that promise permanently pending, freezing the reconnect loop with no further logs (distinct from the already-fixed unhandled-pipe-error crash noted below). While disconnected, IBKR-side closes on 4 MU/AAOI legs never synced back to `position_legs`, surfacing as false "position reconciliation" alerts from the separate hourly health-check connection (which uses its own short-lived connection via `connectIbkr.ts` and kept working throughout). **Fixed**: `openIbkrTunnel()` now has its own 15s timeout on the SSH connect phase. Worker restarted and redeployed same day; stale legs self-healed via the normal reconciliation pass within seconds of reconnecting, confirmed via direct DB check and the next hourly health-check run logging zero discrepancies.
- `src/routes/positions.ts` — build-then-confirm pattern everywhere: `POST /orders` (preview, writes nothing) → `POST /orders/:id/confirm` (transmit gate, `pg_notify`s the worker) → `POST /orders/:id/cancel` (works pre-submission instantly; once submitted, flips to `cancel_requested` and the worker calls `ib.cancelOrder()` — confirmed against a real live order). Roll and close follow the same pattern. Close accepts `contractsToClose`, option-leg-driven (stock quantity always derived, never independently settable).
- `src/ibkr/checkPositionReconciliation.ts` — compares every open local leg against IBKR's real holdings, flags orphans/mismatches in either direction. Wired into the `ibkr_health_check` job (every 10 min). Detection only, no auto-fix. Verified against real forced drift.
- Frontend: shared `OrderReviewPanel.tsx` (review → explicit Confirm → poll to terminal status) used by New Position, Close, and Roll. Genosuke's three order tools call build+confirm sequentially, gated by Telegram's existing Yes/Cancel.

**Root cause chain for why every order was initially rejected (all fixed, 2026-08-24)**: `READ_ONLY_API=yes` was a leftover default (fixed → `no`); IBC's automated config dialog sequence was silently interrupted by an unhandled disclaimer dialog, so the Read-Only fix never actually saved until manually clicked through once via `xdotool`; IBKR error codes 399 and 2100-2169 are informational, not rejections, and were being mishandled as such. **Residual risk closed 2026-08-24**: a bind volume for the Gateway's settings dir now survives full container recreation, not just restarts — verified against a real `docker compose down/up`.

**Recurring bug class hit repeatedly across this build, now fixed everywhere found**: IBKR reports `""`/`0` (not `null`/`undefined`) for a contract field that doesn't apply to that security type (a stock's expiry/strike, an option's multiplier under some paths) — `??` doesn't catch it, `||` does. Hit and fixed for: stock leg `expiry_date`, `strike_price`, `multiplier` (this last one silently zeroed the stock leg's entire contribution to unrealized P&L until fixed); option leg `entryPrice`'s `multiplier` divisor; `sourceAlertId` from Genosuke's manual-entry path. See [[project_ibkr_empty_string_not_null]].

**Other real bugs found via real fills/closes, all fixed**: opening fills weren't recorded into `trades` at all for a brand-new position (fixed by buffering unmatched executions and draining them once the leg exists); `execution.time`'s IBKR format (`"YYYYMMDD HH:mm:ss <IANA zone>"`) isn't parseable by `new Date()` (fixed with a dedicated parser, DST-safe); a partial closing fill was marking the whole leg closed regardless of remaining IBKR-held quantity (fixed — only the reconciliation pass's zero-holding check now closes a leg); BAG combo leg ratios needed reducing to their GCD, not sent as raw quantities (IBKR rejected a 3-contract close outright until fixed); multi-batch opening fills for the same conId weren't accumulating into `position_legs.quantity` (fixed — quantity/entry_price now resync from IBKR's current truth on every pass, safe since IBKR always reports the current total, never an increment).

**Operational**: worker deploys are manual to the VPS (`npm run deploy:worker`), not automatic from a GitHub push — see [[project_worker_runs_on_vps]]. A dedicated every-10-min SSH health-check (forced-command key) verifies the worker's systemd unit is active and restarts it if not, verified against a real forced outage 2026-08-25.

**Rolling a covered call's short leg was silently double-counting the stock, found and fixed 2026-09-10.** A real AMAT and SPCX roll each left the *old* position's stock leg open forever: `runReconciliationPass`'s closing loop only closes a leg whose `ibkr_contract_id` is no longer held by IBKR, but a roll only replaces the *option* conId — the underlying shares never leave, so the stock leg's conId stays held and the leg is never flagged for closing. Meanwhile `upsertSplitCoveredCallPosition` saw the new call's never-before-seen conId, found no existing position for it, and created a brand-new position with a brand-new stock leg for the same physical shares — a permanent duplicate. Surfaced via the hourly reconciliation drift alert ("local total quantity=X but IBKR reports X/2", both off by exactly 2×). **Fixed**: `upsertSplitCoveredCallPosition` now looks for an orphaned stock leg (same conId, sibling option leg no longer IBKR-held) before creating a new position, and reassigns it instead of duplicating. The 2 already-corrupted AMAT/SPCX legs closed via a one-off script (`scripts/closeOrphanedRolledStockLegs.ts`, zero P&L — shares were never sold, just re-tagged by the roll), confirmed via direct prod query (`close_reason='closed_via_app'`, exit_price=entry_price, quantities now match IBKR exactly). Code fix committed and pushed same day, but the VPS worker itself wasn't actually redeployed until checked and caught the next session, 2026-09-10 19:57 UTC (`npm run deploy:worker`) — a GitHub push alone does **not** update it ([[project_worker_runs_on_vps]]), so it had been running the old buggy code the whole time despite PROGRESS.md briefly claiming otherwise. Confirmed via a prod-wide query that no other symbol has this duplicate-leg pattern. First reconciliation pass after the real deploy came back clean.

**Two more real bugs found and fixed 2026-08-27**: (1) an option leg that expired (or was assigned) with no matching `trades` row — the normal case for expiration, since IBKR never generates an execution for it — got `exit_price = null` instead of `0`, silently dropping it out of `realizedPnl`'s `SUM(... WHERE exit_price IS NOT NULL)` and reporting $0 P&L for every expired short option instead of the full premium kept; fixed in `reconcilePositionsFromIbkr`, backfilled two already-affected MU legs in prod, and added a Telegram + in-app toast notification (`notifyPositionExpired`, shared `formatPositionExpiredMessage`) for future expiry/assignment closes. (2) confirming an order tied to a trade alert flipped `trade_alerts.status` to `approved` with nothing ever reverting it if the order was then cancelled/rejected without filling — the alert stayed permanently unusable, breaking "cancel and resubmit with a different quantity." Fixed: both the local-cancel path and the worker's terminal-order-status path now revert the alert back to `pending` (`revertSourceAlertToPending.ts`).

## Performance on the Dashboard (built + verified 2026-10-02, not pushed; transfers not yet verified)
Plain language: the Dashboard now shows how the account performed as a percentage, ignoring money you put in or take out: a "MTD Performance" card after Unrealised P&L, and a "Performance" card above Allocation with since-inception, CAGR (annual growth rate) and a year-by-month grid.
- **Method (approved):** time-weighted return from the nightly snapshots: each day's gain (NAV change minus deposits/withdrawals/transfers) over the previous NAV, chained over the period. Flows count as landing at the close. CAGR is always shown (a few days of history annualises to a meaningless figure until it settles). The first snapshot is the starting point, so the month tracking began shows no return. No stored figures: computed per request (`lib/performanceReturns.ts`, `GET /dashboard/performance`).
- **Exception to the table standard:** the grid has no column show/hide gear (Marcelo: nothing to hide).
- **Transfers between linked accounts (built 2026-10-02, not pushed):** they are in the Flex "Transfers" section, not Cash Transactions. The live query (renamed `Activity Cash Last30 live`, 30-day window, same query id) now carries it; the nightly reconcile reads both sections (`extractExternalCashFlows`). Each flow is filed under the first snapshot captured after it happened, not its calendar date (the 22:38 transfer on 09-23 belongs to 09-24's NAV); Flex times are read as US Eastern (moderate confidence, unconfirmed). Securities transfers are not handled (none made yet); bank deposits' "Deposits & Withdrawals" label is still unconfirmed on the live account. Prod has only 2 snapshots (2026-09-30, 10-01), so inception is 2026-09-30 and the 09-23 funding predates the series.
- **Verified:** 23 unit tests, suite 1021/1021; endpoint output matches an independent SQL recompute on 28 local rows; Playwright desktop and phone, light and dark.

## Closing a single-option position failed with IBKR error 10330 (fixed 2026-10-02, not pushed)
Plain language: closing the NBIS put on staging (10-01, three tries) was rejected because the order carried the expiry as a timestamp ("2026-10-02T00:00:00.000Z") instead of IBKR's "20261002". Covered-call closes and rolls never hit it (they are combo orders, which send no date). The same slip also made the review screen show Oct 1 instead of Oct 2 for viewers west of UTC.
- **Cause:** Close and Roll copied `position_legs.expiry_date` (a Postgres `date`, which `pg` returns as a JS `Date`) straight into the payload. The worker also sent that date next to the conId on single-leg orders.
- **Fix:** Close and Roll select `to_char(expiry_date, 'YYYYMMDD')`; single-leg orders now send the conId alone (`buildContractFromConId`); `findMalformedOptionExpiry` rejects a bad expiry when the order is built (HTTP 500) and `buildLegContract` throws on one.
- **Verified:** conId 910602043 = NBIS 2026-10-02 $230 put (reqContractDetails); paper `whatIf` order reproduced 10330 with the old shape and was accepted with conId-only; Close and Roll routes store `YYYYMMDD`; suite 930/930.

## Commissions visible before you trade (built and verified 2026-10-02, not pushed)
Plain language: commission was already inside the Edge (a flat $0.68 per contract) but nowhere in dollars. Now the order setup shows what the order will cost in commission (IBKR's worst case, with its min-max range), what is left of the premium after it, and warns when commission is more than 5% of the premium. The Signals list keeps using an estimate (no IBKR calls); IBKR is asked once when you open an order setup.
- **Signals list / Edge:** friction's commission term is the mean commission per contract over the last 50 filled option orders of the same side and size bucket (1 / 2-4 / 5+ contracts) from `trades.commission`; a bucket with fewer than 10 orders uses the flat $0.68 (all of prod today: its `trades` is empty). Cached 10 min. Applies to new candidates (sell, the setup form's default size) and held-leg buy-backs. A roll's new leg is re-priced at the held leg's quantity (`recostReplacementCommission`).
- **Order setup (Signal, Roll, chain-contract forms):** one IBKR what-if per form via the web dyno's shared read connection (no worker change); re-asked only when contracts/strike/expiry change, never on a price tick. IBKR returns a min-max range, not one figure (1 contract $0.62-$1.69; 4+4 roll combo $1.38-$10.34; Covered Call entry with 100 shares included); the max drives the net premium and the warning (Marcelo's choice), the range is shown. Falls back to the estimate labelled "est." (option legs only) if IBKR fails. Endpoint `POST /signal-settings/commission-preview`.
- **IBKR what-if facts:** needs `transmit: true` with `whatIf: true` (error 321 otherwise); sends two order states, the first with a placeholder commission of 0; a combo answers in several seconds (timeout 12 s).
- **Warning:** `signal_settings.commission_warn_share_of_premium_pct` (default 5, editable on Risk Limits). Warn only, never blocks Review Order; a net debit always warns.
- **Realized P&L / cycles:** already net of commissions (closing commissions subtracted, opening ones inside `entry_price`); no change.
- **Verified:** 1044 tests; live paper what-if through the endpoint (single leg, roll combo, Covered Call entry; no order rows created, no open orders at IBKR afterwards); Playwright unmocked on the Signal form (desktop, phone, light, dark; one request per quantity change), mocked for the estimate / failed / warning states and the Roll form.

## Close form: estimated wheel cycle P&L at your limit prices (built and verified 2026-10-09, not pushed; mockup https://claude.ai/artifact/RMcgC6eX8proacC3h7XU31, placement B)
Plain language: under the limit prices, the Close form now shows what the whole wheel cycle's P&L would be if the close fills at those prices, minus an estimate of the close's commissions, and how far that is from the live figure. Shows "—" whenever the live figure does (market closed, no quotes). IBKR's exact commission still appears on the review step.
- **Formula (approved 2026-10-09):** live cycle P&L + Σ (limit − live mark) × quantity × (100 for an option, 1 for stock) × (−1 short / +1 long) − Σ commission. Marks match the live figure: option bid/ask mid, stock last. Only the quantity being closed is re-marked. Structured and unstructured forms.
- **Commission:** option per contract from the existing estimator (side, size bucket); new stock estimator = mean commission per share over the last 50 filled stock orders of the same side, flat $0.005/share below 10 orders (dev today: buys real 0.85¢, sells flat). The close-live stream adds `commissionRates` to every state; the app computes the estimate as you type (`lib/closeCycleEstimate.ts`).
- **Verified:** commissionEstimate tests; stream payload on dev; the function on the approved example (−$34.98 incl. $12.98) and partial / long-option / missing-input cases; Playwright with a faked live stream (light, dark, 390 and 360 px). The unstructured form was not seen on screen (no such position on dev).
- **Open option:** Pluto's covered-call close cost (`heldPositionMetrics.ts`) still leaves out stock commission; it could use the new stock estimator.

## Signals screen: Analysing vs Unscored, no stuck rows (built and verified 2026-10-03, not pushed)
Plain language: on 10-02 the Signals screen showed tickers as "Unscored" for about five minutes after the 10:00 capture, and a screen opened in that window stayed that way until it was reloaded. Cause: each ticker's snapshot was published as soon as it was captured, but its surface fit only ran after the whole universe was captured; and a live screen read its data once when it opened and never again. Now each ticker is fitted about a second after it is saved, a row says "Analysing" until then, a ticker that cannot be scored says why, and an open screen picks up changes within 10 s by itself. Mockup approved: https://claude.ai/artifact/1s67QpR3wuPcb1PKrUjJUk
- **Schema:** `option_chain_snapshots.fit_completed_at` + `fit_issue` (migration 20261003000001; existing rows backfilled as analysed). Set in the same transaction as the fit rows; a skipped or errored fit also stamps it, with the reason (`no_spot_price`, `no_risk_free_rate`, `no_quotes`, `error: ...`), so nothing stays "Analysing".
- **Per-ticker fit:** `saveCapturedSnapshot` fits the ticker right after saving it (failure only warns); the fit job after the capture still sweeps and reports on everything.
- **Scoring:** new unscored reason `analysing`; `TickerSignals.unscoredDetail` carries the facts (fit status counts / issue, daily bars vs needed, split date).
