- **Expired-worthless options priced at 0 (2026-10-03, approved by Marcelo):** IBKR reports no mark for an option that expires worthless, so every expiry Friday the job skipped those positions and alerted (09-25: 7, 10-02: 8; in-the-money legs always had a mark). `src/lib/expiredOptionWorthlessPrice.ts`: an unpriced option leg past its 16:00 ET expiry gets 0 only if the expiry-day close in `daily_price_bars` is out of the money (close at the strike counts as worthless, as in the settlement audit); in the money or no bar stays unpriced, skipped and alerted. An IBKR mark is never overridden. Not yet deployed.
- **Findings:** IBKR sends `tickSnapshotEnd` for stocks but NEVER for options in any mode -> no deterministic "done" for a one-shot option price; `reqHistoricalData` returns nothing for options (error 162); REALTIME gives options no price at all, FROZEN does. Standalone scripts that use the shared read connection collide with the running dev server's clientId 44 (looks like a 10s `fetchLivePrices`).
- **Consequence of dropping close:** the one-shot `fetchLivePrices` can no longer finish early for an option with no `last` (no completion signal exists for options), so a batch containing one costs the 6s ceiling (measured 14.6s vs 10.5s incl. connection). Only unused/stock-only one-shot paths remain (backend `/exposure` + `/positions/pnl` GETs, covered-call stock auto-fill), so it's parked until the ceiling is shrunk (see TODO below).
- **Still open (needs a design decision):** (1) stream still has a fixed 3s `frozenGraceMs` and one-shot still has the 6s ceiling — replacing them with "settled" signals gets stuck on an option that never gets a `last`; (2) Positions/Exposure one-shot vs stream-only for options; (3) midpoint valuation and labeling a prior-close fallback on screen (deferred, formula sign-off). **Update (2026-09-25 staleness pass): (1) is very likely moot** — the whole one-shot/stream quote architecture this describes (including `frozenGraceMs`) was replaced by the centralized `marketDataPool.ts` on 2026-09-24 (see "IBKR market-data pools merged into one centralized pool" below). Needs Marcelo's confirmation to close outright; (2) and (3) not re-checked.
- **Dashboard / Pulse / Risk & Limits moved to the exposure stream (2026-09-19, app repo):** `useExposureStream` hook over the existing `GET /risk-limits/exposure/stream`; Dashboard's Portfolio tiles are derived from the same reading (`portfolioFromExposure`) so each page opens ONE IBKR stream, not two. Removed the frontend one-shot `fetchExposure`/`fetchPortfolio` (backend one-shot routes left in place, now unused by the app). **Superseded 2026-09-24 (app `0ed9762`): Dashboard was moved back OFF this stream to a one-shot fetch** ("Dashboard shouldn't live-update", approved 2026-09-24) — Pulse and Risk & Limits stay on `useExposureStream`, Dashboard alone does not. Tested with Playwright desktop + mobile (390px): final values tie out to hand-computed exposure (SPCX 29,725 / NBIS 22,225 / AAOI 10,490); first reading ~7s after page load. **Partial first reading fixed (Option A, approved 2026-09-19):** `streamLivePrices` passes `{ frozenPhaseComplete }` to its callback, and `streamPositionExposures` holds its first emit until every leg is priced or the frozen phase has ended (before this, unpriced legs fell back to `entry_price` and covered calls read 29,443 -> 29,725 in the first ~0.5s). Verified with `tmp/verifyExposureFirstEmit.ts`, 3 runs: first emit is already 29,724 / 32,715. Not tested: the stream-error-before-first-reading path (error message) and dark theme.
- **TODO when the market is open (Mon 9:30 ET+, decided 2026-09-19):** probe whether one FROZEN *streaming* request (snapshot=false, no phase switch) keeps ticking live during market hours. If yes, it replaces the two-phase FROZEN->REALTIME design and deletes the fixed 3s `frozenGraceMs` (and the close-only hold-back tied to it). Fallback if no: two IBKR connections per stream (frozen snapshot + REALTIME stream), which costs an extra handshake per stream. Why the 3s wait exists: `tmp/probeBackToBackFrozenAndRealtime.ts` (2026-09-19) showed the market-data type is applied when IBKR processes the request, so issuing FROZEN then REALTIME back to back on one connection turns the frozen snapshots into REALTIME (options then get no `last`, only a late close). After hours, FROZEN streaming already returns last/close/bid/ask for all legs. Then shrink the one-shot 6s ceiling to a failure-only guard (only stock-only callers remain once Dashboard/Pulse/Risk use streams). **Likely moot as of 2026-09-25 — see the note above: the two-phase FROZEN/REALTIME design this probe was for no longer exists post-`marketDataPool.ts`.**
- Probes: `tmp/probePriceModesForOpenPositions.ts`, `tmp/probeDailyJobPriceSources.ts`, `tmp/probeDailyBarUseRthZero.ts`, `tmp/verifyPriceFixes.ts`. Re-run at 9:35 ET to confirm behavior during market hours (all results so far are after-hours).

## Screen list (finalized, 10 screens + shared modals, all built)
General (cross-strategy): Dashboard, Positions, Trade Blotter, System Health, Risk & Limits, Screener, Price Performance, Calendar (added 2026-08-31).
Strategy-specific (tabs within the screen): Trade Alerts.
Standalone/chromeless (own tab, no sidebar/topbar): Iorio Pulse (added 2026-09-13) — real-time ops-room dashboard, see the entry at the top of this file.
Shared modal: Ticker Detail (live pricing/chart/option chain w/ Greeks, platform-wide — not yet showing position/strike/entry markers on the chart) — consolidated 2026-08-31 to also show the symbol's open positions (legs, payoff diagram, one card per open position; the price target/close-trigger fields were removed 2026-09-11) and closed-position history in one place; the separate Position Detail modal was folded in and no longer exists.

**Codebase audit fixes, 2026-09-24 (committed 2026-09-24, api `a6e168a`; pushed to staging — the "Second/Third round", "Option-chain capture: rolling window", "IBKR line-usage audit fixes", "'Run Alerts Now' removed", "Per-ticker alert Refresh fixed", "Genosuke client handles streamed routes" and "End-to-end review" entries below are all the same commit too; the "(uncommitted)" tag on each was stale as of the 2026-09-25 staleness pass).** From the 21-item audit Marcelo reviewed the same day. Plain-language summary: the daily trade-alert scan had been silently killing the roll alerts it had just created, and asking IBKR for more live quotes at once than the account is allowed, which is why 2026-09-23's scans produced nothing; both are fixed. Also: jobs use fewer database connections so two can overlap safely; a dev switch (`IBKR_MARKET_DATA_LINES_ENABLED`) stops local dev from eating the shared IBKR quote lines; the Signals order-limit checks reuse prices the screens already stream instead of asking IBKR again; the worker no longer flags an order that filled while it was down as an error; the watchdog now knows about all four option-chain jobs; every IBKR connection has a message-rate cap so the combined processes stay under IBKR's 50/s; the six slow "click and wait" endpoints (per-ticker alert refresh, alert refresh, roll candidate, recovery path, option-chain refresh, health check) now stream so Heroku's 30 s timeout cannot cut them off; the Dashboard's "Yesterday's P&L" hides itself when the previous night's snapshot is missing (the number would span two sessions) and explains its 22:30 UTC timing. Detail: `runTradeAlertGeneration.ts` expires only `alert_type='new_trade'` (was unscoped, so the roll pass's fresh rows died seconds later — 130 roll alerts expired / 0 pending in the synced DB); `generateTradeAlertCandidates.ts` quotes in sequential batches of ≤40 (`maximumQuoteBatchSize`, fetchOptionChain.ts) and the scheduled scan reserves them as a priority holder like the chain capture (live screens shed to it; top-bar text generalised); `databaseConnectionBudget.jobKnexPoolMax=2` picked by `db/connection.ts` for anything started from `scripts/` (web 8 + worker 6 + two jobs 4 = 18 of 20); `reserveMarketDataLines` refuses everything with `disabled: true` when the env switch is false (pool pauses all, Day Signals idles with a reason, one-shot quotes throw a clear message); `computePositionExposures`/`resolveSpotPrice` read `peekPooledQuote` first and snapshot only unpooled legs, and `/orders/:id/quote/stream` keeps every open leg + the underlying pooled for its lifetime so the 10 s re-check never opens snapshots; worker: on every connect execution replay → position reconciliation → `reconcileStaleOrderRequests`, which now resolves a non-open row from `reqCompletedOrders` (by permId) or its own `trades` before falling back to "error" (new `ibkrGatewayFetchCompletedOrders.ts`); `ibkrMessagesPerSecondBudget` in constants.ts (shared live 20, shared read 10, one-shot/jobs 15, worker 5) passed as `maxReqPerSec`; `lib/streamedResponse.ts` + `apiStreamedRequest` (app `api/client.ts`) carry the exact status/body a JSON route would have sent; `runJob` takes `staleRunningJobThresholdMs` (capture passes 60 min; the 15 min default would have let a second capture start mid-run — the one-running-row index itself already existed since migration 20260831000002, the audit was wrong on that); `previousOpenSessionDate` (marketSessionStatus.ts) drives the Day card guard; price-performance "behind" grace 90 → 150 min after the job moved to 22:00 UTC. Verified: `npm test` 503 passing, tsc clean both repos, streamed `/trade-alerts/:id/refresh` returns a 404 frame for a bogus id and a real 422-after-heartbeat frame outside market hours (16 s), the Trade Alerts page Refresh button completes through the stream with no console errors, `/dashboard/summary` day = −2,770.53 for 2026-09-23 with 2026-09-22 as the previous open session. Not verified live: the scheduled scan with priority lines (next 13:30 UTC run), the worker reconciliation (needs a fill while the worker is down), the health-check stream (60–90 s, restarts the Gateway on failure).

**Second round of audit fixes, 2026-09-24 (uncommitted).** Plain-language: the health check no longer restarts the Gateway on a single slow probe, and never while another job is running; it also skips the four checks an hour that would start in the same second as a daily job. The Ticker Detail modal now asks IBKR for live quotes only for the expiry tab you are looking at (12 lines instead of 48), switching when you click another tab. Detail: `scripts/check-ibkr-health.ts` skips minutes :58–:02 and :28–:32 UTC; `checkIbkrHealthJob.ts` records `details.probe` and only restarts on a probe failure when no other job_runs row is "running" (2 h lookback) AND the previous check's probe also failed; `streamTickerDetail.ts` takes `expiry` (route `?expiry=YYYYMMDD`), emits `optionChainExpiries` (tabs + strikes + the expiry it chose: requested → first with a pending new-trade alert → nearest) and quotes only that expiry plus must-include strikes elsewhere; the modal (app) runs two streams — overview/spot/chart/technicals per symbol, and spot+optionChain per (symbol, streamKey, requestedExpiry) — building tabs from `optionChainExpiries`. Pool: a failed reservation query no longer subscribes anything, and the 15 s reconcile timer stops once the pool is empty. Deleted dead `streamLivePrices`/`streamLiveGreeks`/`waitForAbortOrGatewayDisconnect`; the per-stream SSE routes (`/positions/greeks/stream` etc.) are NOT dead — the app's `openMultiplexedStream` falls back to them when the multiplexer is unavailable — so they stay. Verified: tests 503 passing, tsc clean both repos; AAOI modal shows two tabs (Sep 25 / Oct 2), pool reservation 19 → 38 lines with the modal open and unchanged across a tab switch (market closed, so cells show "—" on both tabs — expected, same as before). Staging config var `IBKR_MARKET_DATA_LINES_ENABLED=true` set 2026-09-24; prod still needs it before any promotion.

**Third round of audit fixes, 2026-09-24 (uncommitted).** Plain-language: the Signals screen no longer treats yesterday's volatility surface as if it were today's (days to expiry and yields were a day off after a missed capture, and expired contracts lingered); it re-times the surface to today and shows a "surface <date>" badge when no capture ran today. Days-to-expiry everywhere now follows the US market calendar instead of the UTC clock. Detail: `rebaseSlicesToToday` (signalsLiveScoring.ts) recomputes each slice's yearsToExpiry from today's Eastern date, drops expiries already past, and scales the SVI `a`/`b` by T_today/T_fit so every strike's implied volatility stays exactly as fitted (formula choice to confirm with Marcelo: the alternative — leaving total variance untouched — inflates a 7-day slice's IV ~8% per elapsed day); `daysBetween` (fetchOptionChain.ts) now takes `from` on the Eastern calendar, fixing all six DTE call sites at once; `requestRateTracker` prunes on every push; an abandoned job run keeps `finished_at = started_at` and System Health shows "unknown" for its duration. Tests 506 passing, tsc clean both repos. Capture line-holding left as is (Marcelo 2026-09-24: capture speed wins).

**Option-chain capture: rolling window instead of fixed batches, 2026-09-24 (uncommitted, not yet run live).** Plain-language: the nightly 10:00 ET capture used to ask IBKR for 50 contracts, wait for the slowest one (or 8 s), then ask for the next 50 — a contract answered in one second held its line idle until the batch was done. It now keeps 50 lines busy continuously across all tickers: as soon as one contract has its data, the next queued contract takes that line, and the next ticker's contracts are queued while the current ticker is still being quoted. Expected: roughly lines ÷ average settle time, i.e. minutes instead of the 1,118 s measured on 2026-09-23 (to be confirmed on the next scheduled run via job_runs.details and the snapshot capture_duration_ms). Detail: `openCaptureQuoteWindow` (captureOptionQuoteBatch.ts) — shared tick parsing and settle rule (price + delta + open interest, or IBKR error), per-contract 8 s timeout, per-ticker promise resolving when that ticker's last contract settles; `runOptionChainCapture` prepares tickers sequentially and queues each on the one window (saves run in the background as each completes), re-capture pass unchanged and still sequential/time-boxed; `saveCapturedSnapshot` replaces `captureAndSave`; the old `captureOptionQuoteBatch` stays for the backfill path. Tests: 5 new window tests + run tests updated (512 passing).

**IBKR line-usage audit fixes, 2026-09-24 (uncommitted).** Plain-language: every place that asked IBKR for live quotes now (a) reuses what an open screen is already streaming, (b) reserves its lines against the shared budget for only as long as it needs them, and (c) asks for far fewer contracts. Detail (numbers refer to the audit list in chat): (1, 2, 8) new `quoteContracts.ts` — pool first, ≤40 lines reserved (priority for the scheduled scan), rolling window with per-contract settle on price+delta; `generateTradeAlertCandidates` quotes one right only and trims the ±40% strike band to strikes whose delta archived by today's 10:00 capture can land in the target band ±0.10 (`filterStrikesByArchivedDelta`; full band when there is no archive today); the roll pass quotes each short leg once (assignment-risk check hands its quote to `evaluateRollCandidate`); alert refresh quotes closing leg + replacement in one request; recovery path passes its spot into candidate generation. (3) `streamPositionQuote` and its route/frontend opener deleted; the Close Position modal's stock leg reads the pooled stock price; the assistant's `get_ticker_quote` quotes the nearest expiry only. (4) The pool now carries open/high/low/previous-close/volume for stocks and Ticker Detail's overview is built from the pooled line — `streamPricingUpdates` deleted. (5) `fetchPricesPoolFirst` / `fetchGreeksPoolFirst` (pricePool.ts / greeksPool.ts) used by `/positions/pnl`, `/positions/greeks`, the covered-call auto-fill, order limits and exposures. (7) `waitForFirstReading` (marketDataPool.ts): pooled streams resolve their first reading as soon as every contract has what the caller needs, 3 s only as the ceiling. (9) The Order Review stream no longer subscribes every open leg; the 10 s re-check uses the pool-first exposures. (11) The capture fetches every ticker's spot in one priority snapshot before the window opens and queues all re-capture tickers at once (the pass budget closes the window as a deadline). (12) New-ticker snapshot finishes 500 ms after IV arrives. (13) `fetchLivePrices`, `fetchLiveGreeks`, `lookupPricingSnapshot`, `captureMarketDataSnapshot` and the screener's `enrichCandidate` each reserve their lines (short TTL); enrichment also settles 500 ms after all fields arrive instead of always 15 s. (14) Deleted `fetchQuotesForContracts`, `quoteOptionChain`, the batch `captureOptionQuoteBatch` function and the pool-disconnect wait helper. (15) Plain EventSource streams (Ticker Detail, order-leg and contract quotes) open one tick deferred so StrictMode never doubles them. (6) Pool cancel grace built at 2 s (`unsubscribeGraceMs`, marketDataPool.ts): a contract whose last subscriber leaves keeps its line for 2 s and is reused if anyone resubscribes in that window. (10) Day Signals' 10 lines are now a priority reservation (never refused, live screens shed to it). The scheduled scan (13:30 UTC, ~5-7 min) finishes before the 14:00 capture so the two never compete; a manual "Run Alerts Now" during the capture no longer fails — `quoteContracts` takes whatever priority lines are free (down to 1) and runs its window narrower. Verified locally: tests 501 passing, tsc clean both repos, alert refresh over the new path settles in ~5 s outside market hours (was 16 s), reservations released after each call.

**"Run Alerts Now" removed, 2026-09-24 (uncommitted).** Plain-language: the full shortlist scan is scheduled-only now (13:30 UTC); the button that ran it on demand from the Trade Alerts page is gone, along with its background toast. Per-ticker Refresh (Trade Alerts page and Ticker Detail) stays. Detail: `GET /trade-alerts/run-stream` deleted; `openTradeAlertRunStream`, the `trade-alert-scan` job kind and `useJobEvents` removed from the app; empty-state text points at the scheduled run and per-ticker Refresh.

**Per-ticker alert Refresh fixed, 2026-09-24 (uncommitted).** Plain-language: refreshing a ticker's alerts when IBKR has no live quotes (outside market hours) used to delete the existing alerts and replace them with nothing; it now keeps them and says why. It also reuses the app's already-open IBKR connection instead of opening a new one per click (~5 s saved). Detail: `generateTradeAlertCandidatesForTicker` reports `onQuoteStats` (requested / with price+delta); `refreshTickerTradeAlerts` throws before expiring when requested > 0 and usable = 0, and borrows `sharedLiveConnection` with one-shot fallback. Two things that surfaced while verifying it live: the candidate generator used fixed IBKR request ids (1-4), which collide on a shared connection ("Duplicate ticker id", code 322) — now allocated through `nextReqIdFor`; and a pricing snapshot that times out (REALTIME outside market hours, 10 s) aborted the whole refresh — `resolveScanSpotPrice` now falls back to the stored last known price. Verified: refresh outside hours returns the "existing alerts kept" message and AAOI's 3 pending alerts survive.

**Genosuke client handles streamed routes, 2026-09-24 (uncommitted).** The streamed-response change earlier today broke the assistant's `refresh_trade_alert` and `trigger_ibkr_health_check` tools (they parsed the SSE body as JSON). `GenosukeApiClient.request` now detects `text/event-stream`, reads the final `data:` frame and raises the real status — found by the end-to-end action review.

**End-to-end review of every user-triggered action, 2026-09-24 — 19 findings, all fixed (uncommitted).** Plain-language: Marcelo asked for a walk through every button and form in the app, following each one from the click to IBKR and the database and back, looking for what happens on a slow response, a double click, a stale screen or a bad input. Nineteen problems came out; the important ones were about orders: two people (or one person double-clicking) could confirm the same alert twice and place two orders, a covered call could be written against shares already committed to another in-flight covered call, an order confirmed hours after it was reviewed would go to IBKR at a stale price, and a worker restart mid-order could leave the app thinking an order was still waiting when IBKR had already filled it. Decisions Marcelo made: duplicate confirmations are rejected (HTTP 409) rather than merged; a reviewed-but-unconfirmed order is cancelled when its panel closes and is refused after 15 minutes; saving one strategy's risk limits without the existing-position fields keeps the stored values; login is throttled (10 attempts per 15 minutes per IP); the Trade Alerts page gets a Reject button; and the manual health-check button never restarts the Gateway during market hours. What changed, by area:
- *Orders (API `routes/positions.ts`, worker)*: confirm runs in one transaction and refuses a second active order for the same alert/position (`findActiveOrderConflict` → 409); covered-call sizing subtracts shares already committed to in-flight covered calls; limit prices must be > 0; a `no_quote` leg returns 422 instead of a price of 0; cancel and worker status writes are conditional on the current status so a cancel racing a submit cannot resurrect an order; `pendingConfirmationMaxAgeMs` (15 min) plus a 60 s sweep (`lib/stalePendingOrders.ts`) cancels forgotten pending orders; on start the worker reconciles stale requests against IBKR completed orders / trades (`ibkrGatewayFetchCompletedOrders.ts`). App: Ticker Detail, Close Position and Signals modals cancel an unconfirmed order on close; Cancel is disabled while confirming; roll form validates the limit.
- *Trade alerts*: Reject button with a confirm modal (`rejectTradeAlert`); per-ticker refresh does expire+insert in one transaction under a per-ticker advisory lock; deleting a shortlist ticker expires its alerts.
- *Auth*: login throttle, `session.regenerate` on login, app redirects to /login on any 401.
- *Long requests*: Ticker Detail chart, roll-candidate / recovery-path, shortlist backfill, per-ticker refresh and the manual health check are streamed (`lib/streamedResponse.ts`) so Heroku's 30 s limit cannot cut them off.
- *Inputs and errors*: non-UUID ids return 404 instead of a 500 (pg `22P02` mapped in the error handler); unknown symbols return 422 on shortlist and screener; Risk & Limits saves both strategies in one transactional `PUT /risk-limits/settings` with a blank-field check and no rounding; Dashboard day P&L is guarded by `previousOpenSessionDate`.
Verified live on dev (2026-09-24): login throttle (10 × 401 then 429); non-UUID position id → 404; bulk settings PUT → 200 with both rows updated in one transaction; covered_call PUT without existing-position fields keeps the stored 0.20/0.40; cancel of an unknown order → 404; Playwright: Reject button → confirm modal → alert row removed and DB row `rejected` with reviewer and timestamp; Risk & Limits Save writes 15 then 14 back for both strategies. `tsc` clean in both repos, API suite 501 passing. **Not live-tested** (needs a pending order during market hours): the duplicate-confirm 409, committed-shares sizing, panel-close cancel, the 15-minute stale sweep and the worker's start-up reconciliation.

**IBKR health-check "Historical data timeout for SPY" — evidence, 2026-09-24.** 7 occurrences in 3,648 runs since 2026-08-24. The two most recent (2026-09-23 22:20 and 23:20 UTC) both fell inside a running `option_chain_capture` / chained trade-alert scan (22:00–22:24 and 23:04–23:23); the earlier five had no job running (12:30, 12:50, 13:10, 20:20, 23:10 UTC on various days). In both recent cases the check restarted the Gateway underneath the running scan and then reported "restart didn't recover it" because the load was still there. **Already fixed, same day, by the "Second round of audit fixes" entry above** (`checkIbkrHealthJob.ts` now records `details.probe` and only restarts on a probe failure when no other job run is "running" AND the previous check's probe also failed) — this entry was written before that fix landed and never reconciled afterward; "decision pending" is stale.

## Scheduled jobs (Heroku Scheduler)

**Corrected 2026-09-25 (staleness pass): this section's "7/8 jobs" count was wrong** — `runWatchdogCheck.ts`'s `DAILY_JOB_NAMES` and `package.json`'s `job:*` scripts show 10 tracked daily jobs, not 7 or 8. Two are missing from the list below and need adding here: **`option_chain_structure_refresh`** (added 2026-09-23, its own Scheduler entry at 12:00 UTC per `scripts/run-option-chain-structure-job.ts`) and **`day_signals_seed`** (chained inside the option-chain-capture job's script, not a separate Scheduler entry — same as `option_surface_fit` below). The list below (1-7) predates these additions; treat the job count itself as unverified until someone checks the actual Scheduler dashboard entry-for-entry (this doc has been wrong about that count more than once).
All entries set to Basic dyno size in the Scheduler dashboard (manual, no CLI/API access to that setting).
1. **Daily price/IV capture** (`job:daily-market-data`, 10:00 PM UTC — moved from 9:00 PM 2026-09-24) — fixed and confirmed working 2026-08-28 (health check now probes `reqHistoricalData`); see "Currently open" above.
2. **Daily P&L snapshot** (`job:daily-pnl-snapshot`, 10:30 PM UTC — moved from 9:30 PM 2026-09-24; "Day" P&L is therefore measured at 18:30 EDT / 17:30 EST, after-hours included) — `account_pnl_snapshots`+`position_pnl_snapshots` from IBKR.
3. **Option-chain capture → SVI surface fit** (`job:option-chain-capture`; Trade Alert generation removed 2026-09-29 — `job:trade-alerts` script deleted, the staging Scheduler entry already removed by Marcelo, prod's goes at promotion, **two Scheduler entries at 14:00 and 15:00 UTC (10:00 ET); back to 10:00 ET on 2026-09-30 after a 9:30 ET trial (2026-09-29) fitted badly for HOOD/INTC** — the job's own clock guard (`optionChainCaptureClock.ts`) runs only the one that lands in 10:00–10:30 ET and skips market-closed days. Scheduler entries are at 14:00/15:00 UTC on staging and prod (Marcelo moved both 2026-09-30); then capture → fit → retry rounds → seed.) Entries take effect once the Signals build is deployed (job + tables live in it); first run to be done manually with `--force` to check timing; add `option_chain_capture` + `option_surface_fit` to the watchdog's daily list. *Previous entry, now superseded:* **Trade Alert generation** (`job:trade-alerts`) — **confirmed 2026-09-05 against the actual Heroku Scheduler config: only one run/day, 1:30 PM UTC** (market open, added 2026-08-25 so a full freshly-scanned list exists right at open). The second, 10:00 PM UTC EOD run this doc previously described as also configured is not present in Scheduler — either never added or removed at some point; this doc's other references to a 22:00 UTC trade-alert run (and the "buffer before it" framing on the calendar-capture job below) were stale. Redesigned 2026-08-27 — see "Currently open" above.
4. **IBKR Gateway health check** (`ibkr_health_check`, every 10 minutes — changed from hourly 2026-08-29, confirmed via Scheduler dashboard screenshot 2026-09-08) — real handshake-based check (fixed 2026-08-21 after a `nc -z`-only version stayed green during a real outage), plus the worker-systemd check and the position-reconciliation drift check (see Order execution platform).
5. **Watchdog** (`job:watchdog`, daily 11:30 PM UTC — moved from 10:30 PM 2026-09-24; now also checks the four option-chain jobs) — checks that every daily job (market-data, P&L snapshot, calendar capture, screener scan; trade alerts dropped 2026-09-29) ran today (jobs 6-7 added to the check 2026-09-18), health-check ran within 30 min (was 2h; tightened 2026-09-18 since the check runs every 10 min), nothing stuck `running` 30+ min. Telegram-only on actual findings.
6. **Daily calendar capture** (`job:daily-calendar-capture`, 8:00 PM UTC, added 2026-08-31) — TradingView earnings/ex-dividend data and (since 2026-10-07) the major macro events from FRED, the Fed FOMC calendar and the election rule. Feeds the Signals earnings exclusion and macro flag, the recovery-path calendar filter and the order-review warning; no IBKR/market-hours dependency. Manually triggered once to confirm clean in prod: 16/17 tickers resolved, 32 earnings rows, 5 dividend rows, 366 economic events.
7. **Daily screener scan** (`job:daily-screener-scan`, 11:00 PM UTC since 2026-10-22, after the close: the 2026-09-09 move to 6:00 PM for "the scanner only ranks during trading hours" was the market-cap filter bug, and after-close scans return full rows; added 2026-09-05) — IBKR market-scanner discovery job feeding the Screener tab's `screener_scan_results` table (see "Currently open" above). Cleanly spaced after market-data/pnl-snapshot/watchdog.
8. **Option session volume capture** (`job:option-session-volume`, 9:30 PM UTC market days, added 2026-10-15; Scheduler entry still to add, staging first) — each option contract's full-session volume and IBKR IV after the close, for the CC + Put Ladder's 20% rule (IBKR resets the volume overnight, so it cannot be read in the morning). Its own IBKR connection under FROZEN, non-priority lines (up to 40). In the watchdog's list and the pre-open readiness check's expected jobs.

### Telegram notification rules
- Fail-safe ordering: `job_runs` written first, Telegram attempted after, never masks a job failure.
- Alerts on every failure, all jobs. No daily success heartbeat otherwise.
- **Completed with errors (2026-10-15, Marcelo):** a run that did its work but had errors on part of it is `completed_with_errors`, not `failure` — e.g. "⚠️ daily_market_data_capture completed with 1 error: no implied volatility for TWST …; 25 of 26 tickers complete". Still alerts every time and is a ⚠️ Problem in the digest; amber on System Health and Pulse. The next clean run says "completed with no errors; the previous N runs … completed with errors". Used by `daily_market_data_capture` and `option_surface_fit` (failure only when nothing usable came out).
- **Rest of the jobs moved over, plus job wording (2026-10-15, Marcelo; built and tested, not pushed).** Plain-language: a job that has a problem with a few tickers no longer reports itself as "failed", and a job that works again no longer says it was "down for 72h" (a once-a-day job isn't a service; that figure counted the hours between runs). Staging's chain capture "failed" 10-12..10-14 on thin quotes for 2 of 21 tickers each day; replayed, those runs now read "completed with 2 errors". Changes in `runJob.ts`: a success after failures says "✅ {job} succeeded; the previous N runs (since …) failed" with no duration; a throttled job reminder (`ibkr_health_check`, skipped runs) says "Still failing after ~X", not "Still down". Failure only when nothing usable came out, everything narrower completes with errors:
  - `option_chain_capture`: fails on zero tickers, every ticker failed, or no risk-free rate. Failed, weak or fallback-spot tickers count one error each.
  - `option_chain_capture_retry`: fails when every queued ticker is still failing after the last attempt.
  - `day_signals_seed`: fails when the account summary is unavailable or no ticker has today's snapshot.
  - `option_chain_structure_refresh`: fails when no ticker got its structure.
  - `daily_calendar_capture`: fails on zero tickers or a failed **earnings** fetch (the trade gate). Dividends/macro failures and unresolved tickers count as errors.
  - `daily_screener_scan`: fails when every scan is empty, the universe is empty, or every symbol failed enrichment.
  - `daily_pnl_snapshot`: fails when the account-level row is missing or the position price fetch failed entirely. Skipped positions, missing greeks, a Flex reconcile failure, or a missing account value count as errors.
  - `expiry_settlement_audit` stays a failure: legs that need review are a finding, not partial work.
- **Position-expired notification fixed 2026-09-27**: a covered call's real assignment could fire the Telegram/toast "expired worthless — no assignment" before the same-pass ITM-vs-strike audit corrected `close_reason` to `assigned`, with no follow-up ever sent. `reconcileHeldPositions` now queues that notification and sends it only after the audit runs, reading the corrected reason (`ibkrGatewayReconcilePositions.ts`, `expirySettlementAudit.ts`). Also: a marginal (<5¢) ITM/OTM call now adds a "verify manually" note to the message; the emoji is now 🟢/🔴 by P&L when the outcome was the desired one for that strategy (assigned for a covered call, not-assigned for a CSP) and ⚠️ otherwise (`formatPositionExpiredMessage.ts`).
- **Failure summaries + recovery notices (added 2026-09-12, `src/lib/runJob.ts`)** — prompted by a real ~3h20m `ibkr_health_check` outage (2026-09-12 04:50–08:10 UTC, IBKR's own login servers unreachable from the VPS overnight, self-resolved, not a code/infra bug) that produced 20 consecutive failure alerts, each dumping a ~3KB `docker logs` blob into Telegram, and then recovered completely silently. Two changes: (1) `telegramFailureSummary()` truncates the Telegram-bound failure text at the first `"): "` marker (kept full in `job_runs.error_message`) — every restart-script-backed failure message already puts its one-sentence diagnostic before that marker, so this reliably drops just the raw log dump; (2) `findPrecedingFailureStreak()` sends `✅ {job} recovered after N failed attempts (was down since <ts>, ~<duration>)` (reworded 2026-10-15, see Completed with errors above) the first time a job succeeds after failing, closing the gap PROGRESS.md previously flagged as blocked on `job_runs` existing (it now does). New `src/lib/formatDurationHuman.ts` helper for the duration string. Both functions exported from `runJob.ts` for reuse by `tmp/simulateJobNotifications.ts`, a replay script that reconstructs what would have been sent for any historical window straight from `job_runs` — used to verify this change against the last 7 days before shipping (26 notifications reconstructed, including today's incident correctly summarized as 20 failed attempts / ~3h19m downtime).
- **Trading-events catch-all (Marcelo approved 2026-10-07; built and tested, pushed to staging 2026-10-07, live check pending).** Plain-language: every trade now shows up in the alerts chat, whoever placed it (web, Genosuke or Pluto): each order's progress (working, partly filled, filled, cancelled, expired, rejected, failed) with its contracts and prices, and every position opening or closing with its legs and, for a close, the realized P&L. Pluto's and Genosuke's own messages stay, so their trades appear twice on purpose; an expiry/assignment close is not repeated (its existing message counts). Orders: `lib/orderTelegramNotices.ts` on the web dyno (5 s poll, `order_requests.telegram_notified_status`, waits up to 5 min for a new position's fills). Positions: `lib/positionTelegramNotices.ts` in the worker after each reconciliation pass (`positions.telegram_opened_notified_at`/`telegram_closed_notified_at`; a failed send ends the pass and retries next pass). Migration backfills existing rows as already told. Trim later if noisy. Same change fixes Genosuke's own order follow-up: a new contract's opening fill is written only when reconciliation creates its leg, so its "filled" message could arrive with no fills, or a roll with only its buy-back; both now wait for the fills (shared `shouldWaitForFills` in `lib/orderFills.ts`).

## Atomic release phase: backup, restore on failure (2026-10-12, `35f80d7`; prod from the 2026-10-12 promotion)

**In plain words:** a promotion is now one click and all-or-nothing. Before changing the database, the release checks it is safe to deploy and takes a database backup. If anything fails after the database changes, it restores that backup and keeps the old code, so the app, the database and the trading worker never end up mismatched.

- **Order** (`scripts/run-release-phase.ts`, Procfile `release: npm run release-phase`):
  1. Deploy guard. On prod it stops before anything changes when the market is open, during the 15 minutes after the close, or with an order in flight; staging only warns.
  2. Heroku Postgres backup, only when there are migrations to run (`HEROKU_RELEASE_API_KEY`, Juan's key, used for backup and restore only).
  3. Migrations, in one transaction.
  4. Worker deploy over SSH.
  5. On a failure after step 3: drop the tables this release created, restore the backup, check the table list and the latest migration, exit 1.
  6. Record the backup in `release_backups`.
  7. Fill `major_macro_events` once when it is empty.
- **Restore is refused** (the release still fails, with a Telegram alert, fix forward) when orders or trades were written after the backup, or when the worker deploy broke off mid-way, so its state is unknown.
- **Previously** the migrations committed before the guard and the worker deploy ran, so an aborted release left the old code on the new schema.
- **Verified on staging 2026-10-12:**
  - With `RELEASE_PHASE_TEST_FAILURE=after_migrations` (staging-only switch), v192 took backup b025, migrated, failed on purpose and restored. Afterwards the migrations, tables and order/trade/position counts matched the baseline, and staging stayed on v191.
  - Then a clean release, v193: backup b027 → migration → staging worker redeployed over SSH (e11675c → 35f80d7, healthy, bound DUR854038) → `release_backups` row written.
- **Limits:**
  - Heroku's Roll back runs the *target* release's release command, so automatic restore on Roll back only works for releases made from a build that contains this script. The first prod promotion's rollback target (`7c0e137`) does not.
  - The worker still builds `origin/main`, not the promoted commit; a mismatch sends a Telegram warning. Do not push to main during a promotion.
- **Heroku quirk:** a config change after a FAILED release re-runs that failed build's release (seen on staging v193), so after a failed prod promotion any `config:set` retries the promotion.
- **Pending (this week):** Roll back that restores the matching `release_backups` backup automatically.

## Staging environment (built 2026-09-21; design of record and open items below)

**Design decisions 2026-09-21 (Marcelo approved; supersede anything below that conflicts). Execution starting now, step 0 = read-only inventory.**
- **Cutover concept**: today's "current" apps hold paper (i.e. staging) data. New `iorio-staging-*` apps take a one-time full copy of current's DB and pick up exactly where current is; then current is frozen and stays dormant as the future prod (built in a later phase). Old FE shows a "prod is being built, use staging" maintenance page with a link to `staging.ioriore.com`. Freeze order: confirm no orders in flight, stop current worker, **delete/disable current's Scheduler entries** (Scheduler one-offs ignore `ps:scale web=0` and maintenance mode — the `ibkr_health_check` would keep restarting staging's Gateway; UI-only, Marcelo does it, screenshot first), disable GitHub auto-deploy on the old apps. **Real Scheduler list (dashboard screenshot 2026-09-21; 8 jobs at the time, not 7; all Basic dynos, all UTC, `node dist/scripts/<file>.js`)**: `check-ibkr-health` every 10 min; `run-trade-alert-generation-job` 13:30; `run-daily-screener-scan-job` 18:00; `run-daily-calendar-capture-job` 20:00; `run-daily-market-data-job` 21:00; `run-daily-pnl-snapshot-job` 21:30; `run-daily-expiry-settlement-audit-job` 22:00; `run-watchdog-job` 22:30. Staging gets identical copies. **This was the 2026-09-21 snapshot only — it predates the 09-23 `option_chain_structure_refresh` entry, the 09-23 switch from `run-trade-alert-generation-job` to the 14:00/15:00 UTC `option_chain_capture` entries, and the 09-24 time moves; see the "Scheduled jobs" section for the (still not fully re-verified) current picture.** Cutover window = after 22:30 UTC until 13:30 UTC (trade alerts at 13:30 = US open). Step-0 inventory findings: both apps in region eu, owned by Juan's Heroku account (new apps will be Juan-owned too, accepted); no pipelines, no `pg:backups` schedule on prod (gap), dyno metadata not enabled; VPS worker at 76d03a9 vs main ~10 commits ahead (drift exists today); `/opt/ibkr` (compose for paper AND live in one file, image `:latest` unpinned, restart-gateway.sh/check-worker.sh hardcode paper/`iorio-worker`) is not in git; second worker health-check key `IORIO_WORKER_HEALTHCHECK_SSH_PRIVATE_KEY_BASE64`; extra config vars `MARKETDATA_API_TOKEN`, `OPENROUTER_API_KEY`, `GENOSUKE_ENABLED`; the existing Cloudflare token is WAF-only.
- **Two pipelines** (API repo, FE repo), not one. `main` auto-deploys to staging only (with CI-gated deploy); prod is promotion only. FE gets runtime config (`/config.js` served by `server.js` from an env var) so the identical build is promoted, not rebuilt.
- **Full isolation staging vs prod**: separate DB, `SESSION_SECRET`, tunnel + health-check SSH keys (tunnel key `permitopen`-restricted to 4002 staging / 4001 prod), Better Stack sources, Flex token, Cloudflare (same zone: staging gets no write token; single-level hostnames for Universal SSL; ACM records DNS-only until issued). **No prod-to-staging DB copy ever** (different IBKR accounts). The cutover copy is the one exception (current is paper data). The in-app DB-sync screen is dropped; `sync-dev-db` is retargeted to pull **staging to laptop only** and refuses any other app.
- **Deploy = Option A, release phase does everything** (no laptop orchestrator): `migrate:latest:prod`, then an idempotent forced-command SSH deploy of the worker AND the versioned Gateway config (`/opt/ibkr` compose + scripts kept in the repo; restart the container only when the config hash changed) pinned to `HEROKU_SLUG_COMMIT` (needs `runtime-dyno-metadata`). Additive-only migrations stay mandatory. Backfills = knex migrations (quick) or tracked data jobs (long). Worker heartbeat stores its SHA; `job:watchdog` alerts on drift vs the latest Heroku release. Post-boot web self-check posts "release live" to Telegram. Daily `pg:backups` scheduled on both DBs (no Heroku API token in app config). Fail-closed if the VPS is unreachable, with an escape-hatch config var that skips the worker step (setting it triggers a release; the override is announced in Telegram). Live-Gateway auto-restart stays behind an explicit confirm flag until a live restart is proven without 2FA (live container has never connected).
- **Deploy guards**: order-flow is DAY-only everywhere (`TimeInForce.DAY` in both `buildOrder` paths, Adaptive algo), so any `order_requests` row in `confirmed`/`cancel_requested`/`partially_filled`/`submitted` while the market is closed is anomalous. Prod release aborts on any of them, or when the market is open (market calendar table, ~16:15 ET buffer). Staging warns in Telegram and proceeds (else every daytime push would fail). Both have the announced override. A guard-aborted release is not retried automatically. To build: SIGTERM handler in the worker (finish the in-progress order handler, then exit) and an empirical restart test on staging (mid-placement order), since no signal handler exists today.
- **Telegram**: verbose per-step deploy messages (start, guards, migrations, worker SHA old to new, Gateway config, release live/self-check, failing step). Trim later if noisy. Current ops channel + bot = prod (goes quiet until prod is built). Staging gets its own channel (Marcelo + a new Genosuke-staging bot). Two Genosuke bots, one per env. Alerts also prefixed `[STAGING]` on staging.
- **LIVE/PAPER + environment labelling**: top bar always shows PAPER or LIVE; a small STAGING tag only when the environment isn't production. The value comes from the worker's real IBKR account (`managedAccounts`; paper IDs start `DU`, to be verified), never guessed from config. **Fail closed** on mismatch with `IBKR_TRADING_MODE`, and the DB is bound to a specific IBKR account ID on first connect (mismatch = crossed DB/account). On mismatch: no orders, no IBKR-to-DB writes (no reconcile), throttled Telegram alert, red top-bar state, Pulse card. Enforced in the single IBKR connection factory (verify all connect paths use it). Re-binding is an explicit admin script, no override var. (The account-binding fail-closed backend below shipped as WP2; the top-bar mockup/FE label itself was dropped from tracking 2026-09-25.)

Fix: read the whole login body before using the cookie (regression test fails without it). After the fix a cold start logged in and made tool calls with no 401. Same session also verified: readable Yes/Cancel cards (close BE showed BUY BACK call / SELL shares, Cancel created no order row); `list_positions` returns all 7 open positions incl. any unstructured; temporary diagnostics reverted.

Mode/summary code is shared (`readExpirySettlementMode`, `summarizeExpirySettlement`). **The worker now REQUIRES `EXPIRY_SETTLEMENT_MODE` in its `.env` at startup** (staging Heroku is `apply`); add it to `/opt/iorio-worker-staging/.env` BEFORE deploying the worker or it will crash-loop. - **Staging worker on `9e77f2b` since 2026-09-21 22:17 UTC** (deployed by Marcelo with `npm run deploy:worker:staging`; `EXPIRY_SETTLEMENT_MODE=apply` added to its `.env`, `.env.bak-wp1` removed): `worker_health` shows sha 9e77f2b, staging, DUR854038, paper/paper; no startup errors.
- **Phase B WP2 (fail-closed IBKR account binding) BUILT and DEPLOYED to staging, 2026-09-22 (`6966b69`)**: design approved by Marcelo (env var per environment; on mismatch pause orders AND reconciliation, cancels stay allowed; a blocked order is marked `error` and never retried; API also refuses to confirm). Worker: `IBKR_EXPECTED_ACCOUNT_ID` (singular, exactly one id, required, in the VPS `.env` and validated at startup together with `IBKR_TRADING_MODE` and `APP_ENVIRONMENT`; a live account outside production is refused) is compared with the accounts the Gateway reports (`src/lib/accountBinding.ts`, `src/ibkr/ibkrGatewayAccountBinding.ts`); status ok/mismatch/pending is written to new `worker_health.account_binding_status/_reason` columns. Gates: `processOrderRequest` (mismatch -> order `error` + Telegram; pending, e.g. just reconnected -> left `confirmed` to retry), `reconcilePositionsFromIbkr` (skipped unless ok), `recordExecution` (ignored on mismatch), every order now carries `order.account`. The accounts list is cleared at each connect attempt so an old session's list can never make a new one look bound. State-based alerts via `alert_state` key `account_binding_mismatch` (down + recovery, 30 min reminders). API: `POST /positions/orders/:id/confirm` returns 409 unless `worker_health` is fresh (<=120 s), same `app_environment`, and binding ok (`src/lib/tradingGate.ts`; needs `APP_ENVIRONMENT` on the API, set on Heroku staging 2026-09-22). **Verified**: 319 tests, migration up/down on a scratch DB, and live on staging the worker reports binding `ok` ("Bound to DUR854038") ~10 s after startup. **Negative test PASSED on staging, 2026-09-22**: with the VPS `.env` set to a wrong `IBKR_EXPECTED_ACCOUNT_ID` (DUR000000) and the worker restarted, `worker_health` showed `mismatch` ("Expected account DUR000000, but the Gateway reports DUR854038") within ~10 s, reconciliation was skipped each minute, the `account_binding_mismatch` alert row and Telegram alert appeared, `POST /positions/orders/:id/confirm` returned 409 with that reason and left the order `pending_confirmation`, and a synthetic `confirmed` order inserted straight into the DB became `error` ("Trading blocked by account binding…") with no `ibkr_order_id` (never sent). Restoring DUR854038 and restarting returned binding to `ok`, resumed reconciliation and cleared the alert state (recovery message path). Test rows left in `order_requests`: one cancelled API-test order and one `error` synthetic order. **Still not tested**: that IBKR accepts the `order.account` stamp on a real paper order (one small paper order + cancel via the UI, Marcelo to OK). Rollout order that worked: `APP_ENVIRONMENT` on Heroku, `IBKR_EXPECTED_ACCOUNT_ID` in the VPS `.env`, push (migration), then `npm run deploy:worker:staging`; between API and worker deploys confirms were blocked, as designed. **Prod (later)**: needs `APP_ENVIRONMENT=production` on the prod API and `IBKR_EXPECTED_ACCOUNT_ID` in the prod worker `.env` BEFORE their code is deployed.
- **Phase B WP4 (atomic release-phase worker deploy) IN PROGRESS, 2026-09-22.** Marcelo rejected a post-hoc drift-detection watchdog check ("by the time the job runs, we already have wrong versions running — that's unacceptable") in favour of coupling the worker deploy into the API's Heroku release phase so both are provably on the same commit, always — matches the design already on record above ("Deploy = Option A, release phase does everything"). Built so far, both live on staging: (1) **worker SIGTERM handler** (commit `1b511ee`) stops starting new order/cancel handling and reconciliation passes on SIGTERM, drains a bounded 25s for whatever's already running (unit-tested via `waitUntilDrained`), then exits — anything not yet started is safely picked up by the next process's existing 30s poll fallback / initial-reconciliation-on-connect. Verified live on staging via two real restarts: clean "drained cleanly in 0ms" in the idle case (the realistic common case, given order placement is single-digit ms and reconciliation ~2s against a 60s gap between passes — manually racing a restart into a live in-flight window proved impractical by hand, so the actual wait/timeout logic is proven via fake-timer unit tests instead). (2) **worker code hash** (`computeSourceClosureHash.ts`, commit `d1dc7bd`): statically walks the worker entrypoint's transitive relative-import closure (not a hand-maintained file list) plus `package-lock.json`, worker publishes it to new `worker_health.worker_code_hash` at startup. Verified: computing it independently from a fresh local checkout at the same commit produced the byte-identical hash to what the VPS worker published (`c025a7fc...539fa8e`), proving the release phase (a third checkout) will compare correctly. Marcelo asked for messaging either way: every release-phase run will announce whether the worker was deployed or skipped (and why), not just on deploy. **Completed, verified 2026-09-25.** `scripts/deploy-worker-to-vps.sh` now builds to a fresh location (`$REMOTE_DIR.next`, `npm ci && npm run build`) and only swaps it in (`mv` to/from `.prev`) on success, with an automatic rollback to `.prev` on a failed health check — the old in-place `git reset --hard` risk is gone. `scripts/run-release-phase-worker-deploy.ts` is wired into the `Procfile`'s `release` script, actually consumes `computeSourceClosureHash` vs. `worker_health.worker_code_hash` to decide skip-vs-deploy (not just computed-and-stored), includes the market-open/in-flight-order deploy guard, and sends Telegram on every branch (skip/deploy/abort/override). Built across commits `d1dc7bd`/`6808bdd`/`ccf32a0`/`0221ffd`/`dfb5315`/`98612f4` (2026-09-21/22).
- **All-or-none (AON) orders — checked 2026-09-25, deeper gap than previously tracked: the capability doesn't exist in code at all**, not just untested. `@stoqey/ib`'s `Order` type supports `allOrNone?: boolean`, but nothing in `src/ibkrGatewayWorker.ts`'s order construction sets it (only `OrderType.LMT` + `TimeInForce.DAY`), there's no AON/TIF column on `order_requests`, and no historical order payload has ever carried one. Would need to be built before it could be tested. Then Phase B (deployment machinery) starts, beginning with the three WP1 approvals in item 5.

**Concept, clarified 2026-08-31 (supersedes the original framing below)**: this is not "rename current to staging, build a new prod." A brand-new staging environment is built with entirely new Heroku apps/domains/database, active immediately for ongoing paper-trading development. **Current stays as the future production identity** — untouched, kept running until staging is verified working, then **paused** (dynos scaled to 0, worker systemd unit stopped) and left dormant. Current is only woken again for the live cutover (flip `IBKR_TRADING_MODE` to `live`, start the already-existing-but-`Exited(1)` `ib-gateway-live` container, resume current's worker) — a separate future decision gated on Juan confirming the live IBKR connection actually works end-to-end (see the still-open real-time-market-data thread above). Building staging carries zero live-money risk.

**Research finding that simplifies this a lot**: the VPS already has a second IBKR Gateway container defined (`ib-gateway-live`, port 4001, its own `.env.live`, currently stopped) and `IBKR_TRADING_MODE` is already a one-var paper/live switch on both the web app and worker — so the live side of this needs no new IBKR container, just getting that existing one to actually connect (separate blocked item) and un-pausing current.

**Correction, 2026-08-31**: Marcelo initially recalled "we already have a second worker deployed, one for paper and one for live" — checked directly via SSH and this isn't accurate today. There is exactly **one** systemd unit (`iorio-worker.service`) and **one** `/opt/iorio-worker` clone on the VPS, currently pointed at paper (`IBKR_TRADING_MODE=paper` in its `.env`). What actually exists as a pair is the two **Gateway containers** (`iorio-ibkr-ib-gateway-paper-1`, running; `iorio-ibkr-ib-gateway-live-1`, `Exited (1)`) — not two workers. Likely the source of the mix-up. Naming is designed below to prevent this recurring.

**Decided design**:
- **New apps**: `iorio-staging-api` / `iorio-staging-app`, domains `api-staging.ioriore.com` / `staging.ioriore.com`. Own Postgres addon (mini/essential-1). Own Scheduler addon too — reversed 2026-08-31 from the original "no scheduled jobs" requirement: Marcelo wants staging to replicate prod as closely as possible, so it gets its own copy of all scheduled-job entries (at the time: `job:daily-market-data`, `job:daily-pnl-snapshot`, `job:trade-alerts` (once/day), `ibkr_health_check`, `job:watchdog`, `job:daily-calendar-capture`, `job:daily-screener-scan` — this list is from 2026-08-31 and predates everything the Signal Engine/Signals build added since; see "Scheduled jobs" for the current job list), pointed at staging's own app/DB, running independently of current's.
- **Heroku Pipeline**, both apps: staging stage = the new `iorio-staging-*` apps (today's GitHub auto-deploy from `main` moves onto these). Production stage = today's existing `iorio-reloaded-*` apps (dormant now; woken and promoted-into only for the live cutover). Chosen over two fully independent apps because slug promotion guarantees the exact tested build reaches "prod" rather than a second independent rebuild from source — real benefit on the API side; on the frontend it's mostly cosmetic since `VITE_API_BASE_URL` is baked in at build time regardless (see `src/api/client.ts`), so the frontend still needs its own per-environment build either way.
- **VPS worker naming, to prevent the confusion above from recurring**: current's worker gets renamed `iorio-worker-prod` (`/opt/iorio-worker-prod`, systemd unit `iorio-worker-prod.service`) — still pointed at paper today, will point at live after cutover, but the name reflects its permanent *role* (prod), not its current trading mode. Staging gets a new, separately-named `iorio-worker-staging` (`/opt/iorio-worker-staging`, systemd unit `iorio-worker-staging.service`). Both are just directory/unit renames plus a fresh clone for staging — no code changes. Own DB connection, own distinct persistent-connection clientId for staging (not the existing fixed `42`, to avoid any ambiguity even though they'll never run concurrently — see below).
- **Shared paper Gateway container, by design, never concurrently**: staging's worker connects to the *same* paper Gateway (port 4002, same underlying IBKR paper account) current uses today — confirmed acceptable specifically because current gets paused before staging's worker ever starts. Running both workers against the one shared paper account at the same time was explicitly rejected 2026-08-31 ("we can't have two services pointing to the same gateway, it'll be chaotic and it's not a true test of how the app should work") — reconciliation on each side would ingest the other's trades as unexplained position changes. The cutover is a deliberate manual switch: stop `iorio-worker-prod`, then start `iorio-worker-staging`.
- **DB sync**: a real in-app screen (not just a script) to pull current's Postgres into staging's, mirroring [[project_sync_dev_db_script]]'s mechanics. Built as part of this pass, not deferred.
- **Genosuke/Telegram**: staging needs its own BotFather bot token (Telegram allows only one webhook URL per bot token, so it can't share current's) — a manual step only Marcelo can do — plus its own chat/channel and `GENOSUKE_*` config vars on `iorio-staging-api`.
- **Rollback**: Heroku's built-in release rollback covers app code for free once the apps exist. DB migrations aren't automatically reversible — the real strategy is the additive-only-migrations discipline already in practice ([[project_pre_launch_migrations_editable]], superseded note), not new down-migration tooling.

**Housekeeping fixes, thought through 2026-08-31, bundled in regardless of staging** (Marcelo asked for the analysis, not just the flag):
- [x] **`Procfile`'s dead `worker:` line — fixed 2026-09-05.** Deleted entirely (was `worker: node dist/src/ibkrWorker.js`, pointing at a file deleted long ago). The worker's real, permanent home is the VPS by deliberate design ([[project_worker_runs_on_vps]]) — a dead line was a real risk if ever scaled up by mistake against the shared IBKR Gateway. Still needs the same fix applied to the staging Procfile whenever that's built (same file, both apps).
- [x] **`SESSION_SECRET` silent fallback — fixed 2026-09-05.** `src/config/env.ts` now requires it via `requireEnvironmentVariable`, throwing at boot instead of silently accepting a hardcoded dev-only string. Confirmed set on Heroku prod (`iorio-reloaded-api`) before deploying this; added a real generated value to local `.env` (gitignored) so local dev keeps working. Typecheck + full test suite (36/36) confirmed clean.
- **`.env.live.example` key-name mismatch** — confirmed still present, checked directly over SSH 2026-09-25. Documents `TWS_USERID_LIVE`/`TWS_PASSWORD_LIVE`, but the real `/opt/ibkr/.env.live` uses unsuffixed names (`TWS_USERID`, `TWS_PASSWORD`, `AUTO_RESTART_TIME`, `VNC_SERVER_PASSWORD`) — confirmed via `docker-compose.yml`, whose `ib-gateway-live` service reads `.env.live` via `env_file:` with no override interpolation, so the container genuinely uses the unsuffixed names. Low priority (documentation only) — fix directly over SSH whenever convenient.

**Deferred to when the live cutover actually happens, not part of this build**: a `restart-gateway-live.sh` + forced-command SSH key for the live container's health-check (today's `/opt/ibkr/restart-gateway.sh` is hardcoded to the paper container name); deciding whether current's DB carries forward its paper-era history into real prod or starts clean; a live-specific smoke test.

**Execution checklist / "not yet started" note archived — staging was provisioned 2026-09-21 (see the Execution log above).**

## Dependency vulnerability checks (built 2026-10-01, both repos; modelled on menaris)

Plain-language: a known-vulnerable library can now stop a deploy, and we get a Telegram message every day while one is unfixed. (1) **Deploy gate**: `heroku-postbuild` runs `npm audit --audit-level=high --omit=dev` before the build, so a high/critical flaw in a library that ships blocks the staging release (old version keeps running). Production is promotion-only, so it never rebuilds and is covered by (2). (2) **Daily audit**: `.github/workflows/daily-audit.yml`, 00:00 UTC, whole tree incl. dev tools, Telegram alert naming the packages; manual run via the Actions tab. (3) The existing deploy-notify message already lists moderate+ flaws. Since 2026-10-22 (API repo) both messages split them: ⚠️ for flaws in the deployed app (runtime dependencies, `--omit=dev`), ℹ️ for flaws only in dev tools (not deployed); a dev-only daily alert leads with ℹ️. (4) Dependabot is deliberately not used: the daily audit reads the same GitHub advisory database, so it would only duplicate the alerts. Alerts go to the "Iorio Staging" Telegram group (daily-audit alert path tested end to end 2026-10-01).

## Ticker Detail on a shared live connection (2026-09-19)
`streamTickerDetail` now borrows `sharedLiveConnection` (one-shot fallback kept): a second shared connection whose market data type is set to REALTIME once at connect and never changed (`requestRealtimeMarketData` is a no-op for it, via `markMarketDataTypeManaged`). All reqIds on shared connections come from that connection's counter through `nextReqIdFor(ib, fallback)` (fetchOptionChain lookups/quotes, priceBarCache's derived IV-bar id which used `reqId + 1000`, and the four fixed ids in streamTickerDetail). Fixed `ib.once()` misuse in `lookupPricingSnapshot` (`tickSnapshotEnd`) and `lookupContractDetails` (`contractDetailsEnd`); `lookupPricingSnapshot` now also cleans up its listeners and cancels its subscription on timeout.
- **Tested (Saturday, market closed):** solo vs 3 tickers concurrent (AAOI/NBIS/SPCX): identical prices, contract sets, chart bars, technicals; the one quote difference was a genuine late tick on the correct contract (SPCX 2026-09-25 152.5C, delta matches that position's leg). Listener counts on the live connection return to the idle baseline within 20s after full sessions and after disconnects at 2s/4s/7s. One-shot fallback verified by requesting before the live connection was ready. First data ~0.6s once warm (the connect used to come first); option chain ~10s (unchanged work: strike checks + quotes).
- **Known limitation:** if the shared live connection drops mid-session, open modals stop updating until reopened (same as a dropped one-shot connection); not handled.
- **Migration completed, verified 2026-09-25.** `fetchTickerQuoteSnapshot.ts`/`fetchTickerOverview.ts`/`refreshTradeAlert.ts`/`evaluateRollForPosition.ts` now call `borrowSharedConnectionOrConnect` with `nextReqIdFor`'s per-connection allocator; `streamOrderLegQuote.ts` was rewritten onto `marketDataPool.ts`; `streamPositionQuote.ts` was deleted outright in the 2026-09-24 audit (`a6e168a`), confirmed zero remaining references repo-wide.

## Dashboard Portfolio-card load time (2026-09-19)
Measured, not guessed: the exposure SSE stream took ~9s to first data (account+CSP query ~1.0s, DB 4ms, new SSH tunnel ~3.7s + IBKR handshake ~1.7s per stream, FROZEN price ticks ~2.1s). Not a market-hours effect. Fixes: `streamLivePrices` and `streamLiveGreeks` now borrow the shared read connection (one-shot fallback kept); the exposure route starts the account fetch in parallel with the stream; `server.ts` warms the shared connection at startup. Result: Portfolio card ~2.6s in the browser (desktop + mobile checked), first request after a restart ~2.3s, greeks stream first data ~1.6s.
- **Migration completed, verified 2026-09-25** — see the same note above; `streamTickerDetail` was migrated separately (see "Ticker Detail on a shared live connection" above). `checkIbkrHealthJob` stays one-shot by design.
- **clientIds (changed later 2026-09-19):** the shared connections no longer use a fixed id. Each connect attempt draws a random id from its own range (read 1,000,000-1,499,999; live 1,500,000-1,999,999), above the one-shot connections (0-999,999) and the worker's fixed 42. A fixed 44 had let two processes booting together (duplicate dev servers) hang each other for 18.7s.

## Outage alert throttling (2026-09-19, built and deployed, verified in a real outage)
IBKR maintenance on a Saturday produced a Telegram message per systemd restart (worker) and per 10-min run (health check). Fix: state-based alerting. New `alert_state` table (row present = already alerted) and `src/lib/throttledAlert.ts` (`notifyDownThrottled`, `clearDownState`; atomic, so concurrent callers can't double-send). (A) `runJob` takes `failureAlertReminderIntervalMs`; `ibkr_health_check` uses 1h: first failure alerts, same-message repeats are muted, a changed message alerts immediately, recovery uses the existing streak message. (B) `persistentIbkrConnection.start()` no longer rejects on a failed connect; the worker stays up and retries with the existing backoff, so systemd stops restart-looping. The worker alerts once after 5 min disconnected (was 10; lowered 2026-09-19), reminds hourly, and sends one recovery message. Tested locally: throttle sequence (10 cases incl. concurrency), `start()` resolving with an unreachable tunnel, `runJob` 3 failures = 1 alert + recovery + state cleared. **Not tested:** a real Gateway outage end to end. **Deploy order:** GitHub push first (release phase creates `alert_state`), then `npm run deploy:worker` (manual, VPS). Other worker startup failures (DB down, etc.) still crash-and-alert per restart. **Bug found live and fixed (8e378b1; reached the VPS at 17:08 UTC. A 16:53 redeploy shipped nothing new because `deploy:worker` deploys origin/main and the fix wasn't pushed yet, so push first and verify the VPS file):** the worker's disconnect message embedded the elapsed time, and the throttle treats a changed message as a new alert, so it re-alerted every minute. Throttled messages must be constant text; reminders add the duration themselves. The redeploy causes one extra alert (stored text differs), then hourly. **Verified live 2026-09-19 (real IBKR outage, Gateway back at 17:24 UTC):** worker sent exactly 1 alert (17:19), no repeats, state cleared on reconnect; health check sent 1 alert at 16:41 and stayed quiet. 5-min threshold deployed 17:29 UTC (VPS at 2e0ffad, dist file checked). **Deploy lesson:** `deploy:worker` ships origin/main, not the local tree; push first, read the script's stderr warning, and grep the deployed dist on the VPS before calling it done.

## Stream multiplexing (built)
The design plan (D1–D5) that used to be here was implemented: git commit `4341611` "Stream multiplexer (always on), Price Performance redesign, strategy badge standardisation". The original plan, findings and decision log are in `PROGRESS-ARCHIVE.md`.

## IBKR Gateway restart: false-alarm recovery + Pulse going stale on a restart (fixed 2026-09-23)
Two related bugs found from a real Telegram alert (`ibkr_health_check failed: ... restart didn't recover it`). **1) The alert itself was a false alarm, recurring:** `/opt/ibkr/restart-gateway.sh` on the VPS used a flat `sleep 25` after restarting the Gateway container, sized off one 2026-08-17 observation — not always enough (this run's logs show login not completing until ~30s), so the health check's reprobe sometimes hit a Gateway still mid-login and reported a false failure. `job_runs` showed this same pattern recurring at least 4 times in the prior two weeks, always self-clearing on the next 10-min check. **Fixed**: the script now polls Gateway's own "Login has completed" log line (up to 60s) instead of guessing a fixed delay; `restartIbkrGatewayOnVps.ts`'s SSH timeout bumped 90s → 120s to match. Deployed to the VPS by Marcelo (Claude's auto-mode classifier blocks writes/reads over the VPS SSH connection — even `docker logs`/`diff` — so this needs either a settings rule or Marcelo running it; same open gap as `feedback_classifier_blocks_destructive_vps_commands` in memory).

**2) Real bug: Pulse silently went stale during the ~30-60s Gateway restart and never recovered without a manual refresh.** Root cause was backend-side, not the frontend's fault: `streamLiveGreeks`/`streamLivePrices` (backing the `greeks`/`pnl` multiplexer subscriptions Pulse and Positions both use) only waited on the caller's abort signal once streaming started — nothing observed the IBKR socket actually dying, so a Gateway restart left the producer's promise hanging forever. Meanwhile the multiplexer's connection-level heartbeat kept the SSE transport looking perfectly healthy, so the frontend's (otherwise solid) `EventSource` reconnect logic never had anything to react to — a silent hang dressed up as a live connection. Separately, even if that end/error frame had arrived, the multiplexer client deleted a failed/ended subscription permanently and only called the caller's `onError` — Pulse doesn't pass one, so nothing would have resubscribed anyway.

**Fixed, three pieces:**
- Backend: new `waitForAbortOrGatewayDisconnect` (`src/ibkr/requestMarketData.ts`) — resolves on abort OR a real IBKR disconnect/code-1100 "connectivity lost" notice, so a Gateway restart now makes the producer return and the multiplexer send a proper `end` frame. Wired into both `streamLiveGreeks` and `streamLivePrices` (covers `greeks`, `pnl`, and anything else built on those two).
- Frontend: the multiplexer client (`src/api/streamMultiplexer.ts`) now re-subscribes a subscription (same id, backoff 1s→30s) on `error`/`end` instead of dropping it forever — `onError` is still called for any page that wants a visible indicator, but recovery no longer depends on the caller wiring one up.
- Frontend defense-in-depth: the backend's 20s heartbeat is now a real JSON frame (was an invisible SSE comment `: ping`), so the client can detect a connection that's silently wedged at the transport level (server process stuck, nothing at all arriving) and force a reconnect — a case neither of the above two fixes would catch.
- Not yet extended to `streamOrderLegQuote.ts`/`streamTickerDetail.ts`/`streamSignalsOptionQuotes.ts`/`fetchOptionChain.ts`/`fetchTickerOverview.ts`, which have the same `signal.addEventListener("abort", ...)`-only wait pattern — out of scope for this fix (not reported as broken), flagged here in case the same silent-stale symptom shows up on those screens.

Verified: `tsc --noEmit` clean both repos, full suite 436/445 passing (9 pre-existing unrelated failures in `captureOptionQuoteBatch.test.ts`, confirmed failing on `main` before this change too). Not yet exercised live against a real Gateway restart — next real restart (or the VPS script fix above triggering one) is the first live test of the reconnect path specifically.

## IBKR health check: no restart on one failed connection (2026-10-09, Marcelo; pushed to staging `0df4d86`, prod at next promotion)
Plain language: the 10-minute IBKR check restarted the Gateway after a single failed connection attempt, even when IBKR was working fine for everything else. On prod at 10:10 ET on 2026-10-08 that cut the app's live connection for about 40 seconds during market hours and then sent a false "restart didn't recover it" alert, because the check gave up a few seconds before the Gateway was back. Now the check tries once more after 10 seconds before restarting, and after a restart it tries up to 3 times, 10 seconds apart, before calling it a failure.
- **Technical:** `checkIbkrHealthJob.ts`: `unreachableRetryDelayMs` 10 s, `postRestartConnectAttempts` 3 / `postRestartConnectRetryDelayMs` 10 s (all restart causes); `tryConnect` returns the error; `details.handshakeErrors` on success, appended after the script output on failure (kept out of the Telegram summary). A retry that connects sends no alert. `gateway-control.sh` reports "back" on the API's version bytes, before a full handshake works, hence the post-restart retries.
- **Verified:** `checkIbkrHealthJob.test.ts` 65 passing (retry-ok-no-restart, post-restart recovery on attempt 2, Telegram text unchanged), tsc clean. Not yet seen live: the next real "unreachable" run.

## Position reconciliation alerts: "couldn't run" is not a mismatch, and an all-clear (approved by Marcelo 2026-10-19; built and tested, not pushed)
Plain language: when IBKR didn't answer the positions request in time, the alert said "1 discrepancy between IBKR and local data" even though nothing had been compared, and no message followed when the next check was fine (prod, 2026-10-17 16:40 UTC). Now a check that couldn't run says so, and only alerts when it happens on two checks in a row (one timeout is usually a Gateway hiccup; Pluto stays blocked until a check completes either way). Real mismatches say "N mismatches between IBKR and our records". Both send one message, an hourly reminder while the problem lasts, a fresh message straight away if the mismatch list changes, and a ✅ when a later check agrees.
- **Technical:** `src/lib/positionReconciliationAlert.ts` (alert_state keys `position_reconciliation_mismatch` / `position_reconciliation_could_not_run`, via `notifyDownThrottled`/`clearDownState`); `checkIbkrHealthJob.ts` classifies the result (`classifyReconciliationProblems`, previous run read from `job_runs`) and no longer puts reconciliation in the job's notify text. `details.reconciliationProblems` unchanged, so Pluto's system check is unaffected. Runs on the API, not the worker.
- **Verified:** unit tests for both files, tsc clean, and a 10-step sequence against the local `alert_state` table with Telegram disabled (silent first failure, alert on the second, throttled third, ✅ ran again; mismatch, throttled repeat, hourly reminder, changed list re-sent, ✅ agree again, silent clean).

## Positions screen refreshes once a structure change is fully written; "Live data disconnected" no longer sticks (approved by Marcelo 2026-10-19; built and tested, not pushed; ibkrGateway* file changed, so a push redeploys the worker)
Plain language: when shares were moved into a new covered call (staging ALAB, 2026-10-19), the Positions screen showed two rows, the call alone and the shares at $0.00, until reloaded. The data was right; the screen had refreshed halfway through the change and was never told to refresh again. Now the worker says "positions changed" once the whole change is saved, and the screen and the ticker window refresh then. Separately, the "Live data disconnected" message could stay on screen after the connection had come back (prices ticking, Retry doing nothing); it now only shows while the connection is really down.
- **Technical:** `reconcileHeldPositions` fingerprints open positions + open legs per ticker before and after each pass and publishes one `positions_changed { symbols }` (never persisted) in a `finally`. App: `PositionsPage` and `useTickerPositions` re-fetch on it instead of `position_opened` (still published; Pulse and Today's Orders use it). `StreamConnectionToast` shows the outage only while the live status is the outage it was timed for; "Reconnected" is cleared when a new outage starts. `retryMultiplexerConnectionNow` closes the old EventSource before reconnecting (it used to leave it running under the same generation).
- **Verified:** reconcile tests (roll into a covered call: one `positions_changed` after the leftover closed; unchanged pass names nothing), 75/75 with the notification tests; Playwright locally: `pg_notify` refetches (page on any symbol, modal only its own), the toast race forced by holding its timer reproduces the stuck toast + dead Retry on the old code and not on the new, a Retry mid-browser-reconnect leaves one open stream.
- **Not looked into:** staging resets the live connection often (four `ERR_CONNECTION_RESET` within ~11 s; connections ending after 21 s, 15 and 45 min).

## Allocation bar matches its tiles; untraded options valued at last night's mark (approved by Marcelo 2026-10-21; built and tested, not pushed; one migration)
Plain language: on prod the Allocation bar showed Available Cash ($948) wider than Covered Calls ($8,915). Two causes. The TLT hedge (a 2028 Call that hadn't traded that day) was valued at what it cost, not today's price, because prod still prices an option only at its last trade; about $9k of the account was then drawn as "cash". Main already prices options at the bid/ask mid (`b04e886`, prod at the next promotion). Outside market hours there is no bid/ask, so an option that didn't trade that day now falls back to last night's IBKR mark instead of its cost. Separately, the bar's grey slice was "account value minus everything else", not Available Cash; the bar (Dashboard and Pulse) is now the five tiles over their total, so it always matches the numbers under it.
- **Technical:** new `position_leg_price_snapshots` (one row per leg per night, the daily P&L job's closing price: stocks the day's last, options IBKR's portfolio mark); `computeExposureRows` falls back live → latest nightly price → entry. App: `allocationBarPercents` in `lib/portfolioFromExposure.ts` (a negative value, e.g. cash on margin, draws no slice and is left out of the total).
- **Verified:** exposure unit tests (fallback order), 279 tests across the exposure/limits/dashboard files, tsc both repos; the nightly job run locally wrote 6/7 leg prices; local `/risk-limits/exposure` valued the dev TLT hedge at the stored mark ($54,880, not $43,098 at cost); Playwright: bar widths equal tile shares to 0.01% on both screens, 1706/1440/390 px, both themes.

## Pluto is only offered contracts it can size (approved by Marcelo 2026-10-21; built and tested, not committed, not pushed)
Plain language: Pluto's AI could be offered a contract Pluto could not afford, pick it, and see it blocked, which wasted the round's one pick (NVDA $212.5 Put · 23 Oct (2DTE) on staging 2026-10-21: NVDA had $8,286 left under its per-ticker limit, one contract needed $21,162). The code now sizes every contract before the AI sees it, with the same limits it applies after the pick: order size, capital budget, per-ticker and per-sector limits, cash reserve, volume, stress and Kelly. A contract that comes out at zero contracts is left off the list with the reason ("ticker room $8,286 is less than one contract's $21,162"). Rolls and closes are unchanged. The How it works tab lists the rule as "Room for an order". Two more checks moved before the AI the same way: with the open-positions cap reached the AI is offered no new trade (rolls, closes and the Calls code sells on held shares continue), and a ticker with a Pluto order still working is offered nothing until it ends. A trade blocked on size now starts with the one limit that stopped it, every figure after ("Ticker room $8,286 is less than one contract's $21,162. Details: …"). A placed order's "Commits … of the order size" line now names the limit that actually held it below the order size (it said "limited by how many contracts traded today" on every order); orders from before 2026-10-21 show no note. Technical: `sizeOpenOrder` returns `limit` (`SizingLimit`); `passRunner` puts it on the action's sizing gate result, the backtest's stored gates don't carry it.
- Technical: `sizeOpenOrder` (`postModelGates.ts`) is the sizing gate's open sizing; `filterTickerForPluto` runs it (rejection code `sizing`) on the book `passRunner` reads once per round (`tickerSizingBook`, `loadInFlightNotionalsBySymbol`), only for contracts nothing but quote age keeps out. The backtest passes no `sizingBook` and keeps its try-next-choice after a refusal, so its results are unchanged.

## Only one Pluto agent at a time (approved by Marcelo 2026-10-21; built and tested, not pushed; one migration; installCrashHandlers.ts is in the worker's code, so a push redeploys the worker)
