Plain language: on 2026-10-21 Heroku moved Pluto's agent to a new server and ran the old and the new agent side by side for about 90 seconds; the old one then sent a false 🔥 crash alert because its log output had been cut off. Nothing happened (it was before the open), but during market hours both could have placed orders. Now an agent must hold a lease (one database row naming the agent in charge, renewed every 15 seconds) before it does anything; another agent waits until the first stops, or until the first has gone 60 seconds without renewing. The API also refuses to send a Pluto order unless it comes from the agent holding the lease, so an agent that froze and woke up later cannot place one. Losing the log output is no longer treated as a crash. Pluto now also says on Telegram when it stops and starts, stating only what it knows for certain (Marcelo 2026-10-21): stopping because Heroku asked or because another agent took over; started because of a new release, because the previous agent stopped responding, after waiting for the previous agent to stop, or a Heroku restart on the same release, plus "off" or "paused (reason)" when Pluto isn't running normally.
- **Technical:** `pluto_agent_lease` (one row; its own table so the confirm's row lock never holds up `pluto_state` writes). `src/pluto/agentLease.ts`: database-clock lease of 60 s, renewed every 15 s and at every round start, 5 s retries while waiting; lost only to another holder, never to a failed renewal. `plutoAgent.ts` takes the lease before the agent starts (no `agent_started`, pass sweep, order adoption or heartbeat until then), stops on losing it (warning event + fixed-text Telegram) and releases it after `agent.stop()`. The confirm route checks `X-Pluto-Agent-Holder` against the holder `FOR SHARE` inside the confirmation transaction for every order with `pluto_action_id`: 409, which the executor treats as blocked and cancels; a person can no longer confirm a pending Pluto order; cancels stay open to everyone. `installCrashHandlers` drops EPIPE on stdout/stderr. Start/stop lines: `src/pluto/agentLifecycleMessages.ts` (pure), sent from `plutoAgent.ts` whatever the Telegram verbosity; an agent that never held the lease sends nothing; `formatDurationToTheSecond` added beside `formatDurationHuman`.
- **Verified:** lease unit and DB tests (a takeover waits for a confirm in flight; that test fails without `FOR SHARE`), route and client tests, full suite and tsc; a real-process simulation on the local database (`tmp/agentLeaseSimulation.ts`, 38/38): a second agent waits, SIGTERM hands over in one retry, a stalled holder is replaced when its lease runs out and its confirm is refused by the local API while the new holder's passes the lease check, it stops the moment it resumes, a killed holder is replaced when its lease runs out, SIGTERM while waiting exits; local state restored.

## CC + Put Ladder brought to Pluto's safety standard (approved by Marcelo 2026-10-21; built and tested, committed locally, not pushed; one migration)
Plain language: before Pluto can run the CC + Put Ladder on staging instead of Edge Margin, the ladder needed the same protections Edge Margin has. These are now built. Staging keeps running Edge Margin until Marcelo switches it and picks the tickers. Staging's general ladder values already match the lab (shares 65%, rungs 3, shock min 40%; set 2026-10-21). The per-ticker values, allocations and capital budget wait for the tickers.
- **Limits:** new limits across all tickers: 120 open positions and working orders, 100 orders a day, and never more money committed than the capital budget. Per-ticker defaults are raised to 30 open positions and 10 working orders (the backtest held up to 27 positions on one ticker). Sales over a limit wait for a later look.
- **Checks before sending:** each order gets a fresh quote and is checked against the contract rules again (bid, spread, DTE, opening yield, delta for a Call or Put that opens a cycle). The order route checks the book's capital again at confirm. Old band quotes are dropped. A price without a time is not used.
- **Matching the lab:** the entry is now `higher_yield`, and new Covered Calls need a 20% minimum yield. A cycle of Puts alone does not start the ladder until shares are held.
- **Orders:** limit prices are rounded to the cent, sales up and buys down (Marcelo 2026-10-21: no $0.05 step, since SMART accepts any cent). Routine order problems (the price moved, no fresh quote, IBKR refusing the price or the margin) skip that order instead of pausing Pluto. Telegram gets one notice per ticker and reason each day.
- **Technical:** migration `20261021400001` adds `ladder_max_open_positions` and `ladder_max_orders_per_day`, and raises a per-ticker cap only where it is still 5. Shared helpers: `ladderBookCapitalVerdict` and `loadCommittedCapitalOnSymbols` (`orderLimits.ts`), `optionLimitPrice.ts`, `routineOrderProblems.ts`, `ladderNotices.ts`. `admitLadderSales` holds the ticker and book caps. `ladderOrderGates` checks entry-Put delta, told apart from ladder Puts by `contract.saleStep`. The backtest's rule steps are exported from `baselineSaleSteps.ts` and behave as before. The How it works tab and `howItWorks.ts` quote the new numbers. Settings show the two new rows under "Ladder safety".
- **To switch staging to the ladder (Marcelo picks the tickers, after his backtesting):**
  1. Marcelo picks the tickers.
  2. Copy each ticker's lab values (shares, rungs, shock, delta range) into its Pluto ticker settings.
  3. Set each allocation to the ticker's lab pot ÷ the total of the pots, and the capital budget to about the total of the pots ÷ the account value. Show Marcelo a before/after first. The budget is shared with Edge Margin, so it changes only at the switch.
  4. Re-check shares, rungs and shock min against the lab: the two never sync, so a later lab change won't reach staging.
  5. Switch only once Edge Margin's open trades have closed (on 2026-10-21: 10 Covered Calls, 3 Cash-Secured Puts, 1 hedge). Pluto must be paused to switch.
- **Open:** the backtest's replay of Pluto still rounds limits to the nearest cent. Ladder actions recorded before this change carry no `saleStep`, so their Puts are checked as ladder Puts (staging has none yet).
- **Verified:** tsc and the touched test files pass. After the cent rounding, `src/pluto` and `src/lib`: 2,941/2,942, and the one failure is a date test timing out under backtest CPU load. Migration applied to the dev and test DBs. /ui-ux: Settings and How it works at 1706/1440/390 px in both themes, contrast passes, no overflow.

## Steady Stake: a new backtest strategy that beats the CC + Put Ladder (2026-10-21; backtest only, formulas awaiting Marcelo's sign-off; committed locally, not pushed; one migration)
Plain language: a third decision maker for the Backtesting lab, next to Edge Margin and the CC + Put Ladder. Each morning at 10:00 it keeps a steady share of each ticker's pot in the stock (80%, traded back once off by more than 5 points of the pot) and, on a day the ticker has options expiring, sells Calls on the shares and Puts with the spare cash at about |delta| 0.20, both ending at that day's close. On all 37 tickers the ladder can run (2020 → Oct 2026, same pots and fills), it passes Marcelo's bar: median CAGR 17.8% against the ladder's 10.7% (higher on 31 of 37), median Excess Calmar 0.46 against 0.31, above buy and hold's on 33 of 37. The price is a deeper Max drawdown (median −55% against −33%). Most of the edge is the steady stake itself; the expiry-day options add on liquid or rich-premium tickers. Report: https://claude.ai/artifact/LftjcArCiqMwGGyYkJi6zy. Screen mockup (awaiting approval): https://claude.ai/artifact/4oLTnmM2UGeaRw98fHw371.
- **Technical:** decision maker `steady_stake` (`src/backtest/engine/backtestSteadyStakeLook.ts`, dispatched in `runBacktestSession.ts` at the 10:00 look only), settings `steadyStake` (`BacktestSteadyStakeSettings`, defaults stake 80, band 5, Calls/Puts 100% at |delta| 0.20 ± 0.05, max spread 50%), ledger event `buyShares` and action kind `buy_shares` (migration `20261021300001`). API commits `ed71d06`, `f19236d`, `791f32e`.
- **Open:** Marcelo's sign-off on the formulas and values (70% is the lower-drawdown alternative; both pass); the Backtesting screen (mockup above) after approval; Pluto live not started. Research files: `tmp/new-strategy-research/` (NOTES.md has the bar and the pre-registered stake choice).
- **Verified:** Steady Stake unit tests, session-request and route tests; a stored 22-ticker ladder session (30d61cc5) replays identical on every row with these changes.

## Vol Farmer: a new backtest strategy, shares + Covered Call + long Put hedge (Marcelo 2026-10-23; backtest only; on staging, API 3583612 + 2525e61; left as it is since 2026-10-27)
Plain language: a fourth decision maker for the Backtesting lab. A cycle buys the shares, sells the Call with the highest annualized yield (|delta| 0.05–0.30, 1–45 DTE) and buys a Put that protects the shares. Of the Puts that leave the shares and Put not at a loss if the stock reaches the Call's strike by the Call's expiry, it takes the one that protects most against a 20% fall. When the stock reaches the Call's strike, everything is unwound (shares sold, Call bought back, Put sold) and the next cycle starts the next day. A Call that expires below its strike is followed by the next one, the Put kept. 10% of the cash is kept for later Puts. Lots that get no Call and Put are sold only above what they cost. Five tickers ran at $100k and at $50k: sessions "Vol Farmer · <ticker> · $100k/$50k · defaults".
- **Technical:** decision maker `vol_farmer` (`src/backtest/engine/backtestVolFarmerLook.ts`; every look re-reads the open legs' IVs, the 10:00 look opens), settings `volFarmer` (`BacktestVolFarmerSettings`), crossing scans in `runBacktestSession.ts` before each look and to the close, from the 30-minute bars' open/high (`FileIntradayPrices.barsOn`). Crossing fills are Black-Scholes values at the strike ("perfect limit orders"), to be made realistic later. Formulas: `docs/backtesting/FORMULAS.md`, Vol Farmer. No migration.
- **Open:** a Put is bought only with a Call that passes the hedge rule, so after a fall (shares' cost far above the price) the shares go unhedged until the price comes back: 36–61% of the days with shares on HOOD, INTC, CSCO and COIN, which is what drove INTC's and COIN's −53%/−55% Max drawdown. How to keep the shares hedged while no Call qualifies is Marcelo's call. Realistic crossing fills.
- **Variants tried (2026-10-23, sessions "Vol Farmer · <ticker> · $100k · Put min 30/60/90 DTE" and "· Put strike ≥5%/≥10% below"):** longer Puts (min 60 DTE) cut the Max drawdown on all five but cost CAGR on four; Puts struck below the price cost less and raised CAGR on MU and CSCO but deepened the drawdowns on HOOD, MU and INTC. In every run the shares made most of the profit; the Calls net little after the buybacks at the crossings. The Put strike setting now takes −20 to 10.
- **Hedge choice, COIN #1's window (2026-10-24, sessions "Vol Farmer · COIN #1 window · …"):** a held Put never lowers the break-even (rule A); only realized Put P&L does. A Put expiring with the Call (the defaults' own pick there) cashes in a fall at the Call's expiry, so the next Calls sit near the price and far more premium is sold, but buybacks at crossings took back about as much as was sold (near-the-money Calls of a few days); long Puts held through the fall left only far-OTM Calls.
- **Research round vs the ladder (2026-10-24, sessions "VF research · …", files in `tmp/vol-farmer-research/`, dashboard https://claude.ai/artifact/QL6sGLmGU2NiRe1PXdv1RK):** 64 data files screened for option liquidity (Call spread ≤ 12.5% of mid, volume fits on 90% of days, a whole lot on 85%, two years of data): 21 suitable, split 14 tuning / 7 check (AAPL, AMD, GLD, NFLX, QQQ, SPY, TSLA); $100k per ticker, the ladder re-run at 30/50/70% on today's engine. Yardstick: Excess Calmar against the ladder at 50% shares. Holding Calls to expiry and Puts expiring with the Call both lost: the crossing's buyback is the price of resetting the cap, and the shares' gains it keeps outweigh it. Candidate VF3G (Puts ≥ 60 DTE, `putRoll` when_no_call, `hedgeWithoutCall` put): better on 10 of 14 tuning tickers and 4 of 7 check tickers, median Excess Calmar 0.40 vs the ladder's 0.39 there: the pass mark set beforehand, met with no margin; it loses on the low-volatility names (AAPL, GLD, SPY). Six research settings added to `volFarmer` (FORMULAS.md, not signed off), defaults unchanged (repeat runs identical). Per-ticker table since 2026-10-25: each run's CAGR split into stock / Calls / hedge / Puts sold / interest points (`pnl_breakdown_by_to.py`; Stock is the rest, since a position record merges later-assigned shares into one leg); at 16 Oct 2026 VF3G's Calls give a median +0.5 pts (below 0 on 9 of 21 tickers), its hedge −5.2, the shares +11.9; the ladder's Calls +7.1, below 0 on none.
- **VF4, Marcelo's five rules (signed off 2026-10-26; sessions "VF research · R4 VF4 (rules 1–5, Puts 90+ days) · <ticker> · $100k", COIN, NVDA, AAPL, HOOD):** Calls only on the next expiry; a new Call needs shares + Put to be at or above zero at its strike, with the cycle's realized share and Put P&L counted; at the crossing the position is unwound only if the whole cycle (Calls included) nets a gain, else held and checked again at every look; a Put in profit after a Call expires is sold, but only with a new Call and Put; shares called away under water are bought back and the Put kept. Puts of 90+ days. Five research settings (`callExpiry` next, `crossing` unwind_if_net_positive, `hedgeAfterExpiry`, `afterAssignment`, `hedgeRuleStockPnl`; FORMULAS.md), defaults unchanged (VF0 and VF3G re-runs on HOOD identical action by action). Excess Calmar −0.01 / 0.67 / 0.69 / 0.19 (COIN / NVDA / AAPL / HOOD) against the ladder at 50%'s 0.05 / 0.73 / 0.73 / 0.14 and VF0's 0.01 / 0.90 / 0.63 / 0.41: better than the ladder on HOOD only. Rule 4 never fired (no assignment: every Call that ended in the money had crossed and been unwound) and rule 2 held 10 of 282 crossings, all unwound at a later look. The cost is rules 5 + 1: next-expiry strikes sit near the price, so under-water shares get no Call that passes (a Call on 19–64% of days vs VF0's 47–93%, each Call counted from its sale to its expiry or buy-back, both days included), and every day of shares without a Put (COIN 46%, NVDA 16%, AAPL 11%, HOOD 65% of days) began at a Put's own expiry, which no rule covers: a new Put needs a Call that passes rule 1, and with the shares under water rule 1 needed a strike 8–237% above the price (HOOD: 1,200 shares at $57.81, the stock at $11.98 after the $37 Put expired on 21 Jan 2022, unhedged for 705 trading days). The rule-3 rolls step the Put down as the stock falls (median protection 11–43% of a 20% fall vs 49–71% at the other Put purchases), so the Put that expires is often far below the cost (HOOD's last: a $45 Put under shares at $147.39). No hold-out: all four tickers were seen in the research. A held crossing now shows in the day's summary.
- **R5, cheaper hedges on VF4 (Marcelo 2026-10-26; sessions "VF research · R5 VF4 + cheapest Put per year, floor 30%/25%, Puts 90-540 days · <ticker> · $100k", COIN, NVDA, AAPL, HOOD):** the Put is the cheapest per year among those protecting at least 30% (or 25%) of a 20% fall, the rule-3 rolls included, so a roll cannot step the hedge below the floor; Puts of 90–540 days; shares with no Put and no Call buy that Put without rule 1 (`putChoice` and `hedgeWithoutCall` cheapest_per_year, `minPutProtectionPct`; FORMULAS.md). Shares now go without a Put on 0% of days (VF4: 11–65%) but carry a Call on fewer: 17–60% of days (VF4: 19–64%); the Puts' median cost per year is 4–19% at the 30% floor (VF4: 15–40%), struck 12–17% below the price on average (VF4: 6–16%). Excess Calmar at 30%: −0.02 / 0.92 / 0.58 / 0.26 (COIN / NVDA / AAPL / HOOD) vs VF4's −0.01 / 0.67 / 0.69 / 0.19 and VF0's 0.01 / 0.90 / 0.63 / 0.41; medians 0.42 (25% floor: 0.46), VF4 0.43, VF0 0.52, ladder at 50% 0.43. Max drawdown shallower than VF4's on COIN, NVDA and HOOD (−24.4% / −31.2% / −30.6% vs −41.4% / −38.8% / −38.8%), AAPL −20.3% vs −18.0%. Cheaper Puts did not lose less: Put P&L −169k / −49k / −23k on NVDA / AAPL / HOOD (VF4: −153k / −31k / −11k) against Calls' +33k / +6k / +8k; COIN's Puts +7k (VF4: +43k). A rule "Put cost per year below the Call's yield" would have blocked 3 of the 318 Put purchases made with a Call (quoted Call yields, medians 33–106% a year). No hold-out.
- **R6, the hedge cash-in on R5 (Marcelo 2026-10-26; sessions "VF research · R6 A: R5 floor 30% + cash-in / B: floor 50% + cash-in / B: floor 70% + cash-in / C: floor 50% + cash-in, its new Put at 30% · <ticker> · $100k", AAPL, COIN, HOOD, NVDA):** at a 10:00 look that sells no Call, a Put that would sell at a gain is sold so the gain lowers the shares' breakeven in the hedge rule, and a new Put is bought at the same look (`putCashIn` "when_no_call", `cashInPutMinProtectionPct`; FORMULAS.md, Hedge cash-in). Decision notes now say "the cycle's Puts sold or expired" and give the breakeven the hedge rule reads. Excess Calmar (AAPL / COIN / HOOD / NVDA): R5 0.58 / −0.02 / 0.26 / 0.92; A 0.74 / 0.00 / 0.19 / 0.80; B 50% 0.84 / 0.16 / 0.20 / 0.82; C 0.80 / 0.13 / 0.19 / 0.72; medians R5 0.42, A 0.47, B 50% 0.51, C 0.45 (VF0 0.52). B 70% never opened on COIN, HOOD or NVDA: no Put at or below the price offers 70% there. Days with a Call rose little: 42/18/17/60% (R5) to 51/24/21/60% (B 50%). The cash-in banks the gains R5 gave back (hedge P&L at the 30% floor: −32k / +12k / −12k / −136k vs R5's −49k / +7k / −23k / −169k) but sells at the first profit and re-hedges lower on every dip, so in the 2022 crashes the Puts earned less (COIN +11.5% of the peak vs R5 +19.8%) and Max drawdown deepened on COIN, HOOD and NVDA (A −37.5% / −41.9% / −36.5% vs −24.4% / −30.6% / −31.2%). At 50% the Put after a cash-in was often not on offer: HOOD's shares went without a Put on 139 of the 151 days of its −50.3% drawdown; C (new Put at 30%) leaves none. NVDA's Calls turned negative at 50% (−18k vs +33k: lower strikes bought back in rallies). No hold-out. Default path re-run identical (R5 30% AAPL and HOOD, every row).
- **R7, the same strategy without the hedge (Marcelo 2026-10-27; sessions "VF research · R7 no hedge (R5 without Puts, 10% cash kept) / no hedge, all cash in shares · <ticker> · $100k"):** `hedge` "none" (research setting; FORMULAS.md, No hedge): no Put, a Call passes rule 1 on the shares alone. What the hedge made in all (hedged P&L − unhedged with 10% kept, AAPL / COIN / HOOD / NVDA): R5 −123k / +43k / +8k / −983k; A −78k / +48k / +10k / −974k; B 50% −129k / +80k / +27k / −1,133k; C −93k / +77k / +0k / −1,116k. Most of it is the shares (the cash the Puts take, compounded); the Calls the hedge made possible less those it blocked add −49k to +7k. Unhedged Max drawdown −26.7% / −56.9% / −71.4% / −55.6% vs −13.6% to −50.3% hedged; Excess Calmar medians: unhedged 0.40 (all in 0.41), R5 0.42, A 0.47, B 50% 0.51, C 0.45. Unhedged COIN lost money (CAGR −5.3%). The Calls were 1–6% of the P&L on AAPL and NVDA in every run. No hold-out. Default path re-run identical (R6 A AAPL and HOOD).
- **Wheel cycles table, Vol Farmer B-E (Marcelo 2026-10-27):** in a Vol Farmer session the B-E column is the breakeven the hedge rule reads (the hedge Puts' realized P&L in, the Calls' premium only where the session's hedge rule counts it; `cycleCashBreakEven` `optionPremiumsIncluded`), so a Call's row shows the floor it passed: AAPL 11 Aug 2025 $240 Call, 220.96 as its decision note (was 253.12).
- **Wheel cycles table (app, Marcelo 2026-10-23):** a moment's rows read shares, then Calls and other options, then the hedge; an order's description starts with a Buy, Sell or Buy back badge (green, red; 2026-10-24, no badge on hand-overs, assignments, marks or expiries); a share purchase shows its price in B-E; a crossing's rows take the assigned rows' gold frame (class renamed `backtest-cycle-highlighted-row`), flagged by the API from the automatic `vol_farmer_unwind` actions at the row's instant; in a Vol Farmer session the hedge is marked at every hand-over of the shares, each period's change in its net premium (`src/backtest/cycleHedgeHandOvers.ts`, read from the data file's end-of-day quotes; FORMULAS.md). A hedge Put's purchase row shows its yearly cost beside the Calls' yield (Marcelo 2026-10-26): (time value × 100 + commission) ÷ (stock price × 100) ÷ (days ÷ 365) as a negative yield, e.g. "$55 Put · 19 Nov (105DTE) · 12× @ 16.30 · -98.1% a year" (FORMULAS.md). Results write-up: https://claude.ai/artifact/JMVDmFeiudS2NGs4Nc9ReY.
- **Verified:** unit tests (pricing, hedge rule, protection, Put choice, crossing); the whole backtest suite with its DB tests (`src/backtest` + `src/routes/backtest.test.ts`, 555/555); tsc for both repos; smoke sessions on HOOD 2024 Q1 read action by action; /ui-ux of the Lab settings' Vol Farmer section at 1706/1440/390 px in both themes.

## Vol Farmer v2: two Covered Calls at different levels and a LEAPS Call (Marcelo 2026-10-27; backtest research only; not pushed)
Plain language: a fifth decision maker for the Backtesting lab, Marcelo's idea for farming option premium after a big fall. Half the cash buys shares and sells Calls at or above what the shares cost net of the premiums collected. When those Calls pay under 5% a year, the other half sells Puts struck at least 15% below that level; an assigned Put's shares become a second Covered Call position with its own, lower break-even. When even the second position finds no Call at or above its break-even, the free cash buys a LEAPS Call (a year or more out, |delta| 0.70), sold when the second position is called away. No protective Put.
- **Technical:** decision maker `vol_farmer_v2` (`src/backtest/engine/backtestVolFarmerV2Look.ts`, 10:00 look only), settings `volFarmerV2` (group "Vol Farmer v2"; the Calls read `volFarmer`'s |delta| range and spread limit); the LEAPS from the data file's 10:00 Call quotes (`loadCallExpiries`, `loadCallQuotesOfExpiry`), marked daily and settled at intrinsic value at its expiry. Formulas: `docs/backtesting/FORMULAS.md`, Vol Farmer v2. No migration.
- **Verified:** unit tests (break-evens, the Put strike cap, the LEAPS choice, a two-position scenario, merged Call orders); the backtest suite (608/608); tsc; a smoke session (AAPL 2022, $100k) read order by order; Vol Farmer, ladder-hedge and plain sessions unchanged (full-row diff against HEAD).
- **LEAPS, not hedge (Marcelo 2026-10-27):** Hedge means protective Puts and every long Call is a LEAPS. Backtest side built (committed, not pushed): a strategy of its own (`leaps`, orders `buy_leaps`/`sell_leaps`, migration `20261027100001_backtest_leaps`), its own Wheel cycles bucket (`src/lib/cycles.ts`, for legs of LEAPS positions only), a LEAPS part in the session tiles' API, capital = premium paid (positionSelectors and the positions SQL), and the purchase capped at the contracts its 10:00 ask offered. Verified: the backtest, cycles, positions and dashboard tests (867); Vol Farmer, ladder-hedge and plain sessions identical apart from the cycles' zero LEAPS bucket; a smoke session (AAPL 2022 to mid-2023) read order by order.
- **LEAPS live (Marcelo 2026-10-27; built, not committed or pushed).** Plain language: the TLT long Call now shows as LEAPS everywhere, in its own gold colour (dark text, chosen on the mockup), and Hedge means protective Puts only. Any strategy, tile, legend item, chart series or scoreboard row at $0 is no longer shown (N/S included), and the Dashboard's By Strategy donut now uses the strategy colours. **Technical:** migration `20261027200001_relabel_long_call_positions_as_leaps` (positions whose every leg is a long Call: `hedge` → `leaps`); the worker's reconcile files a long Call as `leaps`, a long Put as `hedge` (`ibkrGatewayReconcilePositions.ts`, so a push redeploys the worker); `leaps` in the dashboard, positions, scoreboard, blotter and Genosuke routes, the cycle loader and the option-data universe; LEAPS outside Pluto's book (`strategiesOutsidePlutoBook`). App: `--iorio-leaps` / `.bg-leaps` (`theme.css`), Pulse's strategy column 44 px on desktop and phone, `showsAsZeroDollars` (`formatters.ts`), `strategyColorVariable` (`positionPnl.ts`). Verified: API tests for the touched routes and libraries; app tsc and oxlint; Dashboard, Positions, scoreboard, blotter and Pulse on screen at 1706/1440/390 in both themes.
- **Stage 1 (2026-10-27, 108 sessions):** Step {5, 10, 20, 30}% × Put |delta| {0.10, 0.20, 0.30} × $100k/$200k on AAPL, COIN, HOOD and NVDA, no hold-out ([Vol Farmer v2 · stage 1](https://claude.ai/artifact/Wbi7VPmKozb1jeBGkQPyfY)). v2 led on Excess Calmar only on AAPL; its Max drawdown stayed near buy and hold on COIN, HOOD and NVDA. The Step barely mattered: Puts started whenever no Call at or above B-E existed on the next expiry, 23% of them while a Call was still open.
- **Rule A (Marcelo 2026-10-27):** the Puts' trigger is checked only at a look where the first position needs a new Call; the stage-1 grid ran before it. Names settled (stored settings migrated): "Puts start under" (`putsStartCallYieldPct`) is the Call yield under which Puts start, "Step" (`stepBelowFirstBreakEvenPct`) how far below B-E1 a Put is struck, Put |delta| (`putMaxAbsDelta`).
- **Session page (2026-10-27):** the price chart draws B-E and B-E2 (recorded per look, `backtest_break_even_levels`) and the LEAPS bought ▲, sold ▼ and expired ✕; the 50- and 200-day averages are gone from it. Wheel cycles show each sale's position break-even, B-E¹ or B-E² (recorded with the sale). Sessions run before then show neither line nor superscript.
- **Step rule (Marcelo 2026-10-27; pushed to staging 2026-10-27).** Plain language: the Step follows each stock's own volatility instead of a fixed %: the typical one-month move its options price in, IV × √(21 ÷ 252) (IV 30% gives 8.7%, IV 90% gives 26%), measured at each Put sale. The lab uses it since 2026-10-27; the code default stays fixed, so sessions run before then still read as fixed. **Technical:** `volFarmerV2.stepRule` (`fixed`, `implied_move_at_put_sale`), IBKR's 30-day IV of the latest daily bar before the day (within 10 sessions, else the fixed Step); each Put records `stepPct` and `stepImpliedVolatility`. **Verified:** unit and route tests; a fixed-Step NVDA session re-ran identical row for row. **Test** (Put |delta| 0.30, $200k, no hold-out; AAPL, NVDA, MSTR, AAOI, CVNA, SMCI): measured at each Put sale, 21.4% average CAGR, against 19.5% for the best single fixed Step (25%) and 22.6% for each ticker's best Step in hindsight; measured at the cycle's open (since removed), 19.2%. On the volatile tickers the LEAPS' timing moved CAGR more than the Step did.
- **Research set (Marcelo 2026-10-27):** AAOI excluded: its short-dated Calls had no usable 10:00 bid on many days in every year (22% of its days without a Call), so it tests the option market more than the strategy. The set is AAPL, NVDA, MSTR, CVNA and SMCI.
- **Wheel cycles show each Put's Step and why no Call (2026-10-27):** a v2 Put row's contract details end with "· Step 26.9%" (moved there from the B-E cell, Marcelo 2026-10-27; built, not committed), its tooltip the IV × √(21 ÷ 252) and the strike cap (`putStep` on the cycle timeline rows, from the Put's `candidateScores`); sessions run before the Step rule show none. v2's No Call rows give their reasons, "no Call at or above B-E¹ ($14.20)", from the day notes (`volFarmerV2NoteOn`, `volFarmerV2RunReasons` in `cycleNoCallDays.ts`); they read "no reason recorded" before, as the cycle reader took the notes of Vol Farmer v1 sessions only.
- **LEAPS size (Marcelo 2026-10-27; pushed to staging 2026-10-27; the lab's cap is 10% since 2026-10-27).** Plain language: a LEAPS used to spend whatever cash had piled up, from 3% to 56% of the account at a purchase; it can now be capped at a share of the account. **Technical:** `volFarmerV2.leapsMaxAccountPct` (default 0, no cap): the LEAPS spends min(free cash, cap % × the account's value at the look, positions marked); cap 0 re-ran an MSTR session identical row for row. A reserve carved from the Puts' half was tested at 5% and dropped. **Test** ($100k, lab defaults, six tickers, no hold-out; average CAGR · Max drawdown · Excess Calmar): spare cash 21.3% · −66.2% · 0.36; reserve 5% 20.9% · −49.8% · 0.42; cap 10% 20.7% · −58.5% · 0.38. The cap cut MSTR's LEAPS loss (its LEAPS part of CAGR −10.9 → −3.2 points) and AAOI's LEAPS gain (7.3 → 1.7).
- **VF v2 against the Ladder and buy and hold, 58 tickers (2026-10-27; report https://claude.ai/artifact/UbVPaZqWNVNYy2XphCabA2).** Plain language: over each ticker's years of steady weekly options, VF v2 earned more than the Ladder on 40 of 58 tickers but had the deeper Max drawdown on 53; Excess Calmar was a draw (VF v2 ahead on 27). Buy and hold earned the most and had the deepest Max drawdown. **Medians** (VF v2 · Ladder · buy and hold): CAGR 13.3% · 11.3% · 16.2%; Max drawdown −46.2% · −34.0% · −63.5%; Excess Calmar 0.24 · 0.25 · 0.25. **Setup:** lab values (the Ladder with its per-ticker lab values); window from the first month after which weeklies traded on ≥ 90% of every month's days, 2+ years (17 tickers left out, 15 start after their data); pot $100k, raised for 42 tickers (up to $3.2M) so every Ladder rung covers a Put contract at the highest price traded, the same pot for all three; no hold-out. Free cash (cash not securing an open Put ÷ the account, mean of the days; Marcelo 2026-10-27, replaces Capital in use in the report): 34.7% · 38.5% · 0%.
- **VF v2 as one $100k-a-ticker book (2026-10-27).** Plain language: 24 tickers that fit $100k and had steady weeklies from 2 Jan 2020, each in its own $100k account, added up ($2.4M): VF v2 matched buy and hold of the same tickers on return with a shallower Max drawdown, and trailed SPY held with the same money. **Figures** (VF v2 book · buy and hold, same tickers · SPY; 2020-01-02 → 2026-10-23): CAGR 12.2% · 12.3% · 13.3%; Max drawdown −34.3% · −43.7% · −32.7%; Excess Calmar 0.27 · 0.21 · 0.32; Sortino 1.12 · 0.81 · 1.09; free cash 35.3%. **Setup:** formula F1 (batchCombined.ts); a ticker fits when its highest as-traded price in the window is ≤ $500 (one contract a half), a rule that uses hindsight and leaves out the tickers that rallied past $500.
- **VF v2 book on one shared account (2026-10-28; built, not pushed).** Plain language: the 24 tickers can share one account. Even with no extra money committed, that fell much less than separate accounts did, because no ticker grew into a large part of the book. Pots below $2.4M raise the return on the money used, rarely run short of cash, and give a deeper Max drawdown and less return for the risk. **Figures** (24 tickers, a $100k ticker pot each, 2020-01-02 → 2026-10-23, current VF v2 with LEAPS top-ups): separate $100k accounts (F1) CAGR 12.3%, Max drawdown −34.7%, Excess Calmar 0.27, Sortino 1.11, free cash 35%; shared $2.4M 11.4% · −20.8% · 0.41 · 1.34 · 47%; $2.0M 12.8% · −27.3% · 0.36 · 1.22, no cut; $1.9M 13.5% · −28.4% · 0.37 · 1.23, no cut; $1.8M 13.3% · −29.8% · 0.35 · 1.16, Puts cut on 3 days (Mar 2020); $1.7M 13.5% · −32.0% · 0.33, 48 days; $1.6M 14.1% · −32.6% · 0.34, 112 days; SPY 13.3% · −32.7% · 0.32 · 1.09. On the same $2.4M (the cut cash in T-bills) every smaller pot makes 11.0–11.7% with a −24 to −25% Max drawdown. Why the shared book fell less: in the separate accounts CVNA, PTON and TQQQ had grown to 24.4% of the book by 2021-11-04 and caused 57% of its fall to 2022-10-13. **Technical:** settings `volFarmerV2.tickerAllocationPct` (each ticker's set %, the rest an equal share, as Pluto's allocations) and `volFarmerV2.excessAllowancePct` (w = allocation × (1 + allowance), `src/lib/tickerAllocation.ts`, shared with Pluto; they replaced the first `tickerPotDollars`, so the 6 "Shared pot" sessions can't be run again as they are: the $1.9M one is 26.3158% on an equal share); `runVolFarmerV2Turn`, a lot cap at the free cash and a cash-cut hook in `volFarmerV2Steps.ts` (Pluto uses them too: "Shared pool" under Vol Farmer v2 in Pluto); tickers take turns moved one place a day, LEAPS last; cuts in `backtest_look_shortfalls` (`entry`, `budget_put`). A one-ticker session is identical to main's engine (GDX, full-row diff). Local sessions "Shared pot · VF v2 · …" (6 pots, 24 single runs).
- **LEAPS choice and entry (Marcelo 2026-10-28; branch `vfv2-experiment`, not pushed).** Plain language: a shorter, slightly cheaper LEAPS (6 months or more, |delta| 0.60) earned a little more than the current one (a year or more, 0.70) on the same tickers, with no better return for the risk; waiting to buy the LEAPS until the price is a Step below B-E2 bought half as many and earned less. **Setup:** 28 tickers that fit $100k with usable 10:00 Calls on 91%+ of days (highest as-traded close ≤ $500, 2+ years of steady weeklies), $100k each, each ticker's steady-weeklies start to 2026-10-26, current lab values, run from the branch's code ("Baseline · VF v2 · …" and "LEAPS … · VF v2 · …" sessions), no hold-out. **Figures** (change against the baseline, mean over 28 tickers): 180 days, |delta| 0.60: CAGR +0.35 pts (up on 17), Max drawdown 0.40 pts deeper, Excess Calmar −0.001, LEAPS P&L +$99k; Step entry: CAGR −0.53 pts (up on 5), Max drawdown 0.81 pts shallower, Excess Calmar −0.006, LEAPS P&L −$122k, 60 LEAPS against 112; both: CAGR −0.62 pts, Excess Calmar −0.009. Before the runs, revaluing the baseline's 112 LEAPS: selling at the first look at or above B-E2 would have made $154k against $276k held to the call-away (not run; on hold). **Technical:** `volFarmerV2.leapsEntryRule` (`no_call` default, `step_below_second_break_even`), the Step from `volFarmerV2StepAtLook` shared with the Puts; Pluto keeps `no_call`. Default path identical row for row (SLV, IWM, TQQQ baselines).
- **New lab LEAPS defaults: 180 days, |delta| 0.60 (Marcelo 2026-10-28; lab only, Pluto unchanged).** Selling the LEAPS when the price is back at B-E2 (with the Step entry) and buying another after such a sale were tested against it and both did worse. **Figures** (change against "LEAPS 180d Δ0.6", mean over 28 tickers): Step entry + B-E2 exit: CAGR −1.17 pts (up on 5), Max drawdown 0.88 pts shallower (on 16), Excess Calmar −0.012 (up on 7), LEAPS P&L −$252k; + re-buy: CAGR −1.28 pts, Excess Calmar −0.016, LEAPS P&L −$288k, 66 LEAPS against 60 without it. With 180 days, 21 of 113 LEAPS expired unsold (−$255k; 11 of 112 with 365 days). **Technical:** `volFarmerV2.leapsExitRule` (`second_called_away` default, `second_break_even_reached`, checked first in the look), `volFarmerV2.leapsRebuy` (`never` default, `after_break_even_sale`); Pluto keeps the defaults. Sessions "LEAPS Step entry + B-E2 exit · VF v2 · …" (and "+ re-buy").
- **When the LEAPS is bought (Marcelo 2026-10-28).** Plain language: the current rule buys the LEAPS whenever the second position finds no Call at or above B-E2, which a weekly Call worth under $0.05 already causes: 20 of 113 LEAPS were bought at the first look after the Put's assignment, 14 with the price above B-E2, median 7.1% below it. Two rules that wait until B-E2 is out of reach, both on data known at the look, bought 94 and 98 LEAPS, none above B-E2, median 9.7% and 9.3% below it, and did as well as the current rule apart from two lucky purchases above B-E2 (CVNA Mar 2020, IREN Jun 2025). **Figures** (change against "LEAPS 180d Δ0.6", mean over 28 tickers): half a Step below B-E2: CAGR −0.31 pts (median 0), Excess Calmar −0.002, LEAPS P&L −$56k; a Call at B-E2 under delta 0.05: CAGR −0.25 pts (median 0), Excess Calmar −0.002, LEAPS P&L −$48k; without CVNA and IREN both about even (CAGR +0.02 and +0.04 pts). Strike at B-E2 instead of |delta| 0.60, revaluing the same 113 purchases to their call-away: +2.7 ± 5.8 pts a purchase (better on 46), within noise like every strike from the price to 10% above B-E2; sold after a call-away every strike made 59–65%, expired or still open every strike lost 83–86%. **Technical:** `leapsEntryRule` `half_step_below_second_break_even`, `call_delta_at_second_break_even` (`volFarmerV2CallDeltaAtBreakEven`, IBKR's 30-day IV, none: no LEAPS). B-E2 found wrong where the second position's Puts vary in size (PCG shared pot: 5 Puts of 1 to 44 contracts, $645.94 over the 1-contract Put's 100 shares, B-E2 $5.54 for a $12 strike; per share about $11.61); in the 28-ticker 180-day runs, 75 of 306 Put assignments understated (15 by 1% or more, IREN Feb 2025 $6.95 against $10.51). B-E1 had the same flaw after a partial call-away.
- **B-E fix and no LEAPS at or above B-E2 (Marcelo 2026-10-28; branch `vfv2-experiment`).** Plain language: a position's break-even no longer counts premiums earned on more shares than it holds, and a LEAPS is never bought, topped up or re-bought with the price at or above B-E2, in the lab and in Pluto's shared rules. Every test result above used the old break-even and is to be re-run (26 tickers: CVNA and IREN left out, Marcelo). **Technical:** a position keeps one premium per option (`VolFarmerV2Premium`: leg, net dollars, shares covered); B-E = cost − Σ premium × min(1, shares held ÷ shares covered) ÷ shares held (`volFarmerV2BreakEven`); Pluto settles each option's premium to its fill (`settleVolFarmerV2Premium`) and reads a memory saved with a premium total as one premium counted in full (`volFarmerV2MemoryFromStored`). Verified: 548 tests; BE, BABA and WMT re-runs differ only where the break-even did (BE's partial call-away in Nov 2024: B-E1 $9.82 against $9.15). Before any merge: Pluto's How it works (app, `PlutoHowItWorksTab.tsx`) says a LEAPS is bought when no Call fits at or above B-E² and topped up at the next rounds; both need "with the price below B-E²".
- **LEAPS bought again after each sale: entry × exit grid (Marcelo 2026-10-28; research, tests at mid).** Plain language: buying another LEAPS whenever the entry holds again, with an entry set by the price and a sale on the partial recovery, did best; the current trigger (no Call that week) with a recovery sale did worst. **Figures** (26 tickers without CVNA and IREN, change against "B-E fix baseline", mean): delta entry + quarter-Step exit: CAGR +0.40 pts (better on 14), Excess Calmar +0.017, Max drawdown 2.1 pts deeper, nearly all NKE (−27 pts) and MRNA (−18); half-Step entry + B-E2 exit: CAGR +0.32 pts, Excess Calmar +0.010; "no Call" entry + quarter-Step exit: CAGR −0.43 pts. Selling deep-dip LEAPS sooner (a Step above the purchase, a trailing stop, part of the way back to B-E2) did worse in the simulator. **Technical:** `leapsEntryNeedsNoCall`, `leapsExitRule` `steps_below_second_break_even` with `leapsExitStepShare`, `leapsRebuy` `whenever_entry_holds`; Pluto keeps the defaults.
- **Split freeze fixed (Marcelo 2026-10-28; branch `vfv2-experiment`).** Plain language: a ticker that split 3-for-1 while a cycle held its shares (WMT Feb 2024, TSLA Aug 2022) placed no order for the rest of the session; a cycle now starts on a share scale every contract up to the session's end fits, so WMT trades through. Sessions run before this are wrong for WMT or TSLA after their split. **Technical:** `splitFactorForNewCycle` (splitFactors.ts), set daily per symbol (`ledger.newCycleSplitFactorBySymbol`) and read by `cycleSplitFactorFor` for a flat symbol; `ledger.cycleStarts` gives the records each cycle's scale; call-away and assignment notes and timeline labels read as traded on their day; "held across a split (×N)" now fires only for an option sold before the split. The baseline hedge's kept Puts still need the cycle on today's scale. Verified row by row: BAC, XLE and TQQQ identical apart from notes and labels (TQQQ cycles before 2021 now on scale 1/8).
- **Snapshots of zeros (Marcelo 2026-10-28).** Plain language: a day whose 10:00 option quotes are all zero is missing data, not "no bid": Vol Farmer v2 decides at that day's next look with quotes. 21 such days over the 26 tickers, mostly 30 Apr 2020, all decided at 10:30. **Technical:** `isZeroQuoteSnapshot` in runBacktestSession's look loop, Vol Farmer v2 only.
- **Calls and Puts on a later expiry (Marcelo 2026-10-28; research, against "Fixed baseline").** Plain language: letting a Call or Put use a later expiry when the next one has none lowered returns on most tickers: a 2–6-week Call takes the place of several weekly ones (less premium), and the first position finds a Call yielding 5% more often, so fewer Puts and LEAPS. Tickers with thin weeklies (EWZ, RIVN) gained, since the next expiry often had nothing to sell. **Figures** (26 tickers, mean change): up to 14 days: CAGR −0.66 pts (median −0.7, better on 8), Max drawdown 0.75 pts shallower, Excess Calmar +0.011; up to 45 days: CAGR −1.29 pts (median −1.3, better on 9), Max drawdown 6.1 pts shallower, Excess Calmar +0.019. Call premium $1.97M → $1.84M → $1.55M, Put premium $0.94M → $0.74M → $0.48M, LEAPS 99 → 54 → 28. Decided (Marcelo 2026-10-28): keep the next expiry only. **Technical:** `volFarmerV2.laterExpiryMaxDte` (0 default; Pluto keeps 0).
- **LEAPS fill about two-thirds across the spread (Marcelo 2026-10-28; lab only).** Plain language: the lab buys a LEAPS about two-thirds of the way from the bid to the ask and sells it about two-thirds of the way down from the ask (bid $10, ask $13: bought at $11.995, sold at $11.005), and values a held LEAPS at what selling it would bring; Calls and Puts still fill at the mid. **Technical:** `volFarmerV2.leapsSpreadCostChargedPct` (default 33, set by Marcelo 2026-10-28 in place of 33⅓ so the input shows it exactly: the share of the half-spread given up, $11.995 and $11.005 in the example; Pluto 0), applied to purchases, top-ups and sales, and to every mark of a `leaps` position (`markOfTwoSidedQuote`: 10:00, long-dated, later in the day, at purchase). Verified on a MRNA session: 11 purchases and 3 sales on the rule; on 1,147 days with the LEAPS the only option held, the day's value less cash and shares matched the LEAPS at bid + ⅓ of the spread within $0.01.
- **LEAPS grid re-run at the 2/3 fill (Marcelo 2026-10-28; research).** Plain language: with LEAPS filled two-thirds across the spread, no way of buying LEAPS again after each sale beats the baseline (one LEAPS a holding, sold at the call-away) beyond noise; the best add about 0.2 pts of CAGR with Max drawdowns 2–3 pts deeper. What holds: an entry set by the price beats the "no Call" entry when the LEAPS is sold on a partial recovery. **Figures** (26 tickers, change against "2/3 fill · Fixed baseline", mean): delta entry + quarter-Step exit: CAGR +0.22 pts (standard error 0.66; −0.07 without COIN), Excess Calmar +0.003, Max drawdown 2.3 pts deeper (NKE −28, MRNA −18); half-Step + B-E2 exit: CAGR +0.18 pts; "no Call" + quarter-Step exit: −0.49 pts; the leader against "no Call" + quarter-Step exit: +0.71 ± 0.26 pts. The 2/3 fill itself cost the baseline 0.09 pts of CAGR (LEAPS P&L −$46k over the 26 tickers). Sessions "2/3 fill · … · VF v2 · …".
- **LEAPS entry a set number of Steps below B-E2 (Marcelo 2026-10-28; research).** Plain language: the delta entry buys at a distance that changes with the weekday (0.30 Step below B-E2 on Thursdays, 0.79 on Fridays); the same test on a fixed one-month horizon, k Steps below B-E2, does as well at k 0.5–0.65 and gives the same answer every day. Closer to B-E2 (0.35) trades too often: 341 LEAPS, and the spread cost turns it negative. **Figures** (2/3 fill, re-buy, change against "2/3 fill · Fixed baseline", mean over 26 tickers): with the B-E2 exit, k 0.35 / 0.5 / 0.65: CAGR −0.32 / +0.18 / +0.20 pts, Excess Calmar −0.013 / −0.002 / +0.008; with the quarter-Step exit: −0.19 / +0.10 / −0.09 pts; the delta entry +0.22 (quarter-Step exit) and −0.11 (B-E2 exit); standard errors about 0.65 pts, all within noise. **Technical:** `leapsEntryRule` `steps_below_second_break_even` with `leapsEntryStepShare` (k 0.5 reproduces the half-Step run order for order, MRNA).
- **Adopted (Marcelo 2026-10-28; branch `vfv2-experiment`, merged into main, not pushed).** Plain language: Vol Farmer v2 now buys its LEAPS once the price is half a Step below the second position's break-even, one per holding, sold when the second position is called away, in the lab and in Pluto. The research options are gone: later expiries, LEAPS sales on a recovery, buying again after a sale, the other entries. Kept from the day: the break-even fix, no LEAPS at or above B-E2, the split-freeze fix, zero-quote days, the LEAPS 2/3 fill. **Figures** (the confirming run, 26 tickers, 2/3 fill, against the old "no Call this week" trigger): CAGR +0.11 pts (standard error 0.27; better on 14, worse on 10), Excess Calmar −0.006, Max drawdown 0.9 pts deeper; purchases spread over the week instead of 80% on Mondays. **Technical:** `leapsEntry` in volFarmerV2Steps.ts, B-E2 × (1 − Step ÷ 200); settings `leapsEntryRule`, `leapsEntryStepShare`, `leapsEntryNeedsNoCall`, `leapsExitRule`, `leapsExitStepShare`, `leapsRebuy` and `laterExpiryMaxDte` removed (app: Run again notes a session that used them, session pages label them "retired"); Pluto's How it works quotes half a Step (`leapsEntryBelowSecondBreakEvenPct`). Verified: the lab's default run reproduces "2/3 fill · LEAPS half-Step · call-away · no re-buy" order for order on MRNA, NKE, TQQQ, COIN and WMT, only the purchase description's wording differing.
- **Put trigger and rule-blocked Calls (Marcelo 2026-10-29; looked at, open).** Plain language: 92 of 1,443 Put sales started because a rule blocked a first-position Call yielding 5% a year or more, mostly far out-of-the-money Calls under the |delta| floor. They started after real falls (a median 14.3% below B-E1, against 12.4% for the rest) and were assigned about as often. Unlike the old LEAPS delta rule, the trigger lands about one Step below B-E1 on every weekday. Left as is pending his call.
- **Next:** stage 2: LEAPS 180/365/540 days × |delta| 0.50/0.70/0.85.

## Vol Farmer v2 in Pluto (Marcelo 2026-10-27; staging only; screens built 2026-10-28 from the approved mockup; on staging since 2026-10-28, Pluto off)
Plain language: Pluto can now run Vol Farmer v2 as well as the CC + Put Ladder, one strategy per ticker, v2 by default. It uses the same rules as the lab, but looks at every round all day. A ticker already holding shares is adopted: every share is v2's first position at the cycle's break-even, its open Calls stay the first position's and its open Puts the second's; a ticker holding only Puts waits for them. A cycle's pot is the ticker's allocation; a LEAPS spends at most 10% of it, paid from Pluto's budget; one bought short of its count is topped up with the same contract. Pluto now buys and sells LEAPS, and they count in its budget. Both strategies share the ladder's caps and SPY stress level; under stress v2 opens nothing, but a LEAPS sale still goes.
- **Lab (same branch):** v2's rules moved to `src/backtest/engine/volFarmerV2Steps.ts`, shared by the backtest (`backtestVolFarmerV2Look.ts`, a book over the simulated ledger) and Pluto. New lab settings: Decides (`volFarmerV2.looks`, 10:00 only by default, or every look). A LEAPS cut short by its ask size is now topped up at the next looks (FORMULAS.md). **Verified:** SLV, NVDA (across the 2024 split) and COIN v2 sessions re-ran identical row for row after the move; SLV, whose four LEAPS were cut short, changes with the top-ups (CAGR 9.07% → 8.73%).
- **Pluto, technical:** `pluto_ticker_settings.strategy`, v2's settings in `pluto_settings` (seeded from the lab: Put |delta| 0.30, implied-move Step, LEAPS cap 10% of the allocation, spread limit 50%), memory per ticker in `pluto_vol_farmer_v2_cycles` (migration `20261027900001`). `volFarmerV2Round.ts` runs after the ladder's round; `volFarmerV2LiveBook.ts` holds adoption, the live book and the settling of orders sent at earlier rounds (an order counts as planned until done, then its fills replace the plan; an unfilled one is undone). LEAPS are quoted by a burst on the strikes whose |delta| is nearest 0.70; their strikes come from IBKR when not stored (`liveOptionChainLookups.ts`). Order gates: v2's Calls held to 0.05–0.30, Puts to ≤ 0.30, LEAPS to the book cap only. A strategy change forgets both strategies' memory of the ticker.
- **Verified:** unit and DB tests for the live book, loader, round, gates, route and pass runner; full API suite 5359/5361 (the 2 are `app.smoke.db.test.ts` session flakes).
- **Screens (app, same branch name; mockup approved 2026-10-28):** a Strategy select per ticker on the Tickers tab (a ticker Pluto may trade asks first), a Vol Farmer v2 settings group, a Vol Farmer v2 card on the Live tab (each ticker's positions, B-E¹/B-E², LEAPS, working orders and last round), and a v2 view on How it works. The card warns, from each ticker's last round (Marcelo 2026-10-28): when the ticker commits more than half its pot, since v2's Puts (half the pot) would be refused by the pot check; and when both positions hold shares, a LEAPS may still be bought and the free cash is under the LEAPS cap. The spread limit stays at 50% (Marcelo). Each round saves its notes and figures per ticker (migration `20261028100001`, `GET /pluto/vol-farmer-v2`).
- **Lab fix:** at every look, a day keeps its 10:00 note on why no Call was sold when a later look filled an order.
- **Verified (screens):** Playwright on Live, Tickers (switching COIN to the ladder and back, stored and cleared), Settings (save, read back, refusal) and How it works, at 1706/1440/390 px in both themes, against the branch DB with IBKR blocked; /ui-ux checklist.
- **Left:** a staging run with Pluto paused, to check adoption before resuming; merging `main` (the other session's no-Call reasons in `backtestTypes.ts`/`cycleNoCallDays.ts`, and this file) once that work is committed.
- **Shared pool and excess allowance (Marcelo 2026-10-28; built, not pushed).** Plain language: the tickers share Pluto's budget the way the lab's tickers share one account. Each ticker's allocation is a % of the budget (an equal share unless set; together 100%), and the new **excess allowance** raises every pot by the same share, so the pots can add up to more than the budget (the $1.9M run: 24 tickers on an equal share, 26.3158%, $100,000 pots on $1.9M). A new cycle buys only the lots the free cash pays for; the tickers take turns, alphabetical and one further each day; LEAPS wait until every ticker's other orders are sent; cuts show in the ticker's last-round notes. The lab works the same way (decided: one number for the pool, a % per ticker, no $ pot per ticker). **Technical:** pot = allocation × (1 + allowance) × budget × account (`tickerPotFraction`, every place a pot is computed: both rounds, the order gate's pot check, the Tickers tab's pot, How it works); `pluto_settings.excess_allowance_pct` (migration `20261028300001`, 0 by default, every Pluto ticker, CC + Put Ladder included); `PUT /pluto/allocations` (admin) saves the Tickers tab's allocations and the allowance together, all or nothing, one settings event per change; `volFarmerV2Round.ts` looks in two passes (`cycles_calls_puts`, then `leaps`). **Fixed:** both rounds counted their own orders twice in the free cash (as in-flight orders and again in `committedThisRound`); in-flight orders are now read once at the round's start. **Verified:** the $1.9M session re-run on an equal share with a 26.3158% allowance gives the original's trades, positions, fills and cycles exactly (its pots are 0.0000083% larger, since 26.3158% is rounded). **Screens** ([mockup](https://claude.ai/artifact/461Rr6mKsdYY6quwRypswE) approved 2026-10-28): the Tickers tab's Edit allocations (each ticker's allocation and the allowance typed in place, saved together, admin only), the only place an allocation is set (the ticker's Settings window shows it); the lab's Tickers card with Vol Farmer v2 deciding (allocations and allowance for the session; Run again turns an old session's ticker pot into the allowance that gives the same pots).
- **Option quote band (Marcelo 2026-10-28; built, not pushed; one migration).** Plain language: the live option quotes Pluto reads used the CC + Put Ladder's settings for every ticker. A v2 ticker now has only the expiry it sells quoted (the next one listed after today), with v2's own Call range, during the day and in the after-close volume capture, so a change to the ladder's settings no longer changes what v2 sees. A ticker enabled during the day has no option volume from the previous session, so it trades from the next session; its last round now says so instead of "no Call expiry listed after today". When Vol Farmer v2 takes over a ticker's holdings, Activity shows **Holdings adopted** (shares, break-even, Calls and Puts open) instead of a Warning. On the Live tab, the v2 card sits under Today's orders. **Technical:** "ladder band" renamed to "option quote band" (files, identifiers, `ladder_band_quotes` → `option_quote_band_quotes`, migration `20261028400001`; feed text "Option quotes"); `optionQuoteBandScopeOf` (per strategy: expiry rule and Call |delta| range) is shared by the planner and `runOptionSessionVolumeCapture.ts`; the planner rebuilds a ticker's day when its strategy changes; `volFarmerV2NoCallQuotedReason` gives the live reason (no previous-session volume, the 20% volume rule, quotes not in yet, outside the |delta| band); event `holdings_adopted`. `pluto_settings.execution_strategy` is dropped by the pending Signals migration (around 3 Nov), not separately. **Verified:** tsc; Pluto, lib, server and engine tests; migration up/down on the test DB; /ui-ux on the Live tab at 1706/1440/390 px in both themes, with staging's v2 data served to the local page.

## VF v2 analysis: replaces the Ladder's Analyse window (Marcelo 2026-10-29; designing, nothing built)
Plain language: the Analyse window on Backtesting → Data files is being rebuilt for Vol Farmer v2 from scratch; the CC + Put Ladder version is gone. It is to answer whether v2 can trade the ticker, whether it worked and whether that was luck, where the money came from, what the falls did, what v2 would do today, how big a pot fits, and how the ticker fits the book.
- **Decided:** sections: Claude's summary and verdict; tiles; the analysis backtest against buy and hold and SPY; each year; where the money came from; how it was held; falls and recoveries over all daily bars; can v2 trade it; size; if v2 started today; kept from the old window: price and drawdown, IV against realised, earnings, correlation, data quality, splits, the ticker's backtests. Dropped: shock, rungs, lot and rung fits, Put sales under the cap, too-large, days-to-expiry bands, chain map, per-setting recommendations. Formulas approved (into FORMULAS.md when built): weekly days and the steady-weeklies start, smallest pot, half invested, largest pot by Call volume, a fresh cycle's Call, upside given up at call-aways, falls of a Step, LEAPS round trip. Verdict thresholds set from the 26 tickers on the adopted rules before they are fixed. Backtest from the steady-weeklies start to the file's end, no hold-out; the lab's default $100k pot, with a pot-only trial; a lab save marks analyses out of date ("Re-run out of date" button, no automatic re-runs); Claude's Recommendation = verdict, suggested flag, pot range; the Data files table gets v2 columns.
- **Done (local DB):** the 194 Ladder analyses and their jobs deleted (the 171 "Analysis · …" sessions kept); all 34 flags back to Pending; the Ladder notes cleared (28; TECH keeps "In multi-year bear market."). v2's code defaults are now the lab's values (Put |delta| 0.30, implied-move Step, LEAPS 180 days at |delta| 0.60, LEAPS cap 10%; FORMULAS.md with them; committed to main 2026-10-29, not pushed), after writing the old values into the v2 sessions that lacked them (Step rule on 165, LEAPS cap on 217), so those read as they ran.
- **Under discussion:** a stock profile from all daily bars; ticker-specific v2 settings derived from it; a cycle review saying what happened in each cycle and why, with Claude's suggestions.
- **Next after this (Marcelo, parked):** a portfolio simulator in the lab: choose tickers and allocations, see how correlated they are and the range of CAGR and Max drawdown the book could have.

## Signals retired; the screen becomes Volatility (approved by Marcelo 2026-10-27; pushed to staging 2026-10-27 06:2x UTC, API 191b926 / app bf0dbe9; prod at promotion)
Plain language: the valuation model that graded contracts (Edge Margin, Edge $, Edge Yield, Hist Yield, grades, Kelly sizing) is gone, with everything that fed it: the 10:00 option-chain capture, the surface fit, Day Signals and its alerts. Pluto now runs only the CC + Put Ladder and no longer calls an AI model. The Signals screen is replaced by a Volatility screen: each ticker's implied volatility (IBKR's live 30-day figure, last close as fallback), the 21- and 63-day forecasts, IV Rank and IV percentile, and the best Call premium yields. Clicking a ticker opens a Ticker window with its option chain and order forms. Past rules and AI-model backtests still open; new ones can't be started.
- **Technical:** one market quote loop (`src/lib/marketQuoteLoop.ts`, consumers ladder → assignment risk → volatility, from 9:30 ET, the volatility consumer only while a screen asks) replaces the Day Signals loop; env `MARKET_QUOTE_LOOP_ENABLED` replaces `DAY_SIGNALS_LOOP_ENABLED`. Ladder forwards from put-call parity on the band quotes. Pluto: ladder-only pass runner, round trigger `band_quotes_update`, no model decisions/scoreboard model figures/sizing caps/OpenRouter check; `PlutoSettings` trimmed to the ladder's fields (`daySignalsPollSeconds` → `bandQuotePollSeconds`). Shortlist: no Signals switch, setup in 3 steps, readiness warns under 252 IV days, under 64 bars, or an unknown next earnings date. Backtests: `rules`/`llm` refused (400; resume 409), their engine and the Pluto model modules deleted. Migrations: drop `shortlist_entries_bot_requires_signals`; `pluto_settings.execution_strategy` → `cc_put_ladder`.
- **Commits:** API d2b88e8, 8ebb05c, ad7f11f, cfa1996, 0a87505, b06c398 (Volatility and ticker trading routes put behind the login guard, found by the smoke test), 05ae8e2, 191b926 (the unenforced `maxActionsPerSession` removed; the status card's Orders today counts against the ladder's daily cap); app c3f280c, 4fff6ef, d67c720, 195f996, 5b011a7, bf0dbe9.
- **Verified:** full API suite 5576/5578 (the 2 are `app.smoke.db.test.ts` session flakes that fail the same way on 5c42407, before this work); backtest re-runs identical (baseline ×6, Steady Stake, Vol Farmer ×2, Vol Farmer v2 ×2); Playwright on Volatility, Ticker window, Shortlist, Pluto (Live, History, Settings, How it works), Pulse Top premiums, Risk & Limits, Backtesting; desktop + phone, light + dark.
- **Release steps (staging):** done: Pluto was already off; `MARKET_QUOTE_LOOP_ENABLED=true` set (v243); pushed outside market hours; dev DB migrated. Left: unset `DAY_SIGNALS_LOOP_ENABLED` once the new release is live; Marcelo removes the 14:00/15:00 UTC `run-option-chain-capture-job` Scheduler entries (and any `run-option-surface-fit-job` one) before 14:00 UTC; next-morning checks (loop quoting from 9:30, ladder rounds, Volatility rows). Prod: the same steps at promotion.
- **Follow-ups:** (1) **done 2026-10-27** (API 9df7b14, app 61170a1): the dead Signals scoring code is deleted (candidates, live scoring, roadmap, roll scoring, tilt, uncompensated share, Day Signals store, capture retry and window, Pluto's model paths), the helpers still used moved to neutral modules (`optionContractKey.ts`, `optionFriction.ts`, `volatilityForecast.ts`, `optionChainExpiryRange.ts`), and the minimum annualised yield left Risk & Limits; two backtest sessions re-ran identical, full suite green. The volatility model tables stay (the Baseline wheel's valuation). (2) The table-drop migration is written and checked, waiting in `tmp/pending-migrations/` (README there) to be committed around 3 Nov.

## Screener: runs after the close, 20 symbols at a time; average option volume and spread removed (approved by Marcelo 2026-10-22; built and tested, committed locally with app 03901ba, not pushed; one migration)
Plain language: the nightly Screener scan had grown to over 30 minutes (it adds about 25 tickers a day and quoted them one at a time) and set off the "running too long" alert. It now runs after the market closes and quotes 20 tickers at a time: about 2 minutes. It also no longer collects two figures nobody used: average option volume (always blank) and the bid/ask spread (the Spread % column is gone from the Screener tab).
- **Technical:** the job's per-symbol enrichment runs through `runWithConcurrency` (`src/lib`) with up to 20 workers, as many as `loadAvailableMarketDataLines()` says are free at the start (at least 1). Each symbol still books its own line; `enrichCandidate` retries a refused line 3 times, 2 s apart. It requests generic ticks `101,106,165` and settles once last price, average share volume, IV and call/put open interest are in. `jobDeadlines.ts`: screener `maxRunMinutes` 15. Migration `20261022100001` drops `screener_universe.avg_option_volume` and `bid_ask_spread_pct`; `GET /screener` and the app's `ScreenerScanRow` drop both fields. `market_data_snapshots.avg_option_volume` (Price Performance) is unchanged. Not in the worker's code: a push does not redeploy the VPS worker.
- **Schedule:** staging's Heroku Scheduler entry is at 23:00 UTC (Marcelo, 2026-10-22), and `jobDeadlines.ts` expects it then; the 23:30 UTC watchdog sees it finished. Prod's entry stays at 18:00 UTC until the promotion; move it to 23:00 UTC with that release.
- **Deploy:** the migration runs in the release phase; don't push while a screener run is going.
- **Verified:** tsc (API and app), app lint; affected tests pass (enrichment incl. line retries, `runWithConcurrency`, outcome, deadlines, ops monitor, screener route, smoke); migration on the dev and test DBs; `GET /screener` returns neither field; /ui-ux on the Screener tab at 1706/1440/390 px in both themes. Full local run after the close (lines on for that command): 732 symbols in 2 min 9 s, 0 failed, 23 new rows with name and sector, no line reservation left; against staging's 14:00 values for 728 symbols, price median 0.4% apart (max 8.2%), IV median 0.4% (90% within ±6%).

## CC + Put Ladder hedge: long-dated Puts on the shares an entry brings in (2026-10-22; backtest only, formulas approved by Marcelo; built and tested, not committed, not pushed; one migration)
Plain language: the CC + Put Ladder can now buy insurance on its shares: about one year of Put options, one per 100 shares a Covered Call entry buys or a Cash-Secured Put entry's assignment brings in. It sells them when a ladder rung is assigned or the shares leave, and replaces them a week before they expire. On NFLX (2020 → Oct 2026, $500k pot), the hedge improved every one of 100 ladder settings tested: at-the-money Puts added a median 3.0 points of CAGR and cut the Max drawdown by 31 points. With the hedge, holding more shares pays; 80% shares, a 70% shock and 1 rung gave CAGR 15.7% and Max drawdown −13.5%, against the baseline's 11.2% and −47.5%. Buying at the ask and selling at the bid, it still beat the baseline under the same fills (12.4% against 8.2%). The Puts cost money in rising years and pay out in falls (2022, 2026), so one ticker with two crashes is a thin basis. On the 29 other tickers it did not carry over: the hedge cut the Max drawdown on every one but lowered CAGR on most, and no setting beat the ladder without it (median Excess Calmar 0.32 at best against 0.31). It helps tickers with deep falls (PTON, HOOD, AAOI, COIN, MRNA) and the index funds (SPY, QQQ), and hurts steady risers (AAPL, MU, NBIS, CSCO). Three rules to buy fewer Puts were added and tried: keeping them for the next entry halves the purchases but does not change that. The deepest falls came mostly from shares the ladder bought on the way down, which this hedge never covered, so since 2026-10-23 it can instead cover the whole position: the shares held plus the shares the open short Puts would bring, with 1-year Puts over three staggered expiries, kept until three months are left whatever the shares do. On PTON, SPY, MRNA, SMCI and MU it made the Max drawdown shallower on all five: PTON's −85.6% became −15.7% and MRNA's −70.4% became −23.4% with almost the same CAGR. But on the risers and high-volatility names it cost a lot (MU's CAGR fell from 28.0% to 13.8–17.9%), so risk-adjusted return improved on 3 of the 5 only. Cheaper versions were then tried on the same five (half the Puts, strikes by delta, and no purchase while Puts are too dear: dear against the stock's usual yearly fall, its usual IV, or its recent movement); none did clearly better, and skipping Puts when IV is high left SPY's 2020 fall unhedged. Not decided for live.
- **Technical:** settings `baseline.hedgePut*` and `hedgeInBreakEven` (group "Hedge", off at 0 days); `src/backtest/engine/backtestBaselineHedge.ts` (`BaselineHedgeSteps`, around the look's sales); ledger events `buyHedgePut`/`sellHedgePut`/`settleHedgePut`, strategy key `hedge`, action kinds `buy_hedge`/`sell_hedge` (migration `20261022200001`); Put quotes by expiry past 45 days from the symbol file; `run_statistics.hedge` totals and a run note; the Wheel cycles' break-even can include the hedge's realized P&L (`cycleCashBreakEven.ts`). App: HDG timeline rows, Buy/Sell hedge actions, Hedge group summary. FORMULAS.md "The hedge". Fewer purchases (`hedgePutTurnover`, `hedgePutTurnoverDays`, `hedgePutFarBelowPct`): kept Puts sit in a position of no cycle (`keptSince`), moved by `keepHedgePuts`/`takeKeptHedgePuts` at a price, no trade; `isSymbolFlat` and the cycles leave them out; timeline rows "Put kept for the next entry" / "Put from the kept ones" (optional row labels added to `src/lib/cycles.ts`, unset by live). Whole position (`hedgePutCovers` "position", default "entries"; `hedgePutStaggeredExpiries`, 3; roll limit raised to 120 days): no sale at a rung's assignment or a call-away, kept Puts across cycle ends, all sold before a split and bought again after it. Strike by delta (`hedgePutStrikeBy`, `hedgePutAbsDelta`) and the price gate (`hedgePutPriceGate`: yearly cost vs `hedgePutCostFallShare` × typical yearly fall, or IV vs `hedgePutIvMultiple` × median IV / 21-session realized volatility), all off by default.
- **Open:** the 7 hold-out tickers (GOOGL, MSFT, MSTR, ORCL, SOFI, GLD, XLE) are untouched, awaiting Marcelo's call; a next design could hedge by the ticker's own drawdown history (Marcelo's idea: the shock from it); live not started. Seen in the /ui-ux review, not from this change: N/S badge text 2.91:1, the Actions log's gates link 2.95:1 on dark, Lab settings selects cut off at 210 px.
- **Research:** `tmp/hedge-research/`; local batches "Hedge S1" (216 hedge settings on the baseline ladder), "Hedge S2" (100 ladder settings × no hedge and 4 hedges), "Hedge S3" (fills at the ask/bid), "Hedge S4 · <ticker>" (29 tickers + NFLX: baseline, hedge only, 3 NFLX candidates), "Hedge S5 · <rule> · <ticker>" (the hedged runs under each fewer-purchases rule), "Hedge S6 · <ticker>" (the whole position, 5 tickers). NOTES.md has the hold-out rule written before the runs, and stage 6's Put cost study.
- **Verified:** hedge off replays the baseline identically (full-row diff); hedge unit tests; full API suite (488 files, 6,334 tests); stored hedge sessions replay with identical numbers after the rules were added (one description wording changed); app tsc and lint; /ui-ux at 1706/1440/390 px in both themes; hedge Puts had a fresh 10:00 mark on 99.4% of the days held. Whole position: unit tests; stored keep, keep-unless-far and wait sessions replay identically.

## Pulse chart history survives a refresh (built + verified 2026-09-23)
Same session as above: Marcelo noticed Pulse's Unrealised P&L / Profit Probability charts went blank on a manual refresh too — expected, since 2026-09-13's design deliberately kept no intraday history (client-side-only rolling sample, starts empty every load). Reversed that tradeoff for just the two series the charts actually plot (not raw IBKR ticks): new tables `pulse_unrealized_pnl_samples` (per position) and `pulse_profit_probability_samples` (per option leg), written by `src/lib/pulseChartSampleCollector.ts` — a side-channel the existing `streamPnlHandler`/`streamGreeksHandler` call into with their already-computed values on every update (no new IBKR calls; goes idle the moment nobody has Pulse open). One batched upsert per 60s window (same debounce-and-batch pattern as `priceService.ts`'s `recordStockPrices`, after today's own pool-exhaustion incident from *not* doing that), self-pruning to a rolling 8h window on each flush. New `GET /positions/pulse-chart-history` backfills both series (open positions only) for `PulsePage.tsx` to seed its charts from on mount, before the live SSE streams take over — merge is guarded so a slow backfill response never clobbers live data that already started arriving. `CHART_MAX_SAMPLES` bumped from 240 (4h) to 480 (8h) to match the new buffer — the client-side cap didn't previously match what the design intended to show.

Verified: migration applied to local dev DB, rolled back and re-applied cleanly; direct psql round-trip on both tables; collector's actual flush logic (not just raw SQL) exercised end-to-end against the real DB via a throwaway `tmp/` script — confirmed the 60s batched upsert produces the right row; `GET /positions/pulse-chart-history` curled directly (empty state, then with seeded rows — confirmed correct SUM/GROUP BY and per-position grouping); Playwright-verified in the browser against real seeded backfill rows — both charts rendered the exact historical shape inserted, then a reload with the rows deleted confirmed the empty-state path doesn't error. `tsc --noEmit` clean both repos, full suite 436/445 (same 9 pre-existing unrelated failures as above). Not yet tested with two Pulse tabs open concurrently (the intended no-duplicate-write case) or against Juan's staging login.

## IBKR market-data line dedup + budget fold-in (started 2026-09-23, Phase 1 done)
Follow-on from the line-usage audit above: today, every screen opens its own IBKR `reqMktData` line per contract it watches, with **no sharing across concurrent viewers** — two people with Pulse open on the same 13 positions cost 32 lines, not 16, since `sharedReadConnection.ts` only shares the socket, never the subscription. Combined with `marketDataLineBudget.ts` (built earlier today) only covering the option-quote path, real usage is both uncounted and unbounded in the stock/greeks streams — audit found a **1-viewer worst case of 97-157 lines** against the 90-line budget / 100 IBKR hard cap, worse with any second viewer. Approved plan: (1) a shared, reference-counted subscription pool — one real IBKR line per contract, fanned out to every subscriber, always on `sharedLiveConnection` (fixed REALTIME) rather than the toggling read connection; (2) fold every currently-unbudgeted stream (Pulse/Positions, Dashboard exposure, Price Performance, Trade Alerts, Signals list) through it and into the reservation budget; (3) leave the nightly chain-capture job as-is (already budgeted, already snapshot-mode, dedup wouldn't help a one-shot sweep).

**Phase 1 built and verified**: `src/ibkr/stockPriceSubscriptionPool.ts` — `subscribeToPooledStockPrice(symbol, onUpdate)` ref-counts subscribers per symbol, issues exactly one `reqMktData` for a brand-new symbol, fans out ticks to every subscriber, and — unlike every other live stream in the app — **survives a Gateway restart with no caller-visible interruption**: on `EventName.disconnected` every entry is marked invalidated (not deleted) so subscribers stay registered, and a retry loop re-issues `reqMktData` for all of them the moment `sharedLiveConnection` reconnects, with nobody needing to resubscribe. Reserves against `marketDataLineBudget.ts` as one coarse `stockPricePool` holder sized to the live symbol count. `streamPooledStockPrices()` is a drop-in-shaped wrapper for SSE handlers; migrated Price Performance's `/current-prices/stream` (`routes/pricePerformance.ts`) off `streamLivePrices` onto it as the first real consumer — chosen first since it's stock-only, shortlist-wide, no P&L/greeks math on top, and today's biggest unarbitrated single line count (26).

**Bug found and fixed by the same live test that validated dedup**: two concurrent Price Performance tabs opened together then closed together left the `stockPricePool` reservation at `lines: 1` instead of 0 — concurrent unsubscribe calls (20 symbols × 2 tabs closing in the same tick) each fired an unserialized `reserveMarketDataLines` write for the same holder, and an out-of-order completion left a stale count. Same race class as `priceService.ts`'s `recordStockPrices` incident from earlier today. Fixed with the identical debounce-and-serialize pattern (one write in flight per holder, always reading the current count when it actually runs, not when it was scheduled).

**Verified**: `tsc --noEmit` clean, full suite 436/445 (same 9 pre-existing unrelated failures). Live-tested against the real dev Gateway with two concurrent curl streams to `/price-performance/current-prices/stream`: both received identical live ticks, DB reservation showed exactly one `stockPricePool` row at `lines: 20` (not 40) the whole time both were open, and `lines: 0`/no row after both closed (post-fix; pre-fix reproduced the stale-count bug directly). Playwright-verified the actual Price Performance screen renders correctly end-to-end through the new path ("Live prices streaming", every row priced, no console errors).

**Phase 2 done and verified (same session).** Generalized the pool to cover option contracts too (`pricePool.ts`, renamed from `stockPriceSubscriptionPool.ts`), added a matching `greeksPool.ts` for option greeks (ticks 13/83), and migrated every remaining unbudgeted consumer: Positions/Pulse's `streamPnlHandler` and `streamGreeksHandler` (`routes/positions.ts`), Dashboard/Risk&Limits exposure (`lib/positionExposure.ts`), Trade Alerts (`routes/tradeAlerts.ts`), and the Signals screen + ticker-modal stock line (`streams/signalsProducers.ts`, one dependency-injection swap covers both). Resolved the FROZEN-vs-pooling tension by dropping the real FROZEN snapshot request entirely (a pooled REALTIME-only subscription can't ask for one without breaking dedup) and relying on each caller's own pre-existing fallback (DB last-known-price for stock, `position_pnl_snapshots`/`position_leg_greeks_snapshots`/entry-price for options) for a still-null first reading — the wrapper still waits a 3s settle grace period (same value as the old `frozenGraceMs`) before declaring its first reading final, so the UX timing is unchanged; only a quiet option contract's very first paint on a still-cold pool entry may show a fallback value instead of a true last-trade price for a few seconds, self-correcting the moment a live tick or another viewer's already-warm subscription arrives. `fetchLivePrices`/`fetchLiveGreeks`'s own ONE-SHOT (non-streaming) functions are untouched — pooling only applies to persistent subscriptions.

**Exhaustively tested**: `tsc --noEmit` and full suite clean on every migrated file (baseline unchanged: same 9 pre-existing unrelated failures). Live against the real dev Gateway: two concurrent `pnl` + two concurrent `greeks` streams on the actual 13 open positions/10 legs (7 option) showed `pricePool: 10` and `greeksPool: 7` — not 20/14 — with both viewers receiving identical live numbers; all reservations released to 0 within seconds of both streams closing. Playwright-verified every migrated screen individually (Pulse, Positions, Dashboard, Trade Alerts, Signals, Price Performance) — real data, correct settle-then-populate timing, zero console errors on any of them. Then opened **6 tabs simultaneously** (Price Performance, two Pulse tabs, Dashboard, Signals, home) to simulate the worst-case multi-viewer scenario from the earlier audit: **`pricePool: 28` + `greeksPool: 7` = 35 total lines** — against a pre-fix estimate of 97-157 lines for even a *single* viewer. Closing every tab released both reservations to 0.

**One anomaly observed once, not reproduced**: a single run of the 4-concurrent-stream close test left `pricePool`/`greeksPool` reservations stuck at their prior (not-yet-released) counts for over 10s; re-running the identical scenario twice more released cleanly within 3-4s both times. Root cause not pinned down (suspect transient DB pool contention from concurrent test tooling, not a logic bug — the serialized-reconcile fix from Phase 1 was already in place for both pools). Not a hard risk either way: every reservation carries a 90s TTL and self-expires the moment nothing renews it, so a stuck row can never block the budget for more than ~90s even in the worst case. Worth a note if it recurs, not worth chasing further right now.

**Still deliberately outside the pools**: option-quote consumers that already went through `marketDataLineBudget.ts` directly (Ticker Detail's live chain, Order Review's leg quote, Signals' ticker-modal option quotes, the nightly chain-capture batches) — all transient/snapshot-mode, single-viewer by construction, already correctly budgeted; pooling would add complexity for near-zero payoff there.

## IBKR market-data pools merged into one centralized pool (2026-09-24)
Marcelo wanted full system-wide dedup (not just per-pool) and was explicit that build cost wasn't a concern, only correctness (wasted lines, wrong data) and performance. Two corrections that came out of this: (1) "Ticker Detail's live chain is a snapshot" was wrong — checked the actual code and it's a persistent `live` mode subscription held open for the modal's life, same for Order Review's leg quote and Signals' ticker-modal quotes; only the nightly capture job is genuinely bounded/one-shot. (2) Found that `pricePool.ts` and `greeksPool.ts` were *already* double-subscribing every option leg Pulse/Positions watches — IBKR sends bid/ask/last AND computed greeks on one plain `reqMktData` call (empty generic tick list), so having two separate pools cost 2 lines per option leg where 1 would do, even before Ticker Detail entered the picture.

**Built `src/ibkr/marketDataPool.ts`** — the one place in the app that ever opens a live (non-order-execution, non-nightly-capture) `reqMktData` subscription. One line per contract, always requesting the full field set (last/bid/ask/delta/gamma/vega/theta/IV/underlying) for every option contract regardless of what the *first* subscriber needed — this is what makes a later subscriber wanting different fields (e.g. Ticker Detail joining a contract Pulse already warmed for price-only) work correctly without a second subscription. `pricePool.ts`/`greeksPool.ts` became thin field-projecting wrappers over this core, keeping their exact external signatures — **zero changes needed** to any already-migrated consumer (positions.ts, positionExposure.ts, tradeAlerts.ts, signalsProducers.ts, pricePerformance.ts). New `pooledOptionQuotes.ts` provides the `OptionQuote`-shaped (bid/ask/last/greeks together) wrapper for the three newly-migrated consumers: Ticker Detail's chain + spot price (`streamTickerDetail.ts`), Order Review's leg quote (`streamOrderLegQuote.ts`), Signals' ticker-modal quotes (`streamSignalsOptionQuotes.ts`).
