Deliberately did NOT touch `fetchOptionChain.ts` itself (its `fetchQuotesForContracts`/`quoteOptionChain` `live` mode is now dead code for these three callers, left in place) — another session was actively mid-edit in that exact file throughout this work (confirmed via `ListAgents`); the one-shot, non-`live` callers of `fetchQuotesForContracts` (trade-alert generation/refresh, `streamPositionQuote.ts`'s New Position form) are untouched and correctly out of scope — genuinely one-shot, no persistent subscription to dedup.

**Verified**: `tsc --noEmit` and full suite clean (same 9 pre-existing unrelated failures). Cross-consumer dedup proven directly: a `greeksPool`-shaped subscriber and a `pooledOptionQuotes`-shaped subscriber on the *same* DELL 560P contract received byte-identical delta values updating in lockstep (via a throwaway `tmp/` script, since dev's `option_chain_expiry_strikes` table is empty for every ticker — pre-existing dev-data gap, unrelated to this work, confirmed by checking the table directly; blocked live UI testing of the chain/Signals-ticker-modal specifically, though the underlying mechanism they both call was proven directly). Playwright-verified Pulse, Positions, and Ticker Detail's modal (DELL: spot price, greeks, P&L all live and correct, chain section fails gracefully with a typed error instead of crashing) — zero console errors throughout. Re-ran the Gateway-disconnect test against the merged pool (same method as before: force this dev process's own IBKR socket closed via a temporary debug route, removed after use) — pnl + greeks streams kept flowing before and after, reservation held at a stable count (28, reflecting real concurrent Positions+Ticker Detail+Signals usage from the test session) throughout, never zeroed or doubled.

**Not live-tested**: Order Review's leg quote and Signals' ticker-modal quotes specifically through their actual UI paths (both blocked by the same empty-chain-table dev-data gap — Order Review needs a pending order, Signals' ticker modal didn't open without chain data). Their underlying mechanism (`streamPooledOptionQuotes`) is the exact same one proven directly against DELL 560P, and their wrapper code is a small, mechanical adaptation of the same pattern already verified for `streamOrderLegQuote`'s single-contract case — reasonably high confidence, but flagging the gap rather than claiming full UI coverage. Worth a real check next time `option_chain_expiry_strikes` has data (next nightly capture run, or ask Juan/staging where the table isn't empty).

**Gateway-restart resilience — live-tested 2026-09-24, passed.** Rather than restart the real shared VPS Gateway (would have disrupted two other concurrently-active sessions on IBKR work), added a temporary dev-only debug route that force-closed just this dev server's own IBKR socket — the exact `EventName.disconnected` trigger a real Gateway restart produces from the app's side, without touching the shared container. Two rounds: (1) one `pnl` + one `greeks` stream, forced disconnect mid-stream — both streams stayed open throughout (never errored/closed), live values kept changing before and after (confirmed via real number changes, not just heartbeats), reservations stayed at `pricePool: 10`/`greeksPool: 7` the whole time (never zeroed, never doubled). (2) Two concurrent viewers on each stream (the actual "you and Juan both watching Pulse" scenario) plus the same forced disconnect — all 4 streams recovered in lockstep with byte-identical data on both viewers of each pair, reservations unchanged throughout. Debug route removed after testing (`git diff --stat` confirms `systemHealth.ts` is back to its exact pre-test state).

## Pulse Gateway card: reconnects folded into Uptime, IBKR line usage added (2026-09-24)
Plain language: the Gateway box on IORIO Pulse now reads "Uptime 1h 42m (0 reconnects)" instead of spending a row on reconnects, and gains two rows — **IBKR live connections** (how many market-data lines the app has open with IBKR right now) and **Reserved lines** (how many of the 90-line budget are booked, including the scheduled scans' priority bookings). Both were asked for by Marcelo.

Caveat agreed before building: the Gateway worker on the VPS handles orders/account/positions only and never opens market-data lines, so these two numbers are not Gateway-worker stats. "Live connections" is the web dyno's centralized pool (`marketDataPool.ts`): entries with a real IBKR reqId, so paused/unsubscribed contracts don't count (chosen over `subscriberCount`, which counts screens and double-counts shared contracts, and over `contractCount`, which counts pooled contracts even when the budget has paused them — locally with `IBKR_MARKET_DATA_LINES_ENABLED=false` it reads 0 while 13 contracts sit paused). "Reserved" is the sum of active rows in `ibkr_market_data_line_reservations` (new `currentMarketDataLineReservationTotal()`), served by `GET /system-health/gateway` as `marketDataLineCount` / `reservedLineCount`.

Verified: endpoint curled (`marketDataLineCount: 0`, `reservedLineCount: 0` on dev), both repos `tsc` clean, Pulse Playwright-checked at 1440 and 1920 — each row on one line, no overflow, no console errors beyond the SSE drop from `tsx watch` restarting. **Pre-existing, not fixed:** at phone width (390px) the entire Pulse page is cramped on committed code — KPI tiles clip to "$1,0", every diagram node is ~70–130px wide with subtitles cut off — so the new rows clip exactly like the old ones. Needs a page-level phone layout decision, separate from this change.

## Staging outage 2026-09-24 (market hours): Signals best-line loop + abort-listener leak (fixed 2026-09-24)

**Plain language.** From 13:30 UTC (US market open) the staging API froze within minutes of anyone opening the Signals screen: every request timed out, the screen showed "Live data disconnected", and restarting the dyno only bought a few minutes. The Signals screen keeps one live IBKR price line on each ticker's best contract and moves that line whenever another contract looks better. The line's own live price could make a *different* contract look better (the others were still priced from the 10:00 snapshot), so the line bounced back and forth thousands of times a second, and every bounce left a small piece of bookkeeping behind. The server spent all its time bouncing and ran out of memory. Production was never affected — it runs a build from 2026-09-21, before this code existed. Nothing for Juan to do.

**Technical record.**
- Introduced by `4e68627` (Day Signals, staging v55 at 05:44 UTC). The first market session with it was 2026-09-24: memory climbed from exactly 13:30 UTC, H12 storm from 13:47, `R14 Memory quota exceeded` at ~514 MB on the 512 MB Basic dyno.
- Diagnosed empirically on the live dyno's V8 inspector (`heroku ps:exec` → `kill -USR1 <pid>` → `heroku ps:forward 9229` → Chrome DevTools Protocol from a local script). Gotcha: `ps:forward` drops every forwarded connection after 60 s, so a full heap snapshot or forced GC of a swapped heap never completes over it — use sampling profiles that fit the window. Retained-allocation sampling (42 s): 100% of retained bytes from `syncBestLine` / `childAbort` (`AbortSignal`, listener `Map`, `addEventListener`). CPU profile (20 s): 84% self time inside `EventTarget.addEventListener` under `childAbort`, 99.7% inclusive under the option-quote callback — Node's duplicate-listener scan is O(listeners), so the leak made each flip slower (quadratic).
- Two defects in `src/streams/signalsProducers.ts`: (1) `childAbort` registered an `abort` listener on the parent subscription signal that was never removed when a line was retired; (2) moving the line cleared `liveQuotes`, so the next score compared the new line's live quote against the old line's snapshot quote and flipped straight back — all in microtasks, starving the event loop (dead SSE heartbeats → the toast).
- Fix: `childAbort` unhooks its parent listener when the child aborts; `TickerState.liveQuotes` is a per-contract map that survives line moves (readings of retired contracts are dropped when that ticker's day quotes reload), so a move is judged live-vs-live and settles. Regression test drives the exact flip sequence and asserts the parent signal's `abort` listener count is flat across two line moves.
- Also in the same push: `npm start` runs Node with `--max-old-space-size=384`. The probe showed heapTotal 475 MB with 286 MB live under Node's default 4.5 GB limit — V8 lets a 512 MB dyno drift into swap before it bothers to collect; prod gets this on its next promotion.
- Verification (2026-09-24, staging v63 = `78abebd`, booted 15:02 UTC): API answering instantly again — `/system-health/web-dyno` at 89 req/min with 4 multiplexed stream connections including a live `signalsScreen`; Signals shows "Live prices connected"; no further H12/R14. Node's `--max-old-space-size=384` is in the same release.
- Follow-ups from the same afternoon: (1) Pulse's Gateway card showed "Reserved lines —" after the API deploy because the API renamed the field to `priorityReservedLineCount` (`104188c`) while the staging front end was still on `0ed9762`; pushing the app's `353ef81` fixed the skew. (2) The Day Signals loop reserves its 10 lines with `priority: true` for the whole session, so the top bar's "Live data restricted" (driven by `loadMarketDataLineRestriction`) was on all day. Decision (Marcelo 2026-09-24): the loop is normal operation, not a restriction — `/environment/details` now excludes holder `daySignalsLoop` (`excludeHolders`), so the banner only appears while the chain capture or the trade-alert scan actually holds lines, with its existing hover/focus tooltip explaining the shed rules; `/system-health/gateway` keeps the factual priority count (the card's "(10 reserved)" during the loop).

## Day Signals alert flooding — notification hysteresis + per-contract cooldown (fixed 2026-09-24)

**Plain language.** Marcelo reported the upgrade alerts (Telegram, toast, Pulse Latest Events) firing far too often for the same contract. Confirmed on staging: HOOD's $116 cash-secured put alone sent 8 "weak → good" alerts in 11 minutes. The contract wasn't actually improving — its net Edge was hovering right on the weak/good line (5.0–7.5vp against a 5vp cut point), so ordinary bid/ask noise flipped it back below the line (a silent downgrade, not notified) and back above it (notified again) on nearly every ~1-2 minute refresh cycle. The original design's comment assumed a refresh cycle "takes minutes, so flapping is not a concern" — it doesn't hold once cycles run that fast.

**Fix (both approved 2026-09-24, Marcelo):**
1. **Notification hysteresis** (`clearsNotificationHysteresis`, `daySignalsNotifications.ts`) — a grade transition only notifies if net Edge clears the grade's own cut point (Formula 3j: 0/5/10vp for weak/good/strong) by a **2vp margin** (so weak→good needs ≥7vp, good→strong ≥12vp, avoid→weak ≥2vp). The grade shown on screen is unchanged — `gradeForNetEdge` still grades at the bare cut points; this only gates whether an upward transition is convincing enough to notify. `strongCutVolatilityPoints`/`goodCutVolatilityPoints` exported from `signalCandidates.ts` so the two files share one source of truth for the cut points.
2. **Per-contract cooldown** (`daySignalsLoop.ts`, `daySignalsNotificationCooldownMs = 10 * 60_000`) — once a contract notifies, it stays quiet for 10 minutes regardless of further grade movement, purely by elapsed time (no requirement that it first fall back to a lower grade). Keyed per exact contract (ticker+expiry+strike+right for new-trade candidates, legId+expiry+strike+right for rolls — both scoped separately by a `signal:`/`roll:` prefix). In-memory only (`lastNotifiedAtByKey` on the `DaySignalsLoop` instance), not persisted — a mid-window loop restart resets it, an accepted tradeoff against a DB round trip per check.
3. Applies to both Signal upgrades and Roll Signal upgrades identically.

Grade recording to `day_signal_quotes.last_grade` / `day_signal_roll_grades` is unconditional as before — only the notification is gated, so the next cycle's `isGradeUpgrade` comparison always sees the true last grade, not a suppressed one. Tests: `daySignalsNotifications.test.ts` (new, unit tests on `clearsNotificationHysteresis`/`isGradeUpgrade`), `daySignalsLoop.test.ts` (new cases: hysteresis withholds a marginal Weak notification while still recording the grade; cooldown suppresses a repeat within 10 minutes and allows it again after; existing roll-upgrade test updated since the one marginal roll in its fixture no longer notifies). Full suite (572 tests) and `tsc` clean.

**Live-verified on staging 2026-09-24 (market hours, real data).** Pushed to staging (v66) and checked against real signal-upgrade activity: every `signal_upgraded` notification recorded after the deploy cleared its required hysteresis margin (weak→good ≥7vp, good→strong ≥12vp, avoid→weak ≥2vp) — zero violations across dozens of real upgrades (BMNR, HOOD, COIN), including several right at the boundary (7.04vp, 7.23vp, 2.0vp). Cooldown: the one contract that repeated in a clean, restart-free window (BMNR $28.50 covered call) waited 17m15s between notifications, well clear of the 10-minute window. One apparent 3m23s "violation" turned out to be a false alarm — a staging deploy (pushing the Telegram-suspend commit below) restarted the dyno in between, which resets the in-memory cooldown map by design, not a bug.

## Order Review calendar-warning/risk-free-rate persistence + Live Quote instant seed (fixed 2026-09-24)

**Plain language.** Two related Order Review fixes. First: the "economic events before expiry" warning banner (and the probability-of-profit rate behind it) used to flash on screen and then vanish — it was computed when the order was created but never actually saved, so the very next refresh showed nothing. Second: the "Live Quote" card on Order Review used to sit on a spinner for a moment every time, even though the app usually already had that same quote a second earlier from wherever the trade was picked (an option chain row, a Signals candidate). It now paints instantly from that already-known quote.

**Technical record.**
- `calendarWarning`/`riskFreeRate` persistence (api, `248ad4b`): both were computed at order-creation time but only ever returned on the POST response, never written to the `order_requests` row. The `order_status` notification fired before that computation even started, so the SSE listener's own `GET /orders/:id` could beat the persist and overwrite the panel with nothing. New migration adds `calendar_warning`/`risk_free_rate` columns; both are now persisted before the notification goes out.
- Live Quote instant seed (app, `7a04ef4`): new `initialQuote` prop on `OrderReviewPanel`, display-only — the compliance/signalLimits gate still waits on the real stream's first tick, so Confirm can't unlock early off stale seed data. `TickerDetailModal` passes its existing live quote; `SignalsTickerModal` maps the selected candidate/roll into the narrower seed shape (no gamma/last of its own).
- **Live-verified 2026-09-24 (market hours, staging, paper account).** Placed a real 1-contract BMNR $27 cash-secured put with a genuine calendar warning present (Trump/Xi summit + Durable Goods Orders before the Sep 25 expiry). Confirmed via direct DB query that `calendar_warning`/`risk_free_rate` persisted on the row, and the banner stayed visible across multiple refresh cycles instead of flashing away. Live Quote card painted bid/ask/spread/IV/delta/theta/vega instantly with no spinner. Order cancelled afterward for cleanup (confirmed `cancelled` status, IBKR order id 7 acknowledged the cancel).

## Stale-surface caveat folded into per-ticker model caveats (fixed 2026-09-24)

**Plain language.** The Signals screen used to show a one-off "surface &lt;date&gt;" hover badge for a ticker scored on an old volatility surface, inconsistent with the click-to-explain "model" caveat badge used for every other per-ticker issue (short history, suspected split, etc.). It now renders through that same structured caveat list and popover.

**Technical record.**
- api `1c31833` / app `bd482d7`: new `stale_surface` caveat (status `waiting_on_next_run`) fires when a ticker's `snapshotDateIso` is non-null but doesn't match today; unit-tested (`signalsRoadmap.test.ts`).
- **Live-checked 2026-09-24 (staging), inconclusive on the specific caveat.** Every ticker with a failed capture today (QQQ, SMCI, SMH, IGV, INTC, LITE, MU, NBIS, PLTR, SPCX, TLT) has never had a successful capture at all, so they correctly show `no_snapshot` instead (that caveat takes priority). Did confirm the caveat UI plumbing itself works live: DRAM's `short_history` caveat renders correctly through the same structured popover, and no ticker shows a spurious caveat. `stale_surface` itself needs a ticker whose capture succeeds most days but fails on one particular day — none exist on staging right now, so this one is still unverified against real data.

## Day Signals Telegram delivery suspended (2026-09-24)

Marcelo turned off Telegram delivery for Signal/Roll upgrade alerts (`a98f1d1`) — the in-app toast and Pulse Latest Events still fire on every upgrade, Telegram just doesn't. `formatSignalUpgradeMessage`/`formatRollSignalUpgradeMessage` are unused but kept in place for that path if it's re-enabled. **Live-verified 2026-09-24:** watched several real upgrades (HOOD, COIN) toast correctly in-app during testing. Not verified: that no Telegram message actually went out — needs Marcelo to confirm nothing hit his phone during the test window (~16:06–16:19 UTC, 2026-09-24).

## Day Signals follows the price (built and tested 2026-09-29, pushed to staging; needs a market-hours check there)

**Plain language.** The 9:30 snapshot only stores the options that were out-of-the-money at the open. When a stock moved a lot afterwards (BE +14%), the puts you would now sell had no quotes and the chain showed dashes. Day Signals now re-checks each stock's live price every cycle and quotes the options that make sense at that price, dropping the ones that no longer do (the line count stays 10). If a stock moves far enough, it also re-picks which expiries are worth watching.

**Technical.** Per cycle: a spot pass over every pooled ticker, then per ticker `selectDaySignalContractSet` (`daySignalsContractSet.ts`) = the capture's `selectContractsToCapture` at the live spot, plus previous contracts within one strike step (buffer), plus open-leg contracts; dropped contracts' day quotes are deleted (`pruneDayQuotesOutsideSet`); tickers without a context, IV or spot stay on the snapshot's contracts. Re-rank trigger (approved formula): |spot - last-rank spot| / last-rank spot >= max(1%, 0.5 x ATM IV / sqrt(252)), max 3 per ticker per day, persisted in `day_signal_rerank_state` (migration `20260929000004`; one row per ticker per day, wiped by the seed; no row = reference is the 9:30 spot, so a restart neither repeats a re-rank nor resets the cap). Re-rank quotes the rule's contracts across all fitted expiries, scores with those live quotes, keeps the top 3 expiries by the seed rule plus held-leg expiries (`replaceTickerPoolExpiries`); no positive-Edge candidate keeps the current pool. `scoreTicker` now appends any day/live quote the snapshot lacks, so the chain and screens show them; legend text now "not in today's capture or refresh".
- A contract the loop starts quoting mid-day (stored quotes exist from an earlier cycle, so not the first cycle after the seed) counts as coming from Avoid: it can notify on its first grade, subject to the usual hysteresis and cooldown. Applies to roll replacements too. Expect a burst after a big move or a re-rank into a new expiry.
- **Follow-up 2026-09-29 (same day, after the first staging look):** BE was not in the 9:30 pool at all (the seed found no positive-Edge candidate at the open's prices), and the first version only watched pooled tickers, so it never re-ranked. The loop now also tracks every Signals-universe ticker with today's snapshot and no pool (`loadDayUnpooledTickers`): same spot pass and trigger, and a re-rank can create the pool (`replaceTickerPoolExpiries` now takes the snapshot id). If the re-rank still finds no positive-Edge expiry, the ticker stays unpooled and its chain keeps dashes near the price (open question for Marcelo: pool the best expiry anyway?).
- The Signals chain no longer shows in-the-money contracts (call strike below spot, put strike above): desktop blanks the cell, phone drops the row; a contract under an open order review stays visible. Frontend only, uses the live spot.
- **Lines only for what is on screen (2026-09-29/30, replaces the spot-following live set of 2026-09-29):** the Signals modal subscribes live option quotes only for contracts currently visible (chain rows, Opportunities rows, held-leg/roll rows; a row counts once at least half of it shows, no extra rows either side), via a new `signalsQuotes` stream the modal reopens 300 ms after the visible set settles; it returns those contracts re-scored at the live spot, which the modal overlays (order/filters stay on the ticker stream's values so a re-grade never reshuffles visible rows). `signalsTicker` holds no option lines now. Chain and Opportunities are each sized to 10 rows (~10 lines each while in view). Across the whole app, a browser tab hidden for 2 s unsubscribes every line-holding stream (greeks, pnl, exposure, price-performance, stock prices, all Signals streams) and resubscribes on return; notifications/presence and Pulse topology stay connected. "Active tab" = visible, not focused (two windows side by side both keep their lines; Marcelo OK'd).
- To check on staging: cycle time with the changed contract counts, that a real mover gets its new puts quoted and graded, the chain screen on desktop and phone.

## FV excluding earnings days — backtest, not adopted (2026-09-30)

**Plain language.** Checked whether the volatility forecast (FV) should skip the days right after earnings reports. It barely changes today's numbers (BE 117% → 114%, AAOI 121% → 116%, NBIS 115% → 110%), doesn't forecast better, and the stored earnings dates are unreliable for half the shortlist. The big negative Edge on BE/AAOI/NBIS/DRAM comes from genuinely large non-earnings moves over the last three months, not from earnings days. FV stays as it is.

**Technical.** `tmp/fvExEarningsBacktest.ts` (read-only, staging data, shortlist of 10; exclusion = report date + next trading day; my Yang-Zhang matches production exactly with nothing excluded). Windows with no earnings ahead (what Signals ranks): QLIKE difference ex − now +0.002/+0.003/+0.002 at h = 10/21/42, 95% block-bootstrap intervals all span 0; corr(IV−FV, IV−realized) 0.27/0.13/0.14 now vs 0.28/0.12/0.12 ex. Current FV over-forecasts realized vol by 9.4%/6.7%/3.9% (mean log ratio) in those windows; ex-earnings 6.0%/3.5%/1.2%. Calendar check: the listed date + next day catches the biggest move near the report in 18/20 INTC, 15/16 NBIS, 16/20 SMCI, but only 9–11/20 for AAOI, BE, COIN, HOOD and BMNR (BMNR's "earnings" days move less than an ordinary day — its dates are wrong); API Ninjas' timing fields are paywalled on our plan.

## secDefOptParams timeout investigation — QQQ/SMCI/SMH (resolved by a Gateway restart 2026-09-25; root cause still unknown)

**Plain language.** These three tickers couldn't capture an option chain on staging — refreshing showed a spinner, then reverted to 0 with a warning triangle. Traced it all the way down to IBKR's own Gateway software: it was silently refusing to answer this one type of request for these three tickers specifically, while working fine for every other ticker tested. Restarting the paper Gateway on the VPS cleared it immediately and all three now capture correctly, verified through the real app. We still don't know *why* it got into that state, so it could come back — if it does, a Gateway restart is the known fix.

**Technical record.**
- Confirmed with a standalone script that bypassed the app and IBC entirely: fresh connections straight to IBKR's paper Gateway got **zero** response (no data, no error, no end-of-stream marker, 45s wait) to `reqSecDefOptParams` for QQQ/SMCI/SMH specifically, while AAPL and HOOD resolved cleanly in under 4s each. SMCI's plain stock contract details resolved fine via `reqContractDetails` in under 1s, which rules out a bad/stale `ibkr_contract_id`.
- No server-side diagnostics were available to explain *why*: IBKR's audit trail and its optional "Create API Message Log" output are both written in an encrypted proprietary format (`.ibgzenc`) with no decryption tool available to us — enabling that logging (via a one-time VNC session opened on the paper Gateway container) was a dead end for root-causing this, and was reverted afterward (VNC removed, logging settings restored, generated log files deleted).
- Restarted the paper Gateway (`docker compose up -d ib-gateway-paper` on the VPS) — all three tickers immediately started resolving (SMCI 1.7s, QQQ 3.1s, SMH 2.1s direct; then confirmed through the actual staging app's refresh-option-chain endpoint end-to-end).
- Root cause remains unknown — looks like a wedged/stuck internal request state in IBKR's Gateway process for specific symbols. No app code changes were needed; temporary diagnostic logging added to `fetchOptionChain.ts` (`e32a06a`) was removed again (`472a6ba`) once the fix was confirmed.
- **Side finding, still open:** while investigating, hit a separate, previously-unexplained "We are having trouble loading your settings, they may be corrupted" dialog on Gateway restart (Marcelo: "we've had this issue several times"). Added `stop_grace_period: 90s` to both Gateway services in `/opt/ibkr/docker-compose.yml` on the VPS on the theory that Docker's 10s default was SIGKILLing TWS before it finished writing its settings file — but the dialog **recurred on the next restart anyway with the fix in place**, so that theory is disproven/unconfirmed. Verified the Gateway stays functional even with the dialog showing (API connects fine, option chains resolve), so it's cosmetic for now but unresolved.
- `/opt/ibkr` on the VPS has no git history — a manual `.bak` copy of the pre-change `docker-compose.yml` was made before editing (`docker-compose.yml.bak.20260925001127`) and is the only record of the original config. Open question raised but not yet decided: whether to put VPS infra config (this docker-compose setup) under git for real version history instead of manual `.bak` files going forward.

## Close form: live prices, fill priority, wheel cycle P&L (built 2026-09-28, pushed to staging; live path still to verify in market hours)
Plain-language: every row in Dashboard "Needs Attention" now has a Close button (same close form as Positions). The close form now uses only live prices, shows the ticker's wheel cycle P&L live, lets you pick the fill priority (Patient/Normal/Urgent) up front, and refuses to place a close when the market is closed, live quotes are missing, or the ticker's cycle data is inconsistent.
- New SSE `GET /positions/:id/close-live/stream` (`routes/positionCloseLive.ts`, logic in `lib/closeLiveState.ts`, 12 unit tests): live bid/ask per open leg from the shared pool, live cycle P&L via `deriveCycles` with the live stock last and the position's option leg marked at the bid/ask mid, and a `blockReason`. Live = regular session open + a real bid AND ask (the pool's stock fallback only fills `last`).
- Frontend: `ClosePositionModal` (both forms), `FillPriorityPicker`, Close button on Needs Attention rows; the picked priority seeds the order-review dropdown.
- Gating is UI-only; `POST /positions/:id/close` and the Genosuke tools are unchanged. The Signals/Roll forms keep their own inline priority buttons.
- Not testable locally: real IBKR quotes (market closed, worker offline); the live path was driven with a fake stream. Verify on staging in market hours.

## Positions: Cycle P&L column, tabs and Open/Closed filter removed (built and tested locally 2026-09-28, not pushed)
Plain-language: the Positions table has a new Cycle P&L column, the profit or loss of the whole wheel cycle of that symbol, calculated with the same price the row shows so it lines up with the other figures. The footer adds each symbol once. The All / Covered Calls / Cash-Secured Puts tabs and the Open/Closed toggle are gone; the page now shows open positions only.
- New `GET /positions/cycles/marks` (`positionCycleMarks.ts`, `lib/cycleLiveMarks.ts`, 7 tests): per open ticker the stored-mark cycle total, ledger shares, mark price and each open option position's stored premium mark. Live figure in the browser (`lib/cycleLivePnl.ts`): total + shares x (row Price - mark price) + sum (live unrealized premium P&L - stored mark); a test proves it equals re-deriving the cycle with those marks.
- A mark that isn't available stays at the stored value (last daily close / nightly snapshot), with an "As of" tooltip when the row has no price; a flagged cycle shows a dash with the reason.
- Closed positions are no longer viewable on this page.

## Pulse phone layout + mobile Pulse link (built and tested locally 2026-09-28, not pushed)

*In plain terms:* Pulse now has a proper phone layout instead of the desktop grid crushed into a narrow screen. Approved mockup (Proposal B): [Pulse Mobile Proposals](https://claude.ai/artifact/RFyVDjKoQkV1wzCHULZkCY). At ≤700px wide the page scrolls, the two header rows stay pinned, KPIs sit in a 2×2 grid over a slim allocation bar, and each desktop panel becomes a collapsible with a one-line summary, in the order Charts, Positions, Top Signals, Trades, Latest Events, Systems (Positions and Latest Events open by default; open/closed remembered per panel in localStorage). Systems' collapsed header shows one LED per node and a live pulse flashes both ends of its edge. Trades/Events show 8 rows then "Show N more".

- App repo: `hooks/useMediaQuery.ts`, `components/pulse/PulsePhoneLayout.tsx`, `PulseCollapsiblePanel.tsx`, `PnlSparkline.tsx`, `pulseEdges.ts` (edge list moved out of `TopologyMap.tsx`); `PulsePage.tsx` renders every tile/row/node card once and hands them to either layout; phone CSS block at the end of `PulsePage.css`.
- Decisions (Marcelo): standalone home-screen web app meta added to `index.html` (`viewport-fit=cover`, `apple-mobile-web-app-capable`, black-translucent status bar) with safe-area padding on the Pulse header/bottom and the mobile navbar; phone Pulse link in the mobile badges row opens in the same tab, and Pulse's brand mark links back to `/`; Positions summary exposure % = Σ capital at risk ÷ account value; node LED rules: DB worst of its bands, Gateway red when not connected / amber when reconnects or in-flight orders hit warn, IBKR red while account data fails, others green once their health call answered.
- Verified with Playwright at 440×956 and 1440×900 (desktop unchanged); `tsc` and `oxlint` clean. Not yet checked on a real iPhone (safe-area insets are 0 in Chrome).

## Pulse "Keep live in background" switch (built and tested locally 2026-10-01, pushed to staging)

*In plain terms:* Pulse has a switch in its header that keeps prices and P&L updating while the browser tab is in the background. It is off by default, and each browser remembers its own choice. While it is off, the app behaves as before: a hidden tab drops its IBKR price lines after 2 seconds.

- App repo: `setHiddenTabPauseDisabled` / `isTabLive` in `api/streamMultiplexer.ts`, `hooks/useKeepLiveInBackground.ts` (localStorage `pulse.keepLiveInBackground`), `components/pulse/KeepLiveSwitch.tsx`; Pulse's polls follow the same gate. The override applies only while Pulse is mounted, so other pages in the same tab keep the normal hidden-tab pause.
- Verified with Playwright (desktop + 390 px): off sends unsubscribes when hidden and resubscribes on show, on sends none, the setting survives a reload, and leaving Pulse restores the pause. `tsc` and `oxlint` clean.

## Positions: Today's Orders card, "new" tag, cancelled-after-partial-fill status (built and tested locally 2026-10-01, not pushed)

*In plain terms:* under the positions table there is a new "Today's Orders" card listing every order whose last update was today (New York time), newest first, with what was ordered, what filled and at what price, commission, who requested it, and its status. Orders still working from an earlier day always show too. A small "new" tag sits beside the ticker of any position opened or rolled today. An order that IBKR cancelled after part of it had filled used to be recorded as "partially filled", the same as one still working; it now has its own status, "Cancelled after partly filling".

- API: `GET /positions/orders/today` (`lib/todaysOrders.ts`, 6 tests incl. the New York day boundary); one row per order, fills matched per leg from `trades` via `source_order_request_id`. Close/roll legs copied from a position store the expiry as an ISO timestamp, opening legs as YYYYMMDD; both are normalised before matching.
- New status `cancelled_partially_filled` (migration `20261001000002`): the worker used to test "some filled, some remaining" before "Cancelled", so a cancel or DAY expiry after a partial fill was never recorded and the row kept blocking new orders on that position. Fixed in `ibkr/ibkrGatewayOrderStatus.ts` (+ the startup reconcile). Touches `ibkrGatewayWorker.ts`: needs the worker deployed to take effect. Existing `partially_filled` rows resolve on the next reconnect.
- App: `TodaysOrdersCard`, `NewPositionTag` (`.iorio-new-tag`, 0.6rem), shared `useOrderCancellation` (also used by the Trade Blotter), `positionHasLegEnteredTodayEastern` (any leg entered today ET, so a same-day roll counts).
- Verified with Playwright (desktop + 390 px, light + dark): fills, cancel flow with DB check, tag colours. Not checked against live IBKR (no market data locally).

## Misc UI, 2026-09-24
- Positions: replaced the separate P(Δ)/P(D2) probability columns with a single Net Δ (the short leg's raw signed delta) — `df99158`.
- Positions "Net Δ" column and Pulse "|Δ|" column/chart replaced by **P(Δ)** (2026-10-03, app repo, uncommitted): chance of success from the short option's delta, 0.00–1.00, 1 = success — covered call `|Δ|` (assigned), cash-secured put `1 − |Δ|` (not assigned); long legs (TLT hedge) show —. One shared helper `lib/successProbability.ts` (mirrors `positionSuccessProbability.ts`); the Pulse chart stores raw delta and converts when drawing; `NetDeltaChart` renamed `SuccessProbabilityChart`. The old Net Δ tooltip had the signs backwards (IBKR reports the contract's own delta: short put negative, short call positive).
- Pulse Latest Events: added a `job_started` event type alongside `job_completed` (muted gray text, same `ibkr_health_check` exclusion `job_completed` already has) — `7526eb5`.
- Dashboard Needs Attention table: shows live price/value, reusing the existing trade-alerts SSE stream — `4d9ec6e`.
- Dashboard: added a label for positions closed via `stock_rolled_into_covered_call` ("shares rolled into a covered call") — `7c76bc6`.
- Pulse: EXP % now matches EXP $'s color instead of its own scheme — `0a7d8eb`.

## Pluto — autonomous trading agent (design closed 2026-09-28; merged into `main` 2026-10-06; live on staging, paper account, since 2026-10-06; not on prod)
- **Max per ticker and max per sector are shares of Pluto's budget (2026-10-16, formula approved by Marcelo; pushed to staging 2026-10-16).** Plain-language: the two Edge Margin caps used to be a share of the whole account; they are now a share of Pluto's capital budget, so changing the budget moves them too. The numbers stayed as they were, which halves the room at a 50% budget: max per ticker 10 was $104k on a $1.04M account and is now $52k. Positions people opened on a ticker or sector still count toward its cap. The budget's help text now says what it counts (every position on Pluto's tickers, people's included, hedges not). **Technical:** room = NLV × `capitalBudgetPct` × `maxTickerPctOfBudget` (or `maxSectorPctOfBudget`, 100 = off) − exposure (`computeSizingRoom`). Columns and keys renamed (`max_ticker_pct_of_budget`, `max_sector_pct_of_budget`; payload `max_ticker_pct_of_budget`, system prompt unchanged so no version bump); the migration also renames the keys in the backtest lab settings and each session's snapshot and overrides. Backtests share the formula: `backtestEngineVersion` 2. Pass settings versions, model payloads and audit rows keep the old keys (old audit rows read "(old, % of account)").
- **Pass settings stored once per version (2026-10-14, Marcelo; pushed to staging 2026-10-14 in two releases).** Plain-language: every Pluto round used to save its own full copy of the settings (~1 KB), more than half of the staging database; now each distinct set of settings is saved once and a round points at it, with the same history kept. `pluto_settings_versions` (sha256 of the jsonb text) + `pluto_passes.settings_version_id`; migration `20261014100001` backfills the versions, `20261014200001` maps passes written mid-release and drops `settings_snapshot` (two releases because the old agent dyno writes the column during the release phase). The pass routes read through a join, response unchanged. Rebuilding from `pluto_settings_audit` was rejected: migrations (prompt bumps, added or removed fields) write no audit rows. `VACUUM FULL pluto_passes` on staging 2026-10-14: 112 → 43 MB, 1.2 s lock; not needed on prod (Pluto never ran there).
- **Pluto no longer abstains on its own old reasons (2026-10-19, Marcelo; committed, not pushed).** Plain-language: from 15 Oct 16:14 UTC to 19 Oct the model made no trade on staging. A blocked put said "budget left $844", which was correct because a working SMCI order held about $19k of the budget, but the account figures the model saw didn't include working orders, so it called the data inconsistent and abstained. Each later decision then read its own earlier reason as evidence and abstained again (534 decisions). Now the account block also shows the budget Pluto's working orders hold, and the decision history keeps a reason only for trades and is described as history, never grounds for a concern. Prompt v4.5. **Technical:** `pluto_budget_in_flight_pct` (`loadInFlightNotionals(null).managedNotional`, backtest 0), `recentDecisionsForPrompt` drops non-trade reasons; migration `20261019300001`.
- **Pluto and Signals rank by the model's Edge Yield; Kelly is half-Kelly on Edge $ (2026-10-12, Marcelo; pushed to staging 2026-10-12).** Plain-language: the history behind Hist Yield and Hist Edge covers only at-the-money sales, so for now everything uses the model's own figures. Contracts are ranked by Edge Yield, then Edge $, everywhere (Signals rows and top signal, the ticker modal, the Day Signals seed, Pluto's offers and its logged top pick). Pluto's Kelly cap is now 0.5 × budget × Edge $ ÷ P&L variance (was 0.25 × Hist Edge $); example AAPL $330 Put · 14 Oct (5DTE), staging 2026-10-09: 0.5 × $519,698 × $43 ÷ $402² = 69 contracts, where the order size allows 1. In practice the order size, stress, ticker and budget caps now bind before Kelly, and Kelly no longer sizes to zero the contracts history says lost (AAOI and SMCI on 10-09). The model no longer sees hist_* figures (prompt v4.2). Hist Yield stays on the screens as information. Switch back to Hist once there is out-of-the-money history (ThetaData backtests). **Technical:** `compareCandidatesByModel` (API and app mirror), `rankRowsByEdgeYield`, `computeKellyCap` (μ = `expectedPnlDollars`, `kellyFraction` 0.5), `POST /pluto/sizing-caps` takes `model.expectedPnlDollars`, `summarizeTopPick` records `rankedBy: "edge_yield"` so the Pluto screen labels older rows by the yield that ranked them; held-share calls need Edge > 0 (code `edge`); migration `20261012400002`.
- **Calls on free shares are sold by code, ranked by premium yield (2026-10-12, Marcelo; built locally, not committed or pushed).** Plain-language: when a ticker holds free shares, Pluto no longer asks the AI model whether to sell a call on them; code sells one on every free round lot before the model is asked anything. It picks the call with the highest premium yield (the premium kept after costs, as a yearly rate on the shares' value), across every expiry including ones past an earnings date, among the calls whose strike is at or above the cycle's break-even and whose assignment would still clear the minimum profit. Only execution checks apply (spread, a fresh live quote, a free contract, no working order, the ticker cooldown, the session's action cap); no grade, edge, delta, DTE, open-interest, volume, budget or position-count rule. If such an order goes unfilled or is refused, code waits an hour before trying again. A covered call written on shares already held is no longer closed automatically before earnings (P3b). The platform's delta band no longer applies to any covered call that buys no shares, whatever placed it. The model (prompt v4.4) is no longer offered calls on a ticker holding free shares. The backtest replays the same: code sells the call before the decision rules, and "shares in profit" now only decides what happens when no call clears the rules. Ranked by premium yield by Marcelo's choice, knowing the HOOD/INTC/SMCI 2022–2025 backtest returned less with it (it picks strikes close to break-even: more premium up front, less of a rebound kept) than with the earlier behaviour or with Edge Yield ranking. **Technical:** `selectHeldShareCall` (`candidateFilters.ts`), `scoreHeldShareCalls` (`signalsLiveScoring.ts`, `includeContractsSpanningEarnings` in `signalCandidates.ts`), `checkHeldShareCallStrike` and `heldShareCallGate` (`heldShareCalls.ts`), `runHeldShareCallGates` (`postModelGates.ts`, the model's gates lose their held-share branches), `sendHeldShareCall` (`passRunner.ts`, paced by `perTickerModelCooldownMinutes`), `writesCallOnHeldSharesOnly` / `isDeltaBandGated` (`deltaBandScope.ts`), `heldShareCallPositionIds` from `position_share_sources` (`closeActions.ts`); executor takes `reasons`; backtest: worker scoring returns held-share calls, `backtestLook.ts` sends them through the open path, policy and settings template reworded; migration `20261013200001` (prompt v4.4). App: gate labels and the model's verdict skips automatic actions (`plutoPresentation.ts`).
- **Covered calls on shares already held (2026-10-12, Marcelo; pushed to staging 2026-10-12, `213e7b0`; ranking and filter on Edge since the entry above).** Plain-language: when a ticker holds free shares (usually from an assigned put), Pluto now offers one covered call on them, sized to cover all of them and buying none, and only a call that, if assigned, sells the shares at or above the cycle's break-even plus the same minimum profit as the share sale (1% of the average cost, at least $50), counting the call's own premium. The usual grade, Edge Yield and premium-yield minimums don't apply to that call; it only needs a positive Hist Edge, and among the calls that qualify Pluto takes the best by Hist Yield. A covered-call roll to a lower strike must pass the same break-even test; a roll that keeps or raises the strike is always allowed. No break-even (the ledger can't be trusted) means no call. Example: 100 shares assigned at $40 with $1.00 of put premium (break-even $39.00): a $39 call at 0.55 passes ($54 profit if assigned), a $38.50 call at 0.60 does not ($9). Signals and manual orders are unchanged. Staging replay of 2026-10-09 (read-only): COIN, INTC and SMCI hold 400 / 400 / 1,100 free shares, and every covered call on them had Hist Edge ≤ 0, so none would have been offered. **Technical:** `pluto/heldShareCalls.ts` (pro-rata formula, spread over the shares acquired, as in `cycleBreakEven.ts`); `candidateFilters.ts` (codes `hist_edge`, `below_break_even`, `no_break_even`, `held_shares_runner_up`; optional `cycleBreakEven` input); `postModelGates.ts` (held-share sizing without Kelly, `break_even` gate at the size sent); `passRunner.ts` loads `fetchOpenCycleSummaryByTickerId` only with free round lots or a covered-call roll; prompt v4.1 (`on_held_shares`), migration `20261012400001`. `cycleBreakEven.ts` and `cycles.ts` break-evens matched on all three staging tickers. Future, not built: a "below break-even" badge on Signals; held shares on tickers not enabled for Pluto still get nothing (enable the ticker).
- **ETFs skip the earnings and ex-dividend checks (2026-10-10, Marcelo; committed, not pushed).** Plain-language: ETFs report no earnings and their ex-dividend dates are not checked, so Pluto can now trade DRAM (an ETF). Before, every DRAM contract was blocked as "earnings calendar unresolved" (53 of 53 on 10-09). Stocks are unchanged. **Technical:** `tickerCalendarRelevance.ts` (`isEtfSector` on `tickers.sector = 'ETF'`, `tickerCalendarProfile`): `loadTickerSignalsInputs` treats an ETF as resolved with no earnings dates, `fetchCalendarConflictContext` (Recovery Path) as resolved with no events. Dividends still feed option pricing.
- **Backtest caches no longer run in the live app (2026-10-13, Marcelo; pushed to staging 2026-10-13, prod at the next promotion).** Plain-language: on 10-12 the staging web process climbed to 95% of its memory during the session and stayed there after the close. Two caches built to speed up backtests also ran in the live app, where every price tick adds an entry and nothing ever removes one: about 90 MB once full. They now run only inside backtests; every result is the same. **Technical:** `exactInputCaches.ts`; `enableExactInputCaches()` is called by `scripts/run-backtest-job.ts` and `backtestScoringWorker.ts` only, otherwise `impliedVolatilityFromPrice` and `valueContract` skip `solvedImpliedVolatilities` / `valuationsByKey`. A local copy of the web process on Node 24 with simulated ticks and the same screens open: after 10 min, 170 MB of long-lived heap with the caches, 39 MB without.
- **Memory line: engine and kernel breakdown (2026-10-10, Marcelo; committed, not pushed).** Plain-language: the per-minute memory line now also shows how much memory the JavaScript engine itself holds next to what the operating system sees, to find why the staging web process (306 MB) and Pluto's agent (~295 MB after the 10-08 session) keep memory the engine reports as freed. Read after Monday's session. **Technical:** `formatMemoryDetail` in `processMemoryMonitor.ts` appends `v8Physical malloced peakMalloced contexts detached spaces=` (V8 heap statistics) and `anon fileBacked v8Pages=N (MB)` (resident 256 KB anonymous mappings in /proc/self/smaps, ~2 ms a minute). 10-10: the web process held 731 resident 256 KB pages (183 MB) against heapTotal 46 MB; Node 24.21 in isolation released such pages within 15 s.
- **A deploy no longer pauses Pluto (2026-10-09, Marcelo; pushed to staging 2026-10-09).** Plain-language: a new staging release (a code push or a config change) used to pause Pluto until someone pressed Resume. Now Pluto keeps whatever state it had: running stays running, paused stays paused with the same reason. The restart is still visible in the Event log ("Pluto's program started · version vNNN"). Production would still pause on a new release. The crash-loop pause now only counts restarts of the same release, so several pushes in an hour don't look like a crash loop. **Technical:** `guardBoot` in `agent.ts` pauses with `deploy` only when `APP_ENVIRONMENT=production`; the `agent_started` count filters on `payload->>'release'`. Tests: `agent.guardBoot.test.ts`.
- **Why rounds found nothing, and true model time (2026-10-09, Marcelo; pushed to staging 2026-10-09).** Plain-language: a round where every contract was filtered out used to record only how many were out; it now also says how many each rule removed, and how many were out on that rule alone (the rule actually binding), for opens and rolls, also on rounds that call the model. And the model's recorded response time was only the time until OpenRouter acknowledged the request (about 1–3 s); the answer itself takes about 10–25 s, which is why a round that called the model took 16–34 s. **Technical:** `PlutoRejection.codes` (one `PlutoRejectionCode` per reason, `openCandidateFailures` in `candidateFilters.ts`), `rejectionSummary.ts` (`rejectedBy`, `onlyBlocker`, `rolls`) in the `pass_skipped` and `model_called` payloads; `latencyMs` taken after the body in `modelClient.ts` and `decisionsClient.ts` (rows before this fix understate it about 10×). `tmp/replayPlutoFiltersAtTime.mts` prints the same counts for any past instant.
- **Held positions and macro events, prompt v3.8 (2026-10-08, Marcelo approved F1–F4 and the covered-call variant; built and tested, committed, not pushed).** Plain-language: Pluto now sees every put and covered call it manages, every round: how much of the premium is already earned, the most the position can still make, what closing costs, how far the stock is from the strike, and how much a bad move on the next big US release (Fed, CPI, GDP, elections) would cost. In the 5 sessions before such a release it is offered to close a put, or a whole covered call (the call and the shares together), while it is in profit, and decides; it is asked again as the profit grows or the release gets closer. Never at a loss, never sent by code alone. For new trades, each contract now says how many sessions until the release and how many it stays open after it: a release in the last day or two of a short contract is the riskiest case. Earnings rules are unchanged. **Technical:** `lib/macroEventTiming.ts` (weights heavy/medium/light, k = 2/1/0.5 normal days, `heaviestMacroEventBeforeExpiry`, sessions from `loadOpenDaysBetween`); `pluto/heldPositionMetrics.ts` (F2 and the covered-call formulas; stress at forecast vol after the event, intrinsic on expiry day; no stock commission estimate exists, so a covered call's close cost carries the option's only); F3 in `closeActions.ts` (`evaluateEventPutClose`, `evaluateEventCoveredCallClose`, one offer per leg with P2; gate read from the round's quotes by `closeGateVerdictFromQuotes`, the order route still runs the live gate); F4 `CloseOffer.reviewKey` in the change check; model may answer `close_position`; the model-chosen close path now sends each combo leg at its own limit with the other leg's reference (it used one price for every leg). Migration `20261008200001`. Verified: unit + DB tests incl. the approved worked examples ($25 put, $414/$390 and $20/$93 covered calls); payload built from dev data (COIN, INTC).
- **Pluto History paged, model input on decisions, phone rows (2026-10-08, Marcelo; on staging, API v178 `d738756`, app v120 `81f7120` + `ff6e19e`; phone rows from the approved mockup https://claude.ai/artifact/3cebBqP1QJx2t2VdtVZN98).** Plain language: Orders and Model decisions now show 50 per page with Previous/Next like the Event log, and the filters search everything, not just the newest rows. An expanded decision has "Show what the model saw". On a phone each decision row is readable: when and cost on top, then verdict and trigger, what the model chose, and the top-pick comparison. **Technical:** `GET /pluto/model-decisions` and `GET /pluto/orders` (`{ passes | actions, total }`, since/ticker/verdict/outcome in SQL); app `usePlutoHistoryPage`; page 1 reloads on pass/model/action/order/breaker events.
- **Pluto Settings form no longer undoes other people's changes (2026-10-08, Marcelo; app 889cfe1 pushed to staging).** Plain-language: with the Settings tab open, a setting someone else saved used to show as your own unsaved change, and saving anything would quietly put their value back. Now the form keeps only the fields you typed in; everything else always shows the saved value, and if someone saves a field you are also editing, the save bar and review dialog say "someone else changed it from X while you were editing". **Technical:** app `PlutoSettingsEdits`, `formStateWithEdits`, `applySettingsEdit`, `settingsChangedElsewhere` (plutoParameters.ts); `PlutoSettingsTab` holds edits, not a full copy. Verified with Playwright (four cases, desktop and phone).
- **Stress cap on Pluto's order size (2026-10-08, Marcelo; on staging since API v172 / app v117).** Plain-language: each new Pluto order is now also limited so that a large move against it before expiry (2σ of the stock's forecast volatility by default) would lose at most 1% of the account; longer expiries get fewer contracts. Both numbers are on the Pluto Settings tab (Capital group); 0 turns the cap off. It applies to cash-secured puts and to covered calls that buy shares, not to calls on shares already held; rolls keep their quantity. Code adds 0.5σ for a stock in an elevated-volatility stretch and 0.5σ when it is down more than one normal day today (fixed, Marcelo). Simulation basis: worst-1% loss per order flat at the budget across expiries, book tail 5.4% of the account → 1.5% at a 0.5% budget and 4.6% at 1.5% (1% not run on its own). **Technical:** `computeStressCap` in `postModelGates.ts` (another limit in the sizing gate, detail in the gate text; no forecast or live spot refuses the order), `pluto_settings.stress_risk_budget_pct` / `stress_sigmas` (migration `20261008100001`), sizing-only so no forced round on change (`settingsFieldsThatNeverChangeADecision`), `weekdaysAfterUntil` in `easternIsoDate.ts`; app `plutoParameters.ts`.
- **Decisions after the 10-07 review tests (2026-10-08, Marcelo).** Plain-language: no extra model calls (the 2-of-3 vote and the stress-figure prompt are declined); expiry-day rolls are shelved (a back-play found almost nothing to roll into and no proof rolling beats holding); stress-loss sizing (cap each order so a 2σ move to expiry loses at most a set share of the account) is still being evaluated. Findings: the Edge formula compares implied volatility on calendar days (÷365, from the snapshot date) with the forecast on 252 trading days, which flatters 1–4-day contracts that do not span a weekend (Pluto's SMCI Wed→Fri calls' +6–8 vp would be about −6 to −11 vp on one clock): a formula question awaiting Marcelo. The forecast itself is unbiased since Oct 2024 (variance ratio 1.00–1.12, 1–21 days). Test scripts in tmp/ (`testForecastAccuracy`, `simulateStressSizing`, `backtestExpiryDayRolls`, `backtestPlutoStressSizing`).
- **Fixes from the 10-07 session review (2026-10-08, Marcelo; on staging since API v172, worker 62275c5).** Plain-language: Pluto now records what its orders actually filled at, so its fill-price safety check works on new positions; new positions appear within seconds of filling instead of up to a minute; trade times are IBKR's, not the moment they were saved; a just-filled order still counts against the limits until its position exists; and Pluto no longer asks the model about tickers it may not trade yet (60-minute cooldown after a fill). **Technical:** `watchPlutoOrder` waits with `shouldWaitForFills` and judges slippage only on complete fills (warning event otherwise); worker requests a reconciliation 3 s after the last buffered opening fill (`openingFillReconciliationDelayMs`), ignores a combo's BAG execution, reads IBKR's UTC time form `YYYYMMDD-HH:mm:ss`, logs any unreadable time and falls back to arrival time (not drain time); `orderFillsPendingSql` + `inFlightOrderRequestsQuery` make a filled order with unrecorded fills count as in flight (platform order gate and Pluto's book), as a working Pluto order and as a taken contract for 5 min; ticker cooldown applied before the model (`tickerCooldownStatus`, `opensBlockedReason`), post-model gate kept. Worker files touched: the push deploys the worker. Not done (Marcelo): no-op rounds keep writing full records until he trusts Pluto; 2DTE stays in scope (sizing proposal pending); expiry-day rolls under analysis.
- **Fixes from the first live session, points 1–6 (2026-10-07, decisions by Marcelo; built and tested, pushed to staging 2026-10-07, live checks pending; screen built from the approved mockup https://claude.ai/artifact/HW3teTrnVuzEED68UEtNRi).** Plain-language: Pluto froze for three hours on 10-06 because its instructions contradicted themselves; its process used 800 MB of a 512 MB allowance; it re-quoted options needlessly; and two tickers were never looked at. What changed:
  - *Pluto's book:* every open position on a Pluto-enabled ticker, whoever opened it, except hedges, counts toward Pluto's budget and its 15-position cap; switching a ticker off hands all its positions back to people (supersedes the 10-06 "Pluto's own keep counting" rule). People's working orders on enabled tickers count as in-flight. `book.ts`.
  - *Prompt v3.3:* `managed_positions` replaces `open_pluto_positions`; closes and rolls say `opened_by`; the grade sentence follows the setting; Covered Call entries explained; the trigger's kind only; recent trades carry their outcome. Concerns are per ticker (`{symbol, concern}`); a flagged ticker is never traded (`flagged_ticker` gate), the others are judged normally. Telegram: one message per flagged ticker, a reminder at most hourly, one when cleared (`concernAlerts.ts`), under the existing verbosity switch.
  - *Memory:* the cause was `easternDateIso` building a new `Intl.DateTimeFormat` per call (~25 KB of native memory each until a GC), called ~150 times a second by Pluto's loop; merged into the cached `easternIsoDate` (48 call sites), other per-call formatters cached, a guard test forbids new ones. The elevated-volatility flag no longer copies the bar history per window (same results on all 33 tickers). A memory line every 60 s on the agent and web dyno, an ops alert at 90% of dyno memory (back under at 80%), heap snapshot on SIGUSR2.
  - *Rounds:* a round decides from the Day Signals data whether the model will be called and only then bursts quotes; a round where every ticker is cooling down is skipped at once; the account summary is reused for 60 s. The line-pool race (contracts added mid-reconcile were paused and resubscribed) is fixed.
  - *Day Signals:* the daily cap of three re-ranks is now a 15-minute minimum gap between looks at a ticker; unpooled tickers are also re-checked every 60 minutes (one per cycle, most overdue first). Migration `20261007300002`.
  - *Screen:* the Tickers tab has a "Day Signals today" column (watched, not watched with the next re-check and trigger prices, no surface, waiting for today's capture); on a phone a one-line note under the ticker. `GET /pluto/day-signals-watch`, `daySignalsWatchStatus.ts`. Concerns show their ticker ("All data" for the whole message); an abstention reads "Abstained" everywhere.
  - *Opening look (point 5):* waits for today's `day_signals_seed` run to finish (success or failure), not the first fitted ticker. Not finished by 10:30 ET: runs anyway, logged as a warning event, marked incomplete on the pass and sent to Telegram (whatever the verbosity setting); one more full round, with a Telegram "ready now", follows once the seed lands. Until the look has run, a round offers only closes and rolls of held positions. In memory on purpose: a restart runs the look once more. `openingLook.ts`.
  - *Macro and earnings timing (point 6):* an event counts while still ahead. A macro release counts until its release time (a 14:00 ET release today is flagged at 10:00, not at 14:05); earnings today count unless reported before the open. Changes the Signals screen too. `expirySpansMacroEvent` / `expirySpansEarnings`, `loadEarningsDatesNotYetReported`; `MacroEvent.eventAtIso`.
  - *Prompt v3.4 (point 6):* the macro flag asks for a clearly stronger edge rather than ruling a trade out, weighted by release (v3.5, for the new major-event set: heavy: Fed rate decision, CPI, US presidential election; medium: US midterm elections; light: GDP); earnings outrank any macro release, and no open or roll may still be open when the company reports (code already removes those it knows of); `macro_events` lists only releases still to come. Migrations `20261007300003` (v3.4) and `20261007400002` (v3.5). Wording approved by Marcelo.
  - *Earnings follow-ups (2026-10-07, Marcelo; built and tested, pushed to staging 2026-10-07):* the nightly calendar capture replaces a date TradingView has moved (it used to add the new one and keep the old: HOOD carried 10-27 and 11-04), for earnings and ex-dividend dates, past rows untouched. A new data check ("Earnings dates", ops monitor and pre-open readiness) fails when a ticker has two earnings dates less than 45 days apart, looking 30 days back. Formula P3: a single-leg short (cash-secured put) whose expiry is on or after the next earnings announcement is bought back by code, without the model, in the last 5 sessions before the announcement (the report day counts for an after-close report) and only at a profit at the ask; covered calls are closed whole by Formula P3b (below). Rolls already never cross a known earnings date; now tested. `closeActions.ts`, `tradingviewCalendarService.ts`, `dataInvariants.ts`.
  - *Doubtful earnings dates (Marcelo 2026-10-27; built and tested, not committed):* plain language: when a company's expected report date passes without a report, TradingView shows "today" as the next date each day (NBIS 10-22, 10-23, 10-26), and then jumped NBIS to 2027-02-16, skipping its November report. The capture no longer stores that stand-in (today, no report time) and removes dates between TradingView's last report and today that passed unreported; the earnings fetch looks 150 days back so every ticker comes back by its last report. A next date more than 150 days after the last report, or none more than 60 days after it, is doubtful: Signals marks the ticker's contracts "earnings calendar unresolved" and Pluto won't open them (live only; backtests unchanged), and a new data check "Next earnings date" (ops monitor, pre-open readiness) names the ticker. `tickerCalendarRelevance.ts` (`earningsNextDateDoubt`), `calendarConflict.ts`, `signalsStore.ts`, `tradingviewCalendarService.ts`, `dataInvariants.ts`. The three NBIS stand-in rows were deleted on staging and prod 2026-10-27.
  - *Failed rounds (point 7, 2026-10-07, decisions by Marcelo; built and tested, pushed to staging 2026-10-07; staging's stuck NOK pass closed by the sweep):* a round that throws now closes its pass ("round failed: …") instead of leaving it running; the account summary is read once per round (free cash from the system checks' read); a failed forced round is retried once after 60 s, and the opening look counts as done only once its round has run (housekeeping re-queues a failed one; its data-state messages go out after it runs); three failed rounds in a row send one Telegram alert and one when rounds complete again; passes unfinished 10 minutes after starting are closed as abandoned at boot and every housekeeping tick (clears staging's stuck 10-06 NOK pass). `roundFailures.ts`, `closeAbandonedPlutoPasses`.
  - *Point 8, no change (Marcelo, 2026-10-07):* a leg Pluto only closed (opened by a person) keeps counting in Pluto's realized P&L and scoreboard, as `actionRealizedPnl.ts` does today; intended.
  - *Point 9 (Marcelo, 2026-10-07):* the "Pessimistic" fill figure is removed (Track record tile, Orders column, API, `pluto_actions.pessimistic_pnl` dropped by migration `20261007500001`); not a metric he uses. The fill-slippage breaker is unchanged.
  - *Point 10 (Marcelo, 2026-10-07):* the daily-loss check names the close date plainly ("vs 2026-10-05 close"); a ticker is out for the round when today's move exceeds 3× its normal day (forecast volatility × 100 / √252; setting `maxDayMoveMultiple`, migration `20261007500002`, replaces the fixed ±6%: NOK's limit is 12.7%, BSBR's 7.2%); the scoreboard counts abstentions inside no-order ("92 no-order (38 abstained)"). Nothing Pluto records is deleted any more: the 90-day event pruning is removed, and passes are not pruned (Marcelo: keep all history for later analysis). The loosened staging settings stay as he set them. OpenRouter's `:floor` routing stays, including its fallback to the default tier when flex is busy.
  - *Point 11, connection budget (Marcelo, 2026-10-07):* the Pluto agent was outside the 20-connection budget (pool 4 + LISTEN 1, worst case 23). Now agent pool 2 + LISTEN 1 = 3, web session store 3 → 2: web 7 + worker 6 + two jobs 4 + agent 3 = 20, no spare for psql at the 10:00 ET peak (accepted; 13 in use on an ordinary morning). `databaseConnectionBudget.ts`, `db/connection.ts`. A larger database plan is the fix when Pluto runs on prod.
  - *Covered calls before earnings, Formula P3b (Marcelo, 2026-10-07; built and tested, not pushed):* in the last 5 sessions before an earnings report that the call's expiry spans, code (not the model) closes the whole covered call, call bought back and shares sold in one order with a limit per part at the mids, when it is in profit: live cycle P&L minus half of each spread (call ask − mid, stock mid − bid) above zero. Never offered to the model; not retried for an hour after an attempt that did not fill; skipped while any order on the position is working. P3 (cash-secured puts) now also requires the close gate and the same one-hour rule. New action kind `close_position`; prompt v3.6 (migration `20261007500003`).
  - *Audit of the day's changes (2026-10-07; every finding fixed and tested, not pushed):* plain-language: a full review of everything changed today found bugs, fixed before they reached anyone. Pluto: a round skipped by its data checks no longer counts as the opening look; a failure after the model was called is not retried (no second model call); automatic closes count against the daily action cap; no burst of rounds while opens are blocked; housekeeping cannot overlap itself. Day Signals: daylight-saving midnight, a failed re-rank read no longer looks like "nothing to re-rank", trigger prices rounded so the shown price always triggers. Event log: a ticker filter matches the exact symbol (ED no longer matches "REJECTED"); page 2 no longer gets stuck when an event arrives mid-load. Earnings: today's before-open report is no longer "next earnings"; dates read in Eastern time; the two-dates-too-close check ignores pairs entirely in the past; a moved date is replaced without a moment with no date. Alerts chat: orders cancelled before reaching IBKR (a review panel closed) are not announced; a partly recorded fill says so; shares moved into the next position are not reported as sold; an expiry message counts as told only if delivered. Ticker setup: never runs twice for one ticker; a held ticker stays in the daily capture while its setup runs; one bad row no longer stops the restart of the others. Also: the Signals switch refreshes Price Performance; Genosuke's shortlist tool no longer asks for a strategy. Audit tests are the `*.audit-*.test.ts` files.
  - *Audit follow-ups, round 2 (2026-10-07; decisions by Marcelo where noted; built and tested, pushed to staging 2026-10-07):* plain language: the remaining review notes are fixed too.
    - **Alerts chat.** A new position is announced once its opening order has finished filling, so at its full size. A roll placed in the app reads "closed in the app", even when its buy-back fill lands before the roll reads filled. The most common close reason now has a label. A zero P&L shows no plus sign. Genosuke no longer says "cancelled at IBKR" about orders it discarded itself.
    - **P&L % base (Marcelo).** A closed covered call's P&L % is measured against what its shares cost, not its strike: everywhere positionQueries' capitalDeployed is used (close notice, expiry message, Positions).
    - **Pluto switch.** Switching Pluto (or Signals) off on a ticker where Pluto manages open positions now asks first (Marcelo: warn only). An order Pluto built before its ticker was switched off is refused at confirm. After code sends an automatic close for a ticker, the model is offered nothing for that ticker in the same round (it could only end "blocked").
    - **Readiness (Marcelo).** When only "Pluto running" fails, the 10-minute re-check reuses the passing probes instead of opening an IBKR probe connection every time.
    - **Heroku (Marcelo).** The Procfile runs node directly, so a heap snapshot (SIGUSR2) reaches node and no longer kills npm. The memory alert counts swap (VmSwap) as well as RSS.
    - **Data.** Order calendar warnings and the Calendar page use the Eastern date; today's before-open report no longer warns. A Signals screen left open overnight reloads for the new day. Price Performance shows IV / Avg Vol only from the ticker's latest bar's snapshot. IBKR execution times are correct on DST Sundays.
    - **Ticker setup.** A cut-off setup can no longer overwrite a closed run; a ticker removed mid-setup says so.
    - **Event log.** A repeated `?categories=` works. An index on `pluto_events(type, occurred_at)` (migration `20261007500004`). The session filter says it is an Eastern day. "What the model saw" shows each decision's date and who opened each position.
    - **Checked, no change needed:** staging preboot is off with one web dyno; the memory line reads `limit=512MB` on staging; Node 24 (VPS v24.19.0) gives YYYY-MM-DD for en-CA.
  - *Pluto marks (Marcelo, 2026-10-07; pushed to staging 2026-10-07):* a superscript P after every ticker whose Pluto switch is on, on the Signals screen (table and phone cards) and Pulse (positions, top signals, best signal); a solid "Pluto" badge after the sector badge in the ticker modal. App `usePlutoEnabledSymbols` (reads `/pluto/tickers`, refreshes on ticker_enabled / ticker_disabled events); named classes `.pluto-managed-mark` / `.pluto-managed-badge` in theme.css.
  - *Worker file touched* (`ibkrGatewayParseExecutionTime.ts`, cached formatter): the push deploys the worker.
- **Event log ticker trace, "What the model saw", blocked trades no longer read as done (2026-10-07, Marcelo; pushed to staging 2026-10-07).** Plain-language: filtering the Event log by a ticker now shows that ticker's whole story: events naming it, the model's calls and no-order results from every pass that looked at it, and (muted, tagged "All tickers") settings changes, pauses and other events that apply to every ticker. Every row starts with its category ("Info · Pass started · SMCI"). A model call's row opens "What the model saw" in place: each ticker's context, close offers, rolls and candidates (the filtered ticker first), account, parameters and recent decisions. The Categories menu has All / None. The model's list of its recent decisions now says what happened to each trade (blocked and why, working, filled, cancelled…): before, a blocked buyback read as executed and the model kept abstaining on later passes. Passes with no tickers of their own (opening look, settings change) now log the tickers they look at, so the ticker filter finds them; older such passes stay unfindable by ticker. **Technical:** `GET /pluto/events?ticker=` matches the name, the pass (`passId` of a `pass_started` naming it) or no ticker/pass key, and returns `appliesToAllTickers`; `recentDecisionsForPrompt` in `prompt.ts` (+ `outcome` / `outcome_detail`, system prompt describes them); app `PlutoModelInputs.tsx` reads `GET /pluto/passes/:id`.
- **Pluto readiness check reports an off or paused Pluto (2026-10-07, Marcelo; pushed to staging 2026-10-07).** Plain-language: the 6:00 and 9:20 ET messages used to say "ready" even when Pluto was paused or switched off, and the check did not run at all while Pluto was Off. Now it runs on every open day whatever the state, with a fourth tick, "Pluto running" (on and not paused), next to IBKR / API sign-in / OpenRouter; a paused or Off Pluto alerts at 6:00 ET, again at 9:20 ET (\"Pluto is not running… Resume it or switch it on\"), and says so once when fixed. That test never causes a pause itself, and an Off or already-paused Pluto is not paused again when a probe fails at 9:20 ET. Off Pluto means two Telegram posts every open day for as long as it stays Off (Marcelo's call). Not covered: a pause after 9:20 ET is only caught by the pause's own alert. Not run end to end (needs IBKR); first live proof is the next open-day 6:00 ET post.
- **Pluto screen: Live panels, local times, Event log categories and paging (2026-10-07, Marcelo; pushed to staging 2026-10-07).** Plain-language: the Latest model decision card and the Activity feed were empty because they only looked at the newest 30 passes / 200 events, almost all routine; the server now filters first (`/pluto/passes?modelCalled=true`, `/pluto/events` with `categories`, `types`, `excludeTypes`, `ticker`, `session`, `limit`, `offset`, body `{ events, total }`). The paused banner's restart count reads its own `types=paused` request. Times of events and actions are in the viewer's local time; the top bar and market-hours rules stay ET. Event log: every event has a category (System, Config, Safety, Analysis, Trading, Info; `pass_started`/`pass_skipped` are Info, `session_schedule`/`warning` System), a categories dropdown (Info unticked by default, saved in localStorage), a ticker box, a session date (Eastern trading day) with an All sessions button, 100 events per page, and every stored field shown under each row. Categories live in `src/pluto/ledger.ts` (`plutoEventCategoryByType`). Awaiting: Marcelo may ask to hide fields on chatty event types behind a Show details option.
- **Merged into main (2026-10-06, Marcelo; superseded the same day: switched on for the first staging session).** Plain-language: Pluto's code is on staging but does nothing until it is set up and switched on (the `agent` process is at 0, `PLUTO_ENABLED` is not set, and Pluto starts Off). The weekend promotion carries the code to prod, dormant there too; Marcelo plans to run Pluto on prod in about two months. Shared changes riding along (worker: Inactive / IBKR refusal codes end orders as `rejected`; half-day close read from IBKR at the 09:00 UTC structure job; share-source and partial-close links) apply to everyone from the promotion on. Staging setup is next (runbook below).
- **IBKR lines booked only while used (2026-10-06, Marcelo).** Plain-language: Pluto used to book its quote-burst lines (10) for the whole session; now it books one line per ticker plus SPY, and the burst lines only for the few seconds a burst runs. Pluto's process now books under its own `pluto_agent` row (priority); before, it shared the web dyno's `marketDataPool` row, so the two overwrote each other's count. Pluto sidebar icon is now a paw.
- **Pluto's own Telegram bot (2026-10-06, Marcelo).** Plain-language: every Pluto message (from the agent and from the Pluto screen's actions) now comes from a Pluto bot, one per environment, posting in the same alerts group as everything else. `PLUTO_TELEGRAM_BOT_TOKEN` is required on the agent (no fallback to the ops bot); staging = @pluto_staging_bot, set; prod needs its own bot at Pluto's prod setup. Telegram verbosity is now `actions` or `off` only ("all" never sent anything extra; every decision is on the screen).
- **Pluto pre-open readiness check (2026-10-06, Marcelo).** Plain-language: on market days Pluto tests its own IBKR connection, its sign-in to the API and its OpenRouter key/credit at 6:00 ET (same timetable as the platform readiness). Announced like the platform check (Marcelo): the 6:00 ET result and the 9:20 ET verdict always post from the Pluto bot, passing or not; while failing it re-tests every 10 minutes and posts only changes; if anything still fails at 9:20 ET Pluto pauses until someone presses Resume. The status card shows the result while Pluto waits. Technical: `src/pluto/readiness.ts` (schedule = `readinessSchedule` from `preOpenReadiness.ts`), `readinessProbes.ts` (one-shot IBKR connection + `reqCurrentTime`, fresh service sign-in via `InternalApiClient.verifySignIn`, OpenRouter `/api/v1/credits` ≥ `daily_cost_ceiling_usd`), `pluto_state.readiness` jsonb, pause reason `readiness`, event `readiness_check`.
- **Pluto staging went live 2026-10-06** (first session that day, window 10:00–15:30 ET, 5 tickers: BMNR, COIN, HOOD, INTC, WDC). Screen: activity times in the browser's local time and clock style; top-bar and window countdowns show seconds in their last minute. **Decided (Marcelo, 2026-10-06): an enabled ticker is Pluto's to manage — it may buy back or roll any position on it, whoever opened it** (first case: a human SMCI $42.5 put, buyback chosen by the model at 10:16 ET, blocked by the fresh re-check). Switching a ticker off stops Pluto managing every position there; Pluto's own still count toward its capital and position cap. **Pending:** rotate the Pluto service password (it appeared in a pasted terminal output) and revoke/reissue the staging bot token (pasted in chat); both are config changes, so do them after the close and Resume once.
Plain-language: Pluto is a new bot that will trade Iorio's Signals on its own, on the staging paper account only, for as long as it takes to prove it works. Iorio keeps producing every number; a language model makes the final "trade this one or not" call; code checks that call against hard limits before any order exists. Genosuke stays as the Telegram chat bot and is not being extended.
- **Design of record** lives in the guiding artifact (Phase 4–5 section, "Pluto"). Decisions so far (Marcelo, 2026-09-28): staging-only via an existence layer (dedicated `agent` Procfile process scaled to 0 on prod + env var + `APP_ENVIRONMENT` check); modes off/on only, unsupervised from day one on paper; the model picks a candidate by id and never authors order fields; code sizes; separate Pluto capital budget; scope = opens, rolls (Formula 3j) and constrained closes (unstructured shares at positive cycle P&L, never at a loss; short-leg buybacks only when locking a profit, formula pending sign-off); pause + pause-and-cancel buttons, resume behind a confirm; default paused after a deploy or crash loop, not after Heroku's daily cycle; breakers wait for a human; two LLM calls must agree, second call only when the first says trade; event-gated LLM calls (candidate-set hash), not fixed cadence; OpenRouter (model switchable by config); no shortlist notes in the prompt; per-ticker `bot_enabled` flag on the shortlist, default off; Juan and Marce both can pause/resume; every pass also logs the deterministic Edge $ top pick for comparison; pessimistic bid-fill P&L logged next to the paper fill.
- **Platform gaps to close on `main` first, before Pluto** (all benefit human trading too): no kill switch (needs a DB flag checked in `tradingGate`, in the worker before `placeOrder`, and by Pluto); close gate (`closeLiveState`) and delta-band compliance enforced in the browser only; risk limits enforced only for Signals-originated orders, `strategy_settings.max_*` never enforced; order states lie (`rejected` never written, IBKR `Inactive` ignored, rejection dropped unless the row is `submitted`, partial fill ends the day as `cancelled`); no surface max age; open interest/volume captured but not loaded into Signals.
- **Branch plan**: `pluto` branch in both repos, worked in git worktrees with their own `.env`/local DB/ports, `main` merged in weekly (never rebased), single local merge + push when ready; initial staging deploy with `agent` scaled to 0, full test battery, then scale to 1.
