- **Round 3 approved 2026-09-28** (full detail in the artifact's "Approved 2026-09-28, round 3"): gap-fix designs 1–9 (`platform_controls.trading_halt`, Risk & Limits toggle, worker + confirm enforcement, server-side close gate and delta band, `strategy_settings` maxima at confirm, honest order states + `filled_quantity`, `stale_surface` beyond one session, OI/volume into candidates); Pluto data model (`pluto_settings`/`_audit`, `pluto_state`, `pluto_passes`, `pluto_decisions`, `pluto_actions`, `pluto_events`, `shortlist_entries.bot_enabled`, `order_requests.pluto_action_id`); gates, prompt/output contract, execution policy; every parameter default (amendments: per-ticker 10 %, per-sector off, no contracts cap, model GPT-6 Luna via OpenRouter); Formulas P1 (unstructured close) and P2 (short-leg buyback).
- **Round 4 approved 2026-09-28**: real-time trigger design replaces the 5-min pass (own dyno + own IBKR connection + priority line reservation; 1 stock line per enabled ticker, cap 15, + SPY + 10-line burst pool; spot-move 1.5 % / grade-crossing / Day-Signals-refresh / held-leg triggers; 20 s coalescing, 10 min per-ticker and 60 s global model cooldown; opening look only, other decision points dropped; 60 s housekeeping tick; Pluto 8 msg/s, web pool 35 msg/s; SPY market-stress breaker at −3 %; contracts ≤ 20 % of session volume). Max actions per session raised to 10. Model `openai/gpt-6-luna` (both calls). All design decisions closed; only the screen mockup remains, after the backend exists.
- **Alternative model to evaluate (Marcelo, 2026-09-28): `typesafe/jev-1.13` on OpenRouter** (https://openrouter.ai/typesafe/jev-1.13). Feasibility tests required before it can be a candidate for Pluto: structured-output / JSON-schema support through OpenRouter, latency per decision call, cost per call, and decision quality against `openai/gpt-6-luna` on the same recorded candidate sets (the ledger's deterministic-pick comparison). Checked 2026-09-28: OpenRouter's public model list carries only `typesafe/jev-router` (a routing meta-model with dynamic pricing that "picks the best model and reasoning effort for each request", built on `typesafe/jev-latest`); `jev-1.13` itself is not in the public list, so the exact id/pricing must be confirmed on the page. Caveat to test explicitly: a router that silently changes the underlying model per request conflicts with Pluto's "served model id is logged, never a silent model swap" rule — the test must show which model actually answered.
- **Where the gap fixes live (Marcelo, 2026-09-28): on the `pluto` branch, not on `main`.** They were first committed on `main` (api `8bc2933`…`0f0699d`, app `c1865d8`…`d687202`, never pushed); Marcelo asked for them to be moved into the branch, so `main` is reset to before them and they reach staging with the Pluto merge.
- **Gap fixes 1–3 built 2026-09-28 (trading halt)**: `platform_controls` table (`trading_halt` row: enabled/reason/set_by/set_at), `lib/platformControls.ts`; `tradingGate` reports a new `halted` state that outranks offline/blocked; enforced at `POST /positions/orders/:id/confirm` (409) and inside the worker immediately before `placeOrder` (row → `error`, alert reverted, Telegram), cancels untouched; `GET|PUT /risk-limits/trading-halt` (reason required to halt, ≤ 300 chars, any user); `trading_halt_changed` notification (toast + instant top-bar refresh) and a Telegram audit line. App: Trading-halt card at the top of Risk & Limits (red "Halt all trading" / green "Resume trading" behind a ConfirmModal with a reason box), red "Trading halted" chip in the top bar + popover, "Trading: HALTED/allowed" row on the Pulse Gateway card. Verified locally: routes + DB row, confirm 409 while halted, UI in both themes on desktop and phone. Worker file touched → ask before pushing.
- **Gap fix 4 built 2026-09-28 (close gate server-side)**: `lib/closeGate.ts` — the Close form's leg/contract/cycle loading moved into `loadCloseLiveInputs` (the SSE route now uses it too) and `evaluateCloseGateForPosition` runs the same `deriveCloseLiveState` once (pool subscription, settle grace, unsubscribe; fail closed). Enforced at `POST /positions/:id/close` (409, nothing written) and at confirm for `close_position` rows. Verified locally: close build 409 "market is closed", no order row.
- **Gap fix 5 built 2026-09-28 (delta band server-side)**: `lib/deltaBandGate.ts` — opening orders with an option leg get the option's live pooled delta (settle grace) checked with the same `checkDeltaCompliance` the panel uses, fail closed when no delta; 409 at confirm. Verified locally: opening confirm 409 "Live delta isn't available yet" after ~3 s with no IBKR. Rolls stay ungated (as before).
- **~~Gap fix 6~~ withdrawn 2026-10-01 (Marcelo): orders placed by Juan, Marce or Genosuke are blocked only by the limits that already applied (the Signals tab, for orders built from a Signals candidate); the extra limits are for Pluto only, which already has its own budget and caps on top of the Signals tab. `strategyOrderLimits.ts` removed, `signalOrderLimits.ts` and the confirm/quote-stream wrapper restored to their pre-Pluto form, Risk & Limits help text restored. Original entry:** Gap fix 6 built 2026-09-28 (strategy_settings maxima enforced): `signalOrderLimits.ts` refactored into a generic `evaluateOrderLimits` + pure `applyOrderLimitThresholds` (unit-tested) with the Signals wrapper unchanged; new `strategyOrderLimits.ts` loads the Trade Alerts tab's five maxima (position %, ticker %, sector %, aggregate CSP collateral %, min cash reserve %). `positions.ts`'s wrapper is now `evaluateOrderLimitsForOrderRequest`: Signals orders → Signals tab, every other opening order/roll → strategy settings, fail closed; enforced at confirm and shown in the Order Review quote stream's `signalLimits` field so the panel blocks before confirm. Block messages name the tab. Trade Alerts tab help text no longer says "not enforced". Verified locally: confirm 409 "above the Trade Alerts tab's … max position size" with a temporarily tiny ceiling.
- **Gap fix 7 built 2026-09-28 (honest order states)**: `order_requests` gains `filled_quantity`, `remaining_quantity`, `ibkr_status` (migration `20260928000002`); rules in `lib/orderRequestStatus.ts` (unit-tested): Cancelled/ApiCancelled/Inactive with fills → `partially_filled` and final via `ibkr_status` (no more "cancelled" hiding a partial fill); Inactive with no fills → `rejected`; refusal codes 110/200/201/203/321/322 → `rejected` in any non-final status (was: any code → `error`, only while `submitted`); 202 treated as informational; other codes unchanged. Worker orderStatus listener writes the counts and raw status on every change; stale-order reconciliation uses recorded executions to end a cancelled order as partially_filled. Serializer + blotter expose the fields; app shows "Partially filled — 2 of 5, remainder not filled / still working" and hides Cancel on a final partial; Genosuke reports `rejected`. Worker file touched → ask before pushing.
- **Gap fix 8 built 2026-09-28 (surface max age)**: `loadTickerSignalsInputs` now passes `oldestAcceptableSnapshotDateIso` = the previous open session (market-calendar aware); `scoreTicker` returns `unscoredReason: "stale_surface"` with no candidates when the newest snapshot is older than that, with a "Not scored: the newest surface is from …" caveat; a snapshot from exactly the previous session is still scored with the existing stale caveat. App label added. Unit-tested. Pluto will additionally require today's fit (its own gate).
- **Gap fix 9 built 2026-09-28 (liquidity into candidates)**: `loadQuotes` now selects `open_interest`, `volume`, `bid_size`, `ask_size`; `SignalQuote` carries them optionally and every `SignalCandidate` exposes `openInterest`, `volume`, `bidSize`, `askSize` (from the 10:00 snapshot; day/live quotes keep the snapshot's values; null when the capture had no tick). Stored with orders via `signal_snapshot` automatically. Modal gains an "OI / Vol" column with sizes in the tooltip. No filter on the human side; Pluto's min-OI/min-volume filters read these. - **Pluto backend built 2026-09-28 on the `pluto` branch** (worktrees `../iorio-reloaded-api-pluto` / `../iorio-reloaded-app-pluto`, local DBs `iorio_pluto` / `iorio_pluto_test`, API on :3040, app dev server needs `npm run dev -- --port 3041`; branch commits `5dfa3ba`…`be442fd`+). Plain-language: everything Pluto needs to run on its own exists except its screen — the tables it writes to, the switches that control it, the rules that decide what the model may see, the model call itself, the checks after the model answers, and the process that sends orders through the same routes humans use. Built and unit-tested (46 Pluto tests + the schema round-trip on the Pluto test DB): migration `20260928100001` (`pluto_settings` + audit, `pluto_state`, `pluto_passes`, `pluto_decisions`, `pluto_actions`, `pluto_events`, `shortlist_entries.bot_enabled`, `order_requests.pluto_action_id`); `src/pluto/` — `settingsStore`, `stateStore` (off/on, pause kinds manual/deploy/crash_loop/breaker, breakers need a human reset), `ledger`, `counters`, `orders` (pause drops unsent Pluto orders; pause-and-cancel also cancels working ones), `candidateFilters` (every approved dial; ticker requires today's fit, YZ63, live spot, day-change cap), `inputHash`, `systemChecks` (13 checks incl. early-close days, window, worker, account, daily loss, cost/calls/actions caps), `decisionSchema` (strict JSON schema + parser + two-call agreement), `modelClient` (OpenRouter, strict response_format, provider fallbacks off, served model + cost logged), `prompt` (system prompt + compact payload, top 3 per ticker per strategy), `book`, `postModelGates` (code sizes from the tightest room; covered calls use free shares first, then Covered Call entries sized like a put at the live spot — Marcelo 2026-09-28; volume-share cap; mid limit inside the market), `executor` (build → confirm through `/positions/*` with `plutoActionId`; watch → cancel after the timeout; rejection/error trips `order_error`), `marketWatch` (priority line reservation, one stock line per enabled ticker + SPY, spot-move triggers, quote bursts), `closeActions` (Formulas P1/P2 as offers; odd lots automatic; P2 limited to single-leg positions), `passRunner`, `agent` (boot guards for deploy + crash loop, 45 s heartbeat as `worker_health.pluto_agent`, 60 s housekeeping, 30 s day-quote poll, 20 s coalescing, opening look), `src/plutoAgent.ts` + Procfile `agent:` + `npm run agent` / `agent:dev`; routes `/pluto/*` (state, mode, pause, resume, breaker reset, settings + audit, passes, actions, events, tickers) and `PATCH /shortlist/:id/bot-enabled` (cap enforced); shared `lib/internalApiClient.ts` (Genosuke's client generalized). Env: `PLUTO_ENABLED`, `PLUTO_API_BASE_URL`, `PLUTO_SERVICE_USERNAME/PASSWORD` (service user via `manage-user`), `OPENROUTER_API_KEY`; refuses to start in production. Verified locally: every route by curl, a real OpenRouter call to `openai/gpt-6-luna` (schema-valid trade, $0.0004, 1.6 s), the agent booting/heartbeating/pausing after a simulated deploy/stopping cleanly, and a manual pass recording all system checks outside market hours.
- **Session close from IBKR built 2026-09-28 (Marcelo: "trade the actual hours, expose it on the screen")**. Plain-language: instead of skipping half days, Pluto now asks IBKR when today's session closes and trades up to 30 minutes before it. `market_calendar` gains `close_time` / `close_time_source` / `close_time_read_at` (migration `20260928100002`); `ibkr/fetchLiquidHours.ts` (parser unit-tested on both IBKR formats) reads SPY's liquid hours; `pluto/sessionSchedule.ts` writes each returned day's close (IBKR returns the current week, verified live: 5 days, 4.3 s on the shared connection), and `resolvePlutoSession` gives the effective window end = min(configured end, close − 30 min) and a cancel-by time = close − 2 min. `computeMarketSessionStatus` uses the stored close for every screen. The agent reads it once per day in housekeeping (retried every 60 s, one warning event per day), records a `session_schedule` event; system check `early_close_day` is replaced by `session_close` (fails closed until IBKR has answered today or the fallback list names the day); `trading_window` uses the effective end; `watchPlutoOrder` cancels at the earlier of the configured minutes and cancel-by; `GET /pluto/state` returns `session` for the screen. The hardcoded early-close list remains only as a fallback. Constants (not settings, Marcelo to veto): 30 min window margin, 2 min cancel margin.
- **Pluto screen built 2026-09-28 (mockup approved by Marcelo the same day, round 2: Pause at normal size, all 51 parameters always shown in a full-width card, session close on the status line)**. Plain-language: the page where Juan and Marce watch Pluto and control it — one card with the status, the mode switch, Pause / Pause-and-cancel / Resume and any tripped breaker; four tiles (today's actions, calls and cost; Pluto's book against its budget; working orders; SPY and today's close); a live timeline of everything Pluto did; the model's decisions with its reasons next to the Edge $ top pick; an Actions table with gates and outcomes; the per-ticker Pluto switches (also on the Shortlist tab, same component); and every parameter with help text, saved through a confirm dialog listing each old → new value. App (`pluto` branch, `b8b09c8`): `api/pluto.ts`, `lib/plutoParameters.ts` (catalogue), `lib/plutoPresentation.ts` (event/action wording), `components/pluto/*`, `pages/PlutoPage.tsx`, route `/pluto` + nav (IconPlanet), `PlutoBotToggle` column on `ShortlistTab`. Live updates: every `pluto_event` notification refreshes state/timeline (and passes/actions/tickers/settings by event type, 400 ms coalesced); state re-polled every 30 s for the heartbeat age. API (`0d58aec`): `GET /pluto/state` adds `book` (committed $, positions, last night's NLV) and `session`; `GET /pluto/passes` carries each pass's compact decisions and actions; `GET /shortlist` carries `botEnabled` + who/when. Verified with Playwright against the Pluto worktree (:3040/:3041, needs `FRONTEND_ORIGIN=http://localhost:3041` in the worktree `.env`): mode on, resume, pause, ticker toggle from both screens and a parameter save each checked in `pluto_state` / `shortlist_entries` / `pluto_settings` + audit + `pluto_events`; timeline updated live from the notification; light + dark, desktop + 390 px (no horizontal scroll). Also fixed two pre-existing app type errors from the fill-tracking commit (`OrderReviewPanel` `statusLabel`, Trade Blotter status label) — Vite never type-checks, `tsc -p tsconfig.app.json` does.
- **Settings change schedules a pass (Marcelo, 2026-09-28)**: the agent subscribes to the app notification channel and enqueues a forced `settings_changed` pass when a saved change touches anything that can alter a decision (operational-only fields such as Telegram verbosity, burst sizes, coalescing, timeouts, caps on tickers/messages, prompt version do not); the prompt's window end is now the session's effective end.
- **Realized P&L on Pluto actions, read-time join (Marcelo, 2026-09-28)**: `pluto/actionRealizedPnl.ts` derives each action's realized P&L when the actions/passes routes read them (the `realized_pnl` column is no longer read): a leg belongs to the Pluto action whose order opened it; a leg only closed by Pluto (human-opened, e.g. Formula P1 shares) belongs to the closing action; rolls split by leg; open legs contribute nothing and are reported as `openLegCount`. Uses the shared `legRealizedPnlSql` (net of closing commissions). DB-backed test on the Pluto test DB covers expiry, roll, human-opened close, Pluto-opened-then-closed and a blocked action. The Actions table shows "open" until a leg closes and "(n legs open)" on partial figures.
- **Pluto gap list built 2026-09-28 ("build all you can")**. Plain-language: the pieces that were still missing between the screen and a first paper trade, except the two that need Marcelo's hands (staging setup and the first live session — runbook below). Built and verified (Playwright on the worktree, DB rows checked, API suite 690 green):
  - *Triggers.* The 30 s poll now names a pass after what moved: a ticker's fingerprint keeps the open-candidate part and the held-leg part (rolls, close offers) separable (`tickerFingerprint` / `classifyFingerprintChange`), and the pass row is relabelled `grade_crossing` or `held_leg` with the symbols before the model is called; the Decisions card shows it.
  - *System-checks board.* `PlutoChecksBoard` (all checks as green/red chips with the detail on hover) in a collapsible "Last pass checks" card under the tiles and behind a "checks" toggle on every decision.
  - *Scoreboard.* `GET /pluto/scoreboard` + card: realized and pessimistic totals, hit rate over closed actions, filled-of-sent orders, model-vs-Edge-$ agreement, model cost; refreshed on every pass event.
  - *Events retention.* The agent prunes `pluto_events` older than 90 days once a day (`plutoEventsRetentionDays`, Marcelo to veto); passes, decisions and actions are never pruned.
  - *Blotter link.* An action's outcome badge links to `/trade-blotter?order=<id>`; the blotter jumps to the page holding that order's rows (the order while working/cancelled, its fills once filled — trades now carry `sourceOrderRequestId`) and highlights them.
  - *Market-stress override.* "Allow opens under stress today" switch on the Market tile (confirm on enable, one click off): `pluto_state.stress_override_date` (migration `20260928100003`), `PUT /pluto/stress-override`, audited as `stress_override_changed`, Telegram; the pass's `market_stress` check records the override; clears itself with the Eastern date.
  - *Replay harness.* `npm run pluto:replay -- --latest 20 --model a --model b [--calls 2] [--fixture file] [--json]` re-sends recorded decision payloads to any OpenRouter models and reports schema validity, latency, cost, served model, agreement with the recorded verdict and with the Edge $ top pick, and two-call agreement. This is the prompt-evaluation tool too.
  - *jev-1.13 feasibility, first result:* OpenRouter refuses it on chat/completions ("a decisions model — use /api/alpha/decisions"): modality `text->decisions`, 32k context, prompt $0.042 per 1M tokens, completion free, no `response_format`. Using it means a second client against an alpha endpoint whose request/response schema is undocumented on the public docs (404s) — parked pending Marcelo's call; gpt-6-luna on the same fixture: schema-valid trade, 1.2 s, $0.0006, two seeded calls agreed.
- **Staging runbook for Pluto (updated 2026-10-05, after main was merged into pluto; Marcelo runs; Claude may not set staging config)**: (1) merge `pluto` into `main` and push (auto-deploys staging API + worker + app; the worker changes, so ask first); the 10 Pluto migrations are dated `20261006100001`–`10`, after main's. (2) `heroku run --app iorio-staging-api "node dist/scripts/manage-user.js create pluto Pluto <password>"`, then `heroku run --app iorio-staging-api "node dist/scripts/manage-user.js set-service-account pluto true"` (`npm run manage-user` fails on Heroku: tsx is pruned). (3) `heroku config:set --app iorio-staging-api PLUTO_ENABLED=true PLUTO_API_BASE_URL=<the API's herokuapp.com address> PLUTO_SERVICE_USERNAME=pluto PLUTO_SERVICE_USER_PASSWORD=<password> SERVICE_LOGIN_SECRET=<random, at least 32 characters> OPENROUTER_API_KEY=<key> PLUTO_TELEGRAM_BOT_TOKEN=<this environment's Pluto bot, added to the alerts group>` (SERVICE_LOGIN_SECRET lets Pluto's routed login through while PASSKEY_LOGIN=required). (4) `heroku ps:scale agent=1 --app iorio-staging-api`; watch the Pluto screen's heartbeat; Pluto boots paused. A config change is a new release; since 2026-10-09 it keeps Pluto's state on staging (prod still pauses). (5) Enable one ticker, switch mode on, Resume during the window. Production works the same way (switches only: PLUTO_ENABLED, the agent process, the mode switch, off by default); nothing in code refuses it.
- **Backtest foundation built 2026-09-29 (Marcelo: "store exactly what the model saw, build history, backtest with a different model later" — yes to all three)**. Plain-language: every decision now keeps the exact instructions and data the model was given, every candidate it was offered gets a "what would holding it to expiry have made" label once the expiry settles, and a command replays the whole history through any model and ranks them by that label. (1) `pluto_prompts` (one row per distinct system prompt text, by hash) + `pluto_decisions.prompt_id` + `pluto_passes.settings_snapshot` (migration `20260929000001`); the runner stores both on every pass. (2) `pluto_candidate_outcomes`: per pass and candidate id, the approved hold-to-expiry label — put: premium_bid × 100 − max(0, strike − expiry close) × 100; call: premium_bid × 100 − max(0, expiry close − strike) × 100; expiry close = newest daily bar on or before the expiry (skipped and listed when no bar within 5 days); roll replacements are labelled under the roll's id; close offers have no label. The agent labels once a day (`labelExpiredCandidateOutcomes`), `npm run pluto:label-outcomes` by hand. It is a ranking label, not booked P&L (ignores early closes, rolls, assignment timing). (3) `npm run pluto:backtest [--model a --model b] [--since date] [--limit n] [--allow-partial] [--current-prompt] [--json]`: scores the recorded decision, the Edge $ top pick, best possible and always-no-trade for free, and any replayed models (stored prompt by default) — total and per-case P&L, hit rate, trade rate, cost, latency, and a confidence-bucket calibration line. `typesafe/*` models (pinned `typesafe/jev-1.13` or the `~typesafe/jev-latest` alias, which Marcelo chose 2026-09-29 for the tooling; OpenRouter resolves it and the response names the exact version served, which is what gets recorded) route to the new `decisionsClient.ts` (OpenRouter `/api/alpha/decisions`: a `choice` over the offered ids + `no_trade`, a stand-aside `noul`; mapped onto `PlutoDecision` with the probabilities as the "reason"); the replay harness routes the same way. Tests: formula + extraction (unit), labelling + prompt store (DB), Jev mapping (unit); suite 696 green. Verified: `pluto:backtest` runs (0 scorable cases until the first labelled expiry); `pluto:replay` on the fixture — Jev 0.57 s / $0.0001 and luna 1.2 s / $0.0004 pick the same put.
- **Jev opinion (2026-09-29, after reading TypeSafe's docs and four live calls)**: the decision shape fits Pluto exactly (choice over pre-built ids + no_trade, noul for stand-aside, calibrated probabilities replace the confidence floor, single deterministic pass makes two-call agreement moot, ~20× cheaper and ~4× faster) but its documented weakness is numeric comparison, it gives no reasons for Juan to read, calibration is unproven in this domain, and the endpoint is alpha. Recommendation accepted in principle: shadow mode (log Jev's answer next to luna's on every pass, compare over weeks via the backtest), then at most promote it to the second opinion. Shadow mode not built and not needed (Marcelo 2026-09-29): the stored payloads + labels let the backtest replay Jev later with no look-ahead; the only thing shadow mode would add is Jev's answer as of the day, which matters little while the alias tracks one version.
- **Safety gaps 3–5 built 2026-09-29 (Marcelo: "build 3, 4 and 5")**. Plain-language: Pluto no longer forgets an order when it restarts, it refuses to act while IBKR and the book disagree, and a fill far from the price it expected stops it until a human looks. (3) *Order adoption*: on boot and every housekeeping tick the agent finds Pluto orders that are not final and not watched by this process (`loadWorkingPlutoOrders`, reference rebuilt from the action row by `referenceForAdoptedOrder`), records `order_adopted`, and resumes a watch with the order's original clock, so the unfilled-cancel timeout and cancel-by-close still apply; verified live with a planted 25-minute-old submitted order (adopted → cancel requested on the first poll). The agent also raises the IBKR borrow timeout at boot (the first session-close read used to fail every start). (4) *Reconciliation check + breaker*: new system check `reconciliation` reads the newest *successful* health-check run's `job_runs.details.reconciliationProblems` (no worker change needed; running/failed runs carry no result and are skipped — fixed 2026-09-29, they used to read as "agree"); fails when that run is older than 35 min (`reconciliationMaxAgeMinutes`, Marcelo 2026-09-29: the job skips :00/:30, so 35 tolerates one missed run, not two) or lists a discrepancy, and a discrepancy trips the `reconciliation` breaker (human reset); a reconciliation that could not run only fails the check (Marcelo 2026-09-29). (5) *Fill-slippage breaker*: new parameter `maxFillSlippagePct` (default 5 %, Marcelo 2026-09-29 — since Pluto only sends limit orders at or better than the reference, this is in practice a bug catcher for a wrong limit or reference; migration `20260929000002`, on the screen under Session & breakers); a fill more than that past the reference (below the bid for a sell, above the ask for a buy) trips `fill_slippage` (`fillSlippagePct`, unit-tested). Suite 699 green.
- **Merge parked (Marcelo, 2026-09-29)**: `pluto` is merged into `main` only as the very last step, after everything else is finished. Events retention 90 days confirmed.
- **Combo fills measured on the net (Marcelo, 2026-09-29)**. Plain-language: when Pluto sends a two-part order (a roll, or buying shares and selling the call together), IBKR fills it at exactly the total price we asked for but splits that total between the two parts its own way, so Pluto now judges the fill on the total, not on one part. Before this, every Pluto roll would have tripped the fill-slippage breaker (the SPCX roll read 29 %) and Covered Call entries recorded a meaningless pessimistic figure. `compareFillsWithReference` (executor.ts): reference net = chosen option at its bid plus the combo's other legs (a Covered Call entry's shares at the route's live price, a roll's buyback at the held leg's ask), fills matched by side + multiplier; slippage = (reference net − fill net) ÷ the chosen option's reference value; pessimistic P&L = reference net − fill net; `fill_price` is now the chosen leg's own average (was an average across legs). Other legs stored in `pluto_actions.reference_other_legs` (migration `20260929000004`) so adopted orders keep them. Verified on the real COHR/SPCX/AMAT combo fills: 0 % on the net. Findings behind it: IBKR's docs say Adaptive applies to single-leg orders only, and every one of our 19 filled combos filled at exactly the net limit.
- **Two-part order fills on the screen and in P2 (Marcelo, 2026-09-29)**. Plain-language: for a two-part order the Actions table now shows the option's price implied by the order's total (the other part counted at the price Pluto set), with IBKR's own figure visible underneath ("IBKR split: 6.86") and an explanation on hover; both are stored. The buyback rule (P2) likewise judges "never at a loss" on the price Pluto set on the leg, not on IBKR's split. `pluto_actions.implied_fill_price` (migration `20260929000005`; `fill_price` keeps IBKR's figure), `impliedChosenLegPrice` / `otherLegOrderPrices` (executor.ts); P2: `orderedEntryPremium` = recorded entry + (our leg price − IBKR's leg fill), quantity-weighted over the opening trades, null for legs opened by single-leg orders (keeps the recorded entry's commission treatment).
- **No call-only close on a covered call, for anyone (Marcelo, 2026-09-29)**: it would leave the position unstructured. The close route already refuses it; P2 stays limited to single-leg positions (cash-secured puts).
- **Pluto keeps its shares when they change rows (Marcelo, 2026-09-29, option A)**. Plain-language: when a Pluto covered call expires or a Pluto put is assigned, the shares move to a new position; Pluto used to lose track of them there, understating what it had committed against its budget. Reconciliation now records where each position's shares came from, and Pluto's book follows that trail; a stock position mixing Pluto's and humans' shares counts as Pluto's whole (errs towards less room). New table `position_share_sources` (migration `20260929000006`), written by `ibkrGatewayReconcilePositions.ts` on every stock handoff (leftover row and new covered call) and on a put assignment (the most recent assigned put on the ticker, closed no earlier than an hour before the stock row opened); `plutoPositionIdsCte` in `book.ts` (recursive). Worker file: deploys with the merge. Tests: links asserted in the four reconciliation scenarios; Pluto-book scenarios (covered call expiry and a later call; put assignment vs a human's) live in the reconciliation test file because a pass closes every open leg it is not given.
- **Merge plan with main (comparison 2026-10-05, decisions by Marcelo)**. Plain-language: main has since built its own versions of the kill switch, the server-side order checks, the limits and the order statuses, so the merge keeps main's and re-applies only what is Pluto's own. Decisions: main's order-status model (plus the Inactive/refusal → rejected rules), Pluto follows the shared friction share and its Edge $/grade floors get re-approved, Pluto's orders go through main's live-quote rule, main's unfilled-order cancel replaces Pluto's, Pluto inherits the shared delta band, the order_error breaker stays, Pluto sizing counts other working orders, gap fix 8 dropped, partial-close slices get attributed correctly, the leftover-stock lineage gap fixed, a real service-account login added. Open: where the half-day close is read. Details in memory `project_pluto_merge_with_main_2026_10_05`.
- **main merged into pluto (2026-10-05)**. Plain-language: the branch now carries everything built on main since late September, and Pluto was adapted to it: main's kill switch, order checks, single limits table and order statuses replace the branch's own; Pluto's orders now go through the same live-price rule as everyone's, size around other working orders, and leave unfilled-order cancelling to the platform; the session close (half days) is read from IBKR every morning in every environment; a service account can sign in through the router with a shared secret while passkeys are required. Also: Inactive orders and IBKR refusals end as rejected (or cancelled after a partial fill); partial-close slices record their parent leg (`position_legs.parent_leg_id`), so Pluto's P&L follows the opener; leftover shares split out after a handoff keep their source; prompt v2 describes the configurable friction share. Pluto's Edge $ floor ($30/contract) and grade floor (good) kept as they are under the 50 % friction share (Marcelo, 2026-10-05: more qualifying contracts is fine for now).
- **main merged into pluto again (2026-10-06)**. Plain-language: picked up main's test-coverage pass (the worker's order code now lives in its own modules), the morning digest and calendar fixes, and the app's top-bar centring. Pluto's handling of IBKR refusals and Inactive orders (they end as rejected, or cancelled after a partial fill) moved into main's new order-tracking module, with database tests.
- **Pluto loop and sizing redesign (2026-10-06, decisions by Marcelo)**. Plain-language: Pluto no longer runs on a 30-second timer; it follows Day Signals continuously, analysing a contract only when Day Signals has quoted it since the last analysis (per-contract timestamp), and re-analysing when an order ends, a cooldown ends, a position closes or a setting changes. A price move is an input, never a trigger. Every order commits one standard size: account value × capital budget (50 %) × order size (10 % of the budget) = $50,000 on $1M, 15 open positions max; full/half is gone. Pluto never opens a contract anyone already holds or has an order on (ticker + expiry + strike, puts and calls alike). The model (openai/gpt-6-luna, prompt v3: risk-proportionate yield wording) is called once per decision through OpenRouter's cheapest tier (:floor → flex, ~$0.0002 a call) with the tier recorded; calls are limited by the $1/day cost ceiling only. Jev (TypeSafe) was tested live and ruled out as the decision maker: accurate on single-area scores, unreliable on overall trade-offs and candidate lists; kept for backtests only. An update never replaces a person's pause. Later the same day: no global spacing between model calls and a 5-minute per-ticker cooldown; prompt v3.2 (approved after 5-call-per-variant tests): short-dated near-the-money contracts are welcome when today's move is ordinary for the stock, no elevated vol, no event before expiry, real liquidity and strong edge; abstain is reserved for bad data; each ticker gets a code-computed move_context (today's move in sigmas of the forecast, 1w/1m/3m path, 21d vs 126d realized vol, IV rank). Screen build to the approved mockup is next.
- **Pluto screen rebuilt to the approved mockup (2026-10-06; merged into `main` and on staging since 2026-10-06)**. Plain-language: the screen now shows one status at a time (Off → Stopped → Not responding → Paused → Held → Waiting → Running) with a plain-English headline, the controls that state needs (Pause / Pause-and-cancel menu, Resume, Reset breaker, Cancel the working order, On/Off), a five-figure strip (orders today, capital in use, working orders, model spend, trading window) and a SPY-stress banner with the one-day override; below it four tabs — Live (today's orders, latest model decision with Why/Risks and checks/gates, live activity feed), History (track record, orders, model decisions with every detail, event log, with ticker/outcome/period filters), Tickers (every Shortlist ticker with the readiness columns, the Pluto switch and what Pluto holds there) and Settings (grouped parameters with units and dollar equivalents, unsaved-change bar, review-and-save confirm, recent changes). Technical: API `GET /pluto/state` gained `ordersToday`, `workingOrders`, `unfilledCancelMinutes`, `modeChangedAt/By`, `lastChecks`, session instants and per-symbol book counts; actions carry `exposureDollars` (EXP $ as Positions counts it, negative for closes: put buyback −strike×100×qty, share sale −qty×price, call buyback 0), rolls record the held leg (`fromStrike/fromExpiry`) and closes their contract; passes carry `promptVersion`, decisions `serviceTier`; `model_called` events carry confidence and action kind. App: `src/components/pluto/PlutoPage.css` ports the mockup's `pm-*` classes with both themes; shared Shortlist readiness cells moved to `components/shortlist/readinessCells.tsx`; `lib/plutoPresentation.ts` holds the status derivation and wording. Verified with Playwright at 1440 (dark and light) and 390 against every board, every control and modal, settings validation/save/discard, ticker toggles, filters, column gears and the blotter/Signals/surface links; dev-only fixture script `tmp/seedPlutoScreenFixtures.mts` recreates the mockup scenario. Deviations from the project's generic table rules are the mockup's own table styling (approved). A partly filled order shows the filled part's EXP $ and "2 of 3 filled" (the order's trades are joined on read); cancelled, rejected and blocked orders show none.
- **Disabled tickers (Marcelo, 2026-09-29): the Pluto switch means hands off entirely.** A ticker switched off is not evaluated at all — no opens, rolls, buybacks or share closes; positions Pluto opened there become the humans' to manage. This is how it was already built (only enabled tickers are loaded and watched); orders already sent are still watched and cancelled on timeout.
- **Trivial decisions taken while building (Marcelo to veto)**: market stress (SPY ≤ −3 %) is a per-pass check that blocks new opens and lifts by itself, not a breaker needing a reset; `earnings_calendar_unresolved` candidates are rejected; ~~the ticker cooldown is "not the same session"~~ (changed 2026-09-29, Marcelo: `tickerCooldownMinutes`, default 60 — minutes since the last Pluto action on the symbol whose order filled, fully or partly; unfilled/cancelled/rejected orders don't start the clock; closes are not gated; migration `20260929000003`); ~~Pluto writes covered calls only against shares it already holds free~~ (vetoed 2026-09-28: Pluto may open a Covered Call entry — the open route auto-fills the stock leg at the live price for any shortfall, notional counts only the shares bought); P2 buybacks only on single-leg positions (the close route needs every leg of a covered call); ~~early-close days are a hardcoded list in `systemChecks.ts`~~ (superseded the same day by the IBKR session close above); both agreement calls use the same model with seeds 1 and 2; Telegram verbosity "actions" also announces breakers and pauses; the screen's SPY tile reads the day change from today's newest pass (the API process has no SPY line of its own) and shows "—" before the first pass; the book tile uses last night's NLV, not a live figure.
- **Not built yet** (updated 2026-09-29): staging service user + config vars and the `agent` dyno (runbook above, after the merge); the Jev comparison needs labelled expiries from real passes. Parked by Marcelo 2026-09-29: the veto review of the trivial decisions above.
- **Pluto events and contract labels (2026-10-08, decisions by Marcelo; committed, not pushed)**. Plain-language: the activity log now says when Pluto's trading window opens and closes and when it starts analysing new trades, notes IBKR connection drops, stops logging the same "no order" twice, and writes every contract the same way. Changes: the `no_trade` event is gone — `model_called` now carries `systemConcerns` and `deterministicTopPick` and, on a no-order answer, is written after the no-order action row (the screen reloads on it); old `no_trade` rows still show in the Event log. New events: `window_opened` (first time each day Pluto is inside its window and allowed to act; checked against the timeline so a restart doesn't repeat it), `window_closed` (also "stopped analysing new trades"), `analysis_started` (the opening analysis, once Day Signals is ready), `connection_lost` / `connection_restored` (sampled by the heartbeat inside the window, 2 min grace). Live shows window opened/closed and started analysing; connection rows are Event log only. The analysis loop now stops at the window's end itself instead of waiting up to 60 s for housekeeping (it was starting a skipped round per Day Signals quote until then). `session_schedule` type removed (nothing wrote it). Live shows the category before each title. Labels: one standard on the Pluto screens and in Pluto's API text — contract "SMCI $47 Call · 9 Oct (2DTE)", order "SMCI Sell $46 Call · 9 Oct (2DTE) · 11× @ 0.39", roll "MU Roll $105 Put · 9 Oct → $100 Put · 16 Oct (9DTE) · 2× @ 0.40", close "SMCI Close $46 Call · 9 Oct (2DTE) + sell 100 shares · 1×", shares "AAOI Sell 40 shares @ 101.40"; helpers `src/lib/optionContractLabel.ts` (API) and `formatOptionContractLabel` / `formatOrderSize` (app `formatters.ts`). The close-offer descriptions the model reads changed wording too. Fixed on the way: "Sell SMCI SMCI …" and "Sell 1× SMCI Close …" decision lines, "Model: place order" for a close_position, "Buy back" badge on close_position offers. Follow-up the same day (Marcelo: "update" the shared texts): the limit-price check leg ("AAOI Sell $100 Put · 20 Nov (43DTE) · 2×"), the close check's missing-quote label ("SMCI $152.5 Call · 25 Sep (2DTE)", also seen in the Close modal) and every trade line in the order/position/fill Telegram messages ("• Sell $180 Put · 17 Oct (10DTE) · 2× @ 1.25"; an order's own line adds "limit" so it can't be read as a fill) use the standard too; DTE is left out once the expiry has passed. The candidate_present gate names the candidate in words. Genosuke's Yes/Cancel order cards match ("• Sell $50 Put · 16 Oct (9DTE) · 2× @ 1.35 limit", "• Buy back …"), and the create_position tool description no longer says "Covered Call entry".

## Capture retry rounds + capture back at 10:00 ET (built and tested locally 2026-09-30, not pushed; Scheduler already moved)

**Plain language.** On 2026-09-30 HOOD and INTC came out "Unscored" on staging: the morning price snapshot was taken while the stocks were swinging hard, so the fitted volatility curve missed the real prices. Now, after the first capture and fit, any ticker where fewer than half of its expiries fitted (or that has no snapshot) is re-captured and re-fitted, twice at most, after one 30-second pause per round for the whole list. The last attempt's data is what stays. The capture also moves back to 10:00 ET.

**Technical.** `captureRetryQueue.ts` (selector + DB loader: fewer than half of the non-`insufficient_points` expiries `ok`, or no/failed snapshot), `runOptionChainCaptureRetries.ts` (rounds, one held line reservation, fresh spot per round because `runOptionChainCapture` now takes `{ symbols, linesAlreadyHeld }`), new `option_chain_capture_retry` job row in `run-option-chain-capture-job.ts`. Clock guard and `jobDeadlines.ts` moved to 10:00 ET. Selector on real staging data: 09-30 -> HOOD, INTC; 09-29 -> BE (2/8 expiries ok).
- Scheduler entries moved to 14:00/15:00 UTC on staging and prod (Marcelo, 2026-09-30). App and API wording now says "10:00 ET" instead of "9:30 ET".
- **Deferred (Marcelo, 2026-09-30):** fitting each expiry against its own quote underlying instead of the snapshot's single pre-window spot (INTC refit that way: all expiries ok; needs formula sign-off). Revisit only if the 10:00 ET capture with retries still leaves tickers unscored.

## Short-dated surface fits and rolls on unscored legs (2026-09-30 to 2026-10-01, all committed locally, nothing pushed)
Plain language: a roll on a contract whose expiry had no usable volatility surface was blocked with a wrong message ("no quote"), and short-dated surfaces were being rejected far too often. Three changes: (1) the roll form now prices at the live quotes when the held leg has a quote but no score; (2) each expiry's surface uses the stock price the option quotes were taken at, not the price read before the capture; (3) the surface's forward is now the one the option market itself implies (put-call parity: median over near-ATM strikes with a usable call and put, within 5% of the forward, falling back to (2) when there are none). Re-fitting all 67 stored staging snapshots (619 slices) with the real code: rejected fits 60 → 4, none that were fine got worse.
- **Formula change (Marcelo approved 2026-09-30):** forward `F = K + (C_mid − P_mid)·e^(rT)` per strike, median across strikes; replaces the "spot minus PV of dividends" forward whenever a pair exists. New column `option_surface_fits.underlying_price` (migration `20260930000001`) stores the price the forward is anchored to; live scoring rescales the forward by `liveSpot / that price` (old fits: null → snapshot spot, as before). Stored fits are not backfilled; applies from the next nightly fit. A push to staging runs the migration in the release phase.
- **Capture now stores both sides near the money (Marcelo approved 2026-10-01, committed):** the 10:00 capture also takes the in-the-money side of every strike within 3% of spot (`captureBothSidesWithinFractionOfSpot` in `optionChainCaptureWindow.ts`, opt-in argument of `selectContractsToCapture`; the Day Signals loop keeps the old rule). Before this, each expiry had exactly one call/put pair (the strike nearest spot), so the parity forward rested on a single pair. Estimate on staging data: about +5.5 contracts per expiry, +520 per night (+17%), +57 s on a ~340 s run at 50 lines; no extra market-data lines. The loop only sees the extra contracts where it falls back to the stored snapshot set (a ticker with no live context, or an expiry it cannot window).
- **Watch (first nightly runs after this ships):** the capture's job duration and settle stats; `select ... count(distinct strike) filter (both sides)` per expiry should be ~6 instead of 1; the spread of per-strike parity forwards within an expiry should be well below the 0.1-0.4% seen against other expiries. Earlier note: most expiries had one usable call/put pair

## Partial closes keep their quantity in realized P&L; expiry-audit alert reformatted (2026-10-01, PUSHED TO STAGING: API d1dbf76 = release v104, migration batch 47 + worker deployed healthy; app 5c21819 = v67; prod not touched)
**Plain language.** When shares were sold in several pieces (COHR sold 200 shares as 80 + 80 + 40), the app only remembered the last piece, so realized P&L counted 40 shares instead of 200: COHR's stock loss showed as -$1,560 instead of -$7,792, and HOOD's gain was understated too. The nightly expiry check flagged this as three "needs review" legs and, since 2026-09-30, alerts on them. Now each batch of sold shares becomes its own closed row with its own quantity and the average price it sold at, so the numbers add up. The nightly alert is also laid out one leg per block instead of a single long line.
- **Technical.** `src/lib/partialCloseSlice.ts`: `upsertPositionLeg` calls `carveClosedSliceFromPartialClose` before overwriting an open leg's quantity with IBKR's holding. If the holding dropped and closing fills explain it (whole fills, oldest first, up to the drop), those fills move to a new closed leg (parent's entry price/time, quantity = fills, exit = quantity-weighted average of the fills). `closeLegsNoLongerHeld` now exits at the weighted average of the fills left on the leg (was the last fill's price). Formula unchanged. Applies to option legs too. Tests: `partialCloseSlice.test.ts` + 7 scenarios in `ibkrGatewayReconcilePositions.test.ts` (COHR 80/80/40, HOOD 100+100, fills between passes, unconfirmed fill, drop without fill, idempotent re-run, partial short-put buy-back); the five stock/idempotency scenarios fail on the old code.
- **Known limits.** Needs a closing fill and a holding drop to agree; a drop reported before its fill is recorded (replay after a reconnect) still loses those shares. Fills of a stock conId shared by sibling positions are attached to whichever open leg `recordExecution` finds first, so a sale can be attributed to a sibling that did not lose the shares. Split covered-call option legs (HOOD $118, SPCX $152.5) have trades attached to one sibling only; left as is.
- **Backfill (Marcelo approved, run on staging 2026-10-01 via `tmp/backfillPartialCloseStockLegs.ts`):** COHR stock leg 40 -> 200 sh, exit 279.864 (P&L -1,560.08 -> -7,791.58); HOOD 100 -> 200 sh (15.26 -> 30.52); COIN exit 194.85 -> 194.795 (-165.10 -> -176.10). Verified in the DB.
- **Audit fixes (Marcelo approved 2026-10-01, deployed to staging):** (1) *Blended cost.* When a covered call expires with its shares retained and a put is then assigned, IBKR reports one leg at the blended cost (COHR: 318.82 = avg of 327.49 and 310.15), so the audit never found a leg at strike - premium. `findBlendedAssignmentLegs` now claims such a leg only on positive evidence (entered with the put's close, a handed-over leg right before it at its own entry price, share counts add up, entry exactly the blend or exactly the corrected value) and sets entry = `(heldCost x heldShares + strike x assignedShares) / totalShares` (`expectBlendedAssignmentEntries`). Idempotent. Run on the real COHR/HOOD/AAOI rows copied into the test DB (rolled back): COHR entry 318.8219 -> 322.4955 (-734.72), HOOD 118.60 -> 120.00 (-280.52), AAOI still flagged, second run 0 corrections. (2) *Acknowledge.* New column `position_legs.settlement_audit_acknowledged_at` (migration `20261001000001`): a leg with it set is skipped by the audit entirely. Set by hand only (SQL), no UI. Tests: `expirySettlementAuditDatabase.test.ts`.
- **Assigned-put premium counted twice while shares are open (approved 2026-10-02, BUILT and tested locally, not committed or pushed; ibkrGateway* file changed, so a push auto-deploys the staging worker).** Plain-language: IBKR already lowers the cost of assigned shares by the put premium, and the put also keeps that premium, so an open position's P&L was overstated by the premium until the shares were sold (the audit then corrected it, which was the "-$1,015" alert for COHR/HOOD on 10-02). Now, while shares are still held, the worker stores the true cost whenever it can prove it matches IBKR's own numbers; otherwise it keeps IBKR's value exactly as before and the audit stays as the safety net. Marcelo's IBKR account lot method is FIFO (screenshot 2026-10-02).
  - `src/lib/stockCostBasisLedger.ts` (no new column): each pass rebuild the ticker's lots from `trades` in FIFO order (assignment buys at the strike); verified only if the share count equals IBKR's and the same average built IBKR's way (assigned lots at strike - premium) is within $0.015/share of IBKR's reported average; then entry = IBKR avgCost + the premium still inside the remaining assigned lots. Computed after the assignment fill is saved to the new leg, so no fallback pass. Partial sale of mixed lots: the sold slice takes the lot FIFO sold (only when verified after the sale and the lot differs from the average by more than the tolerance).
  - Same change set: the worker no longer rewrites every open leg every pass (compares at 4 decimals); Pulse positions signature now includes entry price and the capital figures (A/B verified in Playwright: old signature stayed stale over a full 60 s poll, new one updated within one); one shared expiry rule `optionExpiryClock.ts` (an option expires at 16:00 ET on its expiry date) replaces the UTC `CURRENT_DATE` and ET-date checks in the worker and `optionLegRetirement.ts`; `determineLeftoverStockReason` only blames a position closed in the last 30 minutes. The audit needed no change (tests: legs already at the true cost are neither corrected nor reported).
  - Verified: 969 backend tests, app typecheck, and the real verification run read-only against staging (COIN 400 sh and DRAM 600 sh verify and keep IBKR's value).
  - **Payoff card and Recovery Path (approved 2026-10-02, built):** covered-call card: breakeven = the symbol's cycle break-even per share (`position.breakEven`), max loss = breakeven x shares, max gain = (call strike - breakeven) x shares; falls back to stock entry - call premium when the break-even is unavailable; order forms are unchanged (no break-even passed). Recovery Path: loss = max(0, cycle break-even - price) x shares, falling back to the average entry; the modal label reads "Break-even" or "Entry price" accordingly (`costBasisSource`). Verified in Playwright on dev (COIN card shows $189.90 = the table's Break-Even, desktop light and 390px dark, no overflow). The Recovery Path modal itself was not run end to end (needs live IBKR quotes, no leftover-stock position locally); its arithmetic and cost-basis choice are unit-tested. 972 backend tests pass, both typechecks clean.
  - **Payoff chart holds still on ticks (2026-10-19, Marcelo; app repo, committed, not pushed).** Plain-language: when the price sat outside the break-even ±15% window, the chart's price range followed every tick and the curve kept re-animating ("waving"). Now a price within 2% of an edge widens that edge to 5% past the price, the range never narrows while the card is open, and the chart has no animations; between widenings only the Current line moves. Example: break-even $111.69, price $94.95 → range $90.20–$128.44, next change only below $92.04. Technical: app `lib/payoff.ts` (`computeChartPriceRange` with a held range, `computePayoffChartPoints`, `payoffAt` on `PayoffResult`; `computePayoff` no longer takes the price), `hooks/useChartPriceRange.ts`, `PositionCard.tsx`. Verified by driving the real card with a tick sequence: 19 ticks, one redraw of the curve.
- **AAOI Aug $107 put:** (a) DONE by Marcelo 2026-10-01 (`tmp/fixAaoiAugustPutAssignedLotEntry.ts --apply`; leg 376d84e1 entry 104.6532 -> 107, stock P&L 235.68 -> 1.00, verified in the staging DB). (b) DONE by Marcelo 2026-10-01 (`UPDATE 1`; the acknowledgement SQL, run after the staging deploy applied migration `20261001000001`), kept here for reference: `psql "$(HEROKU_API_KEY=$(cat ~/.config/heroku/iorio-api-key) heroku config:get DATABASE_URL -a iorio-staging-api)" -c "update position_legs set settlement_audit_acknowledged_at = now() where id = 'd9c10d04-a44c-4c42-8d51-aee4240d407a';"`. The nightly alert for AAOI stops from tonight's run.
- **P&L % base and feed wording (Marcelo approved 2026-10-01, deployed to staging):** (1) New position field `capitalDeployed` (API `positionQueries.ts`, app `positionPnl.ts` `positionPnlPercentBase`): while shares are still held it is the sum of entry x quantity over ALL the position's stock legs (open + sold slices), otherwise the same as `capitalAtRisk`. P&L % (row, card badge, totals row) now divides by it (SMCI demo: 400.80 / 8,000 = +5.01%, was +24.93% on the 1,600 still held). **`capitalAtRisk` is deliberately unchanged**: it also feeds EXP $, EXP %, Pulse exposure and the close Telegram, which must stay "exposed now". The totals row divides total P&L by the sum of the rows' bases. (2) The "Opened" feed line merges slices of one leg (same contract, same entry price: "Short 5x $300P", "Long 200 sh"); "Closed" lines keep slices apart (each has its own exit price). Verified in Playwright on dev (desktop, 390px, no console errors) and with `positionQueries.test.ts` / `positionEvents.test.ts`.
- **Option slices (Marcelo approved 2026-10-01, built, tested):** `capitalDeployed` for a position with no open stock leg is now the collateral summed over every leg of the same contract (same strike, expiry, type) as the open/latest leg, so a 5-lot put bought back 2 + 3 has base 150,000 while `capitalAtRisk` (exposure) stays 90,000; a rolled-away leg of another strike/expiry is excluded. `positionQueries.test.ts` covers both. The expiry Telegram's "Realized P&L (%)" in `ibkrGatewayReconcilePositions.ts` now divides by `capitalDeployed` too (Marcelo approved).
- **Environment flags (Marcelo approved 2026-10-01, deployed to staging; the staging web dyno now logs `(staging)`).** `NODE_ENV` is no longer read for anything environment-related (it used to switch DB SSL, the cookie `secure` flag and the migration file extension, which made `NODE_ENV=production` look like an environment flag; Heroku gives staging AND prod `NODE_ENV=production` by default and that stays). The environment flag is `APP_ENVIRONMENT` (development|staging|production). Changes: (1) new required `DATABASE_SSL=true|false` (`src/config/databaseSsl.ts`, standalone, not in the eager `env.ts`; read where each connection opens: connection.ts, session.ts, notificationBroadcaster.ts, the worker's LISTEN client, knexfile.ts); (2) session cookie `secure` = `APP_ENVIRONMENT !== "development"`; (3) knex migration extension from knexfile.ts's own file extension; (4) `environment.nodeEnvironment` removed from `env.ts`; server log shows APP_ENVIRONMENT. Verified: dev API boots and logs (development), login cookie has no Secure flag locally; `knex migrate:status` via src (ts) for dev and `--env test`, and via the compiled `dist/src/db/knexfile.js` with NODE_ENV=production (the release-phase path) both work; an empty `DATABASE_SSL` fails loudly and `true` against the non-SSL local Postgres fails on the handshake (the flag is really used); `databaseSsl.test.ts`. **Rollout state:** local `.env` + `.env.example` have `DATABASE_SSL=false`; staging Heroku `DATABASE_SSL=true` SET 2026-10-01 (release v103, release phase ran, worker unchanged); the staging worker's VPS `/opt/iorio-worker-staging/.env` got `DATABASE_SSL=true` appended 2026-10-01 (its old `NODE_ENV=production` line was REMOVED after the new worker deployed healthy). **PROMOTED TO PROD 2026-10-01 11:16 +0800 (Marcelo; release v207 = `ddd5ffa`).** Preconditions done by Marcelo first: `DATABASE_SSL=true` on the prod API app (v206) and appended to the prod worker's `/opt/iorio-worker/.env`. The prod worker's old `NODE_ENV` line was removed BEFORE the promotion (a rollback to the old build would have failed to connect without it; the promotion succeeded, so it did not matter). Result: prod migration batch 31 ran 2 migrations (including `20261001000001`), the worker deployed healthy on `ddd5ffa` (journal: IBKR connected, `order_requests LISTEN` connected over SSL), web dyno up and logging `(production)`. Rule for any future required env var: set it on every app AND every worker `.env` first, remove the old one only after the new code is confirmed running. Other worktrees (pluto) need `DATABASE_SSL=false` in their own `.env`. Laptop scripts against staging now use `DATABASE_SSL=true DATABASE_URL=... npx tsx ...` (no NODE_ENV).
- **Test files run serially (Marcelo approved 2026-10-01):** `vitest.config.ts` `fileParallelism: false`. The reconcile test's pass is global over the shared test DB and raced other files (about 3 of 14 parallel full runs failed, with and without this work); measured serial: 16 of 16 full runs clean at ~14 s (parallel ~3 s). No retries. A fully isolated alternative (one database per file) was not built.
- **Tooltip (approved):** the stock leg row's P&L tooltip in `PositionCard.tsx` now reads "Stock P&L: price movement of the shares held vs. entry, plus any shares already sold (realized, net of closing commissions)" (checked in the browser).
- **Alert format.** `buildExpiryAuditFailureMessage` is a summary line then one block per leg (headline with expiry date, then what needs a person); skipped actions carry `headline`/`detail`. Plain text only, because `notifyTelegram` escapes HTML.

## Orders only from live quotes + faster order-limits check (built and tested locally 2026-10-02, not pushed)
Plain language: a DRAM roll on staging (10-01) was priced from the previous day's closing quotes (limits 1.05 / 0.86, real market 0.41 / 0.28), because the order screen accepted any quote and no live price had arrived yet. Now no order can be built from a quote that is not live, and the check that makes the order screen wait several seconds is quick.
- **Live only (Marcelo approved):** roll, signal and chain order forms block "Review Order" until every quote behind the price is live ("Cannot place now: ... priced from a 10:00 ET snapshot quote, not a live one"); the roll form's dot says "waiting for live quotes" until then. The API refuses to build a Signals order (`POST /orders`, `POST /positions/:id/roll`) whose snapshot has a non-live quote or a live quote older than 15 s (`signalSnapshotLiveQuotes.ts`, 409). Chain-picked orders carry no snapshot, so only the form gates them.
- **Day quotes** are used only if received during today's Eastern session (`loadDayQuotesAsLiveQuotes`); before, a previous close's quotes counted as current until a new snapshot landed.
- **`order-limits-check` took 2-6 s** (also blocked confirm and the review stream's start): it priced every open leg of every ticker, and one leg that never ticks held the snapshot to its 6 s ceiling. Now only the order's own ticker is priced (`computeTickerExposure`), snapshot capped at 1.5 s, unpriced legs at entry price; roll and put forms no longer re-run it on every spot tick. `/risk-limits/exposure` (the Dashboard's allocation card) and `/dashboard/portfolio` took ~6.6 s on staging for the same reason; they now stop 1 s after the last leg price arrives (`settleGraceMs` in `fetchLivePrices.ts`, unit-tested with a fake IBKR connection) and value a never-ticking leg at its entry price, as they did after the 6 s wait. Not yet timed on staging (needs a deploy).
- **Open:** staging ran no option-chain capture on 2026-10-01 (no `job_runs` row, no Oct 1 snapshot), so signals all day used 09-30's snapshot; cause not looked into.

## Staging frontend can sleep when nobody is using it; probe blocking at Cloudflare (2026-10-05, app 7c69398 = staging v89; prod app not promoted; Cloudflare rules live on staging + prod)
Plain language: the staging front-end server (Heroku Eco, sleeps after 30 min without requests) never slept because every open tab asked it "is there a new version?" every 5 minutes, even in a background tab. Prices, orders and everything else go to the API server, which is a separate app and never sleeps, so those were never the cause. Now a tab asks only while it is on screen and you have clicked, typed or scrolled in the last 10 minutes.
- **App:** `NewVersionToast` checks every 5 min while the tab is visible and the user was active within 10 min; also on return to the tab or the first input after an idle spell. The loaded bundle is read from the page's own script tag, not from a first fetch. New `useUserActivity` hook (pointerdown/keydown/wheel/touchstart/scroll, no mouse move). Pulse's top-signals poll now uses `usePollWhileVisible` (it polled hidden tabs too).
- **Tested locally** with a fake clock in headless Chromium (checks at 5/10 min active, none while idle or hidden, one on return, toast on a new bundle). **Not yet observed on staging:** the dyno actually idling with a tab left open.
- **Cloudflare (zone ioriore.com, Free: 3 of 5 custom rules used):** the original `/.env` rule is zone-wide and unchanged. New: block on `staging.ioriore.com` + `app.ioriore.com` for any unknown file extension (allowed: js css svg png woff2 woff ico map) and dotfile paths; block on `api-staging.ioriore.com` + `api.ioriore.com` for dotfiles, `/wp-*` and probe extensions (php asp aspx jsp cgi sql bak zip tar gz env ini log). The API rule is a deny-list because ticker paths contain dots (`/signals/BRK.B`); a ticker whose dot suffix equals a listed extension would be blocked, so delete that word from the rule. Tested live: probes 403, real routes and dotted tickers unchanged. Rule ids: frontend `c9f6a67c…`, API `75f9f1a7…`. Do not turn on Bot Fight Mode for the API zone (Heroku and Telegram callers).
- **Not done (see Optional):** pausing polls/streams when the market is closed.

## Passkey sign-in for production (built 2026-10-05; on staging v135/v136 + app v91 with PASSKEY_LOGIN=required for Marcelo to test; prod has PASSKEY_LOGIN=off and PASSKEY_RP_ID set, code not promoted)
Plain language: on production, signing in will use a passkey (Touch ID on a Mac or iPhone, or a password manager such as LastPass) instead of typing a password. A passkey cannot be phished or reused on another site. The password stays only to set up the first passkey, or a new one after a loss. It is off everywhere until Marcelo switches it on for production, so nothing changes for staging or local use. First time on production: enter your password once, create the passkey, tap it once to confirm. After that it is one tap, once per 30 days per device.
- **Switch:** `PASSKEY_LOGIN=off|required` (required on every web app, no default; the API refuses to boot without it) and `PASSKEY_RP_ID` (`ioriore.com` on staging and prod, `localhost` in dev; changing it later invalidates every passkey). Staging stays `off` except while testing; the Playwright `claude-test` login needs it off.
- **Rules (decided with Marcelo 2026-10-05):** passkey-only; a password never signs anyone in while required: it opens enrolment of a first passkey, or is refused once the user has a passkey that has signed in. Enrolment after the password ends with a mandatory passkey sign-in, so a passkey that was saved but never worked cannot lock anyone out (such a user can enrol again). Every registration sends a Telegram alert. Sessions made before the switch, or by a password, stop working the moment it is `required` (and work again if set back to `off`): no session wipe needed.
- **Genosuke:** `users.is_service_account`; its password login works only without `X-Forwarded-For` (the dyno's own loopback), never from the internet. **Must be set on prod before turning it on:** `npm run manage-user -- set-service-account genosuke_prod true`.
- **Recovery:** `npm run manage-user -- reset-passkeys <user>`, then the password lets them enrol again. No management screen (deferred).
- **Tech:** `@simplewebauthn/server|browser` 14; table `user_passkeys` (migration 20261006000001, additive); routes `/auth/config`, `/auth/passkey/register|login/(options|verify)`; `sessionAuthentication.ts` decides what counts as signed in; discoverable credentials with user verification required, no attestation.
- **Tested:** 28 route tests (WebAuthn crypto mocked); real signature verification end to end in Chromium with a virtual authenticator (enrol, confirm, sign in, reload, password refused, stale passkey, rejected prompt, reset and re-enrol, service-account loopback vs router), desktop and mobile, light and dark. **Not yet tried on real devices:** LastPass (Chrome/Mac, iPhone needs LastPass set as the passkey provider in iOS settings), iCloud Keychain on Juan's Mac/iPhone. Test on staging with `PASSKEY_LOGIN=required` first.
- **Staging state (2026-10-05):** API a940985, app aca976a; `genosuke_prod` flagged on staging; vars set on staging and prod (prod `off`, v217). Staging goes back to `off` after testing (Playwright `claude-test` needs it). Prod still needs: `set-service-account genosuke_prod true` (run on prod BEFORE `required`), promotion, then `PASSKEY_LOGIN=required`.
- **Rollout:** set `PASSKEY_LOGIN` (+ `PASSKEY_RP_ID` when required) on both staging and prod apps BEFORE the push/promotion, or the API will not boot. Then enable on prod, and Marcelo and Juan enrol straight away.

## Order safeguards round 2: kill switch, worker gate, limit-price check (2026-10-05; api bc84c07, app 8340d4c + 1 modal fix, on staging)
Plain language: one button (Risk & Limits, "Halt all trading") stops every order from every source; the trading worker refuses to send an order that skipped the checks or waited over 5 minutes; and an order whose limit price is far from the live market price is refused (thresholds editable).
- **Kill switch:** `platform_controls.trading_halt` (ported from `pluto`; a missing row counts as halted). Confirm and the worker refuse while on, cancels still work. Risk & Limits card, top-bar chip, Pulse row, Genosuke `set_trading_halt`, readiness check.
- **Worker gate (`ibkrGatewayWorker.ts`):** before placing, `orderPlacementEnforcement.ts` ends the order as `error` unless no halt, a stored `gate_evaluation` with no blocks, and that verdict is at most 5 minutes old (a confirmed order used to wait for the worker with no age limit and could fire hours later). The portfolio limits are not re-run there (confirm lock + working-order counting cover it).
- **Limit-price check:** per leg vs its own live mid; a SELL below, or a BUY above, mid by more than max(`price_check_max_deviation_pct` of mid, `price_check_min_tolerance_dollars`) is refused, fails closed without a two-sided quote (defaults 10% / $0.05, Risk & Limits and `update_risk_limits`). At confirm from the pool quotes (`limitPriceCheckGate.ts`, part of `evaluateOrderGates`) and again in the worker from one real-time snapshot per leg (`ibkrGatewayLimitPriceCheck.ts`, request ids 80_000-89_999, delayed quotes refused). A limit crossing a wide spread by more than the allowance is refused: loosen the % if that bites.
- **Genosuke:** the card lists the order as the server built it; taps stay chat-level (no allowlist).
- **Pluto merge:** drop pluto's own `platform_controls` migration (same table now on main) and fix its local DBs first.
- **Not yet exercised live:** the worker snapshot against a real Gateway and the Telegram halt (test at market open on staging).

## Signals spread cost, expired-order labels, fill-time quotes (2026-10-05; pushed to staging)
Plain language: Signals used to assume every sale fills at the bid, but orders go out at the mid. It now charges a share of the half-spread (default 50%, editable on Risk & Limits). An order that expires at the close is now labelled "Expired at close" instead of looking like a failure, and every fill stores the bid/ask of that moment so the 50% can be set from real fills later.
- **Friction:** `λ·(ask−bid)/2 + commission`, λ = `trading_settings.spread_cost_charged_pct`/100 (0-100, default 50; Risk & Limits, `update_risk_limits`). Applies to new trades and both roll legs. The old at-mid fields are gone; the order forms' "expected" figures are the list's own numbers (priority no longer changes them: with a mid limit it only changes how fast it fills). Why 0.5: single-leg limits never fill below the limit, but a fill tends to come after the price moved toward it (up to half a spread below fair at that moment). λ=0 lifts wide-spread contracts into good/strong (6-session replay on staging), a hard spread cap is a cliff, so no cap. Grade cuts (10/5 vp) unchanged, revisit with live data.
- **Covered Call entries:** one net limit; IBKR may split it so the call fills below its bid with the stock cheaper. Net is what makes money; per-leg figures on the position are skewed.
- **Expiry labels:** orders are DAY. `order_requests.cancellation_reason` = `expired_at_close` / `cancelled_by_ibkr` / `not_confirmed_in_time` (null = a user cancelled). IBKR error 202 "Order Canceled" is now a cancel (it used to set status `error` when it beat the status event); a DAY order from an earlier session found on reconnect is `cancelled`, not `error`. Early-close days are called `cancelled_by_ibkr` (market_calendar has no close times). Shown in the Trade Blotter, Today's Orders, order panel, Pulse, toasts and Genosuke's Telegram. Migration relabels history (staging: the 2 `error` 202 rows too).
- **Fill quotes:** `execution_quotes` (key `ibkr_exec_id`): the worker takes one real-time snapshot after each OPT/STK fill (skips combo summaries and replays older than 60 s). Measure the average give-up, then set λ.
- **Unfilled-order cancel (built after the first push):** `trading_settings.order_unfilled_cancel_minutes` (default 15, whole minutes, 0 = never; Risk & Limits, `update_risk_limits`). The worker's existing 30 s poll moves any order resting at IBKR longer than that (counted from `order_requests.placed_at`, set when it is sent) to `cancel_requested` with reason `not_filled_in_time` and sends the cancel through the normal path, so it can land up to 30 s late. Covers every order, rolls and Covered Call entries included; a part-filled order has its rest cancelled. Shown as a neutral "Not filled in time". A fill that beats the cancel clears the reason.
- **Covered Call entry stock leg limit:** the mid of the pooled stock bid/ask when a screen streams that stock (the Signals modal does), else the last price (`stockLegLimit.ts`); not yet checked live.
- **Stuck-order alert fixed (not yet deployed):** the Telegram "Order request stuck" alert counted from order creation, so every cancel of an order older than 5 minutes alerted for the second it was `cancel_requested` (staging HOOD, 2026-10-05). It now counts from the last status change (`ibkrGatewayStaleOrderAlert.ts`, DB test).
- **Not yet exercised live:** the 202 handling, the fill-quote snapshot and the next close against a real Gateway. The unfilled-order cancel worked live on staging (HOOD, 2026-10-05: placed 17:06, cancelled 17:21).

## Pulse Gateway: "drops in 24h" instead of lifetime reconnects (built and tested locally 2026-10-05, not committed)
Plain language: Pulse showed "116 reconnects" next to the Gateway uptime, coloured red, although nothing was wrong. That number only counted up since the worker last restarted and also counted every retry of an outage. Pulse now shows how many times the connection dropped in the last 24 hours, not counting the Gateway's own daily restart, so red means something really broke.
- **Count:** `UnplannedDropTracker` (`gatewayDropTracker.ts`), fed by `handleDisconnect` in `ibkrGatewayPersistentConnection.ts`: every drop counts, failed retries included, except drops 05:30:00 to 05:35:00 UTC (the IBC daily restart). Rolling 24 h, memory-only, so a worker restart empties it. `worker_health.unplanned_drops_last_24h` (nullable), `/system-health/gateway` field `unplannedDropsLast24h`.
- **Pulse:** the Gateway row and the phone Gateway LED use it with the old bands (0 green, 1-5 amber, above 5 red); no row for a worker that predates the column. `total_reconnects` stays in the table and the heartbeat log.
- **Tested:** 7 unit tests, full suite, `tsc`; Pulse on desktop and phone at 0/3/96/null.
- **Prod:** reaches it with the next promotion (worker redeploys then, which also zeroes the old counter).

## Optional / future ideas (not being built unless requested)
- **Contract labels outside Pluto (Marcelo 2026-10-08: "later")**: Positions, Pulse, Signals, Dashboard, Trade Blotter, Close modal, Order Review, Today's Orders and the Day Signals / position-expired Telegram messages still use their own forms ("46C 3DTE", "Oct 9, 2026 (3 DTE)", "Short 11x $46C"). Moving them to "SMCI $47 Call · 9 Oct (2DTE)" is a separate pass; compact forms stay allowed where space is tight.
- ~~Live-stream connection cap in dev~~ **Resolved as a side effect, corrected 2026-09-25.** This item (parked 2026-09-19) weighed several fixes for Chrome's 6-connection HTTP/1.1 cap in local dev, including "(c) multiplex every per-tab stream onto one SSE" as a days-not-hours future option. That option was since built for unrelated reasons — see "Stream multiplexing (built)" above, commit `4341611` — and per its own doc comment (`streamProtocol.ts`), one SSE connection per tab now carries every live subscription that tab has open. The original per-tab connection-count problem this whole entry was about is almost certainly moot as a result (not independently re-verified in dev since the multiplexer shipped).
- Full historical backtesting engine using purchased historical options data (10 years) — cost/ToS permitting. Lightweight forward-simulation (self-accumulated data only) is planned instead, not optional.
- IBKR chart embedding for open positions — superseded by the decision to build our own via lightweight-charts.
- **Adjustment-heavy strategies for Pluto, once fully tested (research only, 2026-09-29, nothing scoped or built)**: candidates picked specifically because they need frequent rule-based monitoring/adjustment a bot handles better than a human — iron condor active defense (roll the untested side for a credit once the tested short strike's delta crosses ~30-35), calendar spread rolling (weekly front-leg roll), diagonal spread rolling, delta-neutral gamma scalping (continuous rebalancing against a long straddle/strangle). 0DTE strategies discussed and explicitly deferred — gamma too explosive for a first automation target. All are new strategy types (code, not config) and none are decided; revisit only after Pluto is proven on CC/CSP.

- **Strategy badge standardisation (2026-09-20)**: one shared `StrategyBadge` (app `components/StrategyBadge.tsx`, palette in `lib/positionPnl.ts`): CC blue, CSP purple, N/S orange ("Unstructured" badge renamed N/S). Used by Positions, PositionCard, Ticker modal alerts, Trade Alerts, Trade Blotter, Cycle bucket badge, Dashboard events; Dashboard history chart and Pulse badges/allocation bar use the same Tabler colours. Spelled-out "Unstructured" text (tiles, tables, legends) not yet renamed — awaiting decision.
- **Shortlist Remove blocked while a position is open (2026-09-28)**: a ticker with an open position can't be removed from the shortlist. Plain-language: removing it would leave the live position without the roll scan and Recovery Path that read the shortlist. The Actions menu greys Remove out with the reason ("1 open position on this ticker. Close it before removing."), and `DELETE /shortlist/:id` refuses with 409 so Genosuke's `remove_shortlist_ticker` and stale tabs get the same answer. `GET /shortlist` rows carry `openPositionCount`; helpers `countOpenPositionsForTicker` / `describeOpenPositionsBlockingRemoval` in `lib/positionQueries.ts`; app gained a shared `pluralize` formatter.
- **Pause polling and live streams when the market is closed (analysed 2026-10-05, not built)**: market-data streams would send one frame then unsubscribe, polls would stop, and everything would resume at the 04:00 ET pre-market start. Plain-language: it would quiet the API at night and on weekends, but it does not affect the sleeping front end (the toast change did that). Needs `GET /system-health/market-status` to return the next pre-market start; today `nextChangeAt` points to the 09:30 open, so a client waiting on it would wake 5.5 h late. Notifications, pulses and the ticker backfill stream are not market data and would stay connected.
